## 4. Static function, every row, interval images and the cutoff

For u,v>0 the displayed f equals x^2/2 on[-v,u], u*x-u^2/2 for
x>=u, and -v*x-v^2/2 for x<=-v. Its derivative is the continuous,
nondecreasing,1-Lipschitz function g. Splitting an interval at -v and u
proves the Lipschitz assertion directly. Thus f is finite, convex,
differentiable and globally1-smooth. Its COMPLETE minimum set is{0},
with f(0)=0. Its true initial radius is exactly |s|.

For every original query, including the terminal, define

    x_0=s, x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i*g(x_i), 0<=i<n_m,
    g_i=g(x_i), f_i=f(x_i), 0<=i<=n_m.

These are all queries of ONE static function. Put y_i=x_i-g_i. Direct
expansion of f_i=x_i*g_i-g_i^2/2 gives, for every ordered pair,

    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+g_i*(x_j-x_i)+(g_i-g_j)^2/2
        =y_j*(g_i-g_j)<=0.                          (4.1)

If y_j>0 then g_j=u>=g_i; if y_j<0 then g_j=-v<=g_i; and if y_j=0
the residual is zero. This proves all remote rows, both orders,
threshold equalities, repeated-query cases and the optimum rows after
adjoining x=g=f=0. The complete Gram matrix is an actual scalar
Euclidean Gram, so its quadratic forms are squares of real linear
combinations. This is not a full-space dual certificate. For general
L>0 set F=L*f; the original steps h_i/L give exactly these positions
and the same normalized payoff.

Globally f(x)<=x^2/2; on a clipped ray the difference is the square of
the distance from its threshold divided by2. Reflection swaps u and v
and changes the sign of every iterate. Hence assume u>=v below.

For a step1+d, d>0, the exact image of[-p,q], p,q>=0, is[-p',q'] with

    p'=max(d*min(q,u), p-(1+d)*v),
    q'=max(d*min(p,v), q-(1+d)*u).                   (4.2)

On each ray, extrema occur at the endpoint or at its included clip
threshold; continuity fills the interval. The first term in either
maximum is nonnegative. Formula(4.2) therefore includes empty sides,
zero endpoints and threshold ties. Let E_d be this pair map and define

    Psi_j([-p,q])=[-p_j(p,q),q_j(p,q)],
    Phi_j(p,q)=(p_j(p,q),q_j(p,q)).

Every complete map is continuous and fixes0; its image is an interval.
The exact pair map is coordinatewise nondecreasing by interval
inclusion and satisfies

    Phi_0=identity, Phi_(j+1)=Phi_j o E_(d_j) o Phi_j. (4.3)

This is exact composition of compatible images. It does not select
independent optimal child histories. No recurrence was executed.

Let r=u/v>=1. The first physical level that can produce a STRICT return
above u from a negative state is

    k_cut=min({j in{0,...,m-1}:d_j>r} union{m})
         =min(m, floor(log_c(r/a))+1).               (4.4)

The value m means no eligible physical level. Since d_0,d_1<1<=r,
k_cut>=2. At r=d_j the level j only touches u and is ineligible for
strict reactivation; the next eligible level, if present, is j+1.

To check the cutoff directly, write a negative query as -w, w>0. A
positive return is (1+d)*min(w,v)-w and is at most d*v, with equality
at w=v. If d*v>u, the exact set producing a return greater than u is

    u/d < w < (1+d)*v-u,                            (4.5)

with the formula split at w=v. Both boundary values produce a tie at
u. If d*v=u they coalesce at w=v. If d*v<u there is no such query.
These are local possibilities, not an assertion that every query in
this interval is reachable from any prescribed initial point.

For a child H_j with all its levels below k_cut, and an actual input
s<=u, the finite-clipping path coincides with the one-sided derivative
max(x,-v) throughout that child: every return is <=u, and a positive
query <=u immediately becomes nonpositive. Induction over the actual
steps proves this guarded comparison. An input above u is not covered
by this comparison. The proof below does not require a one-sided law
above this guard and retains every smaller physical step.

## 5. Exact positive tail of every complete child

For every u>=v>0, every j>=1 and every real s,

    Psi_j(s) <= max(a*v, s-S_j*u).                  (5.1)

More precisely, if Psi_j(s)>a*v, then EVERY nonterminal query of that
child is positive and saturated, the terminal is positive, and

    Psi_j(s)=s-S_j*u.                               (5.2)

Here is a chronological proof. A trajectory hitting0 stays at0. If
the terminal is positive and some negative-to-positive crossing occurs,
take its last such crossing, at a level-k bridge. Its positive landing
is at most d_k*v. Every later nonterminal query is positive saturated:
a positive core query would turn negative at the next step, since all
steps exceed1, contradicting the definition of the last crossing and
the positive terminal. If T is the remaining physical mass, then

    x_terminal <= d_k*v-T*u <= d_k*v-S_k*u.

For k>=1 this is negative by(3.3) and u>=v, impossible. For k=0 it is
at most a*v. Therefore terminal>a*v excludes every such crossing. It
also forces positive initial sign and excludes positive-to-negative
crossings. Every nonterminal query is consequently positive saturated,
which proves(5.2). When the terminal is at most a*v, inequality(5.1)
holds directly. This proves both assertions for all s, including
negative inputs and all clipping ratios. In interval form,

    q_j(p,q)<=max(a*v,q-S_j*u).                      (5.3)

No monotonicity in the clipping ratio was assumed. The terminal need
not be saturated: for example s=S_j*u+v gives terminal v, which is
below u when u>=2v and is still greater than a*v. Every nonterminal
query in this example is saturated because at least the final leaf
remains. Thus the affine-tail assertion refers to queried updates,
without imposing a false terminal saturation condition.

## 6. Joint weak-drift and signed-plateau induction for u>=2v

Assume u>=2v for this section. We prove together, for all j>=1 up to m,

    |Psi_j(s)|<=max(v,|s|-S_j*v/3) for all real s,    (Drift_j)
    Phi_j(d_j*v*b^t,d_j*v*b^t)
       <=(v*b^(t+1),v*b^(t+1)) for all integers t>=0. (Plateau_j)

The proof order is explicit: prove both at j=1; given both at j,
prove Plateau_(j+1), then Drift_(j+1). No unproved parent bound is used to
prove itself.

Base plateau: d_1=beta<1, so the radius d_1*v*b^t is in the common
core. The single leaf maps x to-a*x, giving both endpoints exactly
v*b^(t+1). Base drift: only outputs of absolute value>v need checking.
A positive output>v is covered by(5.2), with displacement ell*u>=ell*v/3.
For a negative output-p, p>v, and a negative input, the input is negative
saturated, so |s|=p+ell*v. For a positive input, the leaf gives p<=a*s,
whether the input is core or saturated. Then

    s-p >= (1-a)*s > ((1-a)/a)*v > ell*v/3,

because a<1/2 gives (1-a)/a>1 and ell/3<1/2. This proves Drift_1.

For the unscaled plateau at j+1, abbreviate A=A_j, S=S_j and d=d_j.
Start with the radius R=d_(j+1)*v=c*d*v. Let[-p,q] be its exact
first-child image. The induction drift and(5.3) give

    p<=max(v,(c*d-S/3)*v),
    q<=max(a*v,(c*d-2*S)*v)=a*v.                    (6.1)

For the equality in the bound for q, observe

    c*d-2*S < (5/4)*A-2*(A-1)
              =2-3*A/4 <=1/2-3*a/4 < a,

using A>=2+a and a>1/3>2/7. Also

    (c-2)*d < A/4 < (A+2)/3,

by d<A/2 and c-2<1/2. Hence

    c*d-S/3 < 1+2*d,
    p <= (1+2*d)*v.                                 (6.2)

Apply the EXACT signed step map(4.2) to this actual first-child image.
Since q<=a*v<u,

    p'=max(d*min(q,u),p-(1+d)*v)<=d*v,
    q'=max(d*min(p,v),q-(1+d)*u)<=d*v.               (6.3)

The first bound uses a<1; the second uses q-(1+d)*u<0. Thus the entire
actual bridge image lies in[-d_j*v,d_j*v]. The second child's Plateau_j at
t=0 bounds BOTH terminal endpoint magnitudes by b*v. This is Plateau_(j+1)
at t=0.

For t>=1,

    d_(j+1)*v*b^t=(c*b)*d_j*v*b^(t-1)
                   <=d_j*v*b^(t-1).

By Plateau_j at t-1 and interval inclusion, the first child's actual image
has both endpoint magnitudes at most v*b^t<v. It is inside both clip
thresholds. The bridge is exactly x -> -d_j*x and leaves both endpoint
magnitudes at most d_j*v*b^t. Plateau_j at t then bounds BOTH second-child
endpoint magnitudes by v*b^(t+1). This proves Plateau_(j+1) for every t.

To prove Drift_(j+1), write

    y=Psi_j(s), z=y-(1+d_j)*g(y), w=Psi_j(z).

If |w|<=v, the drift claim holds. If w>v, the parent version of(5.2)
gives s-w=S_(j+1)*u>=S_(j+1)*v/3. It remains to treat w=-p with p>v.
The child's drift Drift_j gives

    |z|>=p+S*v/3.                                  (6.4)

The child's plateau Plateau_j at t=0 also implies

    |z|>d*v,                                       (6.5)

since otherwise |w|<=b*v<v. There are three possible bridge cases.

Case A: z>0. A negative y could only return to at most d*v, contradicting
(6.5). Hence y>0 remains positive across the bridge, which requires a
positive saturated query and gives y=z+(1+d)*u. In particular y>a*v,
so(5.2) for the first child gives

    s=z+(S+1+d)*u
      >=p+S*v/3+(S+1+d)*v
      >=p+(2*S+1+d)*v/3.

Case B: z<0 and y<0. A negative core input turns positive; therefore
this bridge query is negative saturated and |y|=|z|+(1+d)*v>v.
Applying Drift_j first to s and then using(6.4) yields

    |s|>=|y|+S*v/3
        >=p+2*S*v/3+(1+d)*v
        >=p+(2*S+1+d)*v/3.

Case C: z<0 and y>0. If y<=a*v, the crossing magnitude is at most
d*a*v<d*v, contradicting(6.5). Thus y>a*v and the first child has
the exact affine tail s=y+S*u. Put r_z=|z|. We claim

    s-r_z >= (A+d)*v/3.                             (6.6)

If 0<y<=u, then r_z=d*y and
s-r_z=S*u+(1-d)*y. For d>=1 this is at least(A-d)*u, and

    A-d >= (A+d)/3

follows from d<A/2. For d<1, it is at least S*u>=S*v, and
S>(A+d)/3 because A>2, d<1 imply 2*A>3+d. If y>=u, then
r_z=(1+d)*u-y and

    s-r_z=2*y+(S-1-d)*u >= (A-d)*u >= (A+d)*v/3.

This proves(6.6), including y=u. Combining it with(6.4) gives

    s>=p+(S+A+d)*v/3=p+(2*S+1+d)*v/3.

The value y=0 cannot occur here, and z=0 is excluded by p>v. Since
S_(j+1)=2*S+1+d, these three cases prove Drift_(j+1). This completes the
joint induction, with both signed endpoints retained and all physical
child histories compatible.

For every real 0<R<=d_j*v, select an integer t>=0 with

    d_j*v*b^(t+1)<R<=d_j*v*b^t.

At a geometric equality use that equality as the upper endpoint of
the next cell. Plateau_j and interval inclusion imply both output endpoints
are at most v*b^(t+1)<R/d_j. Therefore

    |Psi_j(s)|<=|s|/d_j, 0<|s|<=d_j*v.              (6.7)

The value0 maps to0. Every real amplitude in the indicated domain is
covered; there is no finite mesh or numerical recurrence.

## 7. Every return is paid through the actual terminal suffix

Still assume u>=2v. Suppose a trajectory on H_m has ANY negative-to-
positive crossing, at a level-k bridge. Its landing is in(0,d_k*v].
If k>=1, the following literal H_k, the first component of(3.6), maps
it to a point of magnitude at most b*v by Plateau_k. At each subsequent
ancestor level l, the incoming point has magnitude at most b*v<v,
so its gradient is the point itself, and the bridge produces magnitude
at most d_l*b*v<=d_l*v. The entire following H_l captures it to b*v
by Plateau_l. Induction through the finite suffix(3.6) proves

    |x_(n_m)|<=b*v whenever such a crossing has k>=1. (7.1)

If k=0, the initial return is at most a*v<v. Its following H_0 is
empty. If this is the final physical leaf, this point is the actual
terminal. Otherwise the next level in(3.6) is l>=1. Its bridge sends
the point to magnitude at most a*d_l*v<=d_l*v; its full H_l then
captures it to b*v. Every further ancestor is treated as just proved.
Consequently the uniform conclusion, including the last leaf, is

    |x_(n_m)|<=a*v                                  (7.2)

after ANY negative-to-positive crossing. Both terminal signs are
covered. Choosing the first, last, or any other such crossing gives
the same legal suffix argument. Repeated upper-clip activations INSIDE
these children were already included in Section6 and have not been
erased or independently optimized.

We now pay the original initial amplitude. If 0<|s|<=d_m*v, then
(6.7), f<=x^2/2 and(3.5) give

    2*f(x_(n_m))/s^2<=1/d_m^2<1/D_m.                (7.3)

If |s|>d_m*v and there was a negative-to-positive crossing, (7.2)
places the terminal in the common core and gives

    2*f(x_(n_m))/s^2<=a^2*v^2/s^2
                          <a^2/d_m^2<1/D_m.        (7.4)

These two estimates cover EVERY initial amplitude for EVERY trajectory
having such a return. There is no remaining cutoff-scale or ratio gap
within u/v>=2. They also cover ratios depending on m, k_cut=m, exact
ties d_j*v=u, return level2, root levelm-1 and the final level0 leaf.

## 8. All remaining histories: exact zero/single-crossing estimate

For completeness the remaining bound is derived here on the SAME word.
At every physical index i let P_i=sum_(t<i)h_t and T_i=sum_(t>i)h_t.
If its level is k, h_i=1+d_k. We claim

    d_k^2/((1+P_i)^2*(1+2*T_i)) <= 1/D_m.           (8.1)

Because the adjacent children are H_k, P_i,T_i>=S_k. Write A=A_k,
d=d_k, M=S_m-S_(k+1)>=0. For some x in[0,M],

    P_i=S_k+x, T_i=S_k+M-x.

Their denominator is (A+x)^2*(2*A-1+2*M-2*x). Its logarithm is
concave on[0,M], being a sum of logarithms of positive affine
functions. A concave function is bounded below by the minimum of its
two endpoint values. The endpoint x=M minus the endpoint x=0 for the
denominator itself is

    M*(2*A*(A-1)+M*(2*A-1))>=0.

Thus the denominator is at least A^2*(2*A-1+2*M). Also

    D_m=4*A+2*d-1+2*M.

At k=0, A=1 and d=a<1/2 imply d^2*(4*A+2*d-1)<A^2*(2*A-1).
At k>=1, A>2 and d/A<1/2 imply

    (d/A)^2*(4*A+2*d-1)/(2*A-1)
       <(1/4)*(5*A-1)/(2*A-1)<=3/4<1,

where 5*A-1<=3*(2*A-1) follows from A>=2. Since also d^2<A^2,
adding the terms2*M*d^2 and2*M*A^2 proves(8.1). Every occurrence,
including the first and final leaves, is covered.

If a trajectory hits0 it has zero terminal gap. Consider a positive
start s and a trajectory with exactly one positive-to-negative crossing,
at index i, and no subsequent return to positive. Every earlier query
is positive saturated, since a positive core query would cross at its
next step. The crossing query is y=s-P_i*u>0 and

    min(y,u)<=s/(1+P_i).

For y>=u this follows from s>= (1+P_i)*u; for y<=u it follows from
s=P_i*u+y>=(1+P_i)*y. The crossing lands at-r, where

    0<r<=d_k*min(y,u)<=d_k*s/(1+P_i).                (8.2)

Every later nonterminal query is negative saturated, since a negative
core query would return to positive. The terminal is -w with
w=r-T_i*v>0. If w>=v, then

    2*f(-w)=2*v*r-(2*T_i+1)*v^2<=r^2/(2*T_i+1),

by completing the square. If 0<w<=v, then r<=(T_i+1)*v gives
w=r-T_i*v<=r/(T_i+1), and

    2*f(-w)=w^2<=r^2/(T_i+1)^2<=r^2/(2*T_i+1).

This includes T_i=0. Together with(8.1)-(8.2), it proves the target
score bound for all such histories at EVERY ratio.

For a positive trajectory with no crossing, all nonterminal queries
are positive saturated. The terminal y=s-S_m*u is nonnegative. If
y>=u, set z=s/u and obtain the exact score

    (2*z-D_m)/z^2 <=1/D_m,

because z^2-D_m*(2*z-D_m)=(z-D_m)^2. If 0<=y<=u, then
s=S_m*u+y>=A_m*y and the score is at most1/A_m^2<=1/D_m.
A negative trajectory with no crossing has the identical proof with
v and absolute positions. Without any negative-to-positive crossing,
a nonzero trajectory has either no sign change, or starts positive
and makes exactly one positive-to-negative crossing. Hence Section7
and this section exhaust every trajectory for u>=2v.

The proof of(8.1) is an upper estimate for a scalar expression inside
this trajectory partition. It does not reverse the accepted lower
bound K_n(h)<=W_n(h) of fact b98.

## 9. Joint small-band derivation and complete all-ratio closure

This section supplies the already known mechanism as a joint premise,
not as a new search result. Put T_band=1/(c-2)>2 and assume

    1<=u/v<=T_band.

For all j, an input s>=A_j*u stays positive saturated throughout H_j
and Psi_j(s)=s-S_j*u. Every physical prefix mass is at most S_j, so
every query is at least u. Similarly, for s<=-A_j*v one has
Psi_j(s)=s+S_j*v. These are exact exterior branches including ties.

We prove the exact capture rectangle

    Psi_j([-A_j*v,A_j*u])=[-v,u].                   (9.1)

At j=0 this is the identity. Assuming it for j, write A=A_j,d=d_j.
The first child of the parent capture interval has exact endpoints
-(A+1+d)*v and (A+1+d)*u, by the inductive central image and the
two exact exterior branches. Formula(4.2) then gives bridge endpoints

    -max(d*u,A*v), max(d*v,A*u).

Since d/A<=c-2 and u/v<=T_band, these are exactly -A*v and A*u.
The second child gives(9.1) by induction.

For every j>=1 and every integer t>=0 we next prove BOTH coordinates
of the plateau

    Phi_j(d_j*u*b^t,d_j*u*b^t)
       <= (u*b^(t+1),u*b^(t+1)).                   (9.2)

At j=1,t=0, the exact step formula gives

    p_1=max(b*u,beta*u-ell*v),
    q_1=a*min(beta*u,v)<=b*u.

The first coordinate is <=b*u because beta*(1-a)*(u/v)<=ell.
For the entire allowed band this follows from

    ell*(c-2)-beta*(1-a)
       =[c*(c-2)-beta*(2-beta)]/c>0,

as c*(c-2)>1 and beta*(2-beta)=1-epsilon^2<1. Also

    (u/v)*b <= T_band*b=eta*beta<1.                 (9.3)

Thus every base radius with t>=1 is in the common core, where the
leaf acts by-a and gives the asserted endpoints exactly.

For j>=2,t=0, put A=A_(j-1),d=d_(j-1),S=A-1. Since d_j>A,
the symmetric input radius d_j*u exceeds both capture endpoints in
absolute value. The exact first-child image, by(9.1) and the exterior
branches, has endpoint magnitudes

    p=d_j*u-S*v, q=u*(d_j-S).

They exceed v and u respectively. The bridge image therefore has

    p'=max(d*u,d_j*u-(A+d)*v),
    q'=max(d*v,u*(d_j-A-d)).

The second term in q' is negative by(3.4), so q'=d*v. For p', writing
r=u/v and using A/d>=T_band,

    r*(c-1)<=T_band*(c-1)=T_band+1<=A/d+1

gives p'=d*u. The ACTUAL image[-d*u,d*v] is contained in[-d*u,d*u].
The second-child induction bounds both endpoints by b*u.

For t>=1, d_j*u*b^t=(c*b)*d_(j-1)*u*b^(t-1) is no larger than
the preceding child's t-1 radius. Its first-child image has endpoint
magnitudes <=u*b^t<v by(9.3), so the bridge is linear and gives
magnitudes <=d_(j-1)*u*b^t. The second child's induction at t yields
both endpoints <=u*b^(t+1). This proves(9.2) for all j and t.

Geometric bracketing exactly as in Section6 proves

    |Psi_j(s)|<=|s|/d_j for 0<|s|<=d_j*u.

For inputs in[-A_j*v,A_j*u] with |s|>d_j*u, (9.1) gives
|Psi_j(s)|<=u<|s|/d_j. Thus the radial bound holds on the entire
capture rectangle. At j=m, f<=x^2/2 and d_m^2>D_m prove the desired
strict objective bound for every nonzero input there. On each exterior
ray, the exact affine branch has score at most1/D_m by the same square
identity used in Section8. This proves(1.1) throughout the band.

Since T_band>2, the band1<=u/v<=T_band overlaps the complete new
domain u/v>=2 established by Sections6-8. Their union is EVERY ratio
u/v>=1. Reflection gives EVERY u,v>0. Therefore(1.1) has no remaining
finite-ratio, amplitude, sign-history, cutoff or enclosing-m exception
inside the stated scalar family.

## 10. Attainment, both endpoint energies and benchmark direction

For any fixed u,v>0, start at s=D_m*u. At every original index i,
including the terminal, let P_i be the actual sum of the first i steps.
Then

    x_i=u*(D_m-P_i)>=u,
    x_(n_m)=A_m*u,
    f(x_(n_m))=D_m*u^2/2.

Thus this ONE static smooth convex function, with complete minimum{0}
and own initial radius D_m*u, attains score1/D_m. The negative start
s=-D_m*v analogously has x_i=-v*(D_m-P_i), terminal-A_m*v, radius
D_m*v and gap D_m*v^2/2. Every row and the full Gram were checked in
Section4. Together with the all-amplitude upper bound this proves the
candidate equality(1.2).

For any R>0, continuity on[-R,R] makes both signed endpoints in(1.3)
attained by actual starts in that interval. Apply(1.1) at each attaining
start, whose own radius is at most R; if a start is0, its output is0.
This proves BOTH inequalities(1.3). Conversely these endpoint bounds
imply the pointwise bound by using R=|s| and the monotonicity of f
away from its minimum on each ray. Thus no endpoint has been omitted.

Accepted931 gives B_(n_m)/D_m<47/50 for every stated m. The candidate
restricted-family optimum relative to U_(n_m)^ZJ=1/B_(n_m) is therefore
less than47/50. This excludes this complete unit-core clipping family
as a benchmark-reaching lower witness on the diagnostic words, if the
joint proof is accepted. It supplies NO upper bound on unrestricted W.
In fact the explicit attainment instances only imply W>=1/D_m, as
lower witnesses must. The full W17/(16D_m) hypothesis and the original
every-n/every-h minimax theorem remain OPEN.

## 11. Adversarial textual audit and exact limitations

The joint induction was checked against the prior known failure modes.
Readback found and corrected an overstatement in the wording of the
positive-tail lemma: only nonterminal queries must be saturated. The
explicit s=S_j*u+v example in Section5 disproves the version that also
requires terminal saturation. All uses need only the exact affine map,
which the corrected chronological proof provides.
The false inequality |Psi_m(s)+S_m*g(Psi_m(s))|<=|s| is never used.
Its root-crossing construction has no negative-to-positive return and
is handled by Section8. The weak drift(2.1) is compatible with that
construction: its displacement allowance is S_j*v/3, not S_j times
the terminal clipped gradient. No objective sublevel replaces a child
image, so the previous quartic-loss enlargement is not invoked.

The proof does not assume one-sided clipping after the first return.
The upper clip may reactivate inside the following H_k. The two actual
signed endpoint bounds in(6.3), and the proof order Plateau_(j+1) before
Drift_(j+1), are the new controls. Cases z>0, z<0 with y<0, and z<0
with y>0 exhaust the drift induction. The exceptional d_j<1 case is
explicitly included; j=1 is its only nonempty-child occurrence.

The cutoff is strict. At d_j*v=u there is no query above the upper
threshold from that bridge, but a threshold touch is allowed and every
formula remains valid. The no-eligible-level sentinel is m, not a new
physical bridge. The smallest eligible physical level is2. The last
leaf is not discarded: it is the k=0, empty-suffix case in(7.2), where
the bound is a*v rather than b*v. Neither the number of returns nor
the ratio may be treated as fixed independently of m; the proof needs
neither restriction.

The initial amplitude is paid twice by an exhaustive partition:
|s|<=d_m*v uses the actual geometric radial bound, and |s|>d_m*v
uses the actual terminal cap only when a negative-to-positive return
occurred. The residual no-return class is separately handled by its
whole-word prefix and suffix masses. A global terminal cap is never
asserted for an all-saturated trajectory.

No numerical test supports any step. Textual counterexample checks of
the small-core, saturated-ray, root-crossing, cutoff-tie and final-leaf
cases did not refute the new candidate. This is not independent or
verifier acceptance. Formal submission was explicitly prohibited in
this allocation. The report remains JOINT UNVERIFIED in its entirety.

The remaining research interface is outside this scalar family: a
full all-function upper certificate, or a richer static smooth convex
lower witness that can reach the original benchmark, is still missing.
There is no residual endpoint inequality claimed open inside the
candidate's all-positive-finite u,v family. Independent positive core
curvatures, more than two clipping slopes and higher-dimensional
functions were not investigated in this invocation.

## 12. Retrieval and bounded handoff

Used skills: query-memory, search-math-results, direct-proving and
construct-counterexamples. The theorem search asked for a sharp
last-iterate bound via signed interval images and clipping thresholds.
Its three hits concerned stochastic ADMM, batch-normalization Hessians,
and generalized Nash algorithms; none applied. Web fallback returned
background on long-step GD but no applicable threshold-conditioned
lemma. The known exact local Taylor--Hendrickx--Glineur paper
`downloads/taylor-hendrickx-glineur-1502.05666.txt` was read at Section2.4
and Lemma2 with both proof directions. No external theorem is imported;
all required scalar interpolation rows were proved directly in(4.1).

All mathematics above was symbolic textual reasoning. Tools were used
for retrieval, administrative text/file operations, shared-memory

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-001245 -->
# A finite-moment obstruction for dependent character measures, including singular limits

Status: **self-contained unverified round-17 joint candidate**. This final
allowed round extends the measure screen and includes the full proof below.
The only correctness predecessors are accepted facts `1252b8823bb5a290`
(necessary ordered interpolation) and `b98bb807d514e80d` (the exact benchmark
and its bound). The previous draft03abbfd5bd957e79 is motivation, not a
mathematical premise. No optimizer, parameter sweep, installation, or fact
submission is used.

The criterion is applied to TWO entirely specified probability laws in
Section 8, including a dependent low-prefix distribution and a common-sign
coarse tail with vanishing full support. It is a necessary-condition
obstruction, not a claimed parameterization or solution of arbitrary Grams.
All positive amplitude laws and all actual common anchors are covered.

## 1. Statement, actual steps, and the exact finite-moment hypotheses

Fix an integer m>=1024. Define

    sigma=1+sqrt(2), delta=1-1/m,
    c_chr=sigma(1+1/m), a_chr=delta/c_chr,
    N=2^m, n=N-1.

Choose ANY epsilon in [0,1/1000000] and put p_chr=1-epsilon. The physical
step word is

    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a_chr c_chr^j,H_j), 0<=j<m.

Every subword retains these ORIGINAL enclosing-m parameters. Equivalently,
h_i=1+a_chr c_chr^(nu_2(i+1)), 0<=i<n, where nu_2(k) is the exponent of 2
dividing a positive integer k. Put

    t=a_chr, l=1+t, u_k=delta c_chr^(k-1) for k>=1,
    D_chr=1+l p_chr, beta_chr=7/10.

Every leaf has physical step l, and a level-k nonleaf bridge has physical
step 1+u_k. No step is deleted, permuted, or padded. Give original sample i
its standard m-bit binary label (i_0,...,i_(m-1)). For a character index
s=(s_0,...,s_(m-1)) in {0,1}^m define

    chi_s(i)=(-1)^(sum_l s_l i_l),
    B(s)=(-1)^s_0,
    C_j(s)=(-1)^(s_1+...+s_j), 1<=j<m.

Take any finite probability weights w_s>=0 summing to one that satisfy
EXACTLY these first and joint moment conditions:

    E B =1-epsilon,
    E C_k=-7/10+4epsilon/5 for every 1<=k<m,
    E[B C_k]=-7/10-epsilon/10 for every 1<=k<m,
    E[C_1 C_2]<=1/2, E[B C_1 C_2]<=1/2.                    (1)

Here E is the finite sum against the specified weights. The map
s -> (B,C_1,...,C_(m-1)) is bijective: (-1)^s_1=C_1 and
(-1)^s_j=C_(j-1)C_j for j>=2. Thus specifying a probability on these signs
specifies every character weight. Section 8 gives concrete formulas satisfying
(1). No conditional independence is assumed in the theorem, and zero weights
are allowed.

For arbitrary positive numbers Gamma_0,...,Gamma_n prescribe real vectors

    g_i=(Gamma_i sqrt(w_s) chi_s(i))_s.                    (2)

The conclusion also holds for any Euclidean realization of the same Gram.
Assume these really are gradients of ONE convex differentiable 1-smooth
function F with a nonempty minimizer set along the actual GD trajectory:

    X_(i+1)=X_i-h_i g_i, g_i=grad F(X_i).

Write R_dist=dist(X_0,argmin F)>0 and F_star=min F. Then

    Gamma_i <=(6/5)Gamma_0 for every original sample i,          (3)
    V_chr:=2(F(X_n)-F_star)/R_dist^2
              <7(199/200)^((n+1)/8).                          (4)

Consequently the ORIGINAL benchmark B_n in accepted fact
`b98bb807d514e80d`, with p_bench=log_2(3), satisfies

    B_n V_chr <7(1+4n^p_bench)(199/200)^((n+1)/8) ->0.           (5)

All constants are uniform for epsilon in the displayed CLOSED interval,
all measures satisfying (1), all positive amplitudes, and all actual common
anchors and realizing functions. In particular epsilon may depend on the
original horizon in any way and may equal zero. The proof makes no passage
to a limiting Gram and assumes no condition-number bound.

This only bounds this measure class on the unchanged dyadic diagnostic
words. It is not an upper bound on unrestricted W_n(H_m) and does not settle
the original all-h minimax question. The conclusion for F=f/L,
g_i=grad f(X_i)/L gives the original normalized objective
2(f(X_n)-f_star)/(L R_dist^2) for every L>0.

## 2. Kernel identities and analytic rational bounds

For a binary difference d=i xor j put d_m=0. The character identity is

    chi_s(d)=B(s)^d_0 product_(r=1)^(m-1) C_r(s)^(d_r xor d_(r+1)),
    <g_i,g_j>/(Gamma_i Gamma_j)=E[chi_s(i xor j)].            (6)

This follows by inserting (-1)^s_j=C_(j-1)C_j with C_0=1. In particular
||g_i||=Gamma_i. At an odd-sample transition comprising a level-k bridge and
its following leaf, the three signs relative to the first sign are
1,B C_k,C_k. The moments in (1) therefore give their correlations

    w_chr=E[B C_k]=-7/10-epsilon/10,
    p_chr=E[B]=1-epsilon,
    z_chr=E[C_k]=-7/10+4epsilon/5.                           (7)

At two consecutive odd transitions of levels 1 and 2, the three odd signs
are 1,C_1,C_1 C_2 and the two even signs are B C_1,B C_1 C_2. Define

    z2_chr=E[C_1 C_2]<=1/2,
    w2_chr=E[B C_1 C_2]<=1/2.                               (8)

These identities apply at every translated occurrence of that ruler pattern.
Multiplying each coordinate by its sign at the first sample does not change
any inner product; the changed prefix variables are still C_1,C_2. Thus no
independence or distributional symmetry of higher prefixes enters (7),(8).

We shall use

    0<=epsilon<=1/1000000, 999/1000<delta<1,
    413/1000<t<83/200,
    1413/1000<l<283/200,
    12/5<c_chr<2417/1000,
    603/250<D_chr<483/200,
    2413/1000<u_2<483/200.                                 (9)

The inequalities 707/500<sqrt(2)<99/70 follow by squaring positive rationals.
Then t>(70/169)(1023/1025)>413/1000 and
t<sqrt(2)-1<29/70<83/200. Also
c_chr<(169/70)(1025/1024)<2417/1000 and
D_chr>1+(1413/1000)(999999/1000000)>603/250. Finally
u_2=sigma(1-1/m^2) lies between
(1207/500)(1-1/1024^2)>2413/1000 and 169/70<483/200.
All remaining bounds follow directly from m>=1024 and the chosen epsilon.
These are finite analytic estimates, including epsilon=0.

## 3. Original pair constraints, with the intervening gradient retained

For the actual values f_i=F(X_i)-F_star, accepted fact `1252b8823bb5a290`
gives EVERY ordered inequality

    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,X_j-X_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2 <=0.             (10)

We use necessary sums of these original inequalities, not a sufficient
surrogate. Every function in the statement must also obey all unused pairs
and both optimum directions.

At consecutive samples with step 1+u, normalized correlation d, and norm
ratio r>0, the sum of the two ordered inequalities is

    r^2+(u-1)d r-u<=0.                                      (11)

For u>0 and -1<=d<=1 this implies r<=max(1,u): the polynomial has one
positive root; its value at 1 is (1-u)(1-d)>=0 if u<=1, and its value
at u is u(u-1)(1+d)>=0 if u>=1. In particular every leaf has next norm
at most its preceding norm, since t<1. This step uses no saturation.

Now take one bridge of excess u=u_k and its following leaf. Let A be the
norm at the original odd sample before the bridge, rA the norm at the
intervening even sample, and qA the norm at the following odd sample.
The leaf bound gives r>=q. The two physical updates are retained:

    X_after-X_before=-(1+u)g_before-l g_middle.

Summing (10) just for the two odd endpoints, and substituting (7), gives
exactly

    [Q_(before,after)+Q_(after,before)]/A^2
      =q^2-u+(u-1)z_chr q+l r(p_chr q-w_chr).                  (12)

Since p_chr q-w_chr>0, replacing r by its lower bound q makes the
right side no larger. Using (7) then yields the necessary inequality

    D_chr q^2+beta_chr(D_chr-u)q
                    +(4epsilon/5)(u+t)q-u <=0.                (13)

All terms involving the discrepancy have their displayed signs. In
particular the last positive term can be dropped for a weaker NECESSARY
bound. Define R_chr(v), for v>0, to be the positive root of

    R_chr(v)^2+(7/10)(1-v)R_chr(v)-v=0.                        (14)

Then every such odd-to-odd transition obeys q<=R_chr(u/D_chr).
This cap does not discard the middle vector: (12) is its exact contribution
and r>=q is a proved inequality on its amplitude.

We need the following explicit consequences of (9),(14):

    level 1: q<=r_low:=59/125,
    level 2: q<=501/500,
    level k>=3: q<eta_chr u_k/D_chr, eta_chr:=9/10.             (15)

For level 1, u_1=delta<1. At q=r_low the left side of (13) without its
positive discrepancy term is at least
(603/250)(r_low^2+(7/10)r_low)-1-(7/10)r_low>0.
For level 2, (14) and the argument for (11) give
R_chr(u_2/D_chr)<=max(1,u_2/D_chr)<(483/200)/(603/250)<501/500.
For k>=3, v=u_k/D_chr is at least u_3/D_chr, which is greater than
[(999/1000)(12/5)^2]/(483/200)>37/18. At q=(9/10)v, the polynomial
in (14) equals v[(9/50)v-37/100]>0. This proves the last cap.

For later use the physical level-2 bridge itself satisfies

    norm_after_bridge <=(15/7) norm_before_bridge.             (16)

Indeed its correlation is w_chr. In (11), at r=15/7, its polynomial is
bounded below by
(15/7)^2-(283/200)(7/10+1/10000000)(15/7)-483/200>0,
using u_2-1<283/200 and -w_chr<=7/10+1/10000000.

## 4. The original directed cycle needs only the two moment upper bounds

Consider original odd samples O_0,O_1,O_2 before and after a level-1
transition and then a level-2 transition. At the first occurrence they are
samples1,3,5, and the intervening even samples E_1,E_2 are samples2,4.
These names are temporary indices, not new iterates. Set

    ||g_(O_0)||=A,
    ||g_(O_1)||=r A, ||g_(O_2)||=q A,
    ||g_(E_1)||=a A, ||g_(E_2)||=b A.                        (17)

The original comparisons in Section3 give

    a>=r, q<=(501/500)r, r<=59/125,
    b<=(15/7)r,
    q<=(501/500)(59/125)<473/1000.                          (18)

Let u=u_1=delta, v=u_2 and form the ORIGINAL directed cycle

    T_cyc=Q_(O_0,O_2)+Q_(O_1,O_0)+Q_(O_2,O_1).              (19)

All function values and the common anchor cancel. The displacements are
exactly

    X_(O_1)-X_(O_0)=-(1+u)g_(O_0)-l g_(E_1),
    X_(O_2)-X_(O_1)=-(1+v)g_(O_1)-l g_(E_2).

Substitution into (19), retaining both original even gradients and using
(7),(8), yields

    T_cyc/A^2 = -u+r^2+q^2+(u-v-1)z_chr r
                +v z_chr r q-z2_chr q
                +l a(p_chr r-w_chr)+l b(p_chr q-w2_chr).   (20)

The two upper bounds in (8) may be used monotonically because q,b>0:
-z2_chr q>=-q/2 and b(p_chr q-w2_chr)>=b(p_chr q-1/2).
By (18), p_chr q-1/2<0; therefore b<=(15/7)r gives
b(p_chr q-1/2)>=(15/7)r(p_chr q-1/2).
Also p_chr r-w_chr>0, so a>=r gives the corresponding lower bound for
the other even gradient. No sign assumption on p_chr q-w2_chr is needed.
Consequently

    T_cyc/A^2 >= -u+D_chr r^2+q^2+C_chr r+E_chr r q-q/2,
    C_chr=(u-v-1)z_chr-l w_chr-(15/14)l,
    E_chr=v z_chr+(15/7)l p_chr.                           (21)

The constants obey

    C_chr>29/25, E_chr>4/3.                               (22)

For C_chr, since u<=1, (7),(9) give

    C_chr >= v(7/10-4epsilon/5)-(13/35)l
       >(2413/1000)(7/10-4/5000000)
                           -(13/35)(283/200)>29/25.

The discarded epsilon contribution in -w_chr-(15/14) is epsilon/10>=0.
For E_chr, the lower estimate is

    E_chr >= -(7/10)v+(15/7)l(1-epsilon)
       >-(7/10)(483/200)
          +(15/7)(1413/1000)(999999/1000000)>4/3.

Suppose q>=2/5. Then (18) gives r>=(500/501)q>(499/500)q. Equations
(21),(22), u<=1 and D_chr>12/5 imply

    T_cyc/A^2 >= P_chr(q),
    P_chr(q)=[(12/5)(499/500)^2+1+(4/3)(499/500)]q^2
                 +[(29/25)(499/500)-1/2]q-1.              (23)

Both coefficients are positive. Hence P_chr increases on q>=0, and

    P_chr(2/5)=216143/11718750>1/100.                      (24)

It follows that T_cyc>A^2/100. One of the THREE ORIGINAL ordered residuals
in (19) exceeds A^2/300. This is illegal for any function obeying (10),
regardless of the anchor and all chosen values. Thus every actual legal
trajectory must obey

    Gamma_(O_2)/Gamma_(O_0)<2/5.                           (25)

This is an all-amplitude constraint. Both intervening leaf losses were
allowed to vary freely and both intervening even gradients were retained.
It uses only the finite moment upper bounds (8), not conditional independence,
strict Gram definiteness, a saturated adjacent inequality, or a limiting
continuity argument.

## 5. Pay the total loss over the entire original word

It is convenient to index the odd samples by r, writing
O_r=Gamma_(2r+1), 0<=r<N/2. Between O_r and O_(r+1) there are exactly
the original bridge of level 1+nu_2(r+1) and its following leaf. This is
indexing only: all the underlying gradients remain in (12),(20).

Let J_0 be the empty list and J_j=(J_(j-1),j,J_(j-1)). The list of these
odd-transition levels is J_(m-1). A block J_2=(1,2,1) first obeys (25)
and then the level-1 cap in (15), so its total amplitude ratio is less than

    Q_chr=(2/5)(59/125)=118/625.                            (26)

For any occurrence of J_(j-1), j>=3, multiply this bound over its
2^(j-3) disjoint J_2 blocks, and the last bound in (15) over the remaining
levels k>=3. The exact counts are

    sum_(k=3)^(j-1)2^(j-k-1)=2^(j-3)-1,
    sum_(k=3)^(j-1)(k-1)2^(j-k-1)=3*2^(j-3)-j.              (27)

These follow by induction in j, starting at j=3 where both sums are empty.
Since u_k=delta c_chr^(k-1), the resulting bound is

    O_after/O_before <= L_j,
    L_j=[D_chr/(eta_chr delta)] c_chr^(-j)
                            Theta_chr^(2^j/8),
    Theta_chr=Q_chr eta_chr delta c_chr^3/D_chr.             (28)

No overlapping transition is charged twice. In particular the first leaf
of the ORIGINAL H_j is outside this odd-transition block and only contributes
an extra factor at most one.

The bounds (9) imply

    0<Theta_chr
      < (118/625)(9/10)(2417/1000)^3/(603/250)
      <199/200.                                           (29)

This is an explicit rational inequality; delta<1 was dropped upwards.
Formula (28) quantifies the TOTAL loss, including every repeated low block
and every high bridge of the unchanged original horizon. In particular

    Gamma_n/Gamma_0
      <=[D_chr/(eta_chr delta)]c_chr^(-m)
                                  Theta_chr^(N/8).         (30)

## 6. Prefix control includes the even samples

First every odd sample has norm at most Gamma_0. To prove this, induct on
the recursive list J_j. For J_1 the cap is r_low<1. For J_2, the norm after
its middle level-2 transition is at most r_low(501/500) times its start,
which is less than one, and the two level-1 edges contract. For j>=3,
the left J_(j-1) has norm ratio at most L_j by (28). Its following level-j
transition has norm ratio less than eta_chr delta c_chr^(j-1)/D_chr.
Their product is

    c_chr^(-1) Theta_chr^(2^j/8)<1.                        (31)

The induction hypothesis controls every prefix in the left block; (31)
controls the starting norm of the right block, where the same induction
hypothesis applies. Initially O_0<=Gamma_0 by the first leaf comparison.
This proves the claim for ALL original odd samples with inherited state.

Every remaining even sample except X_0 lies immediately after one bridge.
For a level-1 bridge, (11) gives even norm at most the preceding odd norm,
since u_1<1. For a level-2 bridge the preceding J_1 supplies a factor r_low;
(11) gives even norm at most u_2 r_low Gamma_0, with

    u_2 r_low<(483/200)(59/125)<6/5.

For a level-j bridge with j>=3, the preceding J_(j-1) and (11),(28) give

    even norm/Gamma_0 <=u_j L_j
       =[D_chr/(eta_chr c_chr)]Theta_chr^(2^j/8)
       <(483/200)/[(9/10)(12/5)]<6/5.                     (32)

At every occurrence the starting odd norm is already at most Gamma_0.
Together these estimates prove (3), including every original intermediate
gradient that was not an endpoint in the cycle calculation.

## 7. True closest-minimizer normalization

The minimizer set is nonempty and closed. Its distance from X_0 is attained:
a minimizing sequence may be restricted to a bounded closed ball in the
finite-dimensional Euclidean space and hence has a convergent subsequence.
Take a closest minimizer X_star, without assuming it is the origin. Since
grad F(X_star)=0, smoothness implies

    Gamma_0<=R_dist.                                      (33)

The exact original mass is

    M_chr=1+sum_(i<n)h_i
         =N+a_chr(c_chr^m-N)/(c_chr-2)<c_chr^m.             (34)

The equality follows by counting the 2^(m-k-1) level-k steps, including
leaves, and summing the geometric progression. The inequality follows from
c_chr>sigma>2 and c_chr(c_chr-2)>sigma(sigma-2)=1>delta,
so 0<a_chr<c_chr-2. Thus the displayed expression is a strict convex
combination of N and c_chr^m, the latter being larger.

Using (3),(33), the actual chronological updates give

    ||X_n-X_star|| <=R_dist+sum_(i<n)h_i Gamma_i
       <=[1+(6/5)sum_(i<n)h_i] R_dist
       <=(6/5)M_chr R_dist<(6/5)c_chr^m R_dist.             (35)

Convexity at X_n gives F(X_n)-F_star<=<g_n,X_n-X_star>.
Combine (30),(33),(35) to obtain

    V_chr < (12/5)[D_chr/(eta_chr delta)]Theta_chr^(N/8)
      <7(199/200)^(N/8),                                 (36)

because (12/5)(483/200)/[(9/10)(999/1000)]<7. This proves (4).
Every radius in this argument is the TRUE distance. If F is an anchored
projection interpolant its minimizers form the appropriate polar cone;
X_star may be nonzero. No inverse-Gram expression for the radius, forced
positive-cone anchor, or origin-distance substitute is used. The cost of
the conservative bound R_dist>=Gamma_0 and the path estimate is visible
in the finite prefactor in (36).

Accepted fact `b98bb807d514e80d` gives B_n<=1+4n^log_2(3). This proves (5).
For completeness its right side tends to zero because the logarithm of
its exponential factor is ((n+1)/8)log(199/200), whereas the logarithm of
its polynomial factor is O(log n). This keeps the original B_n and horizon.

## 8. Two explicit weight laws and an actual vanishing-support sequence

All formulas below are probabilities on the signs (B,C_1,...,C_(m-1)),
converted uniquely to weights w_s by the bijection in Section1. These are
finite real vectors (2), not an arbitrary unspecified Gram.

First, the conditional-product proposal is exactly

    w_s=[(1+(1-epsilon)B(s))/2]
          product_(j=1)^(m-1)[(1+C_j(s)(1/10-4B(s)/5))/2].  (37)

The first moment identities (1) follow by conditioning on B. Given B=1,
each C_j has mean -7/10; given B=-1, its mean is9/10. Directly,

    E C_1 C_2=49/100+4epsilon/25<=1/2,
    E B C_1 C_2=49/100-13epsilon/20<=1/2.

Thus (37) satisfies every hypothesis for ANY epsilon in [0,10^(-6)]. In
particular it covers the original specified sequence epsilon=N^(-1), any
other sequence epsilon_m eventually in this interval, and the exact boundary
epsilon=0. For positive epsilon all weights are positive. The proof is
uniform over all of these choices without using continuity.

Second, here is a genuinely nonproduct low-prefix distribution and a highly
dependent coarse-tail mixture. Choose eta in [0,1]. For b in {-1,1} set

    mu_1=-7/10, mu_(-1)=9/10,
    tau_1=1/2-epsilon, tau_(-1)=9/10,
    pi_b(c_1,c_2)=[1+mu_b(c_1+c_2)+tau_b c_1 c_2]/4.       (38)

These four probabilities are explicit. For b=1 they are
(1/10-epsilon)/4 for(++), (1/2+epsilon)/4 for each mixed pair, and
(29/10-epsilon)/4 for(--). For b=-1 they are37/40 for(++) and1/40 for
each of the other three pairs. All are positive in the stated epsilon range,
and their sum is one. Their two means are mu_b and their product mean is
tau_b, as follows by summing these four values.

Let c_tail=(c_3,...,c_(m-1)); m>=1024 ensures this tail is nonempty. Define

    T_b(c_tail)=(1-eta)[(1+mu_b)/2 * 1_(all tail signs=1)
                        +(1-mu_b)/2 * 1_(all tail signs=-1)]
                    +eta product_(j=3)^(m-1)(1+mu_b c_j)/2. (39)

Here 1_(condition) is1 if the condition holds and0 otherwise. The first
part assigns one common sign to EVERY tail prefix. The second part is a
finite product distribution with full support. Both parts give each tail
sign mean mu_b; their probabilities sum to one. The complete weight is

    w_s=[(1+(1-epsilon)B(s))/2]
             pi_(B(s))(C_1(s),C_2(s))
             T_(B(s))(C_3(s),...,C_(m-1)(s)).              (40)

This specifies every atom. Each prefix has the conditional mean mu_b, so
the first moments in (1) are identical to those for (37). The low joint
moments are now

    E C_1 C_2=1/2-(4/5)epsilon+epsilon^2/2<=1/2,
    E B C_1 C_2=1/2-(17/10)epsilon+epsilon^2/2<=1/2.        (41)

Thus (40) satisfies the theorem for EVERY eta in [0,1], regardless of
how strongly its tail prefixes depend on one another. It is not the
conditional-product policy (37): already pi_b has a different product mean,
and for eta<1 the tail variables share a common component.

One definite original-horizon policy is

    epsilon=N^(-1), eta=N^(-2), standard chronological labels. (42)

For every finite N=2^m all weights in (40),(42) are strictly positive:
P(B=b)>0, all four pi_b are positive, and the eta product component is
positive at every tail pattern. Its full-support tail component tends to
zero at the specified rate, and its low-bit discrepancy also tends to
zero. Nevertheless EVERY positive-amplitude realization on actual H_m that
is legal for one global function has the bound (4),(5). No tuning of a
nonsaturating amplitude or choice of common anchor is left out of that bound.

The exact singular boundary epsilon=eta=0 also satisfies (1): at each
finite m it is supported on eight sign patterns (B=1, four low pairs,
two constant tails). The same inequalities (12),(20),(28),(35) apply
there directly and give the same bound. This makes no continuity assertion
between ambient spaces or between inverse Gram matrices.

## 9. What is closed and what is still open

The proof excludes the two explicit policies (37),(42), all rates at which
their displayed epsilon and eta approach zero, and more generally every
finite sign measure satisfying the moment hypotheses (1). It allows zero
weights, unbounded conditioning, all positive amplitudes with arbitrary
slack, every common anchor, and nonzero closest minimizers. The quantitative
loss pays for every repeated low block and all high bridges at the actual
original horizon. C62 and any proposed Gram-inverse distance formula are not
used. The accepted fixed C59 witness is not constrained by (1) and is not
contradicted.

This is NOT an obstruction to all dependent character constructions. For
example, keep the same law of B, choose ONE sign V conditionally of mean
mu_B, and put C_j=V for every j. Its four atom probabilities are
P(B=b)(1+mu_b v)/2 for b,v in {-1,1}. It has all the required FIRST moments
but E C_1 C_2=1 and E B C_1 C_2=1-epsilon, so it violates the two joint
moment upper bounds. Mixing this law with (37) with coefficient eta=N^(-2)
retains full support at positive epsilon and keeps the first joint moment
strictly above1/2. No legal trajectory, amplitude rule, useful payoff, or
common anchor for that different policy is asserted here. It is a precise
unresolved escape from this theorem's hypotheses, not a counterexample to
its conclusion.

Within the fixed first-moment chart and positive-amplitude convention,
a competitive construction must therefore violate at least one of
E C_1 C_2<=1/2 and E B C_1 C_2<=1/2, or change its labels or the stated
first moments. Arbitrary further dependence of the higher prefixes alone
cannot escape this result while (1) is retained. Changing the two lowest
prefix joint moments still needs a new full interpolation and true-distance
argument; Gram positivity alone says nothing about that missing feasibility.

This entire document is an UNVERIFIED candidate. The original all-schedule
minimax target remains unresolved; no claim is promoted to the fact graph.
Matlas searches returned no applicable theorem, and no external theorem is
used beyond the two accepted facts named at the start. The final allowed
round ends at cumulative17; no extra work or verifier allocation is inferred.

# An endpoint-box exclusion in the full four-step primitive cone

Status: complete joint research DRAFT, not verifier accepted. This is the
one analytical round authorized by TASK.md and master guidance
98d603742ebc21e3. The completed-round count advances from 25 to 26; the
one earlier interrupted attempt is retained separately. No solver,
numerical/model evaluation, word scan, fixed replay, installation, or
fact_submit was used. All calculations below are symbolic proof text.

The accepted inputs are facts 04219554e41d9750, Section 10, and
1252b8823bb5a290 for the interpolation orientation. Drafts
443f4b4325a9901d and 554ec17f7f590bf0 were read for direction, not used
as correctness premises. Every other assertion needed here is proved
within this document. In particular no compact-domain, clipping-branch,
or triangle claim from an unverified draft is a premise.

## 1. Result and exact scope

Let h=(h_0,h_1,h_2,h_3) have arbitrary nonnegative real entries, let
s=sum h_i and C=1+s. There is no full primitive certificate from
accepted fact 04219554e41d9750 with

    C >= 193/25,     7/5 <= h_0,h_3 <= 3/2.                 (1)

All twenty ordered multipliers remain arbitrary and nonnegative, and
positive semidefiniteness is required on all five original formal
gradients. No support, rank, ordering, symmetry, saturation, fixed
capacity, or primitive-prefix assumption is made.

Write a=h_0-1, b=h_1-1, c=h_2-1, d=h_3-1. A stronger quantitative
statement gives an explicit witness alternative. Under (1), either
one of the two middle cut tests fails, giving the explicit scalar
clipped-gradient function in Section 3, or

    C*a*b*c*d > 78744/78125 = 1+619/78125.                 (2)

In the latter case the actual function f(x)=x^2/2, started at x_0=1,
has primitive energy

    V_h < 1-(78744/78125)^2 < 0.                           (3)

This is an exclusion of step data in the full certificate cone, not
failure of a multiplier construction ansatz. It decides a region that
lies strictly outside the previously excluded paired-endpoint region:
every word in (1) satisfies

    d_pair(h) >= 9/25 > 1/20,                             (4)

where

    d_pair(h)=min{max(|h_1-h_0|,|h_2-h_3|),
                  max(|h_2-h_0|,|h_1-h_3|)}.

The proof does not assume bc>=1. It applies to the remaining bc<1
branch too. It is obtained by jointly using necessary cut and quadratic
tests; it is not claimed to be a new scalar test independent of those
tests. No point satisfying all earlier necessary tests is claimed in
the excluded region.

An additional full-matrix necessary endpoint inequality is proved in
Section 6. Its independence from all earlier scalar tests has not been
established. Section 7 audits the sign, rank, zero-step, and congruence
identities in the previous full-support construction interface.

No improving seed or unrestricted four-step nonexistence theorem is
proved. The original all-schedule exact-profile target remains open.

## 2. Full certificate and the actual primitive energy

Indices i,j run from 0 to 4. Put e=(1,1,1,1,1)^T,
P_i=sum_{t<i} h_t, P_0=0, P_4=s. Let T have diagonal 1,
T_it=h_t for t<i, and zero above the diagonal. Define

    omega=(-2h_0,-2h_1,-2h_2,-2h_3,2s)^T,
    D=diag(h_0(2-h_0),...,h_3(2-h_3),-s(s+2)),
    L=diag(Lambda e)-Lambda,
    M=D+sym(LT),          sym(A)=(A+A^T)/2.

The required full certificate has Lambda_ii=0,

    Lambda_ij>=0 (i!=j),       L^T e=omega,       M>=0.     (5)

Here M>=0 means positive semidefinite on all of R^5. For actual
smooth convex data with smoothness 1 and x_{i+1}=x_i-h_i g_i, put

    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2,
    V_h=2 sum_{i<4} h_i(f_i-f_4)
        +sum_{i<4} h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2-s(s+1)||g_4||^2.

The accepted full-space identity is

    V_h+sum_{i!=j} Lambda_ij Q_ij=<M,Gram(g_0,...,g_4)>.

Every actual smooth convex trajectory has Q_ij<=0. Thus (5) implies
V_h>=0 on every such trajectory. We use only this sufficient-certificate
implication; no unproved dual attainment or certificate necessity enters.

For data satisfying f_i=<x_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2, expansion of the GD
update gives

    sum_{i<4}[2h_i<x_i,g_i>-h_i^2||g_i||^2]
       =||x_0||^2-||x_4||^2.

Substitution in V_h then gives

    V_h=||x_0||^2-||x_4+s g_4||^2.                        (6)

When x_4=g_4, this is ||x_0||^2-C^2||g_4||^2.

## 3. Every needed cut inequality, including boundary cases

Fix j in {0,1,2,3}. Let v=h_j, let p=sum_{i<j} h_i and
r=sum_{i>j} h_i. If v>1, set alpha=(v-1)/(1+r)>0 and define

    f_alpha(x)=x-1/2                    for x>=1,
               x^2/2                   for -alpha<=x<=1,
               -alpha*x-alpha^2/2      for x<=-alpha.

Its derivative clamp(x,[-alpha,1]) is continuous, nondecreasing,
and 1-Lipschitz. This is one convex differentiable 1-smooth function,
with unique minimizer 0. Start at x_0=1+p. The gradients through
query j are all 1 and x_j=1. After its step,
x_{j+1}=-(1+r)alpha. Every remaining step has gradient -alpha,
and the terminal point is x_4=g_4=-alpha. Nonnegative step lengths
ensure all intermediate points stay on the stated pieces, including
zero-step repetitions. Every sampled value has the form used in (6).

Consequently

    V_h=(1+p)^2-C^2*(v-1)^2/(1+r)^2.

Any full certificate must therefore obey

    C*(h_j-1)_+ <= (1+sum_{i<j}h_i)(1+sum_{i>j}h_i).       (7)

For h_j<=1 the inequality holds automatically; no division by
h_j-1 was used in that case. Strict failure of (7) is an explicit
negative-energy function, with every original interpolation pair legal.

The actual quadratic f(x)=x^2/2, started at 1, has
x_i=g_i=product_{t<i}(1-h_t). Formula (6) gives

    V_h=1-C^2*product_{i=0}^3(1-h_i)^2.

Hence every full certificate also requires

    C*|product_{i=0}^3(1-h_i)| <= 1.                      (8)

Both (7) and (8) were proved for the original nonnegative-step domain.
Neither uses a second formal kernel or assumes equality in (8).

## 4. A coupled endpoint bound from the two middle cuts

Assume (1). Use a,b,c,d as in Section 1, so

    2/5<=a,d<=1/2,    C=5+a+b+c+d.

At this point b and c have not been assumed positive. Define

    A=2+a,   D_end=2+d,   t=a+d,   S=b+c=C-5-t.

The middle cuts (7) imply Cb<=A(3+c+d) and
Cc<=D_end(3+a+b), since replacing a positive part by the underlying
real number only weakens their left sides. As
3+c+d=C-A-b and 3+a+b=C-D_end-c, these are equivalent to

    b <= A(C-A)/(C+A),
    c <= D_end(C-D_end)/(C+D_end).                        (9)

All denominators are positive. For fixed C>=193/25, the function
z(C-z)/(C+z) has derivative

    (C^2-2Cz-z^2)/(C+z)^2.

On 12/5<=z<=5/2, we have z/C<1/3 because C>15/2; the derivative
is positive since 1-2(z/C)-(z/C)^2>2/9. Thus (9) yields

    b,c <= U(C),      U(C)=(5/2)*(C-5/2)/(C+5/2).          (10)

Put K(C)=C-5-U(C). Both U and K increase strictly on this capacity
range: U'(C)=(25/2)/(C+5/2)^2 is positive and less than 1/8,
so K'(C)>7/8. At C_0=193/25 their exact values are

    U(C_0)=1305/1022 > 51/40,
    K(C_0)=36871/25550 > 36/25.                           (11)

For the first strict comparison the cross-product difference is
1305*40-1022*51=78. For the second it is
36871-36*1022=79. Therefore, at every C>=C_0,

    U(C)>51/40,       K(C)>36/25.

Since 4/5<=t<=1, the sum constraint and (10) imply

    b=S-c>=S-U=K-t>11/25>0,
    c=S-b>=S-U=K-t>11/25>0.                              (12)

In particular the two middle steps are strictly greater than 1 as a
consequence, not a hypothesis. Every zero-step boundary has been handled.

Multiplying the nonnegative factors U-b and U-c gives

    0 <= (U-b)(U-c)=U^2-U*S+bc,
    bc >= U(S-U)=U(K-t).                                (13)

To keep the endpoint product coupled to their sum, use both elementary
inequalities

    (a-2/5)(d-2/5)>=0,      (1/2-a)(1/2-d)>=0.

They imply

    ad >= (2/5)t-4/25,
    ad >= t/2-1/4.                                       (14)

The first lower bound is larger for 4/5<=t<=9/10; the second is
larger for 9/10<=t<=1. Each is positive on its stated interval.
On the first interval the product

    ((2/5)t-4/25)*(36/25-t)

is a concave quadratic, so its minimum is attained at one of the two
ends. The two values are 64/625 and 27/250. On the second interval
the same argument for

    (t/2-1/4)*(36/25-t)

gives endpoint values 27/250 and 11/100. Both latter values exceed
64/625. Equations (11), (14), and positivity therefore give

    ad*(K-t) > 64/625.                                  (15)

Now use (12)--(15), keeping all multiplication factors positive:

    C*a*b*c*d >= C*U(C)*ad*(K(C)-t)
               > (193/25)*(51/40)*(64/625)
               = 78744/78125 > 1.                       (16)

All four shifts are positive, so (16) contradicts (8). If either
middle cut did not hold, Section 3 had already supplied a legal negative
energy witness. This proves the full statement, including (2)--(3).

The capacity was arbitrary throughout C>=193/25. Monotonicity in
(11) justifies comparison to its lower endpoint; no fixed-capacity
slice or attained endpoint of a feasibility interval was substituted.

## 5. Separation from the previous excluded region and remaining domain

For every word satisfying (1), even before testing any cut,

    b+c=C-5-a-d>=193/25-6=43/25.

One of b,c is at least 43/50. Both a,d are at most 1/2, so this
interior shift differs from either endpoint shift by at least
43/50-1/2=9/25. In each of the two possible pairings in the definition
of d_pair, that interior shift must be paired to an endpoint. This
proves (4). Thus the excluded box is disjoint from d_pair<=1/20.

The proof also implies that any actual threshold seed must satisfy

    h_0 outside [7/5,3/2] OR h_3 outside [7/5,3/2].         (17)

This restriction is compatible with retaining the shared candidate
focus bc<1, with the full lower-clipping and lifted-return conditions.
Those other conditions are not dropped: a positive construction outside
the box still has to satisfy every actual legal-function test and all
twenty signs. The present argument makes no claim about existence or
emptiness outside (17), or about the unrestricted asymptotic minimax
question. The excluded step box contains an interval of possible
endpoint data around h_0=h_3=sqrt(2), not merely an equality face.

## 6. An additional necessary inequality from the full PSD endpoint block

Here h is any nonnegative four-step word with
u=h_0>1, v=h_3>0, s>v, and a full certificate (5). Set

    delta_u=2C-(C+1)u,
    delta_v=2C-(C+1)v,
    B_end=s-v.

Then

    2u(u-1)v B_end
       <= u*delta_u*B_end^2+(u-1)^2*s^2*v*delta_v.         (18)

All signs and PSD directions are retained. In particular, simultaneous
saturation delta_u=delta_v=0 is impossible under the stated positive
denominators. No claim of independence from all earlier witness tests
is made for (18).

To prove it, first obtain the constant kernel without an assumption.
The identity sum h_i P_i=(s^2-sum h_i^2)/2 and Te=e+P give
e^T M e=0 whenever the flow holds. PSD then forces Me=0: inserting
e+tau z into the quadratic form for both signs of small tau forces
z^T Me=0 for every z. Its row equations are

    sum_j Lambda_ij(P_j-P_i)=2h_i(1+P_i), i<4,
    sum_{j<4}Lambda_4j(s-P_j)=2sC.                        (19)

Let z_j=Lambda_0j for 1<=j<=4, y_j=Lambda_4j for 0<=j<=3,
m_0=M_00, and m_4=M_44. Put Z=sum_{j=1}^3 z_j. The initial
moment and its diagonal yield exactly

    s*m_0=u*delta_u-(u-1)*sum_{j=1}^3(s-P_j)z_j.

Because s-P_j>=v and all z_j>=0,

    Z <= (u*delta_u-s*m_0)/((u-1)v),
    z_4=(2u-sum_{j=1}^3 P_j z_j)/s
       >=2u/s-B_end*(u*delta_u-s*m_0)/(s*(u-1)v).        (20)

The terminal diagonal is m_4=sum_{j<4}y_j-s(s+2).
Its moment in (19) gives

    v*m_4+sum_{j=0}^2(P_3-P_j)y_j=s*delta_v.

Since P_3=B_end and all y_j are nonnegative,

    y_0 <= (s*delta_v-v*m_4)/B_end.                      (21)

Directly from the full matrix definition,
2M_04=(u-1)y_0-z_4. Its principal 2-by-2 PSD condition therefore
implies z_4<=(u-1)y_0+2sqrt(m_0*m_4). Combine (20)--(21):

    L_0+A_end*m_0+m_4/A_end <=2sqrt(m_0*m_4),
    A_end=B_end/((u-1)v)>0,
    L_0=2u/s-B_end*u*delta_u/(s*(u-1)v)
                   -(u-1)*s*delta_v/B_end.

The difference A_end*m_0+m_4/A_end-2sqrt(m_0*m_4) is
(sqrt(A_end*m_0)-sqrt(m_4/A_end))^2>=0. Hence L_0<=0.
Multiplication by the positive s*(u-1)*v*B_end proves (18).
This proof includes m_0=0 or m_4=0 without dividing by either.

## 7. Audit of the full-support PSD/skew interface used for construction

The purpose of this section is to check the fragile identities in
443f4b4325a9901d, not to claim a second coordinate reformulation as
the outcome. The substantive regional exclusion is Sections 1--5.

Assume s>0. Choose mu strictly between the last distinct nonterminal
prefix P_k with h_k>0 and s. Put phi_i=1+P_i,

    w_i=2h_i/(mu-P_i) when h_i>0, w_i=0 when h_i=0 (i<4),
    w_4=2s/(s-mu), W=sum_i w_i,
    Lambda^c_ij=phi_i*w_i*w_j/W for i!=j.

All nonzero weights are positive. Summing w_i(P_i-mu) gives zero,
so sum w_i P_i=mu W and sum phi_i w_i=(1+mu)W. Therefore
out_i-in_i=w_i(P_i-mu)=omega_i, with this exact flow sign.
Also sum_j Lambda^c_ij(P_j-P_i)=phi_i w_i(mu-P_i), which is
the row moment in (19). Thus M_c e=0; no PSD conclusion follows.
When all h_i>0 the twenty coefficients are strictly positive. When
some h_i=0, their formal coordinates still remain in the matrices.

Let e_i be the standard columns of R^5. Let E have columns e_i-e_4,
i=0,...,3, and let F have columns

    e_j-(1-P_j/s)e_0-(P_j/s)e_4, j=1,2,3.

E is a basis of e-perp. F has three independent middle coordinate
columns and is a basis of span(e,P)-perp, including at repeated
prefixes. An arbitrary homogeneous flow/kernel perturbation Delta L
must satisfy Delta L e=0, Delta L^T e=0, Delta L P=0. Conversely
these equations preserve flow and the kernel. They are equivalent to

    Delta L=E X F^T, X an arbitrary real 4-by-3 matrix.

The signs are precisely

    (E X F^T)_ij <= Lambda^c_ij, every i!=j.               (22)

Let B be the 5-by-4 matrix with columns e_0,...,e_3. Put
R=F^T T B, K_c=B^T M_c B. Since B^T E=I,

    K=K_c+sym(XR).

The constant kernel determines the full matrix as

    M=[K,-K*1; -1^T*K,1^T*K*1].                          (23)

Its quadratic form is (g_0-g_4,...,g_3-g_4)^T K times that same
column. Hence full M>=0 is equivalent to K>=0. This is a full-space
identity, without imposing any equality between formal gradients.

Define p_0=1, p_{i+1}=(1-h_i)p_i and
q=(p_0-p_4,...,p_3-p_4)^T. Direct substitution gives Tp=e and
T^{-1}P=e-p. Since s>0, p and e are independent. Therefore
ker(F^T T)=span(e,p); intersecting with the subspace having last
coordinate zero shows ker R=span(q), q!=0, and rank R=3.
This proof remains valid at zero steps and at h_i=1.

The telescope h_i(2-h_i)p_i^2=p_i^2-p_{i+1}^2 yields

    q^T K_c q=p^T M_c p=1-C^2 p_4^2=:c_quad.

For a proposed symmetric K with q^T Kq=c_quad, let H=K-K_c and

    S_0=[q(Hq)^T-(Hq)q^T]/(q^T q).

The sign check is S_0 q=-Hq, because q^T Hq=0. Every real completion
of the symmetric equation, with no signs imposed yet, is

    X=(H+S_0+Z)R^T(RR^T)^{-1},
    Z^T=-Z, Zq=0.                                       (24)

Indeed A=H+S_0+Z annihilates q, and R^T(RR^T)^{-1}R is the
orthogonal projection onto q-perp, so XR=A. Conversely XR-H is
skew and has value -Hq on q, leaving exactly such a Z. The skew
operators on the three-dimensional q-perp have three independent
coordinates. No rank assumption on K entered.

At c_quad>0 no second kernel is forced. At c_quad=0, and only with
K>=0, the identity q^T Kq=0 forces Kq=0 by the same quadratic-form
argument used for e. Choosing a calibrated PSD K does not establish
any of the twenty inequalities (22). The center, calibration, and
three skew coordinates remain an unsolved construction problem outside
the newly excluded endpoint box.

## 8. Scope checks, unsuccessful completion, and handoff

The direct construction attempt kept every entry of X free and did
not infer PSD from the positive center or signs from PSD calibration.
It produced no actual K,Z,Lambda satisfying the full threshold
requirements. The full-cone witness alternative in Section 4 was the
successful branch of this round. It rules out every K and every skew
completion for the stated step region, not just the center or a
rank-one choice.

The fragile points were checked textually against possible counterexamples:

* b,c were permitted to be nonpositive until (12) ruled them out.
  Thus the quadratic-product sign was not silently assumed.
* The endpoints a,d were coupled to their sum in (14)--(15). Using
  their separate worst bounds would lose the claimed strict gap.
* Both intervals in the concave-product argument were retained, with
  all four endpoints evaluated by the displayed exact expressions.
* C was free. U and K monotonicity, rather than a numerical capacity
  slice, justifies (11) over the entire unbounded requested range.
* Formula (18) uses only u>1, v>0, s>v. It does not divide by a PSD
  diagonal, assert tail heredity, or require either endpoint to saturate.
* The zero-step/rank/congruence audit in Section 7 retains all formal
  gradients. The new exclusion itself forces positive middle shifts.

No counterexample to the stated restricted claims was found by these
symbolic checks. This is not verifier acceptance. Broadening the box
or inferring full four-step infeasibility has no proof here.

Next construction region: C>=193/25, d_pair>1/20, with at least
one of h_0,h_3 outside [7/5,3/2], and every applicable clipping and
lifted test still retained. In the current shared positive-shift
coordinates this means a or d outside [2/5,1/2]. The exact full
coefficient problem is still (22)--(24) together with K>=0.

The round ends with a complete scoped obstruction, not a seed. The
verifier gate remains closed; no result in this document is a new fact.
There is no automatic renewal of the one-round allocation.
