# Global finite circulation and two-way optimum flow: original-space obstruction

JOINT ANALYTICAL DRAFT — UNVERIFIED. Worker max, allocated invocation77
after observed baseline76, ceiling77/attempted78, cap1. No controller
completion/counters are inferred. TWO concrete laws are screened. No
mathematical program, numerical/arithmetic replay, executed recurrence,
trajectory execution, optimizer, formal call, fact_submit, installation,
paid consult or subagent was used. No automatic renewal is allocated.

The result is a scoped failure, not the original theorem. It excludes
every nonnegative mixture of a complete finite-node rank-product
circulation and a common-profile two-way optimum circulation on the
specified inherited base. This includes arbitrary mixture coefficients,
not merely the two displayed choices. The obstruction is a negative
direction of the FULL original matrix. It is explicitly NOT an
interpolable primal run or a lower bound on W.

A second, general calculation shows why Eulerian balance alone cannot
give a free PSD correction: a nonnegative balanced finite circulation
has a strictly positive physical-prefix contact moment unless all its
edges join identical physical prefixes. Every claim and calculation
in this report belongs to this ONE joint unverified draft.

## 1. Target, accepted inputs, and original variables

For every finite nonnegative physical word h=(h_0,...,h_(n-1)) and
L>0, define W_n(h) as the supremum of

    2(f(x_n)-min f)/(L||x_0-x_star||^2)

over every finite-dimensional Euclidean space, every convex C1 function
with L-Lipschitz gradient and a minimizer x_star, and every x_0!=x_star,
under x_(i+1)=x_i-(h_i/L)grad f(x_i). Thus W=2R, where R_n(h)=W_n(h)/2. The original benchmark is

    C_0=B_0=1,
    Omega(x,y)=(x+y+sqrt((x+y)^2+4xy))/2,
    G(x,y)=2x+y/2+sqrt(y^2+8xy)/2,
    C_n=max_(0<=k<n)Omega(C_k,C_(n-k-1)),
    B_n=max_(0<=k<n)G(C_k,B_(n-k-1)), U_n^ZJ=1/B_n.        (1)

All splits k are integers. The original question is whether
lim_n B_n inf_(h in [0,infinity)^n)W_n(h)=1. No schedule restriction
in the present certificate experiment is a restriction of that target.

Fix ONE enclosing m>=1536. Define

    lambda=1+sqrt(2), epsilon=1/(4m), beta=1-epsilon,
    c=lambda(1+epsilon), a=beta/c,
    Hword_0=(), Hword_(j+1)=(Hword_j,1+a*c^j,Hword_j),
    n_j=2^j-1, S_j=sum Hword_j, A_j=1+S_j,
    d_j=a*c^j, D_j=2A_j-1, t_j=d_j/A_j, tstar=1/lambda. (2)

All children retain these SAME m and epsilon. The final actual word is
h=Hword_m=H_(m,1/4), with length n=n_m and D=D_m. The auxiliary symbol
Hword is distinct from a conjugate quadratic in fact1252b8823bb5a290.

Accepted fact1252b8823bb5a290 was read in full. It supplies the exact
smooth-convex interpolation convention, full formal-space certificate
interpretation, and incoming-terminal-free selection theorem. The
present circulation may add incoming terminal rows, and no selected
terminal support theorem is misapplied to the altered array.
Accepted fact04219554e41d9750, Sections10–11, supplies the complete
primitive/objective conventions. Accepted fact5fd396eba75651e7,
Section10, supplies only the unconditional benchmark consequence

    B_(n_m)/D_m < 237/250, every m>=1536.                 (3)

Therefore a genuine full PSD certificate at q=21/(20D) would imply
B_(n_m)W_(n_m)(h)<4977/5000, a fixed relative gap. Neither new law
has such a certificate. In fact both fail at EVERY real q.

The complete previous joint report d031c723cebd5dde was read as
unverified awareness. No result from it is adopted as a premise by
citation. The base definitions and every needed identity are given
and derived within this joint draft. TASK.md, guidanceff3d21d24d20197e,
and plan1fa89a2e348197ec govern this invocation. Latest guidance
58e2501222fb10fe was read and explicitly leaves this allocation unchanged.

Normalize L=1 and the optimum jet to x_star=g_star=f_star=0. Keep
EVERY original independent formal vector (x_0,g_0,...,g_n), including
every zero-step query if present. Put h_n=0 and

    x_i=x_0-sum_(t<i)h_t g_t,
    P_i=sum_(t<i)h_t, S=sum h, D=1+2S,
    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2.          (4)

Actual legal samples have Q_ij<=0 by fact1252b8823bb5a290. The general
algebra below allows all finite nonnegative words: zero, huge and
unordered steps included. The eventual failure claim concerns only(2).

## 2. Complete matrix and all endpoint charges

Let K_ij>=0 be all ordered finite-node multipliers, K_ii=0. Let
nu_i>=0 multiply Q_(i,star), and xiCert_i>=0 multiply Q_(star,i).
Full value cancellation is

    sum_j K_ij-sum_j K_ji+nu_i-xiCert_i=2*1_(i=n).           (5)

No original row is omitted from the array; an unspecified coefficient
is explicitly zero. Once(5) holds, the full polynomial is

    M(q)=q||x_0||^2-2f_n+sum K_ij Q_ij
                        +sum nu_i Q_(i,star)+sum xiCert_i Q_(star,i)
        =q||x_0||^2-sum nu_i<x_0,g_i>+Cgrad(g).           (6)

Writing out_i=nu_i+sum_j K_ij, every entry of its gradient matrix is

    C_ii=out_i-1_(i=n)-h_i sum_(v>i)K_iv,
    2C_ij=-(K_ij+K_ji)-h_j sum_(v>j)K_iv
                         +h_i[nu_j+sum_(v<=i)K_jv], i<j. (7)

Half the total incident mass is out_i-1_(i=n), by(5). The source
position in a forward row supplies the diagonal -h_i K_iv. For i<j,
the gradient-difference terms give -(K_ij+K_ji), source i's later
destinations supply the second term, and source j's earlier
destinations and outgoing optimum row supply the last term. Thus(7)
derives ALL entries, including the independent terminal diagonal and
initial mixed column -nu/2. For q>0 the entire PSD condition is

    C - nu nu^T/(4q) >= 0.                              (8)

Let e be the all-ones column in R^(n+1), csum=Ce, Xin=sum xiCert_i,
Ein=sum(1+P_i)xiCert_i. Summing(5) gives sum nu_i=2+Xin. Weighting(5) by
P_i and summing yields the exact common-gradient block

    M(x_0=r,g=t e)=q r^2-(2+Xin)rt+(D+Ein)t^2.             (9)

For completeness the finite position contribution is
sum K_ij(P_i-P_j)=2S-sum(nu_i-xiCert_i)P_i; inserting the optimum
contributions gives e^T C e=D+Ein. Nothing is discarded at n or star.

The one-sided Huber function Hub_1 is 0 on z<=0, z^2/2 on[0,1],
and z-1/2 on z>=1. Its full minimum set is (-infinity,0]. Starting
at D gives all gradients1 and positions D-P_i>=1. Its OWN closest
radius is D. On this actual static trajectory, all finite and outgoing
optimum residuals vanish, while Q_(star,i)=-(2S-P_i). Consequently

    M(D,e)=(qD-1)D-Hprice,
    Hprice=sum_i(2S-P_i)xiCert_i=D*Xin-Ein.                    (10)

This is one WHOLE-WORD Huber charge. It is not renewed at each node.
For a transverse column v with e^T v=0, write nuv=nu^T v and csv=csum^T v.
If Delta=q(D+Ein)-(2+Xin)^2/4>0, completing both x_0 and the common
gradient gives the exact additional expenditure

    Price(v)=[(D+Ein)nuv^2/4-(2+Xin)nuv*csv/2+q*csv^2]/Delta. (11)

Indeed the common block is [[q,-(2+Xin)/2],[-(2+Xin)/2,D+Ein]],
and its coupling to v is (-nuv/2,csv). Inverting this 2-by-2 matrix
gives(11). Full PSD requires v^T C v>=Price(v) for every such v,
in addition to PSD of the common block. If that block is singular,
its kernel must annihilate every coupling. These are full-space
conditions, not claims that the charges have been paid.

## 3. Explicit inherited base on the actual hierarchy

Here is the base used in the experiment. These definitions fix every
coefficient without a feasibility oracle. Summing the finite geometric
series in the word definition gives

    v_h=a/(c-2),
    A_j=v_h c^j+(1-v_h)2^j,
    t_j=(c-2)/[1+(1/v_h-1)(2/c)^j].                    (12)

Since c(c-2)>1>beta, 0<v_h<1. Hence t_j increases and t_j<c-2<1/2.
Put b=1+a, the first physical step. Direct expansion of(2) gives

    b=sqrt(2)-2epsilon/[lambda(1+epsilon)],
    A_1=lambda-2epsilon/[lambda(1+epsilon)], d_1=1-epsilon,
    A_2=lambda^2-epsilon[1+4/(lambda(1+epsilon))],
    d_2=lambda(1-epsilon^2),
    lambda d_2-A_2
       =epsilon[1+4/(lambda(1+epsilon))-lambda^2 epsilon]. (13)

The last bracket is positive since lambda^2<6 and epsilon<=1/6144.
Also lambda d_1-A_1=-epsilon[lambda-2/(lambda(1+epsilon))]<0.
It follows that

    t_0,t_1<tstar<t_2,
    0<t_j-tstar<lambda epsilon for j>=2,
    A_j>=2^j, 7/5<b<sqrt(2), A_1>12/5, A_2>5.          (14)

For A_1 use lambda>241/100 and 2epsilon/[lambda(1+epsilon)]<epsilon;
then A_2=2A_1+1-epsilon>5. The scalar estimates in this proof are
written inequalities, not machine evaluations of any hierarchy.

Define a primitive array Lambda^j on0..n_j, starting Lambda^0=0.
At a join of two Hword_j copies, let p=n_j be the bridge, z=n_(j+1)
the terminal, and i range over nonterminal queries of the right child.
Embed the left array with weight1 and the right array with weight k_j;
add precisely

    i->p: u_j h_i,  i->z: v_j h_i,  z->p: w_j,  p->z: z_j,
    u_j=2lambda,
    v_j=[2lambda d_j+2(A_j+d_j)]/A_j,
    k_j=2+lambda+(lambda+1)t_j,
    w_j=4A_j+2d_j-2lambda(A_j-1), z_j=2A_j.             (15)

The unrolled coefficient of any original ordered pair is the finite
sum of these four indicators over every dyadic node, weighted by the
product of k_l over precisely the ancestors whose right child contains
that node. Left ancestor weights are1. Repeated pairs are summed.
This finite sum/product is the intended explicit formula; no recurrence
has been executed. All entries are nonnegative. For w_j, write
w_j=2lambda+2(d_j-A_j/lambda); use(14) at j>=2, w_0=4+2a,
and w_1>2lambda-2. The identities
u_j+v_j=2(k_j-1) and u_j(A_j-1)+w_j-z_j=2(A_j+d_j)
prove primitive divergence -2h_i at each nonterminal query and
2(A_(j+1)-1) at the terminal.

Define

    V_j=2sum_(i<n_j)h_i(f_i-f_(n_j))
         +sum_(i<n_j)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2
         -A_j(A_j-1)||g_(n_j)||^2,
    Psrc_j=V_j+sum Lambda^j_iv Q_iv.                    (16)

At a join set r_R=sum_(i right,i<z)h_i g_i,
Y=r_R-(A_j-1)g_z and Z=g_p-g_z. The four weighted-row types before
their scalar multipliers in(15), with value parts suppressed only
after their stated divergence cancels them, have gradient parts

    d_j<r_R,g_p>+||r_R||^2/2+sum h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2/2
                                            +(A_j-1)||g_p||^2/2;
    -||r_R||^2/2+sum h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2/2
                              +(A_j-1)||g_z||^2/2-<r_R,g_z>;
    d_j<g_z,g_p>+<g_z,r_R>+(||g_p||^2+||g_z||^2)/2;
    -(d_j+1/2)||g_p||^2+||g_z||^2/2-<g_p,r_R>-<g_p,g_z>.

Collecting r_R^2,r_R*g_p,r_R*g_z and the diagonals gives the full identity

    Psrc_(j+1)=Psrc_j^left+k_j Psrc_j^right
      +(tstar-t_j)[(lambda+1)||Y||^2-2lambda A_j<Y,Z>
                           +A_j^2(2+tstar+t_j)||Z||^2]. (17)

For example the three listed coefficients before adding V and children
are(u_j-v_j)/2, u_j d_j-z_j, w_j-v_j. Substitution of(15) gives(17).
The bracket is positive definite: its determinant divided by A_j^2
is (lambda+1)(2+tstar+t_j)-lambda^2>=lambda>0.
Thus positive epsilon does NOT make these primitive children PSD.

Define objective arrays Kbase^r and mubase^r with Kbase^0=0,
mubase^0_0=2, and zero incoming optimum rows. At a join of two depth-j
children put A=S_j, bjoin=1+d_j, Dp=D_(j+1), Dc=D_j, and

    s_j=(A+bjoin)/(3A+2bjoin), theta_j=1-s_j.

With maps L_l=l and R_i=n_j+1+i, let

    Kbase_(L_l,L_v)=Lambda^j_lv/Dp,
    Kbase_(R_i,R_v)=Kbase^j_iv,
    Kbase_(R_i,p)=s_j mubase^j_i,
    mubase_(L_l)=2h_l/Dp for l<n_j,
    mubase_p=2s_j-2A/Dp,
    mubase_(R_i)=theta_j mubase^j_i.                    (18)

All other entries are zero. The bridge optimum coefficient is positive
because (A+bjoin)Dp-A(3A+2bjoin)=A^2+4A*bjoin+2bjoin^2+A+bjoin>0.
Here s_0=1/2 and 1/3<s_j<1/2 for j>=1. Each right query has unchanged
flow since s_j+theta_j=1. Left primitive flow is canceled by its
displayed optimum rows and the bridge row. Thus(5) holds and
sum mubase=2. The base has no incoming terminal row.

To make(18) an explicit finite formula, in a depth-r word take the
right-spine node of depth l at offset o_l=2^r-2^l and bridge
p_l=n_r-2^(l-1), for1<=l<=r. Include Lambda^(l-1)/D_l on
[o_l,p_l]. For each of that node's right queries add
s_(l-1)mubase^(l-1)_i into p_l. These are all finite rows.
The optimum weight of a left nonterminal i at that node is

    (2h_i/D_l) product_(t=l)^(r-1)theta_t;

the optimum weight at p_l is

    [2s_(l-1)-2S_(l-1)/D_l] product_(t=l)^(r-1)theta_t;

and its final optimum weight is 2product_(t=0)^(r-1)theta_t.
Each query is in exactly one such left block, bridge, or final category.
The same explicit product formula within the relevant right child
specifies every weight used in a spine edge. Together with(15), this
gives every base coefficient as a finite sum/product on original indices.

Write Cbase^j for its gradient matrix and Mbase_j(q) for(6). At a join,
let T_R=sum_i mubase^j_i g_(R_i), x_p=x_0-sum_(l<n_j)h_l g_(L_l),
Rchild_R=Cbase^j(g_R)-Dc||T_R||^2/4, and
kappa_j=Dc-Dp theta_j^2. Expanding all original rows(18) gives

    Mbase_(j+1)(1/Dp)=Psrc_j(g_L)/Dp+Rchild_R
      +||x_p+(A-Dp s_j)g_p-(Dp theta_j/2)T_R||^2/Dp
      +(kappa_j/4)||T_R-2g_p||^2.                    (19)

To check the completion, after removing the source and Rchild, the
remaining polynomial before completing squares is

    ||x_p||^2/Dp+2(A/Dp-s_j)<x_p,g_p>
    +(A^2/Dp+2s_j)||g_p||^2-theta_j<x_p,T_R>
    +(bjoin-s_j)<g_p,T_R>+Dc||T_R||^2/4.

Expanding the two squares in(19) reproduces this polynomial by
s_j(3A+2bjoin)=A+bjoin. No child PSD is asserted at epsilon>0.
The common-gradient contact identity is

    Cbase^j e=D_j mubase^j/2.                         (20)

Indeed Psrc is zero with zero derivative at all-common gradients by(17).
At x_0=Dp and all gradients1, x_p=Dp-A and T_R=2; both squares
in(19) have zero vector argument. Induction from ||x_0-g_0||^2
therefore gives zero value and derivative of the full quadratic, which
is precisely(20). Contact is algebraic, not a PSD claim.

## 4. Two explicit GLOBAL laws and their entire polynomial

At the final depth m abbreviate mu=mubase^m. In the following formulas
the coefficients eta,chi are arbitrary nonnegative scalars. Define

    K_ij=Kbase_ij+eta*mu_i*mu_j, i!=j; K_ii=0,
    nu_i=(1+chi)mu_i,
    xiCert_i=chi*mu_i,                         0<=i,j<=n. (21)

The two concrete laws are

    Law A: eta=64epsilon, chi=0;
    Law B: eta=0, chi=128epsilon.                     (22)

For a calibration child r<5 use eta=chi=0; for r>=5 use(22) with
the SAME enclosing epsilon. At epsilon=0 both laws use eta=chi=0 at
every depth. The scope extension to arbitrary eta,chi in(21) is an
exclusion of the whole fixed two-parameter family, not another law
or a parameter search. No retuning is performed after the obstruction.

Every final coefficient is a finite formula supplied by(15),(18),(21).
There are no decrements, so no zero coefficient is subtracted from.
All finite pairs, including pairs INTO and OUT OF the actual terminal,
are retained. Law A adds equal mass to each pair i->j and j->i;
Law B adds equal mass to i->star and star->i. Thus every original
value equation(5) stays exact. These are global balanced circulations,
not source-dependent terminal paths or changes of physical GD steps.

Their total additions over the ONE word are

    finite addition = eta*(4-sum_i mu_i^2) <=4eta,
    outgoing optimum addition = 2chi,
    incoming optimum addition = 2chi.                (23)

In A the finite addition is at most256epsilon=64/m<=1/24.
In B each optimum addition is256epsilon=64/m<=1/24 and their
combined addition is at most1/12. There is no per-node allowance.

For the entire original vector family define

    Tmu=sum_i mu_i g_i,
    Xmu=sum_i mu_i x_i,
    Vmu=sum_i mu_i[||g_i||^2-<g_i,x_i>].              (24)

Value cancellation and Q_(i,star)+Q_(star,i)=||g_i||^2-<g_i,x_i>
give the exact polynomial

    M_(eta,chi)(q)=Mbase_m(q)
           +eta*[2Vmu-||Tmu||^2+<Tmu,Xmu>]+chi*Vmu. (25)

For the finite term, sum over unordered pairs mu_i mu_j(Q_ij+Q_ji).
The difference-square part is 2sum mu_i||g_i||^2-||Tmu||^2 because
sum mu=2. The position part is <Tmu,Xmu>-2sum mu_i<g_i,x_i>.
This proves(25) with every coordinate, including x_0 and g_n, present.
In the eta term the x_0 contributions cancel; the chi term contributes
-chi<Tmu,x_0>. The full initial mixed column is therefore
-(1+chi)mu/2 as required by(21), and its Schur expenditure is
(1+chi)^2 mu mu^T/(4q). The terminal diagonal and every incoming
terminal row are retained by(7),(21); a unit terminal-square coefficient
from an incoming-free theorem is NOT assumed for this new array.

Here is the exact terminal completion for the ACTUAL altered array.
The base terminal has total outgoing mass2 and no incoming rows, so
Cbase_nn=1. The final original terminal entries are

    K_(i,n)=eta*mu_i*mu_n for i<n,
    K_(n,i)=Kbase_(n,i)+eta*mu_n*mu_i for i<n,
    nu_n=(1+chi)mu_n, xiCert_n=chi*mu_n,
    gammaTerm=C_nn=1+eta*mu_n*(2-mu_n)+chi*mu_n>0.

With C from(7), put

    vterm=(nu_n/2)x_0-sum_(i<n)C_(i,n)g_i,
    Rterm=q||x_0||^2-sum_(i<n)nu_i<x_0,g_i>
              +sum_(i,j<n)C_ij<g_i,g_j>-||vterm||^2/gammaTerm.

Then the COMPLETE original polynomial, without a re-selection, is

    M_(eta,chi)(q)=gammaTerm*||g_n-vterm/gammaTerm||^2+Rterm.

Every coefficient in this identity is fixed by the finite formulas
above. Thus the actual terminal expenditure ||vterm||^2/gammaTerm
is retained, and full PSD is equivalent to PSD of the entire Rterm.
It is not enough to check individual prefix planes or the common block.

## 5. Whole-word common/contact payment for the two laws

Set Pbar=sum_i mu_i P_i and Utail_i=sum_(j>i)mu_j. From(21),

    Xin=2chi, Ein=chi(2+Pbar),
    Hprice=chi(2D-2-Pbar), 0<=Pbar<=D-1.              (26)

At q=21/(20D), both laws have a positive initial/common block. For A
its determinant is1/20. For B, chi<=1/48, and

    q(D+Ein)-(1+chi)^2
       >=21/20-(49/48)^2>0.                         (27)

The final strict inequality follows by cross multiplication:
21*2304>20*2401. B's WHOLE Huber charge is paid within that advertised
slack, since

    0<=Hprice/D<2chi<=1/24<1/20.                    (28)

A has Hprice=0. These checks do not imply full PSD. They retain the
same whole-word D and do not rescale a child or count slack twice.

For clarity, the full contact vector can also be written explicitly.
The finite addition has row-sum vector

    dfin_i=eta*mu_i*(P_i-Pbar/2),                    (29)

and Vmu's gradient-matrix row sum is

    cstar_i=mu_i*(1+P_i/2)+(h_i/2)*Utail_i.          (30)

To derive(29), vary all gradients around the common value1. Balanced
finite position variations cancel; the remaining row-i position
difference is eta mu_i(2P_i-Pbar), half of which is the matrix row sum.
For(30), write Vmu=sum mu_i||g_i||^2-<Tmu,x_0>
+sum_i mu_i<g_i,sum_(t<i)h_t g_t> and take its gradient row sums.
Thus the final row-sum vector is

    csum=D*mu/2+dfin+chi*cstar.                       (31)

Equations(11),(21),(26),(31) give the EXACT remaining transverse
expenditure, including every common, initial and terminal coupling.
No bound paying this transverse expenditure is claimed.

For A alone, dcontact=csum-D*mu/2=dfin. Completing the common block
at q=zeta/D, zeta>1, gives the simpler exact expression

    Price_A(v)=(csum^T v)^2/D
                  +(dfin^T v)^2/[D(zeta-1)].        (32)

At zeta=1 the singular common block instead requires dfin=0.
As mu has positive weights at different physical prefixes, A at
epsilon>0 does not satisfy that singular-contact requirement.
At zeta=21/20 the cost in(32) is finite but remains a real full-space
expense. The counterdirection below defeats both laws even before
any initial Schur expenditure is deducted.

## 6. General balanced-circulation contact obstruction

This calculation is not limited to the rank-product law. Let J_ij>=0
be ANY zero-diagonal finite-node array with equal row and column sums
at every node. On ANY finite nonnegative word define

    R_J(g)=sum_(i!=j)J_ij[<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2].

Its value terms cancel by balance and its x_0 terms cancel in position
differences. Let C_J be its full symmetric gradient matrix. At common
gradients, R_J(e)=0. Its row-sum vector satisfies

    (C_J e)_i=(1/2)sum_j J_ij(P_i-P_j),
    P^T C_J e=(1/4)sum_(i,j)J_ij(P_i-P_j)^2.         (33)

For the first equality, the derivative of the source gradient gives
sum_j J_ij(P_i-P_j). The derivatives of the positions cancel because
row and column masses agree. The difference-square derivative vanishes
at e. Divide the derivative by2. For the second equality, expand the
sum of squared differences and use balance to equate the P_i^2 and
P_j^2 sums. This proves both equalities directly without invoking a
graph theorem.

It follows that C_J e=0 if and only if every positive J_ij joins
equal P_i and P_j. Necessity follows from the nonnegative sum in(33);
sufficiency follows from its first equality. With nonnegative h,
P_i=P_j means all intervening steps are zero, hence x_i=x_j as a
FORMAL identity even with independent gradients. In this case

    R_J(g)=(1/2)sum J_ij||g_i-g_j||^2 >=0.           (34)

Conversely, if some positive edge has unequal physical prefixes,
C_J e!=0 while e^T C_J e=0. Taking v=C_J e and expanding
R_J(e+t v)=2t||C_J e||^2+t^2 v^T C_J v shows a negative value
for sufficiently small negative t. Thus C_J is not PSD. In particular,
on a strictly positive word EVERY nonzero nonnegative balanced finite
circulation is an indefinite correction, despite exact value balance.

Moreover no collection of such additions can cancel its physical-
prefix contact work: the right side of(33) is additive and nonnegative.
At an exactly contacting base with zero incoming optimum rows and
q=1/D, the common kernel condition forces C_J e=0. Hence no nonzero
nonnegative finite circulation preserves full PSD at that exact level
on a positive word. This is NOT a statement at q>1/D, about signed
changes with paid decrements, about changed optimum profiles, or about
arbitrary full certificates. The explicit paid test in the next
section is needed to exclude our concrete laws at larger q.

## 7. Entire zero-displacement restriction of BOTH global corrections

Consider any original gradient assignment supported strictly within
the root's first left child, with x_0=0, zero gradient at that child's
terminal, and

    sum_(i in support)h_i g_i=0.                     (35)

All later formal positions are zero. On the supported indices(18)
gives mu_i=2h_i/D. Therefore Tmu=0 exactly. By(24),(25),

    D[M_(eta,chi)(q)-Mbase_m(q)]
      =(2eta+chi) sum_(i in support)h_i(2-h_i)||g_i||^2. (36)

Here is a direct verification retaining all prefix positions. Since
x_i=-sum_(t<i)h_t g_t,

    sum_i h_i<g_i,x_i>
      =-(1/2)||sum_i h_i g_i||^2+(1/2)sum_i h_i^2||g_i||^2
      =(1/2)sum_i h_i^2||g_i||^2.

Multiplying Vmu by D consequently gives exactly the diagonal sum
in(36). Every unsupported term in Vmu has gradient zero, and Tmu=0
kills BOTH global rank terms in(25), including their terminal and
remote-query contributions. Thus(36) is an entire subspace identity,
not a selected-edge or conditional-covariance approximation.

## 8. The inherited base itself is negative on a deep original wave

We now strengthen the earlier two-coordinate screen by retaining the
negative terminal-source accumulation at ALL depths. Let pterm_j be
Psrc_j evaluated with only its terminal gradient equal to1. Define

    Fterm_j=A_j^2(1+t_j)-2A_j+(lambda+1).

Substitute Y=-(A_j-1), Z=-1 into(17). The bracket is precisely Fterm_j,
and the left child is zero. Thus the finite closed expression is

    pterm_j=sum_(l<j)(tstar-t_l)Fterm_l
                              product_(v=l+1)^(j-1)k_v. (37)

This is a symbolic finite formula and has not been executed. At depth1,
pterm_1=2-b^2<3epsilon. At depth2, k_1<7,
0<tstar-t_1<epsilon, and Fterm_1<13, so

    pterm_2<34epsilon<2epsilon*A_2^2.                 (38)

For the last inequalities use(13),(14): k_1<2+5/2+(7/2)/2<7;
tstar-t_1=(lambda epsilon-2epsilon/[lambda(1+epsilon)])/(lambda A_1)
<epsilon; and Fterm_1=A_1^2-(1+epsilon)A_1+lambda+1<13.
Also A_2>5 makes34<2A_2^2. The step from depth2 to depth3 adds a
negative term and multiplies the normalized old term by a number in(0,1).
It follows that pterm_3/A_3^2<2epsilon, regardless of its sign.

For every l>=3, monotonicity of t_l and the exact relation
t_(l+1)=c*t_l/(2+t_l) give

    t_3>c*tstar/(2+tstar)=(1+epsilon)tstar,
    t_l-tstar>(2/5)epsilon.                          (39)

Since A_l>=8 and t_l>2/5, Fterm_l>A_l^2. Also A_(l+1)=(2+t_l)A_l
and t_l<1/2. Hence

    (t_l-tstar)Fterm_l/A_(l+1)^2>(8/125)epsilon.          (40)

The normalized propagation factor is exactly

    rnorm_l=k_l/(2+t_l)^2
       =1-(t_l-tstar)(1+t_l)/(2+t_l)^2,
    1-epsilon<rnorm_l<1, l>=2.                          (41)

Indeed (1+t)/(2+t)^2<=1/4 and t_l-tstar<lambda epsilon< (5/2)epsilon.
The product of any at most m such factors is greater than
(1-epsilon)^m>=1-m epsilon=3/4; the elementary last inequality
follows by induction from (1-u)^k>=1-ku for0<=u<=1.
No recurrence or product has been numerically evaluated.

Unroll(37) from depth3, divide by A_j^2, bound the initial propagated
term above by2epsilon, and apply(40),(41) to EVERY negative term.
For3<=j<=m,

    pterm_j/A_j^2
       <[2-(6/125)(j-3)]epsilon.                    (42)

There are j-3 terms, l=3,...,j-1. Each negative contribution has
magnitude greater than (8/125)epsilon times3/4. This explains the
coefficient and retains the entire same-m depth range. No PSD of a
positive-epsilon child has been used.

Choose ANY integer62<=j<=m-3. Let rbridge=2^j-1 and Hstep=1+d_j
be its actual physical step. Assign the original scalar vectors

    x_0=0, g_0=-Hstep/b, g_rbridge=1,
    g_i=0 for every other original i, including i=n.  (43)

The displacement is exactly b*(-Hstep/b)+Hstep=0. All later points and
the root left terminal are zero, so(35) holds. In the first Hword_j,
only its first and terminal gradients are nonzero. For j>=2 there
is no source cross coefficient between them: g_0 enters only the
first depth1 bracket in(17), while the final terminal enters disjoint
brackets. The first diagonal is2-b^2 and the final one is pterm_j.
At its next join, Y=0 and Z=1, contributing

    -Edef_j, Edef_j=(t_j-tstar)A_j^2(lambda+t_j)>0.

All larger source joins have Y=Z=0. At the final objective join(19),
x_p, g_p, and T_R all vanish. Thus the ENTIRE base value, for any
real q, is exactly

    D*Mbase_m(q;43)=(2-b^2)(Hstep/b)^2+pterm_j-Edef_j.       (44)

Because A_j>5, d_j<A_j/2, and b>7/5, Hstep/(b A_j)<1/2.
Using2-b^2<3epsilon, then(42), and discarding only the negative -Edef_j,
gives

    D*Mbase_m(q;43)
      <[11/4-(6/125)(j-3)]epsilon*A_j^2
      <=-(41/500)epsilon*A_j^2
      <-(1/20)epsilon*A_j^2, j>=62.                 (45)

At j=62 the displayed coefficient is11/4-354/125=-41/500,
and it decreases with j. In particular j=m-3 is allowed for EVERY
m>=1536 and uses exactly the original physical horizon and enclosing m.

## 9. All nonnegative mixtures fail: no parameter retuning can repair them

On(43), the full diagonal sum in(36) is

    b(2-b)(Hstep/b)^2+Hstep(2-Hstep)
       =2Hstep[1-(1-1/b)Hstep].                             (46)

Since b>7/5, 1-1/b>2/7. Also j>=62, d_j>2A_j/5 and A_j>=2^j,
so Hstep>7. Therefore the quantity in(46) is strictly negative.
Combining the complete identities(36),(44) with(45),(46) proves,
jointly within this unverified draft, that

    M_(eta,chi)(q;43)<-epsilon*A_j^2/(20D)<0          (47)

for EVERY m>=1536, EVERY62<=j<=m-3, EVERY eta,chi>=0,
and EVERY real q. This includes eta=chi=0 and arbitrary unbounded
mixture coefficients. Positive coefficients only make this direction
worse. Thus no nonnegative tuning of the two specified global
generators can produce a full PSD certificate on this base. Laws A
and B are stopped at this declared-domain obstruction; no third law
or tuned variant is proposed.

This is broader than failure of the three previous conditional paths:
it permits every finite-node pair of the rank-product generator,
includes the independent incoming terminal rows, includes both
optimum directions with nonzero whole-word expenditure, and excludes
the complete nonnegative cone of the two global generators. It still
does NOT exclude other circulations, non-product weights, signed
reallocations with paid decrements, different optimum profiles,
different bases or arbitrary certificates.

In particular(43) is NOT a primal hard function. It has x_0=0 and
a later x_i=0 but different assigned gradients. A differentiable
static function cannot have those two gradients at the same point;
also an actual start at the normalized optimum0 cannot have nonzero
gradient. Its only purpose is to test PSD on the required independent
original variables. No W upper or lower bound follows from(47).

## 10. Exact Silver and zero-step calibrations

At epsilon=0 the actual word in(2) has A_j=lambda^j,
d_j=lambda^(j-1), t_j=tstar. All coefficients eta,chi in the TWO
concrete laws vanish. Formula(17) gives Psrc_j=0 at every depth.
For the base objective completion(19), set A=S_j and
bjoin=1+tstar(A+1). Direct expansion yields

    (3A+2bjoin)^2*kappa_j
      =2(1-tstar)A^3+(6-8tstar)A^2
       +(1+tstar)(8tstar-2)A+(1+tstar)^2(1-2tstar)>0. (48)

Every coefficient is positive because1/4<tstar<1/2. The empty base
is ||x_0-g_0||^2. If the right child is PSD at1/Dc, its exact initial
Schur remainder Rchild_R is PSD by completing that child's x_0
square using(20). Equation(19) and(48) therefore prove full Silver
PSD inductively on the ORIGINAL word, with every gradient retained.
At the advertised larger q, add(q-1/D_r)||x_0||^2. This is a proof
only at epsilon=0, not a transfer of child PSD to positive epsilon.

For the finite positive-epsilon calibration depths1 and2 the concrete
laws make no addition. At depth1 direct expansion gives

    Psrc_1=(2-b^2)||g_0-g_1||^2,
    Mbase_1(1/D_1)
      =||x_0-D_1(g_0+g_1)/2||^2/D_1
                         +(3-2b)||g_0-g_1||^2/4.   (49)

Both are PSD since b<sqrt(2)<3/2. At depth2 the bridge is2-epsilon.
Here D_1>19/5, D_2<75/7 and
theta_1=(2b+2-epsilon)/(3b+4-2epsilon)<59/100.
For the upper ratio use b<10/7 and2-epsilon>199/100; the ratio
increases with b and decreases with that bridge. Cross multiplication
gives the claimed bound. Consequently D_2 theta_1^2<15/4 and
kappa_1>1/20. Equation(19), the PSD source in(49), and the full
right-child Schur remainder prove depth2 PSD with x_0 retained.
These finite calibrations do not establish positive-epsilon large-depth PSD.

For arbitrary zero entries in a word, ALL algebra(4)–(11),(21),(25),
(33) and the general balanced-flow statements keep every formal query.
If P_i=P_j, nonnegative steps make x_i=x_j a formal identity, but
g_i and g_j are still independent and remain in(34). No gradient
identification or compression is made.

For an explicit all-zero-word calibration, use the accepted direct
base Kbase=0, mu_n=2, mu_i=0 for i<n, D=1. The finite generator in(21)
is exactly zero. The two-way optimum generator yields the entire form

    M(q)=q||x_0||^2-2(1+chi)<x_0,g_n>
                              +(1+2chi)||g_n||^2.   (50)

Every other original gradient has a zero coefficient. Its exact PSD
condition is q>0 and q(1+2chi)>=(1+chi)^2. Thus Law A has the usual
all-zero threshold q=1, and Law B at chi<=1/48 is PSD at q=21/20
by(27); at q=1 it fails if chi>0. For the empty/depth<5 calibration
the chosen coefficients vanish and(50) is ||x_0-g_n||^2 at q=1.
This explicitly records the extra endpoint cost instead of hiding it
behind zero-step compression. No assertion about an arbitrary positive
word's PSD is inferred from these zero-word checks.

## 11. Remaining theorem-level obstacle and resource handoff

The attempted global laws do not survive. The exact mechanism is not
an unpaid scalar Huber budget: Law B pays that WHOLE budget and both
laws have a strictly positive initial/common block at21/(20D). Their
complete original gradient matrices still have(47). On the decisive
subspace the dense finite and optimum corrections both collapse to
the same negative long-step form(36). Increasing either mass, changing
their nonnegative ratio, or inflating q cannot repair an x_0=0 failure.

Separately,(33) explains why finite Eulerian balance cannot be promoted
to a free PSD correction or zero Huber-contact defect. At inflated q,
the exact charge(11) must still be paid in the entire transverse space.
A new construction must change the signed original-space action on
the zero-displacement subspace, for example through a different
non-product support/profile or a paid reallocation of existing rows.
Those are unproved possibilities, not supplied laws or solved lemmas.

The missing global nonnegative-coefficient PSD construction at an
objective level with a fixed gap against(1), or a static primal lower
witness ruling out that direction, remains a THEOREM-LEVEL obstacle.
Neither exists in this report. The original exact-profile goal is OPEN.

No additional graph paper is needed for this branch: the proposed

# Paid remote leaf consistency: two explicit laws and their full-space failures

JOINT UNVERIFIED analytical report, worker max81. Original target OPEN.
Governing assignment: TASK.md and guidance60cc1ca7a8ad2d07. One invocation,
at most two mechanisms, no automatic renewal. All calculations below form
one unverified proof attempt. No intermediate draft is used as accepted truth.
No mathematical program, numerical/arithmetic/trajectory/recurrence replay,
optimizer, solver, formal call, fact_submit, installation, subagent or consult.

Two new original-index arrays are constructed, outside the old E80 support.
Both have nonnegative coefficients and exact objective value flow. Neither
has full PSD, at the requested level or even at an arbitrary initial-norm
coefficient. We give explicit original-vector counterdirections. A larger
contact-routed fan family is excluded by the same calculation. The second
law also gives a quantitative necessary restriction on receiver distributions:
mass sent across an earlier checkpoint must decrease on the scale of the
reciprocal of that checkpoint's physical step, when the two checkpoint
degrees agree. These are scoped coefficient obstructions, not W bounds.

## 1. Scope, accepted inputs and literal hierarchy

For every n>=0, L>0 and predetermined h in [0,infinity)^n, W_n(h) is the
supremum of 2(f(x_n)-f(x_star))/(L||x_0-x_star||^2) over every finite
dimension, convex differentiable L-smooth f with an attained minimizer,
every such x_star and x_0!=x_star, with all n actual updates
x_(i+1)=x_i-(h_i/L)grad f(x_i). Thus W=2R. The original question concerns
lim_n B_n inf_h W_n(h), with every nonnegative word, including zero and
arbitrarily large steps. No result below changes those quantifiers.

The benchmark is the original all-integer-split recurrence

    C_0=B_0=1,
    Omega(u,v)=(u+v+sqrt(u^2+6uv+v^2))/2,
    G(u,v)=2u+v/2+sqrt(v^2+8uv)/2,
    C_n=max_(0<=k<n) Omega(C_k,C_(n-k-1)),
    B_n=max_(0<=k<n) G(C_k,B_(n-k-1)), U_n^ZJ=1/B_n.

Accepted1252b8823bb5a290, read in full, supplies the original interpolation
orientation, full independent-vector certificate interpretation and exact
actual-terminal completion. It deletes no queried-prefix inequalities.
Accepted931b9653ce0f7201, read in full, supplies B_n/D<47/50 for the
following SAME-enclosing-m hierarchy. Its implication from W<=17/(16D)
is conditional; that hypothesis is still unproved. No other accepted
mathematical result is used here. Prior finding0073a83e08ff2e21 was read
in full as awareness; the reset construction needed below is rederived.

Fix m>=1536. Define

    lambda=1+sqrt(2), epsilon=1/(4m), beta=1-epsilon,
    c=lambda(1+epsilon), a=beta/c, d_j=a*c^j,
    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+d_j,H_j),
    n=2^m-1, h=H_m, S=sum_(i<n)h_i, D=1+2S,
    z=n-1, b=1+a, M=1+b, N=2^(m-1), A=D-b^2,
    P81_i=sum_(t<i)h_t, Tail81_i=S-b-P81_i (i<z),
    K81_i=A-P81_i-1=2S-b^2-P81_i (i<z), qtar=17/(16D).

All children use this same m. Index z is the last queried leaf, not the
unqueried terminal n. All these hierarchy symbols are local to this report.
Let level(i)=v2(i+1), the exponent of 2 dividing i+1. Then
h_i=1+a*c^level(i), leaves are the even indices, and checkpoints the odd
indices. In particular h_z=b. Write Leaf={0,2,...,z}, Check={1,3,...,n-2}.

Elementary bounds used throughout are

    1/3<a<1/2, 4/3<b<sqrt(2)<3/2,
    12/5<c<5/2, beta>7/8,
    d_1=beta, d_2=beta*c>21/10,
    S>2^m-1, D>2^m, A>0, K81_i>Tail81_i>0.

For example a>1/3 follows from beta>7/8 and c<5/2. The bound on c
follows from lambda<17/7 and epsilon<=1/6144. Also
b=sqrt(2)-2epsilon/[lambda(1+epsilon)]. The other comparisons follow by
substitution. K81_i-Tail81_i=S+b-b^2>0 since b<2 and S>=b. These observations
include positivity of every denominator in the arrays below.

Define a dyadic cell of height j to be

    I_(j,r)={r*2^j,...,(r+1)*2^j-2},
    1<=j<=m, 0<=r<2^(m-j).

For distinct queried indices u,v, J(u,v) is the least height of a cell
containing both. Such a cell exists and its height is unique. This defines
the exact least common dyadic ancestor in ORIGINAL indices. For i in the
left half, including the whole-word root, J(i,z)=m.

Normalize L=1, x_star=g_star=f_star=0, and retain independent original
vectors x_0,g_0,...,g_n. Positions are always
x_i=x_0-sum_(t<i)h_t g_t. For distinct labels put

    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2.

Legal samples have Q_ij<=0. An original coefficient array with
out_i-in_i=2*1_(i=n) defines

    M_cert(q)=q||x_0||^2-2f_n+sum lambda_ij Q_ij.

After value cancellation, it must be PSD on ALL original vectors to be an
upper certificate. A negative formal vector is not a primal instance.

## 2. Mechanism I: position-sensitive remote routing to the last leaf

First construct the entire family with independently chosen 0<=theta_i<=1
for each i<z. Set

    r81_i=2theta_i*h_i/K81_i,
    w81_i=2(1-theta_i)*h_i/Tail81_i.

The frozen LCA-sensitive law tested in Sections 6--8 is

    eta=1/(4D), theta_i=1-eta/J(i,z).                    (I.1)

Every remote pair then has positive weight. The literal nonzero array is

    lambda_(n,z)=2,
    lambda_(z,star)=2-sum_(i<z)r81_i,
    lambda_(i,star)=r81_i,
    lambda_(z,i)=r81_i+w81_i, lambda_(i,z)=w81_i, i<z.       (I.2)

All unlisted rows are zero, including all incoming terminal and optimum
rows. This array includes checkpoint-to-last-leaf rows at arbitrary
distance and leaf-to-last-leaf rows. Its support is not contained in E80.

All signs can be checked without an unknown feasibility condition. Put
Kbase=S+b-b^2>0. Since K81_i=Kbase+Tail81_i, and Tail81_i decreases by h_i as i
increases, integration of the decreasing function 1/(Kbase+t) gives

    sum_(i<z) h_i/(Kbase+Tail81_i)
      <= integral_0^(S-b) dt/(Kbase+t)
      =log((2S-b^2)/(S+b-b^2)) <log 2<1.

Indeed h_i/(Kbase+Tail81_i) is bounded by the integral over
[Tail81_i-h_i,Tail81_i], and these intervals partition [0,S-b]. The ratio is below
2 because b<2. Thus sum r81_i<2 for every allowed theta. Every coefficient
in (I.2) is nonnegative, with strict outgoing optimum mass at z.
At i<z the two finite directions have net inflow r81_i, exactly discharged
to star. At z the finite/optimum outgoing net is 2, supplied by (n,z).
Terminal flow is 2. This proves all value cancellations.

This is a new source-routing base, not an addition to any inherited
certificate. Its terminal distribution is concentrated at the actual last
leaf; its entire optimum distribution is given in (I.2).

### 2.1 Full polynomial and the paid contact equation

Write g=g_z, v_i=g_i-g for i<z and u=sum r81_i v_i. Put

    B(v)=sum_(i<z)[r81_i+w81_i(1-h_i)]||v_i||^2
         +sum_(i<t<z)(h_i*r81_t-w81_i*h_t)<v_i,v_t>,
    Jrem(v)=B(v)-A||u||^2/4.

The exact full identity, for EVERY real q, is

    M_I(q)=||g_n+(b-1)g||^2
       +q||x_0||^2-2<x_0,g>+A||g||^2
       -<x_0,u>+A<g,u>+B(v).                         (I.3)

Here is a direct derivation. Starting with rows 2Q_(n,z)+2Q_(z,star),
replace r81_i units of Q_(z,star) by Q_(z,i)+Q_(i,star), and add w81_i
units of Q_(i,z)+Q_(z,i). The two added expressions, after GD, are

    Q_(z,i)+Q_(i,star)-Q_(z,star)
       =-<v_i,x_0>+(P81_i+1)<v_i,g>
                          +||v_i||^2+sum_(t<i)h_t<v_i,v_t>,
    Q_(i,z)+Q_(z,i)
       =||v_i||^2-Tail81_i<v_i,g>-sum_(i<=t<z)h_t<v_i,v_t>.

The original base has terminal square ||g_n+(b-1)g||^2 and remainder
q||x_0||^2-2<x_0,g>+A||g||^2+2sum h_i<v_i,g>.
The defining relation

    w81_i*Tail81_i=2h_i-K81_i*r81_i                              (I.4)

changes each last cross coefficient to A*r81_i, giving (I.3). In particular,
remote symmetric pairs were NOT treated as PSD. Their negative diagonal
and mixed displacement terms appear in B.

At q=1/A, (I.3) is equivalently

    M_I(1/A)=||g_n+(b-1)g||^2
      +||x_0-A*g-(A/2)u||^2/A+Jrem(v).               (I.5)

Equation (I.4) is an intentional design restriction of this family. It is
not claimed necessary for a general certificate. No zero-contact rule is
imposed on an unrestricted optimum. The term -A||u||^2/4 is the ONE
global initial/common-gradient charge for this deliberately balanced law.

### 2.2 Full-space failure for every member of the contact family

Take p=2^(m-1)-1, H=h_p>4. Only v_p is nonzero, with v_p=1. The coefficient
of Jrem on this vector is

    E_p=r81_p-w81_p(H-1)-A*r81_p^2/4.                       (I.6)

If theta_p<=1/2, then r81_p<=H/K81_p and w81_p>=H/Tail81_p. Thus
r81_p-w81_p(H-1)<=H/K81_p-H(H-1)/Tail81_p<0 because K81_p>Tail81_p and H>2.
If theta_p>=1/2, then r81_p>=H/K81_p>H/A>4/A, and
r81_p-A*r81_p^2/4<0; subtracting w81_p(H-1)>=0 preserves negativity.
So E_p<0 for EVERY choice of the whole profile theta.

The complete scalar original-vector counterdirection is

    x_0=0,
    g_i=-r81_p/2 for every queried i!=p,
    g_p=1-r81_p/2,
    g_n=(b-1)r81_p/2.                                  (I.7)

It sets g=-r81_p/2, u=r81_p, makes the terminal square and the square in
(I.5) zero, and gives M_I(q)=E_p<0 for every real q. The change from
1/A to q multiplies ||x_0||^2=0. Hence even unlimited initial-norm slack
cannot repair this coefficient family. This is stronger than rejection
of the frozen small-eta profile, but only for family (I.2)--(I.4).

## 3. Mechanism II: dense earlier-leaf transport with paid reverse cycles

Put Tc=sum_(p in Check)h_p and t_p=h_p/Tc, so sum t_p=1. For EVERY
checkpoint p and earlier leaf ell<p define

    raw_(p,ell)=[1+(p-ell)/(p+1)]/J(p,ell),
    Z_p=sum_(ell in Leaf,ell<p)raw_(p,ell),
    omega_(p,ell)=raw_(p,ell)/Z_p,
    mu_ell=sum_(p in Check,p>ell)t_p*omega_(p,ell).    (II.1)

There is at least one earlier leaf for every checkpoint, so Z_p>0.
The weights depend on the exact least common ancestor and position within
the enclosing chronological range. They are not uniform fan averages.
The formula for mu_ell is used only for ell<z. The new optimum distribution is mu_ell at
earlier leaves, zero at every checkpoint, and mass 1 at z.

For a global reverse-cycle amount kappa>=0, the array is

    lambda_(n,z)=2, lambda_(z,star)=1,
    lambda_(z,p)=t_p, p in Check,
    lambda_(p,ell)=(1+kappa)t_p*omega_(p,ell),
    lambda_(ell,p)=kappa*t_p*omega_(p,ell), ell<p,
    lambda_(ell,star)=mu_ell, ell in Leaf, ell<z.      (II.2)

Every unlisted entry is zero. The frozen law is kappa=1. All signs are
nonnegative. One unit flows z->star and one flows z->p->ell->star.
Every reverse cycle has zero net flow. Thus (II.2) has exactly terminal
flow 2, with no incoming terminal or optimum edge. In particular it
contains Q_(n-2,0) with a strictly positive coefficient.

The complete original-row expression is

    M_II(q)=q||x_0||^2-2f_n+2Q_(n,z)+Q_(z,star)
      +sum_p t_p[Q_(z,p)+sum_(ell<p)omega_(p,ell)
                                      (Q_(p,ell)+Q_(ell,star))]
      +kappa*sum_(p,ell<p)t_p*omega_(p,ell)
                                      (Q_(p,ell)+Q_(ell,p)). (II.3)

There are no omitted charges in this expression. Each finite row is its
original ordered interpolation row. Equations (II.1)--(II.3) are a
coefficient law, not an instruction to solve for unknown PSD blocks.

### 3.1 Complete quadratic, including all independent prefix gradients

Set W_(p,ell)=t_p*omega_(p,ell). Eliminating only g_n gives
M_II(q)=||g_n+(b-1)g_z||^2+R_II, where

    R_II=q||x_0||^2-<x_0,g_z+sum_(ell<z)mu_ell*g_ell>
       +(2-(b-1)^2)||g_z||^2+sum_p t_p||g_p||^2
       +sum_(ell<z)mu_ell||g_ell||^2
       -sum_p t_p<g_z,g_p>-sum_(p,ell<p)W_(p,ell)<g_p,g_ell>
       +<g_z,sum_(i<z)h_i*g_i>
       +sum_p t_p<g_z,sum_(p<=i<z)h_i*g_i>
       +sum_(p,ell<p)W_(p,ell)<g_p,sum_(ell<=i<p)h_i*g_i>
       +sum_(ell<z)mu_ell<g_ell,sum_(i<ell)h_i*g_i>
       +kappa*sum_(p,ell<p)W_(p,ell)
          [||g_ell-g_p||^2
                  -<g_ell-g_p,sum_(ell<=i<p)h_i*g_i>]. (II.4)

The formula follows by expanding the four types of prefix rows in
(II.3). For example, the two half-squared-difference contributions at p
give t_p||g_p||^2; the two at each leaf give mu_ell||g_ell||^2.
Both original displacements of each reverse cycle remain in the last line.
No common-gradient, terminal or initial-point relation is assumed.

### 3.2 A two-checkpoint zero-displacement counterdirection

For a queried checkpoint r<p put
Omega_p(r)=sum_(ell in Leaf,ell<=r)omega_(p,ell). On the full original
subspace x_0=0, every leaf gradient zero and g_n=0, (II.4) becomes

    (1+kappa)[sum_p t_p||g_p||^2
       +sum_(r<p in Check)t_p*h_r*Omega_p(r)<g_r,g_p>]. (II.5)

This is a necessary test of the full matrix, not a claim that checkpoint
compression is lossless. All omitted formal coordinates are explicitly
set to zero only to exhibit a negative direction.

Choose r=2^(m-2)-1 and p=3*2^(m-2)-1, the roots of the two children of
the whole word. Both physical steps equal H=1+a*c^(m-2), and t_r=t_p.
There are 2^(m-3) earlier leaves at or before r and 3*2^(m-3) before p.
Every raw weight is at least 1/m and less than 2, so

    Omega_p(r)>1/(6m).

Also H>2^(m-2)/3>12m. For the latter comparison, 2^(m-2)>36m at
m=16 and doubling preserves it thereafter. Consequently H*Omega_p(r)>2.
Set the complete original vector to

    x_0=0, g_r=1, g_p=-1, all other g_i=0, including g_n. (II.6)

Its physical displacement is H-H=0. Equation (II.5) gives

    M_II(q)=(1+kappa)t_p[2-H*Omega_p(r)]<0.           (II.7)

This holds for EVERY real q and EVERY kappa>=0. Paying a larger common
reverse-cycle factor cannot repair the law.

More generally, for arbitrary positive checkpoint masses summing to 1 and receiver
probabilities with the same routing support and one common kappa, the
two-coordinate determinant in (II.5) requires

    h_r*Omega_p(r) <= 2*sqrt(t_r/t_p), r<p.          (II.8)

Indeed its diagonal entries are (1+kappa)t_r and (1+kappa)t_p, and its
off-diagonal entry is (1+kappa)t_p*h_r*Omega_p(r)/2. Thus comparable
checkpoint degrees need receiver leakage at most a constant divided by
the earlier physical step. The displayed raw kernel has only an inverse-m
lower bound on that leakage, which is too large. This requirement alone
does not certify any more localized law; all other directions remain.

## 4. One global initial/common charge and the exact terminal account

Both laws have no incoming terminal row and lambda_(n,z)=2. Directly,

    -2f_n+2Q_(n,z)
       =||g_n+(b-1)g_z||^2-2f_z+[1-(b-1)^2]||g_z||^2. (4.1)

Thus the true terminal is free, with center -(b-1)g_z. Nothing identifies
it with the last queried gradient. The entire original prefix remains.

For either law let mu_i=lambda_(i,star), finite K_ij=lambda_ij, and h_n=0
only for notation. There are no incoming optimum rows and sum mu_i=2.
The complete polynomial is q||x_0||^2-sum mu_i<x_0,g_i>+g^T C g, with

    C_ii=mu_i+sum_v K_iv-1_(i=n)-h_i*sum_(v>i)K_iv,
    2C_ij=-(K_ij+K_ji)-h_j*sum_(v>j)K_iv
                      +h_i[mu_j+sum_(v<=i)K_jv], i<j. (4.2)

To check this formula, the incident half-squared-gradient terms contribute
mu_i+sum_v K_iv-1_(i=n), by flow; each forward row out of i contributes
-h_i on that diagonal. For i<j the squared differences give the first
term, forward rows from i crossing j the second, and rows from j reaching
index <=i or star the third. This accounts for every original coordinate.

Eliminate g_n using (4.1), obtaining the explicit prefix matrix
Cpre=C_(0:z,0:z)-(b-1)^2 e_z e_z^T, where e_z selects coordinate z.
Let g_i=G+v_i on the queried prefix, with v_z=0. Define

    u=sum_(i<=z)mu_i*v_i, k=e^T Cpre v, T=v^T Cpre v,

where e is the all-ones prefix column. The coefficients Cpre,mu are
entirely specified by the two literal arrays and (4.2). Substitution of
all prefix gradients G gives e^T Cpre e=A. Therefore the full prefix form
is exactly

    q||x_0||^2-2<x_0,G>+A||G||^2-<x_0,u>+2<G,k>+T. (4.3)

At q=qtar the initial/common determinant Delta=qA-1 is positive, because
D>17b^2. Eliminating this ONE two-coordinate block charges

    Charge(u,k)=[A||u||^2/4-<u,k>+q||k||^2]/(qA-1). (4.4)

This follows by inversion of [[q,-1],[-1,A]] with cross column
(-u/2,k). No separate local charge is substituted for it. Full PSD is
equivalent to T>=Charge for every original prefix variation v_z=0.
At a singular determinant a range condition would additionally be needed;
the advertised qtar is not singular. For Law I, k=A*u/2 by (I.3), and
(4.4) is exactly A||u||^2/4. Its minimizing initial/common values are
x_0=0,G=-u/2, explaining why increasing q cannot repair (I.7).
For Law II, (4.2) and (II.4) retain k explicitly; (II.6) already gives
a negative value before any attempted elimination. No PSD claim is left
to an unspecified matrix problem: both full matrices have been refuted.

## 5. Complete reconstruction of the E80 reset transcript

For reuse as a SCREEN only, define Rreset=a*c^2/M=beta*c/M and sbit(k)
to be the number of ones in the binary expansion of integer k>=0. Put

    v_k=(-1)^k Rreset^k c^(-sbit(k))/M, 0<=k<N.

At leaf 2k prescribe x=M v_k,g=v_k,f=(b+1/2)v_k^2. At checkpoint
2k+1<n prescribe x=g=v_k,f=v_k^2/2. Binary carry gives
sbit(k+1)-sbit(k)=1-v2(k+1), hence
v_(k+1)/v_k=-a*c^(1+v2(k+1))/M. Thus the leaf step takes M v_k to v_k
and the following actual checkpoint step takes v_k to M v_(k+1).
Every physical update is satisfied, x_0=1, and the final position is

    t=x_n=v_(N-1)=-Rreset^(N-1)/(M*c^(m-1)).

Complete the actual terminal by g_n=u=2t/(b+2), f_n=t^2/(b+2), and set
the optimum jet to zero. This is a scalar Gram transcript; no function is
asserted to interpolate it. Substitution gives the full row table:

    Q_(leaf(v),star)=0, Q_(star,leaf(v))=-b*v^2;
    both optimum rows at check(w) are 0;
    Q_(check(w),check(w'))=0;
    Q_(leaf(v),check(w))=0;
    Q_(check(w),leaf(v))=b*v*(w-v);
    Q_(leaf(v),leaf(w))=b*w*(v-w);
    Q_(n,check(w))=Q_(n,star)=Q_(star,n)=-b*u^2/4;
    Q_(n,leaf(v))=-b*(v-u/2)^2.                       (5.1)

In particular both adjacent queried directions are nonpositive: following
a leaf has w=v, while at the preceding checkpoint the next leaf parameter
is -d*w/M with d>0. Every E80 row is accounted for by this table. E80 means
both optimum orientations at every finite index, all checkpoint pairs,
all leaf-to-checkpoint rows, adjacent checkpoint-to-leaf rows, and all
outgoing actual-terminal rows. No leaf-to-leaf or remote checkpoint-to-
leaf rows belonged to E80.

The growth estimate is included, rather than imported from the old draft.
Since M=lambda-2epsilon/[lambda(1+epsilon)],

    Rreset-1=epsilon[2/(lambda(1+epsilon))-lambda*epsilon]/M
              >epsilon/8=1/(32m).

For the strict bound, epsilon<1/8 and lambda<5/2 make the bracket exceed
32/45-5/16>5/16, while M<5/2. For m>=16, induction gives
2^(m-1)-1>=96m^2: the base is 32767>=24576 and the induction difference
96m^2-192m-95 is positive. Hence N-1>32m(2m+1).
The binomial inequality (1+1/(32m))^(32m)>=2 yields
Rreset^(N-1)>2^(2m+1). Because M*c^(m-1)<2^(2m), we have t<-2.

This transcript is explicitly not interpolable. At pbad=n-2 the checkpoint
parameter w=v_(N-2)=-M*t/beta>2M, whereas v_0=1/M. Consequently

    Q_(pbad,0)=b*v_0*(w-v_0)>0, Q_(0,pbad)=0.        (5.2)

Law I omits this row. Law II includes it with positive weight. The new
failure below is insufficient paid action even though that violated row
is now present. Neither (5.1) nor (5.2) is a lower bound on unrestricted W.

### 5.1 A symbolic tail bound; no trajectory or recurrence was executed

For k=N-1-j, binary complementation gives exactly

    |v_k/t|=Rreset^(-j)c^sbit(j), 1<=j<N.

Since an integer with r binary ones is at least 2^r-1,
c^(2sbit(j))<=(j+1)^4. Put rreset=Rreset^(-2). The previous lower bound
on Rreset gives 1-rreset>=1/(64m): with zeta=1/(32m)<1, use
1-(1+zeta)^(-2)>=zeta/2.
For every integer d>=1,
j^d<=d!*binom(j+d-1,d), and summing the geometric-series identity gives
sum_(j>=1)j^d r^j<=d!/(1-r)^(d+1), 0<r<1. This identity follows by
multiplying d+1 geometric series and counting compositions, or by d
successive differentiations of their convergent series. Therefore

    sum_(k<N-1)v_k^2/t^2 <=768(64m)^5=:Moment5,
    sum_(p=2k+1 in Check)h_p*v_k^2/t^2
                                  <=92160(64m)^7=:Moment7. (5.3)

For the second bound, v2(k+1)=v2(j), so
h_p=1+beta*c^v2(j)<=1+j^2<=2j^2. Apply the d=6 inequality to
2j^2(j+1)^4<=128j^6. For the first use (j+1)^4<=16j^4; the displayed
constant 768 is a harmless enlargement of 16*4!.

All coarse exponential comparisons used below follow from

    2^(m-1)>2^24(64m)^7, m>=1536.                    (5.4)

At m=1536, m<2^11 makes the right side less than 2^143, whereas the
left side is 2^1535. The ratio of the left side to m^7 increases at each
integer increment, since (1+1/m)^7<sum_(j>=0)(7/m)^j<2 for m>=1536.
This proves (5.4) for all relevant m by induction. This is symbolic
reasoning, not a generated arithmetic table or executed recurrence.

## 6. Complete reset screen for both frozen laws

On (5.1), Law I has exact value

    M_I(q)=q-2t^2/(b+2)-2b*(t-u/2)^2
      +b*sum_(earlier leaves ell=2k)
           [r81_ell*v_k*(t-v_k)-w81_ell*(t-v_k)^2]
      +b*sum_(checkpoints p=2k+1)w81_p*t*(v_k-t).      (6.1)

Every selected original row appears here: outgoing optimum rows vanish,
last-leaf-to-checkpoint rows vanish, leaf pairs and both other orientations
are evaluated by (5.1). For the frozen eta=1/(4D), w81_p<=2eta. Use
v(t-v)<=t^2/4 and t(v-t)<=v^2/4, and sum r81_ell<2. Formula (5.3) and
(5.4) give

    last sum in (6.1) <=(b*eta/2)Moment5*t^2 <t^2/8.

Indeed its coefficient is at most 96b(64m)^5/D, which is less than 1/8
by b<3/2, D>2^m and (5.4). The leaf relay contribution is at most
b*t^2/2 and its symmetric-pair part is nonpositive. Finally

    2/(b+2)+2b*(b+1)^2/(b+2)^2-b/2
       >4/7+7b/18 >121/126.

Here (b+1)/(b+2)>2/3 and b>1. Thus

    M_I(qtar)<qtar-(3/4)t^2<0.                       (6.2)

The inequality 121/126-1/8>3/4 and t^2>4 justify the displayed margin.
This reset conclusion is for the frozen eta law. The broader arbitrary-
theta contact family is excluded instead by (I.7), without a claim that
every such theta has the same sign on the reset transcript.

For Law II with kappa=1, the EXACT corresponding expression is

    M_II(q)=q-2t^2/(b+2)-2b*(t-u/2)^2
       +2b*sum_(p=2k+1)t_p*sum_(ell=2s<p)
                         omega_(p,ell)*v_s*(v_k-v_s). (6.3)

The reverse leaf-to-checkpoint rows are zero, as are the source-to-
checkpoint and outgoing optimum rows. The displayed 2 is the actual
1+kappa forward weight. Since v_s(v_k-v_s)<=v_k^2/4, the last sum is
at most (b/2)sum_p t_p*v_k^2. The total checkpoint mass satisfies
Tc>=h_(2^(m-1)-1)>2^(m-1)/3. Equations (5.3)--(5.4) prove

    sum_p t_p*v_k^2/t^2 <=Moment7/Tc<1/4.

Therefore

    M_II(qtar)<qtar-[2/(b+2)-b/8]*t^2
                       <qtar-t^2/4<0.              (6.4)

We used 2/(b+2)-b/8>4/7-3/16>1/4 and qtar<1. The actual negative
terminal-row cost was retained in (6.3) and only dropped for this upper
bound. The included remote rows are too weak under this global degree
normalization. Their mere presence does not pay the required reset cost.

## 7. The older two-gradient deep waves

For 2<=j<=m-3 set p=2^j-1, H=h_p, x_0=0, g_0=-H/b, g_p=1, and every
other original gradient, including g_n, zero. The physical displacement
is exactly zero. This is a formal full-space screen only.

For the frozen Law I, direct substitution in (I.3) gives

    M_I=(r81_0+w81_0)H^2/b^2-(H-1)(r81_p+w81_p).           (7.1)

Both J(0,z) and J(p,z) equal m. Define, only for this calculation,

    F(P)=(2-2eta/m)/(2S-b^2-P)+(2eta/m)/(S-b-P).

Then r81_i+w81_i=h_i F(P81_i) for i=0,p, and F is strictly increasing.
For j>=3, H>4 and b/(b-1)<4; using F(P81_p)>F(0) in (7.1) gives

    M_I<F(0)H[H/b-(H-1)]<0.                        (7.2)

At j=2, H=1+beta*c and P81_p=2b+1+beta<6. Now
b-(b-1)H=1-beta^2>epsilon. Also

    [F(P81_p)-F(0)]/F(0) <= P81_p/(S-b-P81_p).

It follows from (7.1) that M_I>0 if
15/(S-8)<1/(6m), which follows from S>90m+8. This last inequality
holds since S>2^m-1 and m>=1536 (the ratio 2^m/m increases for m>1,
and the asserted comparison already holds at m=16).
Thus Law I passes exactly this j=2 screen and fails all j>=3 in its range.

Law II with kappa=1 passes every one of these old deep waves. Write

    L_le=sum_(r in Check,r<=p)t_r*omega_(r,0),
    L_gt=sum_(r in Check,r>p)t_r*omega_(r,0).

Its exact value is

    M_II=[(2-b)L_le+2L_gt]*H^2/b^2
                 +2t_p-t_p*omega_(p,0)*(2b-3)*H/b>0. (7.3)

To derive it, the routed base contributes
(L_le+L_gt)H^2/b^2+t_p-t_p*omega_(p,0)(b-1)H/b.
For a reverse cycle ending before p the interval displacement is b*g_0;
for one ending after p it is zero. The cycle at p contributes the
remaining mixed term with coefficient b-2 and its checkpoint diagonal.
Adding these gives (7.3). Its signs follow from b<3/2 and t_p>0.
The new equal-scale wave (II.6) still refutes its full matrix.

## 8. The older H4 positive/negative block direction

Set x_0=0, g_i=1 at i=0,1,2,3, g_i=-1 at i=8,9,10,11, and all other
original gradients zero, including g_n. The two active four-query blocks
have identical physical mass, so displacement is zero. Put

    H1=1+beta, H2=1+beta*c,
    P2=b+H1, B4=2b+H1+H2, C4=b+H2.

Here P2<7/2, B4<9, C4>4. All active original prefix masses P81_i are
below 100, their total active step mass is 2B4<18 (in fact <17), and
all J(i,z)=m.

For Law I put k0=(2-2eta/m)/(2S-b^2) and l0=(2eta/m)/(S-b).
Freeze r81_i=k0 h_i,w81_i=l0 h_i only within this calculation. Zero physical
displacement makes the exact frozen polynomial

    (k0+l0)/2 * sum_active h_i(2-h_i).

The sum is twice
2b(2-b)+(1+beta)(1-beta)+(1+beta*c)(1-beta*c), which is less than -1:
the first term is <=2, the second <1/4, and the third <1-(21/10)^2.
Thus the full sum is <-2 and the frozen value is <-(k0+l0).

The actual coefficients differ by at most
|r81_i-k0 h_i|<=200 k0 h_i/S and
|w81_i-l0 h_i|<=200 l0 h_i/S for each active i, since P81_i<100 and S>400.
In the polynomial (I.3) with g_z=x_0=0, the factor multiplying each
r81_i or w81_i has absolute value at most 18: |g_i|<=1 and the total
active step mass is <17. Thus the total perturbation is at most
61200(k0+l0)/S<(k0+l0)/2, since S>2^18. Consequently M_I<0.

For Law II use its exact routed expression (II.3), setting all values
f_i=0 after their cancellation. Checkpoints p<=13 contribute at most
4000/Tc in absolute value. To verify this bound, there are seven such
checkpoints, h_p<8, every formal position has absolute value <9,
each finite row has absolute quadratic value <=20, and each optimum
row <=19/2. The bracket
Q_(z,p)+sum omega[2Q_(p,ell)+Q_(ell,p)+Q_(ell,star)]
has absolute value <=70. Summing t_p<=56/Tc gives less than 4000/Tc.

For p>=15, g_p=0 and its ancestor height J(p,ell) is the SAME for all
four active leaves ell=0,2,8,10. Every such p is outside the cell
{0,...,14} containing them. The full bracket equals

    2*sum_active_leaves omega_(p,ell)*L_ell,
    (L_0,L_2,L_8,L_10)=(1,1+P2,1-B4,1-B4+P2).

Since raw_(p,ell)=(2p+1-ell)/[(p+1)J], its numerator before the positive
denominator (p+1)J Z_p is

    N_p=8p-16+6P2+(16-4p)C4 <69-8p <=-3(p+1), p>=15.

This uses C4>4 and P2<7/2. Hence every such checkpoint contributes
negatively. At the whole root p0=2^(m-1)-1, J=m and Z_p0<=p0+1,
so its contribution is at most

    -6h_p0/[Tc*m*(p0+1)].

This one term already dominates 4000/Tc. Indeed
h_p0/[m(p0+1)]>(7/16)(6/5)^(m-2)/m>4000.
At m=1536 use (6/5)^4>2 and 383 blocks of four powers, which give a
lower bound exceeding 2^383 before division by m and multiplication by
7/16. Thereafter (6/5)^(m-2)/m increases since m>5. Therefore M_II<0.
This is an all-depth analytical sign proof for the actual first-block
embedding, not a finite numeric H4 experiment or a statement about all
four-block embeddings.

## 9. What is excluded, omitted rows, and the remaining interface

Law I fails for every independent theta_i in [0,1] satisfying its
definition, even though the exact optimum distribution and all remote
fan weights are allowed to vary. The obstruction uses its deliberately
balanced contact equation (I.4). It does not exclude a different contact
relation, a different terminal distribution, or added independent rows.
One omitted potentially relevant row is Q_(n-2,0), explicitly positive
on the reset transcript. Adding it would define a different law, not
repair (I.2) within its parameters.

Law II fails for the literal LCA/position kernel (II.1), every common
kappa>=0, and degrees t_p proportional to h_p. The necessary condition
(II.8) applies to the specified routed support with arbitrary degrees
and receiver probabilities, but is only one necessary two-coordinate
test. It is not an enlarged-support impossibility theorem. For example,
the omitted direct checkpoint pair Q_(p,r), with r,p from (II.6), and
its reverse are not in (II.2). Either could alter that two-coordinate
matrix; neither is presumed to be a free PSD repair. Non-last leaf-to-
leaf rows and nonuniform reverse-cycle amounts are also outside this law.

The concrete failure mechanisms are different. In Law I, exact contact
balance combines a large optimum charge at a long-step coordinate with
negative forward-pair diagonal costs. In Law II, a low-decay ancestor
tail creates a mixed term of size h_r Omega_p(r) between equally weighted
checkpoint coordinates. Increasing a common reverse-cycle coefficient
scales the offending matrix. Its normalization by the whole checkpoint
mass also underpays the reset transcript despite including its known
violated row. Passing the older two-gradient waves does not address this.

An unresolved full-function construction must pay the reset while
controlling all such ancestor-crossing mixed terms, and must also prove
the complete remainder after the single charge (4.4) and actual terminal
square (4.1). Merely requiring unknown blocks to be PSD restates the
missing theorem. We give no third coefficient law or automatic next round.

No positive primal candidate is asserted. Consequently no static function,
minimum set, closest radius or attained W lower bound is attributed to any
of the formal negative directions or to the incompatible reset transcript.
The original unrestricted exact-profile minimax question remains OPEN.
