# Actual H2 primitive reserve and the retained U remainder

JOINT UNVERIFIED. Worker max2, invocation79, actual ended baseline78.
Project gd-exact-profile-20260926. Guidance e7cc96e528402c8e.
One allocated invocation, completed ceiling79/attempted80, controller cap1.
No formal calls or fact_submit are authorized. Every new assertion below
belongs to this ONE joint candidate proof, not to the accepted fact graph.

## Verdict and scope

The literal same-m H2=(A,B,A) has an explicit candidate full original-space
primitive certificate: eight nonnegative ordered interpolation multipliers
and three positive adjacent-gradient squares. It proves, within this joint
draft, V_H2 >= (epsilon^2/2) sum_(i=0)^2 ||g_i-g_(i+1)||^2.
No gradient is identified or deleted. This closes the previously unproved
low-d H2 stress test analytically, subject to the unavailable verifier gate.

For every compact convex body D containing0, every start, and every d>=1,
the same candidate gives U_d(T_H2 x)<=U_(d+2S2)(x), with the explicit
reserve above divided by d+2S2, plus further nonnegative terms. The
primitive certificate also gives the full smooth-convex three-step result
W_3(H2)=1/(1+2S2). This is a finite-block assertion, not an asymptotic result.

The full all-depth U law remains OPEN. A general bridge identity displays
the precise signed defect, which turns negative beginning at the H3 join.
Its magnitude is bounded by 27 epsilon C_j^2 times an actual gradient-norm
drop. The initial-maximization remainder is retained exactly. It pays the
defect on a complete, fixed-interval cap/cap family with U ratio below1/4,
even though both child primitive reserves vanish. No uniform payment for
all bodies and starts is proved, and no uniform 1+C/m loss is proved.

One substantive mechanism is used: primitive energy with its actual
projection-normal and maximization reserves. The optional second H2
mechanism is not opened because the first H2 certificate succeeds.
The original unrestricted minimax target is OPEN. No new fact_id exists.

## 1. Parameters, accepted inputs, and original rows

Fix ONE integer m>=1536 and set

 epsilon=1/(4m), beta=1-epsilon, lambda=1+sqrt(2),
 c=lambda(1+epsilon), a=beta/c, s=sqrt(2)-1=1/lambda,
 H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j), S_j=sum H_j.

The symbol s in this report is the constant sqrt(2)-1, not a word mass.
For the physical H2 use A=1+a, B=1+beta, S2=2A+B.
For a general child use C_j=1+S_j and k_j=a*c^j.
All children inherit the SAME enclosing m. No function or radius resets.

For a convex differentiable 1-smooth function f, actual GD points satisfy
x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i g_i, g_i=grad f(x_i), f_i=f(x_i). Retain the actual
terminal gradient as a mathematical sample, even though no extra step is
taken there. Use the accepted residual orientation

 Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2 <=0.       (1.1)

This orientation and its necessity are in accepted1252b8823bb5a290,
proof paragraph1, which was read. For a word h with mass S define

 V_h=2 sum_(i<N) h_i(f_i-f_N)
      +sum_(i<N) h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2-S(S+1)||g_N||^2. (1.2)

Accepted04219554e41d9750, Sections10-11, supplies this definition, the
full original-space certificate criterion, the exact-root primitive join,
and the dominant identity J_(h,(h,S+1))=V_h. Those applicable sections,
including the coefficient audits and original-coordinate qualifications,
were read completely. Their exact-root join is NOT treated as a theorem
about shortening physical steps. The missing variant is derived below.

Accepted931b9653ce0f7201 has only the previously assigned scalar comparison
and conditional negative-reduction scope. No rate is inferred from the
affine-height representation d8b59c7d92425121. This report needs neither
as a new mathematical premise. The original normalization remains W=2R.

## 2. Exact elementary row identities

For one physical step x_v=x_u-h g_u, direct substitution in (1.1) gives

 V_(h)=-2Q_uv-2(1+h)Q_vu+(2-h^2)||g_u-g_v||^2.       (2.1)

To check every coefficient, the two rows are

 Q_uv=f_u-f_v+(1/2-h)||g_u||^2
             -<g_u,g_v>+||g_v||^2/2,
 Q_vu=f_v-f_u+||g_u||^2/2
             +(h-1)<g_u,g_v>+||g_v||^2/2.

Adding2Q_uv+2(1+h)Q_vu to V_(h) cancels both function values and
leaves diagonal coefficients2-h^2 and mixed coefficient-2(2-h^2).
Consequently the leaf is primitive for0<=h<=sqrt(2), without a product
equality assumption.

For h>0, adding the same two interpolation rows gives
Q_uv+Q_vu=||g_u-g_v||^2-h<g_u,g_u-g_v>. Therefore

 ||g_u||^2-||g_v||^2
   =(2/h-1)||g_u-g_v||^2-(2/h)(Q_uv+Q_vu).           (2.2)

This norm drop is nonnegative for0<h<=2, with its complete original-row
certificate retained. Its sign is NOT asserted for an arbitrary long step.

## 3. General off-root primitive join: the missing term

Let l,r be any nonnegative words, with capacities
P=1+sum l and C=1+sum r. They need not already be primitive for the
identity in this section. Insert the actual bridge1+t, where0<=t<C.
Write p for the bridge query index, z for the terminal index, and j for
the nonterminal right-child indices, with physical steps r_j.
Thus x_z=x_p-(1+t)g_p-sum_j r_j g_j.
Put

 T=P+C+t, K=(2P+C+t)/(C-t),
 Delta=PC-t(P+C+t), R=(P+t)Delta/(C-t),
 E=sum_j r_j Q_(j,p)+Q_(z,p)+t Q_(p,z)
                                      +sum_j r_j Q_(j,z).       (3.1)

Then the exact identity, on the actual original formal space, is

 V_(l,1+t,r)=V_l+K V_r-(K-1)E
                              +R(||g_p||^2-||g_z||^2).         (3.2)

No changed trajectory at an exact-root bridge appears here. All child
energies use their actual positions in the shortened or lengthened word.

Here is the complete coefficient audit. Writing G=sum_j r_j g_j, expansion
of E cancels all mixed gradient terms. Its value part is

 2sum_j r_j f_j+(t-C)f_p+(2-C-t)f_z,

and its gradient part is

 sum_j r_j(1-r_j)||g_j||^2
       +[(C-t)/2-t^2]||g_p||^2+(C+t)||g_z||^2/2.    (3.3)

For example, in sum r_j Q_(j,p) the position and squared-difference
terms combine to t<G,g_p>+||G||^2/2
+sum r_j(1-r_j)||g_j||^2/2+(C-1)||g_p||^2/2.
The analogous sum r_j Q_(j,z) contributes -||G||^2/2
+sum r_j(1-r_j)||g_j||^2/2-<G,g_z>+(C-1)||g_z||^2/2.
Q_(z,p) contributes t<g_z,g_p>+<G,g_z>
+(||g_p||^2+||g_z||^2)/2, and t Q_(p,z) contributes
-t(t+1/2)||g_p||^2-t<g_p,G>+t||g_z||^2/2-t<g_p,g_z>.
These four displayed expressions sum to (3.3).

The choice (K-1)(C-t)=2(P+t) matches every value coefficient in (3.2).
After that cancellation the two remaining gradient coefficients in
V_parent-V_l-K V_r+(K-1)E are

 P^2-Kt^2=R,
 KC^2-T^2=-R.                                      (3.4)

The first equality follows by multiplying by C-t and factoring
(P+t)[PC-t(P+C+t)]; the second follows since the two expressions sum to0.
This proves (3.2) with every value, gradient, and mass term accounted for.

At Delta=0, one recovers the accepted exact-root identity, including
K=(T/C)^2. Off that root, discarding R is unjustified. If Delta>=0
and the actual bridge-plus-right-child history has a certified gradient
norm drop, (3.2) does give a valid shortened-bridge primitive certificate.
The following application proves that drop from two actual short steps.

## 4. The literal H2: signs and complete multiplier array

Set C=1+A=2+a in this section, t=beta, and define

 K=(3C+beta)/(C-beta),
 Delta=C^2-beta(2C+beta), R=(C+beta)Delta/(C-beta),
 delta_A=2-A^2.                                    (4.1)

All these parameters refer to the ACTUAL H2=(A,B,A).
One has1<A<sqrt(2), 1<B<2, C>beta, K>1, delta_A>0.
The sign of Delta needs the actual bridge comparison, not just B<2.
Using a=s(1-epsilon)/(1+epsilon) and2s+s^2=1 gives

 s C-beta=epsilon(1+epsilon-2s^2)/(1+epsilon)>0,
 Delta=(s C-beta)(lambda C+beta)>0.                 (4.2)

This proves beta<s C without changing either leaf step.

For original indices0,1,2,3, equation (3.2) becomes

 V_H2=V_(A;0,1)+K V_(A;2,3)
       -(K-1)(A Q_21+Q_31+beta Q_13+A Q_23)
       +R(||g_1||^2-||g_3||^2).                    (4.3)

Apply (2.2) to the actual bridge B between1,2 and to the actual last
leaf A between2,3, then use (2.1) on the two leaves. The result is

 V_H2=-sum_(i!=j) Lambda_ij Q_ij
        +d0||g_0-g_1||^2+d1||g_1-g_2||^2
                              +d2||g_2-g_3||^2,    (4.4)

where

 d0=delta_A,
 d1=R(2/B-1)=R epsilon/B,
 d2=K delta_A+R(2/A-1),                             (4.5)

and the complete nonzero multiplier list is

 Lambda_01=2,
 Lambda_10=2(1+A),
 Lambda_12=2R/B,
 Lambda_21=(K-1)A+2R/B,
 Lambda_13=(K-1)beta,
 Lambda_31=K-1,
 Lambda_23=2K+(K-1)A+2R/A,
 Lambda_32=2K(1+A)+2R/A.                            (4.6)

All other entries, including every diagonal entry, are zero. Every entry
in (4.6) and every coefficient in (4.5) is positive. The full remainder
is the four-gradient path quadratic, with matrix

 [ d0,    -d0,       0,     0;
  -d0, d0+d1,     -d1,     0;
    0,    -d1,  d1+d2,   -d2;
    0,      0,    -d2,    d2 ].                     (4.7)

It is PSD on the entire original four-gradient space because its
quadratic form is exactly the three squared differences in (4.4).
Its kernel is the constant-gradient subspace; no equality among those
formal gradients has been imposed. The original initial-position row is
zero, consistently with the primitive criterion in the accepted fact.

As an independent flow audit, the function coefficients in
-sum Lambda_ij Q_ij are (2A,2B,2A,-2S2). For the bridge coefficient,
-2A+(K-1)(C-beta)=-2A+2(C+beta)=2B. The last nonterminal
coefficient is -2(K-1)A+2KA=2A. Thus no function-value term is hidden.

There is a simple uniform reserve. Since epsilon<1, s>1/4, and
A=sqrt(2)-2s epsilon/(1+epsilon),

 delta_A=[2s epsilon/(1+epsilon)](sqrt(2)+A)>epsilon/2.

Since s<1/2, (4.2) gives sC-beta>epsilon/4 and lambda C+beta>4.
Hence Delta>epsilon and R>epsilon. With B<2, this proves

 d0>=epsilon^2/2, d1>=epsilon^2/2, d2>=epsilon^2/2,
 V_H2 >= (epsilon^2/2)sum_(i=0)^2||g_i-g_(i+1)||^2. (4.8)

The bounds are deliberately conservative; they are symbolic inequalities,
not arithmetic or numerical evaluations.

## 5. Projection bodies, optimum normalization, and V exactly

Let D be any nonempty compact convex subset of a finite-dimensional
Euclidean space containing0. Define

 P_D(x)=argmin_(g in D)||x-g||^2,
 F_D(x)=max_(g in D)(<x,g>-||g||^2/2),
 U_d(x)=max_(g in D)(2<x,g>-d||g||^2), d>0,
 T_h(x)=x-h P_D(x).                                (5.1)

Squared-distance minimization along every segment in D gives
<x-P_D(x),g-P_D(x)><=0 for every g in D; expansion proves sufficiency.
Adding this condition for two projections gives
||P_D(x)-P_D(y)||^2<=<x-y,P_D(x)-P_D(y)>.
Thus P_D is1-Lipschitz. Completing the square gives the unique maximizer
P_D(x) in F_D. The maximizing inequalities bound the first-order remainder
by ||t||||P_D(x+t)-P_D(x)||<=||t||^2, proving grad F_D=P_D.
Therefore F_D is finite, convex, differentiable, and1-smooth.

The minimum is0 and the complete minimizer set is

 K_D={z:<z,g><=0 for every g in D}.                  (5.2)

If z is in K_D, all candidates for F_D(z) are nonpositive and g=0 attains0.
If <z,g>>0 for some g, sufficiently small positive multiples of g belong
to D and give positive objective. This proves both directions.
For every z_star in K_D and every M>0, completion of the square gives

 U_M(x)<=||x-z_star||^2/M.                           (5.3)

Indeed2<x,g>-M||g||^2<=2<x-z_star,g>-M||g||^2
<=||x-z_star||^2/M for each g in D. This bound uses the true minimizer
distance, including when0 is not a closest minimizer.

For an actual word trajectory, write x=x_0, y=x_N, g_i=P_D(x_i).
Here f_i=<x_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2, so telescoping the actual squared
distances in (1.2) gives the EXACT identity

 V_h=||x||^2-||y+S P_D(y)||^2.                      (5.4)

In detail, ||x||^2-||y||^2=2sum h_i<x_i,g_i>-sum h_i^2||g_i||^2.
Substitution in V_h leaves this expression minus2S f_N
minus S(S+1)||g_N||^2, which is (5.4). No s-composability definition
or equality between mass and product is invoked.

Also every original finite row becomes
Q_ij=<x_j-g_j,g_i-g_j><=0. All rows in (4.6) are therefore actual
same-body projection normals, including the two nonlocal terminal rows.

## 6. Exact U gap with the actual maximizer remainder

For any complete word of mass S and any d>=1, put

 M=d+2S, q=P_D(y/d), g=P_D(y), p0=P_D(x/M),
 zbar=[x-y+(d+S)q]/M,
 Rmax=M||p0-zbar||^2+2<x-Mp0,p0-zbar>.               (6.1)

The point zbar is in D: x-y=sum h_i g_i, and the nonnegative weights
h_i and d+S sum to M. All these points use the same fixed body.
Projection optimality at p0 gives Rmax>=0, term by term.

There is the exact identity requested in the assignment:

 U_M(x)-U_d(y)
   =[||x||^2-||y+S q||^2]/M+Rmax.                  (6.2)

To verify it, the difference between the initial maximum at p0 and its
value at zbar is exactly Rmax. Moreover x-M zbar=y-(d+S)q. Expanding
the value at zbar and subtracting2<y,q>-d||q||^2 gives the numerator
||x||^2-||y||^2-2S<y,q>-S^2||q||^2, divided by M.

For1<=d1<d2, let q_i=P_D(y/d_i). Comparing each maximizer with the
other feasible point gives

 d1(||q_1||^2-||q_2||^2)
 <=2<y,q_1-q_2><=d2(||q_1||^2-||q_2||^2).

The squared-norm difference must be nonnegative, since otherwise the
displayed lower bound would exceed its upper bound. The inner-product
difference is then nonnegative too. Consequently

 Lrad=2S<y,g-q>+S^2(||g||^2-||q||^2)>=0,
 U_M(x)-U_d(y)=(V_h+Lrad)/M+Rmax.                   (6.3)

Combining the explicit original-row identity (4.4) with (6.3) gives
the complete candidate H2 equality and lower bound

 U_(d+2S2)(x)-U_d(T_H2 x)
   =[-sum Lambda_ij Q_ij+sum_(i=0)^2 d_i||g_i-g_(i+1)||^2
                                                    +Lrad]/(d+2S2)+Rmax
   >= [epsilon^2/(2(d+2S2))]sum_(i=0)^2||g_i-g_(i+1)||^2.       (6.4)

This holds at arbitrary starts, arbitrary finite dimension, and EVERY
d>=1, including the low-d regime left unpaid previously. It also retains
the actual maximization and radial reserves rather than silently spending
them twice. At d=1, U_1=2F_D, so (5.3) gives

 2F_D(T_H2 x)<=dist(x,K_D)^2/(1+2S2).                (6.5)

Scaling f=L F_D and using steps h/L preserves the trajectory and the W=2R
normalization. The projection-class implication is correctly normalized.

## 7. Finite H2 full-function consequence, without a class restriction

The certificate (4.4) is valid for all convex1-smooth f, not only F_D.
Translate a selected minimizer to0 and subtract its value. Let
u=(h_0,...,h_(N-1),S+1), whose sum is Dmass=1+2S.
The accepted identity from04219554e41d9750 is

 V_h=||x_0||^2+2sum_i u_i Q_(i,star)-2Dmass f_N
                                      -||x_0-sum_i u_i g_i||^2.       (7.1)

Its direct audit is also short: the last vector is y-(S+1)g_N;
expanding the terminal optimum row cancels its mixed term, and the
remaining expression is (1.2) after telescoping squared distances.
The weights and -Q_(i,star) are nonnegative. Thus V_H2>=0 proves
W_3(H2)<=1/(1+2S2), uniformly over the full original function class.

The matching lower example is the scalar Huber function with threshold
tau=1/(1+2S2): f(t)=t^2/2 for |t|<=tau and
f(t)=tau|t|-tau^2/2 outside. It is convex,1-smooth, and uniquely minimized
at0. Start at1. Every query is in the positive linear region since every
prefix mass is at most S2 and1-S2*tau=(1+S2)/(1+2S2)>=tau.
Thus x_3=1-S2*tau and2f(x_3)=2tau-(2S2+1)tau^2=tau.
This proves the finite equality claimed in the verdict, within this draft.
It supplies no all-depth schedule bound.

## 8. Higher joins: explicit signed debt and its scale

Keep the SAME enclosing m. For the join forming H_(j+1), put
C=C_j=1+S_j, k=k_j=a*c^j, and T=2C+k. Its mass is T-1.
The general identity (3.2) has

 K_j=(3C+k)/(C-k),
 Delta_j=C^2-k(2C+k), R_j=(C+k)Delta_j/(C-k),
 V_parent=V_left+K_j V_right-(K_j-1)E_j
                             +R_j(||g_p||^2-||g_z||^2).       (8.1)

Here E_j is precisely (3.1), on the actual two child histories and bridge.
All its weights are nonnegative. No radius or child parameters reset.

The needed scalar mass facts can be derived symbolically. From the word
definition C_(j+1)=2C_j+a*c^j and C_0=1. With v=a/(c-2),

 C_j=(1-v)2^j+v c^j, 0<v<1,
 k_j/C_j=a/[v+(1-v)(2/c)^j] <c-2.                  (8.2)

Substitution proves the displayed closed form. Since c>lambda>2 and
c(c-2)>lambda(lambda-2)=1>beta, one has0<a<c-2 and hence0<v<1.
The ratio in (8.2) increases with j. Also lambda<49/20 and
epsilon<1/100 give c<5/2, so k_j/C_j<1/2 and K_j<7.

At j=1 the H2 comparison is Delta_1>0 from (4.2). At j=2,
C_2=5-epsilon+2s(1-epsilon)/(1+epsilon) and k_2=lambda(1-epsilon^2),
so, using lambda=5s+2s^2,

 k_2-s C_2=epsilon[s+4s^2/(1+epsilon)-lambda epsilon]>0.       (8.3)

Indeed epsilon<1/8, s>2/5, and lambda<5/2 imply
lambda epsilon<5/16<2/5<s. Monotonicity in (8.2) then gives
Delta_j<0 for EVERY j>=2. Thus the primitive sign genuinely changes
at the H3 join, not at H2.

The magnitude is uniformly controlled. Write r=k_j/C_j; then
s<r<s+lambda epsilon, and

 0<-Delta_j/C_j^2=2r+r^2-1
   <2sqrt(2)lambda epsilon+lambda^2 epsilon^2<9epsilon.

The last estimate uses sqrt(2)<3/2, lambda<5/2, epsilon<1/8.
Since (C+k)/(C-k)<3, it follows that

 0<-R_j<27epsilon C_j^2.                            (8.4)

For actual gradients the negative contribution in (8.1) is therefore
bounded below by

 -27epsilon C_j^2 (||g_p||^2-||g_z||^2)_+,

where t_+=max(t,0). This estimate quantifies the debt; it does not pay it.
The factor C_j^2 and the uncontrolled actual endpoint norm drop cannot be
suppressed inside an unspecified o(1) or a claimed uniform1+C/m loss.

## 9. An actual whole-parent payment by the retained remainder

This section rederives the previously found cap/cap diagnostic, then
computes its actual remainder explicitly. The family itself is NOT claimed
as a new counterexample. Its precise payment in (6.2) is the new use here.

Fix2<=j<m, write C=C_j, k=k_j, r=k/C, T=2C+k, P=T-1, and use ONE
static interval D=[-r,1], with initial point x=C. Its F_D is a legal
convex1-smooth function uniquely minimized at0 because0 is interior to D.

Throughout the complete left H_j, an actual prefix of mass v<=C-1
has position C-v>=1 and projection1. Its endpoint is1. The bridge1+k
sends it to-k. Throughout the complete right H_j, a prefix of mass
v<=C-1 has position-k+vr<=-r and projection-r. The final point is-r.
All queries, both children, and the last physical leaf are present.
Every prefix assertion follows from nonnegative physical steps; no
trajectory or recurrence was executed on a machine.

Both child primitive residues are zero by (5.4): their shifted endpoints
equal their respective inputs. Every row of E_j is zero: the bridge
normal at1 is zero, the terminal normal at-r is zero, and every right
gradient equals-r. Nevertheless the parent has

 V_parent=C^2-T^2r^2=R_j(1-r^2)<0.                 (9.1)

For the second equality, multiply the definition of R_j by1-k^2/C^2
and use C^2+kT=(C+k)^2. This exhibits actual failure of primitive gluing
from child signs or their adjacent-gradient reserves alone.

Now retain the actual U points, for ANY d>=1. Set M=d+2P. Since C/M<1,

 p0=C/M, q=-r/d, zbar=(C-P r/d)/M,
 x-Mp0=0,
 Rmax=P^2 r^2/(d^2 M).                             (9.2)

The final scaled projection is inside the same interval, including its
boundary at d=1. These formulas are actual maximizers, not chosen reserves.
Substitution into (6.2) yields

 [C^2-r^2(1+P/d)^2]/M+P^2r^2/(d^2M)
       =C^2/M-r^2/d
       =U_M(C)-U_d(-r).                            (9.3)

Thus the auxiliary square pays the real negative primitive defect at d=1;
for larger d the exact radial adjustment in (6.3) is also present.

This payment has a uniform margin on this entire endpoint-cap family.
Since a>1/3 and beta>9/10, C_2=4+2a+beta>5, so C_j>5 for j>=2.
Here a>1/3 follows from beta>9/10 and c<5/2. With r<1/2 and
P=2C+k-1<(5/2)C-1,

 U_d(-r)/U_M(C)=r^2 M/(d C^2)
   <(5C-1)/(4C^2)<1/4.                            (9.4)

The last inequality follows from C^2-5C+1>0 for C>5.
This proves an actual whole-parent gap greater than3/4 of its initial U
on the stated family. It does NOT prove such a margin on arbitrary starts
or bodies. In particular it cannot be used as a blanket lower bound on
Rmax in an unrelated trajectory.

## 10. Remaining interface and tested failures

First direct attempt: extend primitive positivity from H2 by child
nonnegativity and their square reserves. Equations (8.1) and (9.1)
decisively refute that general-body strengthening. Even two perfectly
legal children with every adjacent-gradient reserve equal to0 can have
negative parent V. This is consistent with the earlier H3 counterexample.

Second direct attempt: retain the exact initial-maximization remainder
and quantify the parent payment. Equations (6.2), (8.4), and (9.2)-(9.4)
give respectively the exact interface, the uniform size of its signed
primitive term, and a complete payment on a legal diagnostic family.
They do not give a lower bound for Rmax or the radial term in every
same-body trajectory. No counterexample to the desired U law was found.

For the first unclosed parent H3, both actual H2 children now have the
explicit original-row reserves (4.4). The remaining arbitrary-start
inequality is a comparison of those reserves and all the retained
nonlocal normals, radial correction, and initial-maximization remainder
against (-R_2)(||g_p||^2-||g_z||^2). It is an unproved inequality, not a
result obtained merely by displaying the exact identity. At higher
depths the child V themselves may be negative and also need a genuinely
quantified U invariant. An unknown SDP or an exponential cell list has
not been substituted for that missing estimate.

The new H2 certificate is stronger than a projection-only calibration,
but its finite three-step full-class consequence is not a full-class
all-depth rate. Nor can any all-body height-zero projection result be
silently extended through the accepted affine-height representation.
Every eventual full-function estimate still needs its uniform body,
height, dimension, start, and enclosing-m quantifiers.

## 11. Retrieval, honesty, and allocation closeout

Skills applied: query-memory, direct-proving, search-math-results, and
construct-counterexamples. The verify-proof instructions were read to
check the gate, but no submission is permitted in this allocation.
All new steps have therefore been kept inside this joint unverified draft.

Search context: mature_subproblem; active program actual primitive reserve

# Quantitative queried cuts of full objective certificates

Status: complete self-contained candidate calculations, NOT verifier-accepted.
Current TASK/master_guidance closes routine verification and numerical search;
neither a solver nor fact_submit is used. Every new calculation below belongs
to this single proof package, not to a chain of unverified premises. The only
accepted mathematical dependency is fact `1252b8823bb5a290`, for the exact
normalized problem, interpolation necessity, and attained full certificates.

## 1. The unchanged target and the full certificate hypothesis

For a finite nonnegative schedule h=(h_0,...,h_(n-1)), W_n(h) is the
supremum of 2(f(x_n)-f(x_star))/(L||x_0-x_star||^2) over every finite
dimension, convex differentiable f with L-Lipschitz gradient and a minimizer
x_star, and x_0 != x_star, using x_(i+1)=x_i-(h_i/L) grad f(x_i).
The exact benchmark is U_n^ZJ=1/B_n, B_n=2S_bullet(n)+1, with the original
phi/psi recursions in accepted fact `b98bb807d514e80d`. The affirmative
target remains: for every epsilon>0, all sufficiently large n and EVERY
nonnegative length-n schedule have W_n(h)>=(1-epsilon)/B_n. A negative
answer requires a fixed positive relative improvement along unbounded
horizons by actual full-objective upper bounds. Neither outcome is claimed.

Work at smoothness one and let x_0,g_0,...,g_n be independent formal vectors.
Set x_i=x_0-sum_(t<i)h_t g_t and x_star=g_star=f_star=0. All finite f_i
are independent formal scalars. Put I={star,0,...,n}, lambda_ii=0, and

 Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2.

A supplied full certificate consists of a finite real q_cert and ACTUAL
lambda_ij>=0 for which

 M=q_cert||x_0||^2-2f_n+sum_(i!=j)lambda_ij Q_ij                 (1)

has every value coefficient zero and is PSD on the full original formal
space. In particular the finite-node flows obey

 out_i-in_i=2*1_(i=n).                                         (2)

No optimum-source edge is forbidden. No support, symmetrizability,
positive-definiteness, h_i<2, or Huber equality assumption is made.
Zero-step gradients remain independent.

Necessity of Q_ij<=0 on legal instances (accepted1252b8823bb5a290) gives
W_n(h)<=q_cert for any such certificate. Conversely, that SAME accepted
fact supplies a full certificate at q_cert=W_n(h) for every nonnegative
h, including empty/all-zero schedules, by positive-step attainment and
explicit zero-step lifting. Thus any universal necessary inequality proved
below can be applied to at least one attained certificate at the actual W.
An inequality involving the multipliers of a particular supplied certificate
is not by itself a multiplier-free lower bound on W.

Every feasible q_cert is positive. Setting all gradients to zero first
gives q_cert>=0. Summing (2) yields
sum_i lambda_(i,star)=2+sum_i lambda_(star,i)>=2. If q_cert=0, set all
finite gradients to the same scalar1. The coefficient of scalar x_0 in
M is then -sum_i lambda_(i,star)<0, so M is unbounded below. This excludes0.

## 2. A nontrivial queried-cut capacity inequality

Fix a queried index j<n, write b_cut=h_j, and let T_cut={j+1,...,n}.
Its complement in I includes the optimum and indices0,...,j. Define

 s_tail=sum_(j<t<n)h_t,
 ell_tail(k)=sum_(j<t<k)h_t for k in T_cut,
 C_in=sum_(i outside T_cut,k in T_cut)lambda_ik,
 C_out=sum_(i in T_cut,k outside T_cut)lambda_ik,
 F_cut=sum_(k in T_cut)lambda_(j,k),
 B_cut=sum_(k in T_cut)lambda_(k,j),
 I_cut=sum_(i!=j)lambda_(i,j),
 J_forward=sum_(k in T_cut)ell_tail(k) lambda_(j,k),
 J_in=sum_(i outside T_cut,k in T_cut)ell_tail(k) lambda_ik,
 mu_query=lambda_(j,star),
 mu_tail=sum_(i in T_cut)lambda_(i,star).

These are actual array sums, including every remote edge and optimum edge.
Summing (2) over T_cut gives C_out-C_in=2. Set

 A_cut=I_cut-b_cut F_cut,
 D_cut=1+2s_tail+C_in+J_in,
 K_cut=b_cut C_out-F_cut-B_cut-J_forward.                       (3)

The following matrix is necessarily PSD:

              [ q_cert       -mu_query/2       -mu_tail/2 ]
 T_query   =  [ -mu_query/2    A_cut             K_cut/2   ].    (4)
              [ -mu_tail/2    K_cut/2            D_cut     ]

Proof. Evaluate (1) on the formal subspace

 x_0=z,  g_i=0 (i<j),  g_j=u,  g_i=v (i>j).

All value coefficients have already canceled, so they may be set to0.
Only finite-source edges to star contribute z; their total is
-<z,mu_query*u+mu_tail*v>. The coefficient of ||u||^2 in the norm
terms is (out_j+in_j)/2=I_cut, since j<n. The only additional diagonal
terms are -h_j lambda_(j,k)||u||^2 for k>j. This gives A_cut.

For the ||v||^2 coefficient, set u=z=0. A tail point k then has
x_k=-ell_tail(k)v, and a non-tail finite point has position0. Within-tail
position terms sum to

 sum_(k in T_cut)ell_tail(k)(out_k^internal-in_k^internal).

Use (2) to replace the internal divergence by
2*1_(k=n)-out_k^cross+in_k^cross. The outgoing cross position terms
add back sum_k ell_tail(k)out_k^cross. This leaves
2s_tail+J_in. The cross norm terms are
(C_out+C_in)/2=1+C_in; internal norm terms are zero. Hence D_cut.

For the coefficient of <u,v>, norm terms give -(F_cut+B_cut).
An edge j->k with k in T_cut contributes -ell_tail(k)<u,v>, totaling
-J_forward. An edge sourced in T_cut and targeting its complement
has the position shift +b_cut*u, totaling b_cut C_out<u,v>.
All other position terms have either zero source gradient or cancel
the common tail shift. These are exactly K_cut. Thus the restriction is

 q_cert||z||^2-<z,mu_query*u+mu_tail*v>
                   +A_cut||u||^2+K_cut<u,v>+D_cut||v||^2,

whose scalar coefficient matrix is (4). Since the original polynomial
is PSD on every formal vector, its restriction is PSD. This restriction
is a necessary test, not an assertion that its formal vectors are legal
trajectories, and it deletes no edge from the original certificate.

Complete the independent z square, using q_cert>0. Put

 a_cut=A_cut-mu_query^2/(4q_cert),
 d_cut=D_cut-mu_tail^2/(4q_cert),
 k_cut=K_cut-mu_query*mu_tail/(2q_cert).

Then (4) is equivalent to

 a_cut>=0, d_cut>=0, k_cut^2<=4a_cut*d_cut.                     (5)

In particular, when d_cut>0 the quantitative incidence cost is

 I_cut >= b_cut F_cut + mu_query^2/(4q_cert)
              + [b_cut C_out-F_cut-B_cut-J_forward
                       -mu_query*mu_tail/(2q_cert)]^2/(4d_cut). (6)

When d_cut=0 the square numerator in (6) must be zero and the remaining
condition is I_cut>=b_cut F_cut+mu_query^2/(4q_cert). There is no division
by a vanished residual and no strict-PSD assumption. Equivalently,

 |b_cut C_out-F_cut-B_cut-J_forward
                     -mu_query*mu_tail/(2q_cert)|
       <=2 sqrt((I_cut-b_cut F_cut-mu_query^2/(4q_cert))
                 *(1+2s_tail+C_in+J_in-mu_tail^2/(4q_cert))).   (7)

If every edge incident to j vanishes, I_cut=F_cut=B_cut=J_forward=
mu_query=0. Then (5) forces b_cut C_out=0. Since C_out>=2, b_cut=0.
This recovers the qualitative incidence obstruction, now as the zero-cost
case of an exact quantitative condition at every finite certificate level.
If b_cut=0 there is no contradiction; the zero-step case is included.

For an alternative exact q cost define

 Z_cut=[ A_cut K_cut/2 ; K_cut/2 D_cut ],
 m_cut=(mu_query,mu_tail)^T.

Condition (4) is equivalent to Z_cut>=0, m_cut in Range(Z_cut), and

 q_cert >= (1/4) m_cut^T Z_cut^+ m_cut.                         (8)

Here Z_cut^+ inverts its positive eigenvalues and vanishes on its kernel.
For proof, diagonalize the real symmetric Z_cut. A component of m_cut
on a zero eigenvector would give an unbounded linear polynomial in that
free direction. Once these components vanish, complete one square for
each positive eigenvalue. This proves necessity and sufficiency, including
all singular cases. If 4A_cut D_cut-K_cut^2>0, the right side is explicitly

 (D_cut*mu_query^2-K_cut*mu_query*mu_tail+A_cut*mu_tail^2)
                         /(4A_cut D_cut-K_cut^2).             (9)

## 3. Why total cut flow cannot replace its position moment

Here is ONE exact supplied certificate. Take n=2, h=(1,1), q_cert=1/5,
with the following nonzero multipliers and every other multiplier zero:

 lambda_02=1, lambda_10=1, lambda_21=2,
 lambda_(1,star)=1, lambda_(2,star)=1.                          (10)

All coefficients are nonnegative. The finite flows are respectively0,0,2.
With x_1=x_0-g_0 and x_2=x_0-g_0-g_1, direct expansion of (1) gives

 M=(1/5)||x_0-(5/2)(g_1+g_2)||^2
                                      +(3/4)||g_1-g_2||^2.   (11)

For an explicit coefficient check, the sum of the position terms in (10)
is -<x_0,g_1+g_2>-||g_0||^2+<g_0,g_1>+<g_0,g_2>
+3<g_1,g_2>. The norm terms add ||g_0||^2+2||g_1||^2+2||g_2||^2
-<g_0,g_1>-<g_0,g_2>-2<g_1,g_2>. Their sum is exactly the expansion
of (11) after adding q_cert||x_0||^2. This is full PSD with the
original independent g_0 present as a kernel direction.

For j=0, T_cut={1,2}, the exact data are

 b_cut=1, s_tail=1, C_in=1, C_out=3,
 F_cut=B_cut=I_cut=1, J_forward=J_in=1,
 mu_query=0, mu_tail=2,
 A_cut=0, D_cut=5, K_cut=0.

Thus (4) is the singular rank-one square (z-5v)^2/5, and (8) gives
the exact cost1/5. Dropping the target-position moment J_in would assert

 mu_tail^2 <= 4q_cert(1+2s_tail+C_in),                          (12)

which here reads4<=16/5, false. Equivalently, that proposed scalar cost
would falsely require q_cert>=1/4. Dropping J_forward from K_cut likewise
produces K_cut=1 while A_cut=0, falsely rejecting the same full certificate.
Therefore unweighted cross-cut flow cannot be substituted in these exact
capacity tests. The actual remote edge0->2, whose target lies one unit
of step mass into the tail, supplies the missing moment. This refutes
(12), not every conceivable scalar invariant or an existential re-selection.


## 7. The correct mass-only relaxation, with its explicit loss

One CAN remove the actual first moments at a single cut if their loss is
paid explicitly. Nonnegative h and lambda imply

 0<=J_forward<=s_tail F_cut,
 0<=J_in-J_forward<=s_tail(C_in-F_cut).

In particular the valid scalar tail bound is

 q_cert>=mu_tail^2/[4(1+2s_tail+(1+s_tail)C_in)].               (24)

Proof: in(4), the z,v principal minor gives mu_tail^2<=4q_cert D_cut,
and D_cut<=1+2s_tail+(1+s_tail)C_in. The supplied certificate(10) has
equality in(24). Thus(24), which pays the full possible elapsed mass,
survives that example whereas the unweighted replacement(12) does not.

There is also a closed elimination of the TWO moment bounds from the
coupled query test. Write a=a_cut from(5) only within this paragraph, and
define the scalar data

 R_mom=b_cut C_out-F_cut-B_cut-mu_query*mu_tail/(2q_cert),
 E_mom=1+2s_tail+C_in+s_tail(C_in-F_cut)-mu_tail^2/(4q_cert),
 U_mom=s_tail F_cut.

Every true certificate necessarily satisfies

 a>=0, E_mom+R_mom+a>=0,
 dist(R_mom+2a,[0,U_mom])^2<=4a(E_mom+R_mom+a).              (25)

Here dist denotes the ordinary distance of a real number from the stated
closed interval. For proof put t=J_forward. Then0<=t<=U_mom and
d_cut<=E_mom+t. Equation(5) gives

 (R_mom-t)^2<=4a(E_mom+t), E_mom+t>=0.

Completing the scalar square gives
(t-(R_mom+2a))^2<=4a(E_mom+R_mom+a). For a>0, this is possible
for t in the interval exactly when the last two conditions in(25) hold;
the square inequality itself forces E_mom+t>=0. For a=0 the same
conditions require t=R_mom in[0,U_mom] and E_mom+R_mom>=0, which is
the correct zero-residual rule. This proves(25) including degeneracy.

Conversely(25) is sufficient for the ABSTRACT moment relaxation of(5):
choose such a t and set J_forward=t, J_in=t+s_tail(C_in-F_cut).
These values satisfy the two displayed interval bounds and yield(5).
No claim is made that those abstract moments arise from actual target
indices or a common array across several cuts. It is precisely that
cross-cut realizability/correlation information that(25) relaxes.
The scalar relaxation is therefore a genuine necessary inequality,
not a constant-preserving F/G separator or an exact iterative closure.

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-001252 -->
# One paid first inner reversal and an actual-energy child state

**ONE JOINT UNVERIFIED REPORT.** Project gd-exact-profile-20260926,
worker max3, invocation83 after ended82; completed ceiling83 / attempted84,
controller cap1. Operative TASK.md and master_guidance cadd37d70aded16f;
plan eab346254c0666ea.

No fact_submit, formal verification, mathematical program, numerical or
arithmetic replay, executed trajectory/recurrence, solver, optimizer,
benchmark DP, installation, consult, or subagent was used. This report is
one joint proof draft. Every ingredient used below is proved within it.
No earlier unverified finding is a correctness premise.

**Outcome.** For every positive first inner reversing level J, there is a
complete full-word objective bound strictly below 4/(5D) for every start
whose FIRST complete H_J ends in the closed core. All starts of radius
at most v Q automatically meet that condition. The first reversing
bridge is allowed to leave the core; its entire following H_J is retained.
After that complete child, an actual radius/gradient/energy state proves
that every remaining physical step stays in the core. The original
crossing price is paid once, and the complete quadratic suffix is kept.

This is a restricted trajectory theorem, NOT an all-start odd-family
bound. Starts for which the first H_J ends outside the core remain an
unresolved interface. The whole odd-family rate and the original
all-function minimax problem remain OPEN. The second, capacity-amplitude
mechanism is not activated in this allocation.

## 1. Fixed data and the precise scoped result

Fix ONE integer m>=1536. Every child uses these SAME parameters:

    epsilon=1/(4m), beta=1-epsilon, lambda=1+sqrt(2),
    c=lambda*(1+epsilon), a=beta/c,
    d_j=a*c^j, h_j^lev=1+d_j,
    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,h_j^lev,H_j),
    S_j=sum H_j, A_j=1+S_j,
    D=1+2S_m, n=2^m-1,
    ell=1+a, theta=a*beta.

Here h_i denotes a chronological physical entry of H_m when no level
superscript is present. d_m is auxiliary, not an extra physical step.

Fix 0<kappa<=1 and finite B>=kappa, and keep the ONE function

    v=1-kappa, u=v+B,
    G(r)=min(B,max(kappa*r,r-v)), r>=0,
    g(x)=sign(x)*G(|x|), g(0)=0,
    f(x)=integral_0^x g(t)dt, F(r)=f(r), r>=0.

Let Psi_j(s) be the actual output of the complete word H_j on this
fixed function from s. Its exact interval radius is

    M_j(R)=max_(|s|<=R)|Psi_j(s)|, R>=0.

For the theorem below assume that

    J=min{j in {0,...,m-1}: kappa*(1+d_j)>1}
    exists and 1<=J<=m-1.                              (1.1)

Thus every physical core step in H_J is nonreversing. Define

    Q=(c-1)/(c-2)=1+1/(c-2),
    t=kappa*d_J, q=kappa*(1+d_J)-1=t-v,
    L_J=S_J/d_J, N=m-J-1,
    K_fr(z)=z for 0<=z<=1, K_fr(z)=exp(z-1) for z>=1,
    Z_fr=K_fr(q)*exp(-L_J*t).                                (1.2)

K_fr is only a scalar comparison function, not a new gradient law.

For a nonzero actual initial point s put R=|s| and

    y=|Psi_J(s)|.

**Scoped full-word theorem (joint unverified).** If y<=1, then the actual
trajectory under the complete H_m obeys

    2f(Psi_m(s))/R^2 < 4/(5D).                          (1.3)

If the initial H_J contains no strict sign crossing, the stronger bound
1/(2D) holds. A zero hit gives zero payoff.

All 0<R<=v Q satisfy y<=1, hence satisfy (1.3), uniformly in B, kappa,
m and the positive level J. The initial H_J may have any number of
exterior crossings before it enters the core. It may enter the core
on its actual last leaf, including by a same-side middle-to-core step.

For the complete first block H_(J+1), let r_(J+1) be its terminal radius.
The proof supplies the actual-energy state

    r_(J+1)<=y Z_fr,    Z_fr<1/4,
    kappa*d_(J+1)*r_(J+1)<25/32,
    G(r_(J+1))=kappa*r_(J+1),
    2F(r_(J+1))=kappa*r_(J+1)^2.                       (1.4)

The first reversing bridge may have landed outside radius1. That
excursion is proved to return during its following COMPLETE H_J.
It is not treated as a quadratic trajectory.

For each later complete initial block H_l, J+1<=l<=m, its actual
terminal radius r_l remains in the core and satisfies

    kappa*d_l*r_l
      <(25/32)*beta^(2(l-J-1)),
    r_l<=y Z_fr*theta^(l-J-1),
    G(r_l)=kappa*r_l, 2F(r_l)=kappa*r_l^2.            (1.5)

Every intervening physical prefix also remains in the core after
completion of that first H_(J+1). The exact final suffix formula is

    |Psi_m(s)|
      =r_(J+1)*product_(l=J+1)^(m-1)(q_l*P_l),
    q_l=kappa*(1+d_l)-1,
    P_l=product_(h in H_l)|1-kappa*h|.                 (1.6)

An empty product is1. All factors and every final physical leaf are
present. These are actual objective and gradient values, not merely
an absolute gradient cap.

The two excluded cases J=0 and no physical inner reversal are not
claimed as new domains here. Prior drafts cover specified portions of
them; those prior statements are not needed below.

## 2. Legality, the true energy, and ordered interpolation rows

Direct integration gives

    2F(r)=kappa*r^2,                          0<=r<=1,
    2F(r)=(r-v)^2+kappa*v,                    1<=r<=u,
    2F(r)=2B*r-[B^2+v*(2B-kappa)],            r>=u.    (2.1)

At r=1 the first two values equal kappa. At r=u the latter two
values equal B^2+kappa*v. Their derivatives also agree at the knots.
B=kappa collapses the middle interval; kappa=1 makes v=0.

The derivative g is continuous, nondecreasing, and has slopes kappa,
1 and0 on its pieces. Partitioning an arbitrary interval at its knots
proves |g(x)-g(y)|<=|x-y|. Thus f is finite, convex, differentiable,
and globally1-smooth. Since G(r)>0 for r>0, its unique minimizer is0,
and the true initial distance is R. The objective normalization is W=2R
in the project's notation: our payoff is 2f(Psi_m(s))/s^2.

For x<y let Delta=g(y)-g(x). Then 0<=Delta<=y-x and

    g(t)-g(x)>=max(0,Delta-(y-t)),
    g(y)-g(t)>=max(0,Delta-(t-x)),  x<=t<=y.

Integration proves both directed smooth-convex remainders are at least
Delta^2/2. Hence for EVERY pair of actual physical, terminal, or optimum
samples (x_i,g_i,f_i),

    f_i-f_j+g_i*(x_j-x_i)+(g_i-g_j)^2/2<=0.          (2.2)

Repeated positions have identical values and gradients. The full scalar
Gram matrix is positive semidefinite because each of its quadratic
forms is a square of a real linear combination of the actual vectors.
No formal terminal gradient or interpolation pair is removed.

For advertised smoothness L_adv>0, the objective L_adv*f with steps
h_i/L_adv has the same trajectory and normalized payoff.

We use the elementary lower bound

    G(r)>=kappa*min(r,1), r>=0.                       (2.3)

Inside the core it is equality. Outside the core, both B and
max(kappa*r,r-v) are at least kappa. This lower bound does not replace
the actual law, its cap, or its kink.

## 3. Exact interval map and branch ties

For a physical h>=1 put d=h-1 and q_h=|1-h*kappa|. The exact interval
image radius is

    E_h(R)=max{
      q_h*min(R,1),
      [d*min(R,u)-h*v]_+,
      [R-h*B]_+
    },                                               (3.1)

where [z]_+=max(z,0).

For a positive input radius r the signed update is

    (1-h*kappa)*r,                 0<=r<=1,
    h*v-d*r,                      1<=r<=u,
    r-h*B,                        r>=u.

At r=1 the two expressions agree because h*v-d=1-h*kappa.
At r=u they agree because h*v-d*u=v-d*B=u-h*B.
Thus the knot ties, and the collapsed middle interval at B=kappa,
are retained.

On each available affine piece the maximum absolute value occurs at an
endpoint. The core contributes the first term. On an available middle
piece the positive endpoint is bounded by the core endpoint, and the
negative endpoint contributes the second term. On an available tail
piece, its negative endpoint is the middle endpoint, and its positive
endpoint contributes the third term.

The formal middle term does not create a spurious extremum when R<1:
d*R-h*v <= (h*kappa-1)*R, since their difference is h*v*(R-1)<=0.
Its positive part is therefore bounded by the actual core term.
If R<u, a positive formal tail value satisfies
R-h*B<=R-h*G(R), the actual positive endpoint value, which is already
covered by the core or middle endpoint. This also handles R=0.

The signed update is continuous, odd, and fixes0. Its image of [-R,R]
is the entire symmetric interval with radius (3.1). Thus exact
chronological composition yields

    M_(j+1)=M_j composed with E_(h_j^lev) composed with M_j. (3.2)

No independently chosen incompatible child start is used. The quantitative
result below does not rely on a numerical replay of this identity.

## 4. Same-m masses and the core prefix lemma

For all m in the assignment,

    0<epsilon<1/1000, beta>99/100,
    12/5<c<17/7<5/2,
    2/5<a<1/2, 2/5<theta<1/2,
    c*theta=beta^2<1.                                 (4.1)

Use 7/5<sqrt(2)<17/12 for the c bounds. The lower a bound follows
from a>(99/100)*(7/17)>2/5. For theta use
theta=beta^2/c>(99/100)^2*(7/17)>2/5.
The other inequalities follow directly.

Put eta=a/(c-2). Since lambda*(lambda-2)=1 and c>lambda,

    0<eta<1,
    A_(j+1)=2A_j+d_j,
    A_j=(1-eta)*2^j+eta*c^j.                           (4.2)

The formula is verified by substitution and A_0=1. In particular,

    A_j>d_j/(c-2),    A_j<c^j for j>=1,
    D=2A_m-1<2c^m=2d_m/a.                            (4.3)

Also for every j>=1,

    S_j/d_j>7/5.                                      (4.4)

At j=1 the ratio is ell/beta>7/5. If L=S_j/d_j>7/5,
the next ratio is (2L+1+1/d_j)/c>(19/5)/(5/2)>7/5.
This is a symbolic induction, not an executed recurrence.

Define P_0=1 and
P_j=product_(h in H_j)|1-kappa*h| for j>=1.
For every 0<kappa<=1, jointly,

    every physical prefix product of H_j is at most1,
    P_j<=1,
    kappa*d_j*P_j<=theta for j>=1.                    (4.5)

For the leaf, |1-ell*kappa|<=1. Moreover
kappa*|1-ell*kappa|<=a:
on kappa<=1/ell its maximum is 1/(4ell)<a;
on kappa>=1/ell, kappa*(ell*kappa-1) increases up to a at1.
Multiplication by d_1=beta gives the last bound.

Suppose the claims are known for H_j, j>=1. If the level-j core
bridge reverses, its magnitude is
|1-kappa*(1+d_j)|<=kappa*d_j. Therefore

    kappa*d_(j+1)*P_(j+1)
      <=c*(kappa*d_j*P_j)^2
      <=c*theta^2=theta*beta^2<theta.

The prefix ending at the bridge has multiplier at most
kappa*d_j*P_j<=theta<1. Multiplying it by any prefix product of
the right H_j preserves the prefix bound.

If that bridge is nonreversing, every effective core step in H_(j+1)
belongs to [0,1]. Its prefix products are at most1, and

    P_(j+1)<=exp(-kappa*S_(j+1)),
    kappa*d_(j+1)*P_(j+1)
      <=1/[exp(1)*(S_(j+1)/d_(j+1))]
      <5/14<theta.

Here z*exp(-Lz)<=1/[exp(1)*L] follows by differentiation.
These arguments include zero factors and equality at a reversal
threshold, and prove (4.5).

Consequently a complete H_j started anywhere inside the closed core
remains there at EVERY physical prefix. Its actual output radius is
P_j times its input radius. This statement concerns a complete child,
not an arbitrary suffix begun at an arbitrary physical index.

## 5. The actual first-crossing price in a nonreversing child

Consider any complete H_b with kappa*(1+d_(b-1))<=1; for b=0 there
are no steps. Core entry is absorbing in H_b.

Suppose an actual trajectory in H_b has a first strict sign crossing,
at a level-k physical step h=1+d_k. Let r be its source radius,
p=G(r), and P its actual original prefix mass inside H_b.
Core absorption forces r>1. For every exterior source,

    r>=p+v.

Before the first crossing all radii decrease, and every queried gradient
is at least p. The complete left H_k immediately preceding this occurrence
gives P>=S_k. Therefore, for the original radius R_b of this child,

    R_b>=r+P*p>=v+(1+P)*p.

Strict crossing means (1+d_k)*p>r>=p+v, so p>v/d_k. It follows that

    R_b>v*(1+(1+P)/d_k)
        >=v*(1+A_k/d_k)>v Q.                         (5.1)

This is a price measured from the actual child's original start.
When H_b is the initial H_J of H_m, it is measured from the ORIGINAL
full-word start R. It does not reset that denominator.

The same proof also gives a local no-crossing criterion used below:
a start with radius at most v Q cannot have a strict crossing in H_J.
A zero hit is absorbing.

## 6. The first reversing bridge and its complete right child

From the definition of positive J,

    kappa*(1+d_(J-1))<=1,
    kappa*(1+d_J)>1,
    v>=a/(1+a)>2/7,
    v<t<=c*v,
    0<q=t-v<3v/2,
    q<(3/5)*t,
    Q>3,  L_J>7/5.                                   (6.1)

The bound on v uses J>=1 and hence kappa*ell<=1.
The t upper bound follows by multiplying
kappa*d_(J-1)<=v by c. The q/t bound follows from q<3v/2.

Assume the initial H_J has actual terminal radius y<=1.
Its level-J bridge has actual landing radius z=q*y<=q, and the
sign reverses unless y=0. No core approximation is made if z>1.

The following complete H_J, begun at z, has NO strict crossing:
its own core is absorbing, while an exterior first crossing would
require z>v Q by (5.1), contrary to z<=q<3v/2<v Q.
It must enter the core by its actual terminal. Otherwise all queried
and terminal radii exceed1 and every gradient is at least kappa,
so its final radius would be at most

    z-kappa*S_J<=q-L_J*t<0,

a contradiction. A same-side terminal core entry is included here.

To bound the returned radius, use the scalar comparison update

    C_h(r)=r-h*kappa*min(r,1).

This comparison map is nonnegative and nondecreasing on [0,infinity)
because every effective step of H_J has h*kappa<=1. Along the ACTUAL
noncrossing trajectory, (2.3) gives

    r_next=r-h*G(r)<=C_h(r).

Induction through the physical steps therefore bounds its final radius
by the composition of these comparison maps from z. This is an inequality
for the original fixed function; B has not been replaced or rescaled.

For r>0 define b(r)=log(r) if r<=1 and b(r)=r-1 if r>=1.
For an effective step 0<=alpha<=1,

    b(r-alpha*min(r,1))<=b(r)-alpha                 (6.2)

whenever the output is positive; a zero output stays zero.
Inside the core this follows from log(1-alpha)<=-alpha.
For a tail stay it is equality. For a tail-to-core transition it
follows from log(w)<=w-1 at w=r-alpha.

The final comparison radius lies below1, because
b(z)<=max(0,z-1)<L_J*t for z>0. Summing (6.2) and exponentiating
therefore bounds the actual returned radius by K_fr(z)*exp(-L_J*t).

For 0<=y<=1,

    K_fr(q*y)<=y*K_fr(q).                                   (6.3)

For q<=1 it is equality. If q>1 and q*y<=1, it follows from
q<=exp(q-1). If q*y>=1, the derivative of exp(q*y-1)/y has the
sign of q*y-1, so its maximum over that interval is at y=1.

Consequently, with r_(J+1) the output radius of the complete initial
H_(J+1),

    r_(J+1)<=y*Z_fr.                                    (6.4)

Both core and middle/tail parts of the possible excursion have been
retained in this estimate.

Two absolute bounds will pay for this one transition. Write e=exp(1).
The elementary series gives e>8/3>2.

If q<=1, then L_J*t>7q/3, so

    Z_fr=q*exp(-L_J*t)
       <=sup_(z>=0) z*exp(-7z/3)
       =3/(7e)<1/4.

Also q<(3/5)t and differentiation of t^2*exp(-L_J*t) give

    c*t*Z_fr
      <(3c/5)*4/(e^2*L_J^2)
      <1350/3136<1/2.                               (6.5)

If q>1, then q<(3/5)t gives

    Z_fr=exp(q-1-L_J*t)<exp(-1-(4/5)*t).

Equivalently using t>5q/3 gives
Z_fr<exp(-1-4q/3)<exp(-7/3)<1/4. Moreover

    c*t*Z_fr
      <c*sup_(z>=0) z*exp(-1-(4/5)*z)
      =5c/(4e^2)<25/32.                             (6.6)

Thus Z_fr<1/4 and c*t*Z_fr<25/32 for the full parameter range.
These constants are absolute. This proves the first child state (1.4).

If the excursion entered the core at a physical index tau inside the
right H_J, its exact remaining radius is its radius at tau times the
product of ALL remaining nonnegative factors 1-kappa*h. Equations
(6.2)-(6.4) bound that entire actual expression. They do not start
the quadratic product at the reversing bridge before core return.

## 7. All remaining ancestors and their actual terminal energy

After the complete INITIAL H_(J+1), the remaining literal word is

    h_(J+1)^lev,H_(J+1), h_(J+2)^lev,H_(J+2), ...,
    h_(m-1)^lev,H_(m-1).                             (7.1)

There are no skipped levels here: these are consecutive ancestors of
the leftmost H_(J+1). This is why the precise location in the statement
matters.

Suppose a radius r_l is the output of the complete initial H_l,
for l>=J+1, with r_l<=1 and kappa*d_l*r_l<1.
Every such level-l bridge reverses in the core, and its landing is

    q_l*r_l<=kappa*d_l*r_l<1.

The following complete H_l stays inside the core by (4.5). Hence

    r_(l+1)=q_l*P_l*r_l,
    r_(l+1)<=theta*r_l,
    kappa*d_(l+1)*r_(l+1)
      <=c*theta*(kappa*d_l*r_l)
      =beta^2*(kappa*d_l*r_l).                        (7.2)

Starting with (1.4), induction gives (1.5), every intervening prefix
is core, and (1.6) follows by multiplication.

In particular the true final objective is exactly

    2f(Psi_m(s))=kappa*r_m^2.                         (7.3)

The actual final gradient has magnitude kappa*r_m.
No middle-area constant is omitted: middle energy is used only before
the proved core return, while (7.3) applies after that return.
There cannot be a later core exit in this scoped theorem, by the
quantitative landing bound in (7.2).

Using (4.3), (6.4) and (7.2),

    D*kappa*r_m^2/R^2
      <(2c*t/a)*Z_fr^2*(y/R)^2*(c*theta^2)^N.           (7.4)

Since c*theta^2=theta*beta^2<1, ancestor depth creates no additional
loss. The first transition is paid once.

## 8. Objective closure using the original prefix price

If y=0, the result is zero. Assume y>0.

First suppose the original first H_J has no strict crossing.
Its radii decrease, and every queried gradient is at least kappa*y.
Thus its original start satisfies

    R>=y+S_J*kappa*y=(1+L_J*t)*y.                    (8.1)

The factor in (7.4), without its final factor at most1, is at most

    (2c/a)*t*Z_fr^2/(1+L_J*t)^2.                        (8.2)

For q<=1, use 2c/a<25/2, q<(3/5)t, and
(1+L_J*t)^2>=L_J^2*t^2. Expression (8.2) is less than

    (9/(2*L_J^2))*t*exp(-2L_J*t)
       <=9/(4e*L_J^3)
       <1125/2744<1/2.                              (8.3)

For q>1, use t>5/3 and
Z_fr^2<exp(-2-(8/5)t)<1/16. Then (8.2) is less than

    (25/2)*(25/49)*(3/5)*(1/16)
       =375/1568<1/2.                               (8.4)

For the exponential bound, e^2>4 and (8/5)t>8/3>2,
so each of the two exponential factors is less than1/4.
This proves the stronger 1/(2D) bound on the no-crossing-prefix branch.
This branch may still have the first inner reversal and its temporary
exterior excursion.

If the initial H_J has a strict crossing, its original start pays
R>v Q by (5.1). It ends at y<=1 by the theorem's explicit hypothesis.
Use t<=c*v and Z_fr<1/4 in (7.4). The coefficient is less than

    c^2/(8a*v*Q^2)
       <(25/4)/[8*(2/5)*(2/7)*9]
       =875/1152<4/5.                               (8.5)

This branch allows any earlier exterior crossings and any core-entry
time in the initial H_J. That core is absorbing up to the first
level-J bridge, so the theorem does not drop an earlier exit.
The first ORIGINAL crossing is priced only once.

Together (8.3)-(8.5) prove the complete full-word scoped claim (1.3).

## 9. An automatic input domain, and what remains outside it

Assume 0<R<=v Q. By (5.1) the initial H_J has no strict crossing.
If its terminal radius y were greater than1, every earlier queried
radius would also exceed1, and every queried gradient would be at
least kappa. Consequently,

    R>=y+kappa*S_J>1+kappa*S_J
       =v+kappa*A_J
       >v+(v/d_J)*A_J
       >v Q,

using t=kappa*d_J>v and (4.3). This contradiction proves y<=1.
In fact this automatic domain uses the stronger no-crossing-prefix
bound 1/(2D).

The actual gate is more general than this sufficient radius condition:
large starts can also enter the core in the first H_J and are covered
by (1.3). Membership in the gate is a condition on the ACTUAL fixed-law
trajectory, not an interval radius substituted into the payoff.

The unresolved complement is now explicit:

    positive J as in (1.1), R>v Q,
    |Psi_J(s)|>1.                                    (9.1)

For such a trajectory the first level-J bridge can have an exterior
source. Its later first core entry may lie inside a different child.
Its remaining ancestor list can skip levels, and the state
kappa*d_l*r_l<1 proved at the first leftmost block is not available.
Repeated later exits/returns and a final same-side middle-to-core entry
are not covered merely by (1.3). We do not label those histories closed.

This is the exact obstruction to declaring an all-start result from the
current proof. The whole odd-family sufficient bound

    sup_(s!=0) 2f(Psi_m(s))/s^2 <=(1+C/m)/D

for an absolute C remains unproved. No full-word objective counterexample
to that statement is claimed here.

## 10. Direct-proof screen and falsification limits

Two direct extensions were screened within the FIRST mechanism.

First, one can try to treat the landing q*y of the first reversing
bridge as already quadratic. This is unjustified when q*y>1, which
is permitted by (6.1). The proof above repairs that step with the
complete noncrossing right H_J, the comparison potential b, and the
actual return before starting the remaining quadratic suffix.
No universal product P_J may be attached to that bridge landing
without this return argument.

Second, one can try to reuse (7.2) after an arbitrary later core entry.
That induction requires kappa*d_l*r_l<1 at the NEXT actual bridge.
A mere radius bound r_l<=1 does not provide it at a large bridge.
If levels are skipped, d_l is larger by c to the number of skipped
levels while the corresponding complete-child factors were not run
after the entry. Multiplying in those absent factors would be a
different physical word. The original prefix mass may be large enough
to pay this jump, but that estimate has not been proved.

Counterexample screening keeps these two statements distinct:
the local assertion "every bridge preserves the core" fails whenever
kappa*(1+d_l)>2 and its input is1. This is a local test only, NOT an
improving complete-word objective witness and NOT a refutation of the
state (1.4)-(1.5). The latter has the stronger paid threshold and was
checked symbolically at all branch ties, zero factors, J=1 and J=m-1.

The earlier universal-amplitude counterexample in d34bc8275bb55379,
with kappa=B=1 and an O(1/m) necessary loss, remains awareness only.
Nothing in this proof assumes that failed assertion, spends its loss
at each level, or changes B to an amplitude w.

The A_(j-1) amplitude alternative is not asserted, refuted, or used.
The present state closes a concrete first-entry gate but does not
decide the exterior-source state (9.1). No separate second mechanism
or different function family is introduced to hide that gap.

## 11. Verification status, retrieval, and bounded handoff

All new mathematics is JOINT UNVERIFIED. The allocation explicitly
forbids fact_submit and all formal calls, so no new fact_id exists.
The global-memory record is a proof_attempt, not a fact.

Recall used TASK.md, current master_guidance cadd37d70aded16f,
shared obstacle674a0cfefd2ce34c and dead_end28f45c92b2b510df, own local
notes/events, and the fully read reports d34bc8275bb55379 and amended
7b0bb1b9c220b840. Initial oversized tool output was truncated; bounded
reads supplied the relevant complete texts. Unrelated truncated results
are not represented as fully read.

Fact searches found no accepted first-reversal kink theorem. The
statement of accepted931b9653ce0f7201 was read from the graph: its
benchmark ratio and conditional full-class implication do not turn
this restricted theorem into a minimax resolution. Its full-class upper
hypothesis remains unavailable. No accepted fact is imported into the
joint mathematical proof above.

The search-math-results skill was used in mature_subproblem/repair mode.
Matlas returned small-step self-contraction, constant-step summability,
and sufficiently-small-step definable-gradient results, whose step
hypotheses do not fit this word. Web fallback returned silver-schedule

no new external machinery or new function regime was introduced.

Skills used: query-memory, direct-proving, construct-counterexamples,
search-math-results. The counterexample skill was a falsification screen;

PROBLEM.md, TASK.md, W=2R, the true initial distance, the cap B, and the

# C35. Every ordered fresh-child tree relay is too weak on the near-critical ruler

Status: UNVERIFIED joint candidate, no verifier or solver. All ingredients used
below are proved internally. The result covers the exact incidence-flow class
defined here, not arbitrary tree-indexed vectors or arbitrary simplex walks.

## 1. A genuine real tree family with all normal inequalities

Partition a schedule into N ordinary blocks of masses S_i and checkpoints
b_i>1 after each block except the last. Set M_i=S_i+1, L_i=b_i-1 and
T_i=M_i+L_i for i<N-1. Choose T_(N-1)>=M_(N-1).

Choose ANY rooted tree on vertices 0,...,N-1 with root0 and with each parent
strictly earlier than its children. Write Ch_i for the children of i, and
W_i={j>i:parent(j)<i} for the waiting vertices at phase i. Define positive
energies E_i and actual vectors in the standard basis of R^N:

    alpha_i=sqrt(E_i)/T_i,
    beta_ij=sqrt(E_j)/T_i  (j in Ch_i),
    p_i=alpha_i e_i-sum_(j in Ch_i) beta_ij e_j.                (1)

The vector matrix is triangular with positive diagonal, hence these are
linearly independent vectors with an explicit positive definite real Gram
matrix. Distinct normals have negative inner product exactly along a tree
edge, and zero inner product otherwise. Let C=conv(0,p_i) and

    F(x)=max_(p in C)(<x,p>-||p||^2/2).

Its maximizer is the Euclidean projection P_C(x). The projection variational
inequalities give firm nonexpansiveness, hence Lipschitz continuity, of P_C.
Bounding maximization differences with the two maximizing linear forms gives
grad F=P_C. Thus F is one fixed everywhere-defined convex 1-smooth function.

Start at x_init=sqrt(E_0)e_0. At the beginning of phase i the nonzero
coordinates are sqrt(E_j) for the current vertex i and for j in W_i.
During ordinary partial mass u the current coordinate is
(T_i-u)alpha_i, each waiting coordinate stays sqrt(E_j), and each child
coordinate is u beta_ij. After the checkpoint, the current coordinate
vanishes and every child coordinate becomes sqrt(E_j). This is the
prescribed GD recursion whenever its projection normals hold.

For y=x-p_i, all its coordinates are nonnegative. Its own normal value is

    delta_i(u)=(T_i-u-1)alpha_i^2
                         -(u+1)sum_(j in Ch_i)beta_ij^2.      (2)

A past vertex has nonpositive dot product with y: its positive coordinate
has been exhausted, and all its remaining coordinates are nonpositive.
A future vertex j has no mass yet in its children, since the tree has a
unique parent. Thus its dot product with y is exactly

    E_j/T_j                      if j in W_i,
    (u+1)sqrt(E_j)alpha_j/T_i     if j in Ch_i,
    0                            otherwise.

The comparison with zero is also required. The own value (2) decreases in u;
the child comparisons increase; the waiting comparisons are constant.
Therefore ALL comparisons against ALL past/future vertices and zero are
equivalent to the following endpoint conditions for i<N-1:

    L_i E_i-M_i sum_(j in Ch_i)E_j >=
       T_i^2 max(0,
           max_(j in W_i) E_j/T_j,
           (M_i/T_i) max_(j in Ch_i) E_j/T_j).                 (3)

Empty maxima are zero. The terminal phase has no children or waiting
vertices, and its condition is just T_(N-1)>=M_(N-1).
These checks include every actual ordinary query and checkpoint query.
They construct a fixed legal smooth-convex function, without oracle resets.

The minimizer set is C^polar: a violated dot-product inequality yields a
positive maximand at a small multiple of the corresponding vertex.
Its inequalities force every coordinate of a minimizer nonpositive, by
backward induction through the tree. Thus 0 is a closest minimizer to
sqrt(E_0)e_0, with true squared initial distance E_0.

Writing T=T_(N-1) and D=2S_(N-1)+1, the exact normalized objective is

    score=(2T-D) E_(N-1)/(T^2 E_0).                            (4)

For fixed tree, phase masses, T and terminal energy E_(N-1)=1, setting (3)
to equality in decreasing order of i constructs the componentwise least
positive feasible E_i. Indeed every right hand side is a nondecreasing
function of already-set future energies. This proves, without numerical
optimization, that the recursion attains the best score in this exact class.
There is always a positive future waiting or child energy until the last
phase, so the constructed E_i are positive.

The primitive defect is also exact on the SAME normalized trajectory.
At each projection query F_t=<x_t,g_t>-||g_t||^2/2. Consequently

    2h_t F_t+h_t(1-h_t)||g_t||^2
                         =||x_t||^2-||x_(t+1)||^2.

If s is the entire schedule mass and
V=2sum_t h_t(F_t-F_end)+sum_t h_t(1-h_t)||g_t||^2
  -s(s+1)||g_end||^2, then

    V/E_0=1-[(T-S_(N-1)+s)/T]^2 E_(N-1)/E_0.                 (5)

No raw negative sign is substituted for objective or distance control.

## 2. A chronology bound valid for EVERY tree shape

Put H_i=E_i/T_i. The next chronological vertex i+1 is either a child of i
or already waiting. If it is waiting, (3), even after dropping all child
costs, implies

    H_(i+1)/H_i <= L_i/T_i.

If it is a child, retaining its own term in the child sum and its own
normal comparison gives

    H_(i+1)/H_i <= L_i T_i/[M_i(T_i+T_(i+1))].

Hence every legal tree relay satisfies

    H_(i+1)/H_i <=
        max(L_i/T_i, L_i T_i/[M_i(T_i+T_(i+1))]).             (6)

This is only a necessary bound; the constructive recursion (3) still keeps
every other comparison. The upper bound (6) is used only to exclude payoff.

## 3. Uniform quantitative exclusion on H_m

Fix m>=128, lambda=1+sqrt(2), c=lambda(1+1/m),
a=(1-1/m)/c, H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a c^j,H_j).
Let S_j be the mass of H_j and M_j=1+S_j. Geometric summation gives

    M_j=A c^j+(1-A)2^j,  A=a/(c-2) in (0,1).

Choose ANY dyadic blocking depth r<m. Put k=m-r, N=2^k, M=M_r and
q=a c^r/M. The N ordinary blocks are all copies of H_r and

    L_i=M q c^(nu_2(i+1)),
    T_i=M(1+q c^(nu_2(i+1)))  (i<N-1).                       (7)

The following simple uniform bounds will suffice:

    12/5<c<39/16,      2/5<q<=c-2<7/16.                    (8)

For the upper bound, sqrt(2)<99/70 and
(169/70)(129/128)<39/16. Also a<c-2 because ac<1<c(c-2).
This gives 0<A<1 and q<=c-2. Since M_r<=c^r, q>=a.
Finally a=(m-1)/[lambda(m+1)] >=127/(129lambda)
>2540/6321>2/5, using lambda<49/20. This proves (8).

Every even index i<N-1 has the low checkpoint q_i=q. In (6), its
waiting option is at least its child option. For a nonterminal next phase
this is q+q^2<=1+q_(i+1), which follows from q<1 and q_(i+1)>=q.
For the terminal next phase it follows from q+q^2<1<=T/M.
Thus every such low transition obeys

    H_(i+1)/H_i <= q/(1+q).                                  (9)

Every odd index has q_i=q c^ell for some ell>=1, and is followed by
a low checkpoint. Its child option dominates the waiting option:
q_i^2+q_i>1+q, because q_i>24/25 while q<7/16.
Therefore

    H_(i+1)/H_i <= q c^ell / D_ell,
    D_ell=(2+q+q c^ell)/(1+q c^ell)>1.                       (10)

There are N/2 low transitions and 2^(k-ell-1) transitions of level ell>=1.
Counting binary valuations gives

    product_(i<N-1)q c^(nu_2(i+1))=(qc)^(N-1)c^(-k).

Since (2T-D)/T<2, (4), (9), and (10) yield

    score < [2/(M(1+q)c^k)]
             (qc)^(N-1) /
             [(1+q)^(N/2) product_(ell=1)^(k-1)
                           D_ell^(2^(k-ell-1))].             (11)

Let K=1/(2M_m-1), the global Huber score. The exact mass relation is

    M_m=M[(1-q/(c-2))2^k+(q/(c-2))c^k] <=M c^k.

It follows that score/K<B_k, where

    B_k=4(qc)^(2^k-1) /
       [(1+q)^(2^(k-1)+1)
          product_(ell=1)^(k-1)D_ell^(2^(k-ell-1))].          (12)

For k=2,

    B_2=4(qc)^3(1+qc)/[(1+q)^3(2+q+qc)].

This increases in both q and c. Its logarithmic q derivative is
3/[q(1+q)]+(c-1)/[(1+cq)(2+(1+c)q)]>0. Its logarithmic c
derivative is 3/c+q/(1+cq)-q/(2+(1+c)q)>0. Consequently (8) gives

    B_2 <
      273^3*529/[1024*23^3*897]
      =10763254593/11175730176 =: eta <1.                    (13)

The recurrence is exact:

    B_(k+1)/B_k^2 = (qc)(1+q)/(4D_k)
                  <6279/16384 =: zeta <1.                  (14)

Thus, for every k>=2,

    score/K < B_k
             <=zeta^(2^(k-2)-1) eta^(2^(k-2)) <1.            (15)

This excludes EVERY ordered rooted tree and EVERY legal positive energy
choice in the specified family. If k grows, the ratio tends to zero
exponentially in 2^k. In particular no choice of tree shape avoids the loss.

When k=1 the sole tree is the two-vertex path. The exact endpoint condition
gives, with T_0=M(1+q) and D=2M-1,

    score=q(2T-D)/[T(T+T_0)].

The maximum is attained at T=(D+sqrt(D^2+2D T_0))/2. Writing epsilon=1/M
its ratio to K is

    2q[2(2+q)-epsilon] /
       [3+q-epsilon+sqrt((2-epsilon)(4+2q-epsilon))]
      <=4q(2+q)/[2+q+sqrt(3+2q)].

The last expression increases in q>=0 because
sqrt(3+2q)/(2+q) decreases. As q<9/20 it is less than
441/[245+10sqrt(390)]<1; the latter inequality is 196^2<39000.

Allowing r=m leaves just one vertex and one ordinary block. The choice
T=2S_m+1 attains K. Therefore the union over ALL blocking depths, ALL
ordered rooted trees and ALL legal parameters has exact best score K.

## 4. The obstruction and the next genuinely different mechanism

Tree branching reduces the number of edges from root to terminal, but it
creates waiting coordinate masses. Their supporting planes must be below
the active plane at every intervening query. Inequality (9) is the
quantitative price at each low checkpoint, independent of the chosen tree.

The unique-parent condition is load-bearing. With two parents contributing
to one child coordinate, two earlier gradient vectors can have positive
correlation. A future plane can then have a negative term from a child
already preloaded by another parent. The waiting comparison E_j/T_j used
above is no longer correct, so (6) and (15) do not cover directed acyclic
split-and-merge constructions. Nor do they cover signed coordinate mass,
revisiting exhausted coordinates, other simplex geometries, or general
smooth-convex functions. The original all-schedule minimax target is open.
