# Unsaturated terminal mass: full original coefficient interface and a source-capacity obstruction

Status: JOINT UNVERIFIED. Worker max4, continuation invocation92, guidance
52e9d99015702be8, plan7000802aea7e2692. This is one joint analytical draft.
The current TASK grants zero formal calls; no new statement in this report is
a verifier-accepted fact. Original target OPEN. W=2R throughout.

## 1. Result and scope

The general terminal-square and original-row flow interface is derived below
without imposing q=pi^2. The proposed constant-gradient Gram is correct when
all optimum-to-query multipliers vanish. When those multipliers are allowed,
the two additional terms are displayed explicitly in (3.1).

At q0=1/D, nonnegative multipliers and the actual tight Huber trajectory force
those optimum incoming rows to vanish. The constant-gradient restriction then
forces V+1=D*pi and pi^2<=q0. For the specified terminal distribution supported
only at l=n-1, this fixes pi=b/(S+b), strictly below sqrt(q0). It leaves real
freedom in the mixed initial-point/gradient coefficients; this freedom is not
present under saturation q=pi^2.

The ONE literal paid family tested at q=17/(16D) is

 M_trial = ||g_n-pi*x0+r^T g||^2
         + delta ||x0-u^T g||^2 + gamma ||dsrc^T g||^2
         + kappa ||x0+theta*dsrc^T g||^2 + xi ||dtail^T g||^2,       (1.1)

where theta is any real number, xi>=0, and all columns and coefficients are
specified in Section5. Its paid block has rank at most two, genuine mixed x0
terms, zero-sum gradient columns, and an adjustable terminal-three detail.
That detail can pay the elementary last-query column deficit of the baseline.

Nevertheless the ENTIRE family is excluded by an exact original source cut:

 a * sum_(j=1)^l lambda_(0,j) = mu0-P00
  = -2ab*k + a^2*b^2/T^2 - gamma/b^2 + kappa
                              - (kappa/b^2)(theta+b)^2
  < -3a/(4S) < 0.                                            (1.2)

Here P=sym(N Ltri), mu_i=lambda_(i,star), and k=(S-ab)/((S+b)T).
Every weight on the left must be nonnegative. Equation (1.2) holds for every
theta, every xi>=0, every compatible skew completion, and every enclosing
m>=1536. It is not the failure of a particular chosen array.

The whole unit-quadratic polynomial separately forces theta=0 or theta=-2.
Both survive all linear flow/skew compatibility equations and both fail (1.2).
The theta=-2 case explicitly spends the loose target in mixed terms. Thus the
failure is not an artifact of retaining only the q0 certificate search.

This excludes the displayed family, not all unsaturated pi, not all tail-source
certificates, and not the hierarchy itself. No primal lower bound or full W
upper bound follows. The missing hypothesis of accepted931b9653ce0f7201 remains
unproved.

## 2. Original rows and the general full coefficient interface

Normalize smoothness to1 and the optimum to x_star=g_star=f_star=0. Let h be
any positive word of length n>=2, let l=n-1, and retain the original formal
vectors x0,g0,...,g_l,g_n. The final vector g_n is not queried in the updates:

 x_i=x0-sum_(u<i)h_u*g_u,   0<=i<=n.
 Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2 <=0.

All distinct ordered rows involving star, queries, and terminal are available.
In the selected architecture only incoming-terminal multipliers are set to
zero. Accepted fact1252b8823bb5a290, parts(b),(c), licenses that selection for
tight certificates and gives the exact terminal-square interface. It does
NOT license deleting either orientation of any queried-prefix row.

Write

 lambda_(n,star)=2pi, lambda_(n,i)=2alpha_i,
 alpha_i>=0, pi>=0, pi+sum_i alpha_i=1,
 lambda_(i,n)=lambda_(star,n)=0.

Initially retain BOTH optimum directions:

 mu_i=lambda_(i,star)>=0,  nu_i=lambda_(star,i)>=0.

Let e be the n-dimensional all-ones column, e_i its coordinate columns, and
Ltri the n-by-n matrix with diagonal1, entry h_j in row i,column j when j<i,
and zero above the diagonal. Write g=(g0,...,g_l)^T; expressions involving its
columns mean the corresponding sums of Euclidean vectors and inner products.
Set

 y=e*x0-Ltri*g,  zeta_i=f_i-||g_i||^2/2,
 t=Ltri*e,  t_i=1+sum_(j<i)h_j,  S=sum_i h_i, D=1+2S.

Direct expansion gives

 Q_ij=zeta_i-zeta_j+<g_i,y_j-y_i>,
 Q_(i,star)=zeta_i-<g_i,y_i>,  Q_(star,i)=-zeta_i.

Define an n-by-n real matrix N from the original prefix multipliers by

 N_ij=-lambda_(i,j) for i!=j,
 N_ii=mu_i+sum_(j!=i)lambda_(i,j).

Exact cancellation of every f_i is equivalent to

 Ne=mu,             N^T e=2alpha+nu.                       (2.1)

Indeed the coefficient of f_i before subtracting terminal flow is prefix
outflow minus prefix inflow, namely (N^T e)_i-nu_i; the terminal outgoing
row contributes -2alpha_i. There is no unexamined reversed flow convention.
After this cancellation the prefix interpolation sum is

 2sum_i alpha_i*zeta_i - sum_ij N_ij <g_i,y_j>.

Define

 r=h-Ltri^T*alpha,
 r_i=h_i*(pi+sum_(j<=i)alpha_j)-alpha_i,
 V=e^T r=S-alpha^T t.                                     (2.2)

These identities follow by expanding column i of Ltri^T alpha and using
sumalpha=1-pi. Consequently

 x_n-sum_i alpha_i*y_i = pi*x0-r^T g.

Expanding the outgoing terminal rows now gives the COMPLETE identity

 M=q||x0||^2-2f_n+sum_(i!=j)lambda_ij Q_ij
  =||g_n-pi*x0+r^T g||^2
   +(q-pi^2)||x0||^2
   +2<x0,(pi*r-mu/2)^T g>
   +g^T [sym(N Ltri)-r r^T] g.                            (2.3)

Here sym(B)=(B+B^T)/2. No gradient equality, rank condition, or actual-history
restriction was used in obtaining (2.3). Since g_n is a free formal vector,
M is PSD exactly when the entire prefix block in (2.3) is PSD.

For completeness this gives a full coefficient recovery interface. Suppose a
proposed residual is

 (q-pi^2)||x0||^2+2<x0,z^T g>+g^T K g,

where K is symmetric. Define

 P=K+r r^T, mu=2pi*r-2z, w=Ltri^(-1)e.

For a chosen nu>=0, all coefficient matches hold exactly when a skew matrix
A satisfies

 A^T=-A,
 A e=E:=P e-Ltri^T(2alpha+nu),
 A w=F:=mu-P w,
 N=(P+A)Ltri^(-1).                                       (2.4)

One must also impose mu>=0 and N_ij<=0 for EVERY i!=j. These are genuine
additional sign conditions, not consequences of PSD or of the moments.
The complete original array is then

 lambda_(i,j)=-N_ij (distinct queries),
 lambda_(i,star)=mu_i, lambda_(star,i)=nu_i,
 lambda_(n,star)=2pi, lambda_(n,i)=2alpha_i,
 all incoming-terminal multipliers zero.                  (2.5)

There is an explicit entry formula, so (2.4) hides no coordinate deletion:

 N_ij=P_ij+A_ij
       -h_j sum_(v>j)(P_iv+A_iv) product_(j<u<v)(1-h_u).   (2.6)

The inverse used here has diagonal1 and, for v>j, entry
-h_j product_(j<u<v)(1-h_u). Multiplication by Ltri proves it by the finite
product telescoping identity; entries above its diagonal vanish.

Because w0=1 and w1=1-h0!=1, e,w are independent. The necessary and sufficient
linear compatibility conditions for the two skew moments in (2.4) are

 e^T E=0,  w^T F=0,  e^T F=-w^T E.                        (2.7)

An explicit sufficiency construction is useful. Put

 wbar=e^T w/n, cvec=w-wbar*e,
 fvec=F-wbar*E+(E^T w/n)e.
 Acan=(E e^T-e E^T)/n
          +(fvec cvec^T-cvec fvec^T)/(cvec^T cvec).        (2.8)

The conditions imply e^T fvec=cvec^T fvec=0. Multiplying (2.8) by e and w
therefore gives exactly E and F. Every compatible skew is Acan+C with
C^T=-C, Ce=Cw=0. Formula (2.6) specifies all coefficients even for such a
completion. Nonnegativity of the recovered array remains a separate problem.

## 3. Constant-gradient screen and optimum incoming rows

Restrict only the queried formal gradients to a common vector G, keeping x0
and g_n independent. From (2.1),

 sum_i mu_i=2(1-pi)+sum_i nu_i,
 e^T N Ltri e=2alpha^T t+nu^T t.

The residual after the terminal square has the exact two-vector Gram

 [ q-pi^2,  pi(V+1)-1-(sum nu)/2;
   pi(V+1)-1-(sum nu)/2, D-(V+1)^2+nu^T t ].              (3.1)

Thus the suggested Gram is correct with nu=0. Under nonnegative multipliers,
its absence of the sum-nu term forces EVERY nu_i=0. In the general cone it is
incorrect to discard those optimum rows without this assumption or an argument.

For nu=0 write Y=V+1. The exact PSD region of this 2-by-2 restriction is

 q>=1/D,  0<=pi<=sqrt(q),
 (qY-pi)^2 <= (q-pi^2)(Dq-1),                            (3.2)

intersected with pi<=1 and the actual alpha moment constraints. To verify this,
the two diagonal conditions are q-pi^2>=0 and D-Y^2>=0. The determinant is
qD-1+2pi Y-qY^2-Dpi^2. Its product by q equals
(q-pi^2)(Dq-1)-(qY-pi)^2. Necessity of q>=1/D also follows by evaluating this
Gram on (D,1): the value is D(qD-1)-(Dpi-Y)^2. Conversely, for q>=1/D and
q-pi^2>0, the determinant condition forces the lower diagonal to be
nonnegative. If q-pi^2=0, (3.2) gives Y=1/pi and the lower diagonal is
D-1/q>=0. This proves the boundary cases as well as the interior.

For the fixed hierarchy below t0=1 and t_l=T=S-a. As alpha ranges over
nonnegative columns of mass1-pi, its moment range gives

 b+pi*T <= Y <= S+pi,  b=1+a.                            (3.3)

This is a necessary affine screen only. It is not a full-space certificate.

At q0=1/D the vanishing of nu is actually forced, rather than optional.
Use the legal scalar Huber function f(x)=x^2/2 for |x|<=1 and
f(x)=|x|-1/2 otherwise, starting at x0=D. All queried points and the terminal
lie in the positive affine branch; all their gradients equal1. Every finite
ordered row and every query/terminal-to-optimum row is zero. The remaining
rows are Q_(star,i)=-(D-t_i)<0. The objective gap q0*x0^2-2f_n is zero.
Consequently M=-sum_i nu_i(D-t_i). PSD and nu>=0 force every nu_i=0.
Also the terminal square vanishes on this tight trajectory, so

 Y=D*pi, pi^2<=1/D.                                      (3.4)

The same conclusion follows directly from (3.2). Equations (3.3),(3.4) imply
pi>=b/(S+b), and also pi<=1/2. Thus concentrating alpha at the latest query
selects the smallest possible pi in this q0 affine screen.

For q>q0, no general vanishing claim for nu is made. The same Huber trajectory
only implies the necessary budget

 sum_i nu_i(D-t_i) <= D^2(q-q0).

## 4. Frozen tail source and the genuine unsaturated freedom

Fix the specified enclosing m>=1536, epsilon=1/(4m), beta=1-epsilon,
c=(1+sqrt(2))(1+epsilon), a=beta/c, and

 H0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j), h=H_m,
 n=2^m-1, l=n-1, S=sum h, D=1+2S,
 b=1+a, T=S-a, Ctot=S+b, Ctot+T=D.

Every child uses this SAME enclosing m. Put

 alpha=(1-pi)e_l, pi=b/Ctot, q0=1/D,
 delta=q0-pi^2=S(S-2ab)/(D*Ctot^2)>0.                     (4.1)

Then r_i=pi*h_i for i<l, r_l=a+pi, and V=a+pi*T=D*pi-1.
All coefficients in (4.1) are fixed; there is no alternative alpha search.

It is useful to state the COMPLETE q0 flow constraints, beyond the affine
screen. Write the residual prefix matrix as [delta,z^T;z,K]. Its constant
restriction vanishes at (x0,g)=(D,e). PSD of the full residual then forces
z^T e=-delta D and K e=-D z: a zero quadratic value for a PSD form forces its
bilinear product with every direction to vanish, as follows by varying a real
scalar in that direction. Equivalently every possible PSD residual at q0 has

 z=-delta*u, e^T u=D,
 K=delta*u*u^T+K0, K0>=0, K0 e=0.                        (4.2)

The Schur complement proves K0>=0; the displayed moments prove K0 e=0.
Conversely (4.2) makes the residual PSD with exactly that affine null direction.
Its remaining complete flow interface is

 mu=2pi*r+2delta*u>=0, nu=0,
 P=r r^T+delta*u*u^T+K0,
 A e=P e-2Ltri^T alpha,
 A w=mu-P w,
 N=(P+A)Ltri^(-1), N_ij<=0 for all i!=j.                 (4.3)

Compatibility (2.7), and every entry formula (2.6), remain required.
There is no imposed support restriction on finite multipliers in (4.3).

Under saturation q=pi^2, the residual x0 diagonal is zero. Full PSD then
forces its entire x0 mixed column to vanish, fixing mu=2pi*r. In contrast,
(4.2),(4.3) permit the additional vector 2delta*u in mu, with e^T u=D,
and a full PSD metric K0 on the e-orthogonal gradient directions. This is
real coefficient freedom. It neither follows from nor is excluded by the
saturated common-column report ba770750b559361d, read in full for this round.
That report's original Q orientation agrees with (2.1)-(2.3); its special
metric and common-column constraints have not been imported as necessities.

## 5. ONE explicit paid rank-two family

Define the physical column v by v_i=h_i for i<l and v_l=1, so e^T v=T. Freeze

 u=(D/T)v, r=pi*v+a*e_l,
 s=beta^(2^m-1)*c^(-m), gamma=a^2/T^2-s^2,
 q=17/(16D), kappa=q-q0=1/(16D),
 dsrc=(e0-e1)/b.

The last three physical steps are (b,gbar,b), where gbar=1+beta. On their
ORIGINAL indices define

 dtail=a*gbar*e_(l-2)+(1-a*beta)e_(l-1)-b*e_l.             (5.1)

Both columns have zero sum. The only family in this mechanism is (1.1), for
all theta real and xi>=0. In the requested paid-block coordinates this means

 ell=kappa*theta*dsrc,
 R=kappa*theta^2*dsrc*dsrc^T+xi*dtail*dtail^T.              (5.2)

Thus sumell=0, Re=0, and

 [kappa,ell^T;ell,R]
 =kappa*(1,theta*dsrc)^T*(1,theta*dsrc)
                   +xi*(0,dtail)^T*(0,dtail)

is literally PSD of rank at most two. No feasible but unspecified matrix R is
used. In particular theta=-2 uses a nonzero mixed block. The tail column is
included from the outset to allow last-query diagonal repair without changing
the source coordinates; it is not a second post-failure family.

All baseline signs can be proved at the stipulated threshold. The inequalities
sqrt(2)<10/7 and epsilon<=1/6144 give

 a>(7/17)(6143/6145)>2/5, while a<1/2; hence 1<b<3/2.

Every h_i>1, so S>2^m-1>64. Formula (4.1) proves delta>0.
To prove gamma>0, set vword=a/(c-2). Since c>1+sqrt(2) and a<1/c,
0<vword<1. Summing the two children, or substituting in their defining
recursion, gives

 1+S_j=(1-vword)2^j+vword*c^j.

Since c>2 and j>0 this is less c^j, hence T<S<c^m.
Also n>=8m for m>=6: it holds at6 and the inequality is preserved when m
increases by1. Put x=1/(4m). Since (1-x)^(-1)>1+x and the binomial expansion
gives (1+x)^(4m)>2, one has beta^(4m)<1/2 and beta^n<1/4. Therefore
s*T<beta^n<1/4<a, proving gamma>0. No recurrence or numerical calculation was
executed; these are symbolic finite inequalities.

Consequently (1.1) is a PSD polynomial for every theta and xi>=0. Whether
it equals a nonnegative combination of the required original rows is the
separate issue answered negatively below.

## 6. Full constant and unit-quadratic polynomial checks

For a common queried gradient G, e^T u=D and both difference columns vanish.
The complete trial polynomial restricts to

 ||g_n-pi*x0+V*G||^2+delta||x0-D*G||^2+kappa||x0||^2.     (6.1)

This is exactly the nu=0 affine Gram (3.1) at q=q0+kappa, with the specified
pi and V. Its terminal square still contains the original free g_n.
On the actual Huber trajectory x0=D*G,g_n=G, (6.1) is kappa D^2||G||^2,
which matches the original objective polynomial. No child is restarted.

The optimum incoming restriction is also forced within THIS paid family,
even though q>q0. Coefficient matching gives

 z=-delta*u+kappa*theta*dsrc,
 mu=2pi*r+2delta*u-2kappa*theta*dsrc.                      (6.2)

Its sum is 2pi*(Dpi-1)+2delta D=2(1-pi). The sum of (2.1) therefore gives
sum nu=0; nonnegativity forces nu=0. This is a family-specific consequence,
not a deletion from the unrestricted loose-target cone.

For the full unit-curvature quadratic f(x)=||x||^2/2, let
w_i=product_(j<i)(1-h_j). Then Ltri*w=e and g_i=w_i*x0. The hierarchy has
signed full product w_n=-s. To see its magnitude, note that the level at i
is the exponent of2 dividing i+1. Its total across i=0,...,2^m-2 is
sum_(j=1)^(m-1)(2^(m-j)-1)=2^m-m-1=n-m. Multiplying the factors
a*c^level=beta*c^(level-1) gives beta^n*c^(-m)=s.

At the original endpoints,

 w0=1, w1=-a,
 w_l=s/a, w_(l-1)=-s/(a*beta), w_(l-2)=s/(a^2*beta).

These follow from the literal last factors -a,-beta,-a. They prove

 dsrc^T w=1, dtail^T w=0,
 u^T w=D/T, r^T w=pi+s.                                 (6.3)

For u use sum_(i<l)h_i*w_i=1-w_l; for r use r=pi*v+a*e_l.
The terminal square in (1.1) consequently vanishes on the ENTIRE actual
unit-quadratic trajectory. Every original Q_ij, including optimum and terminal
rows, is zero on that trajectory. A certificate identity must therefore have
value (q-s^2)||x0||^2.

The baseline has exactly the correct q0 value, because

 delta*(1-D/T)^2+gamma=q0-s^2.                            (6.4)

Indeed 1-D/T=-Ctot/T and
Ctot^2-D*b^2+D*a^2=T^2; multiplying (6.4) by D*T^2 proves it.
The paid tail term vanishes, and the paid source square contributes
kappa*(1+theta)^2||x0||^2. Thus full polynomial matching forces

 theta*(theta+2)=0, namely theta=0 or theta=-2.            (6.5)

This is a necessary identity of the original polynomial, not a test of only
an aggregate moment. It does not decide any individual multiplier sign.

## 7. Complete linear flow constraints for the literal family

For (1.1), write

 Gamma=gamma+kappa*theta^2,
 P=r r^T+delta*u*u^T+Gamma*dsrc*dsrc^T+xi*dtail*dtail^T,
 mu=2pi*r+2delta*u-2kappa*theta*dsrc, nu=0.                (7.1)

The actual coefficient equations are exactly (2.4)-(2.7) with these P,mu and
the specified alpha. These formulas, followed by (2.6),(2.5), recover every
original coefficient for any compatible skew; there are no omitted rows.

One can check ALL scalar compatibility conditions, rather than assume them.
First

 e^T E=V^2+delta D^2-2(1-pi)T=0,

because V=Dpi-1, delta=1/D-pi^2 and (1-pi)T=S-V. Next (6.3),(6.4) give

 w^T F=pi^2-s^2+delta*(2D/T-D^2/T^2)-gamma
                                  -kappa*(theta^2+2theta)
       =-kappa*theta*(theta+2).

Finally P is symmetric and Ltri*w=e, so

 e^T F+w^T E=e^T mu-2alpha^T Ltri*w=0.

Thus for BOTH allowed values theta=0,-2, every compatibility equation holds
for every xi>=0. Formula (2.8) provides an actual real skew completion;
adding an arbitrary neutral skew does not alter the cuts below. This proves
that the scoped failure is specifically nonnegative original multipliers,
not a silently inconsistent choice of skew moments.

For explicit optimum-row signs set

 k=(S-ab)/(Ctot*T)=(1-pi*b)/T>0.

Since r=pi*v+a*e_l and u=(D/T)v, (7.1) simplifies to

 mu=2k*v+2pi*a*e_l-2kappa*theta*dsrc.                    (7.2)

The equality uses pi^2*T/D+delta=(1-pi*b)/D. At theta=0 all mu entries are
positive. At theta=-2 only source0 and source1 change:

 mu0=2kb+4kappa/b, mu1=2k*gbar-4kappa/b,
 mu_i=2k*h_i for 1<i<l, mu_l=2k+2pi*a.

One has k>1/(2S): Ctot*T<S(S+3/2), S-ab>S-3/4, and S>3 suffice. Hence
mu1>1/S-1/(8S)>0, because 4kappa/b=1/(4Db)<1/(8S).
All optimum outgoing signs pass at both unit-compatible theta values.
The terminal outgoing signs also pass: 0<pi<1 and alpha=(1-pi)e_l.
The forced optimum incoming coefficients are zero, and all incoming terminal
coefficients remain zero as selected at the start.

## 8. Original tail tests, including the repair actually afforded by xi

The last column of Ltri is e_l. Consequently the exact coefficient identity
P=sym(N Ltri) gives

 Pll=Nll.

The original last column and row capacities therefore require

 Pll-2(1-pi)=sum_(i<l)lambda_(i,l)>=0,
 Pll-mu_l=sum_(j<l)lambda_(l,j)>=0.                       (8.1)

The source paid square has no support at the last three indices, so

 Pll=Pll0+xi*b^2,
 Pll0=(a+pi)^2+delta*D^2/T^2.                            (8.2)

Define J=pi^2+delta*D^2/T^2. There is the exact identity

 J=2k-a^2/T^2.                                          (8.3)

For verification, put everything over Ctot^2*T^2. The numerator of J-2k is
b^2*T^2+D*S(S-2ab)-2Ctot*T(S-ab)=-a^2*Ctot^2.
Using (8.3) in (8.2) gives

 Pll0-mu_l=a^2*(1-T^(-2))>0.

Thus the last row capacity passes already, whereas the baseline last COLUMN
capacity fails. In detail, pi<b/S<3/(2S)<1/32 and delta<1/D give

 Pll0<(17/32)^2+D/T^2<1/2.

For the last inequality, D/T^2<3/S: after replacing a by1/2 it follows from
S^2-4S+3/4>0, valid at S>64. Also 3/S<3/64, and
(17/32)^2+3/64=337/1024<1/2. On the other hand 2(1-pi)>31/16.
Therefore the exact positive tail price is

 xi >= [2(1-pi)-Pll0]/b^2.                               (8.4)

The family explicitly permits such xi; this elementary tail mismatch has
been repaired within the chosen family, not ignored or confused with an
obstruction to every unsaturated certificate.

The next original tail pair also has a complete orientation check. Put
p=l-1. Since h_p=gbar,

 N_(p,l)+N_(l,p)=2P_(p,l)-gbar*Pll
   =gbar*[2k-a^2*(1+T^(-2))]
                  -xi*b*[2(1-a*beta)+gbar*b].            (8.5)

The left side must be nonpositive. Its right side is strictly negative for
all xi>=0: k<1/T<2/S, so 2k<4/S<1/16<a^2, and 1-a*beta>0. Hence this
last-pair necessary condition passes. It does not assert the individual signs.
These tests retain both orientations of each original row and the actual
last-three products; no terminal node has been substituted for a query.

## 9. Decisive original source cut for the ENTIRE family

The first physical step is h0=b=1+a. Column0 of Ltri has entry1 in row0 and
entry b in every later row. Since P=sym(N Ltri), its first diagonal obeys

 P00=N00+b*sum_(j>0)N0j
     =mu0+a*sum_(j>0)N0j.

Thus any nonnegative original multiplier array must satisfy the nonnegative
cut

 mu0-P00=a*sum_(j=1)^l lambda_(0,j)>=0.                   (9.1)

Every original queried destination is retained. There is no assumption about
support or choice of the completing skew, and mu0 is the ACTUAL outgoing
optimum multiplier fixed by the x0 mixed coefficients.

In (7.1), dtail0=0, r0=pi*b, u0=Db/T, and dsrc0=1/b. Therefore

 mu0=2kb-2kappa*theta/b,
 P00=b^2*J+(gamma+kappa*theta^2)/b^2.

Using (8.3) and completing the theta square gives exactly

 mu0-P00
 =-2ab*k+a^2*b^2/T^2-gamma/b^2
                   -kappa*(theta^2+2btheta)/b^2
 =-2ab*k+a^2*b^2/T^2-gamma/b^2+kappa
                   -(kappa/b^2)(theta+b)^2.              (9.2)

It is independent of xi. It is also independent of ALL remaining neutral
skew freedom. The paid x0 block can improve this source deficit by at most
kappa, regardless of theta.

Here is an explicit bound uniform at the stipulated enclosing threshold.
We proved k>1/(2S), b>1, gamma>0, S>64, 2/5<a<1/2. Also T>S/2. Hence

 -2ab*k < -a/S,
 a^2*b^2/T^2 < a*9/(2S^2) < a/(8S),
 kappa=1/(16D)<1/(32S)<a/(8S).

The second comparison uses S>36; the third uses a>1/4. Dropping only the two
nonpositive square terms in (9.2) gives

 mu0-P00 < -3a/(4S) <0.                                 (9.3)

Equations (9.1),(9.3) contradict one another for EVERY theta real and EVERY
xi>=0. In particular both theta values that pass the whole unit-quadratic
identity are excluded, even after paying (8.4) and even though all their
linear compatibility equations and optimum signs pass.

This is an exact nonnegative cut against the complete explicitly chosen
rank-two paid family. No enumeration, optimizer, numerical witness, or
particular-skew failure was used. There is no need to guess and test additional
arrays after this cut. No third family has been opened.

## 10. What remains and the status of the original problem

The reusable candidate interfaces are (2.1)-(2.8), the nu-corrected affine
Gram (3.1), its exact nu=0 region (3.2), and the q0 unsaturated form (4.2)-(4.3).
They are part of this JOINT UNVERIFIED draft, not accepted building blocks.
They can guide a future authorized verification or a new architecture.

The failure identifies a precise local deficiency. For this u, the baseline
source coefficient P00 exceeds the available mu0 by order1/S. The loose
payment kappa is too small to fix it with the frozen dsrc column. Arbitrarily
large PSD energy on the chosen last-three column does not change P00 or mu0.
A future different mixed column or different u could change this cut. This
report does not assert that either repair succeeds, and does not attempt one.

Accepted1252b8823bb5a290 is used at its exact original-row/terminal-reduction
scope. Accepted931b9653ce0f7201 supplies the benchmark envelope and CONDITIONAL
implication from a full W<=17/(16D) bound. No such bound has been obtained here.
Accepted1e0bf3f41457d8e4 was acknowledged as the closed previous gate and is not
used as an unstated all-depth premise. Original PROBLEM.md is unchanged.

# Growing Newton temporal spaces on the actual critical word

Status: complete draft candidates, UNVERIFIED. No solver, numerical evaluation,
installation or fact_submit. Accepted facts 1252b8823bb5a290 (interpolation and
normalization) and b98bb807d514e80d (the exact benchmark and its elementary
envelope) are the only correctness inputs. Every new estimate is proved here.
These are obstructions for specified growing temporal spaces, not an upper
bound for the unrestricted worst-case objective and not the original target.

## 1. Actual word, queried prefix and a genuinely restricted growing space

Fix m>=128 and keep the following parameters fixed within that horizon:

    lambda=1+sqrt(2), c=lambda(1+1/m), a=(1-1/m)/c,
    d_j=1+a c^j, H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,d_j,H_j),
    n=2^m-1, q=n-1.

The prescribed queried-prefix steps h_0,...,h_(q-1) are H_m with its last
entry d_0 removed. Queries are indexed 0,...,q; the last free step is b in
[0,2], including the original b=d_0. Write

    P_i(z)=product_(t<i)(1-h_t z), 0<=i<=q.

Choose a REAL monic polynomial chi of degree r, with 1<=r<=q. For a
polynomial p let [p] denote its remainder modulo chi. This is an r-dimensional
real vector space; use coefficients in 1,z,...,z^(r-1) as its real coordinates.
Let T be ANY real linear map from this space into any finite-dimensional
Euclidean space, with ||T[1]||=1. Put

    x_i=T[P_i],       g_i=T[z P_i],     0<=i<=q.          (1)

The map, ambient dimension, metric, coefficients and polynomial can all depend
on m. Singular maps are allowed. Initial correlations are unrestricted. The
identity P_(i+1)=P_i-h_i zP_i proves the ACTUAL GD recursion for (1).

All queried values f_i remain joint variables. Assume that the data (1), those
values and the optimum jet (0,0,0) belong to ONE convex differentiable function
F with 1-Lipschitz gradient. Thus the initial distance to this designated
minimizer is exactly one. All conclusions below hold for EVERY interpolant of
these jets; no terminal gradient identity is assumed after the last step b.

This is a fixed r-dimensional temporal space, even when the physical image
dimension is larger. For r=m it retains m independent Newton coefficients.
More generally the theorem below permits a degree much larger than m but
strictly smaller than the full n+1-node representation of arbitrary data.

## 2. The full computable all-pair law and function realization

Let J be multiplication by z modulo chi, let v_0 be the coefficient vector of
1, and define

    v_(i+1)=(I-h_i J)v_i,    w_i=Jv_i,    M=T^T T.

Then x_i=T v_i, g_i=T w_i, M is a REAL PSD matrix, and v_0^T M v_0=1.
Conversely every real PSD M has a real Euclidean factor T. With
v_star=w_star=0 and f_star=0, the necessary and sufficient full interpolation
law is, for EVERY distinct ordered pair i,j in {star,0,...,q},

 f_i-f_j+w_i^T M(v_j-v_i)
                   +(w_i-w_j)^T M(w_i-w_j)/2 <=0.       (2)

This is exactly Q_ij<=0 in fact 1252b8823bb5a290, evaluated on the stated
vectors. No pair or common value is replaced by a componentwise condition.
For feasible data that fact constructs an actual function explicitly:

 cjet_j=<g_j,x_j>-f_j,
 H(v)=max_j {cjet_j+<x_j,v-g_j>+||v-g_j||^2/2},
 F_max(x)=sup_v {<x,v>-H(v)}.

The maximum includes the optimum jet. The function is finite, convex and
1-smooth, interpolates every queried value and gradient, and has minimum zero
at zero. This provides a function realization, not just a Gram representation.

For a Newton basis with roots theta_1,...,theta_r, repetitions allowed,
the basis polynomials are N_k(z)=product_(ell=1)^k(z-theta_ell), 0<=k<r.
Multiplication obeys zN_k=theta_(k+1)N_k+N_(k+1), with N_r=0 in the quotient.
Thus J is a bidiagonal Newton chain in that basis. For nonreal conjugate
roots the original real monomial coordinates, or real and imaginary parts of
the Newton coordinates, give the same REAL law (2); a complex matrix is not
being called a real PSD metric.

## 3. Exact level resonance: a complete growing-span annihilation obstruction

The level d_j occurs 2^(m-j-1) times in H_m. In the retained prefix the
counts are

    N_0=2^(m-1)-1, N_j=2^(m-j-1), 1<=j<m.              (3)

Let mu_j=1/d_j. Suppose

    chi(z)=product_(j=0)^(m-1)(z-mu_j)^(r_j),
    0<=r_j<=N_j,     1<=sum r_j<=q.                    (4)

Since P_q(z)=product_j(1-d_j z)^(N_j), chi divides P_q. Thus x_q=g_q=0
in (1), for EVERY map T and every common metric, regardless of its condition
number or initial correlations. Every legal interpolant has

                    2F(x_q-b g_q)=0                    (5)

for every real b. This includes one independent Newton direction at EVERY
recursive large-step level (r=m), as well as every growing multiplicity
permitted by the actual counts (3). It also handles confluent derivatives:
vanishing to order r_j, not merely vanishing once, is the exact requirement.

One can add a SINGLE zero root, chi=z product_j(z-mu_j)^(r_j), provided its
degree is <=q. Then chi divides zP_q, so g_q=0. The position need not be zero,
but legality says it is a minimizer of F; again (5) holds. Adding a second
zero root is not covered: a nonzero constant terminal gradient may survive.

For the simple positive roots r_j=1, all factors have occurred by the first
central top-level step (index 2^(m-1)-1). The model already reaches zero after
that step. This uses the actual chronology; (3) is the more general terminal
criterion for growing confluent blocks.

## 4. Near-unit roots: quantitative obstruction with growing Jordan depth

Suppose EVERY complex root theta of the real polynomial chi satisfies

                         |theta-1|<=1/(32m).            (6)

Roots may be arbitrarily close, repeated, nonreal, or depend on the horizon.
There is no bound on T or on a Jordan-chain condition number. For every legal
prefix (1) and every b in [0,2],

 2F(x_q-b g_q)
 <=3600 c^(-2m)(384m r^(3/2))^(2r)
                              exp[-3n/(2m)].           (7)

Consequently the ratio to the EXACT benchmark U_n^ZJ=1/B_n tends uniformly
to zero whenever

             r log(384m r^(3/2))=o(n/m).                (8)

In particular r=m is covered. There is also an explicit larger range:

 1<=r<=floor(n/(32m^2))  implies
 2F(x_q-b g_q)<=3600 exp[-11n/(8m)].                    (9)

This closes an entire growing polynomial/Jordan space, not just a direct sum
of individually legal modes. It does not cover a fixed-width disk about one,
slow roots near zero, or arbitrary spectra.

### 4.1 Elementary parameter and product estimates

The rational inequalities 12/5<lambda<17/7 and m>=128 give

    12/5<c<5/2, 3/8<a<1/2, 11/8<d_0<3/2.             (10)

For example c<2193/896<5/2 and
a>(127/128)/(5/2)=127/320>3/8. Also ac=1-1/m.

Let mathcal_P_m(z) be the product for the COMPLETE word H_m. Its multiplicity
formula gives

 mathcal_P_m(z)=product_(j=0)^(m-1)(1-d_j z)^(2^(m-j-1)),
 |mathcal_P_m(1)|=(ac)^n c^(-m).                       (11)

Indeed sum_j 2^(m-j-1)=n and sum_j j2^(m-j-1)=n-m, each proved by induction
using H_(j+1)=(H_j,d_j,H_j). For z=1+w with |w|=R=1/(16m),

 |1-d_j z| <= a c^j (1+4|w|),

because d_j/(a c^j)=1+1/(a c^j)<11/3<4. Therefore 1+t<=exp(t) and
1-1/m<=exp(-1/m) imply

 |mathcal_P_m(z)|<=c^(-m) exp[-3n/(4m)].               (12)

On that circle, |1-d_0 z|>=a-d_0R>1/3 by (10). Since the retained product
is P_q=mathcal_P_m/(1-d_0z), put

 E_m=3 c^(-m) exp[-3n/(4m)].

Then

             |P_q(z)|<=E_m,  |zP_q(z)|<=2E_m          (13)

on the whole circle. The damping estimate is for complex z, not a real-axis
estimate incorrectly differentiated across a growing Jordan block.

### 4.2 Legal early queries bound every polynomial image, without conditioning

Write D_r=product_(t<r)(1+h_t). Fact 1252b8823bb5a290 gives
||x_i||<=product_(t<i)(1+h_t)<=D_r for all 0<=i<r: this follows directly from
||grad F(x)||<=||x|| and the actual recursion. All these queries are retained.

The polynomials P_0,...,P_(r-1) have successive degrees and nonzero leading
coefficients, so they form a basis of degree-<r polynomials. Moreover

                zP_i=(P_i-P_(i+1))/h_i.                (14)

Every h_i>1. Thus multiplying a polynomial of degree <=r-2 by z increases
the l1 norm of its coefficients in the P-basis by at most a factor two.
Multiplying instead by z-theta increases that norm by at most 2+|theta|<=4
for the roots in (6). Starting with P_0=1 proves

                     ||T[N_k]||<=4^k D_r, k<r.        (15)

If N_k has complex coefficients, extend T complex linearly and use the usual
Hermitian norm. Its expansion in the REAL x_i still proves (15) by the
triangle inequality. No inverse Vandermonde or inverse root-separation factor
occurs, and no bound on the common metric was imposed.

In the first r chronological steps, a level j occurs only if 2^j<=r. Since
c<5/2<2^(3/2), h_t<=1+(1/2)r^(3/2) there. Hence

                     D_r<=(3r^(3/2))^r.               (16)

This is deliberately a loose legal-trajectory bound. Its exponential cost
depends on the temporal degree r, not on the full horizon n.

### 4.3 A separation-free Newton remainder bound, including repeated roots

For completeness the required contour identity is proved here. For any
ordered roots theta_1,...,theta_r inside the circle |z-1|=R, put
N_k(t)=product_(ell<=k)(t-theta_ell). Direct telescoping gives

 1/(z-t)=sum_(k=0)^(r-1) N_k(t)/N_(k+1)(z)
                         +N_r(t)/[(z-t)N_r(z)].        (17)

For a polynomial p define

 C_k=(1/(2pi i)) integral_(|z-1|=R) p(z)/N_(k+1)(z) dz.

Multiplying (17) by p(z) and integrating shows
p(t)-sum C_k N_k(t) is divisible by N_r(t)=chi(t): for t inside the circle,
the remainder term is chi(t) times an analytic function. Thus it and its
required derivatives vanish at every root, with the specified multiplicities.
A polynomial vanishes with those multiplicities iff it is divisible by chi,
as follows by successive division by each linear factor. The polynomial sum
has degree<r, so it is exactly [p]. The integral identity for polynomials is
also obtainable by expanding in powers about the circle's center and
integrating each finite/geometric series; no node-separation hypothesis occurs.

For (6), each |z-theta_ell|>=1/(32m) on the circle. If |p(z)|<=E there,

                       |C_k|<=2(32m)^k E.              (18)

Combining (15), (18) and the geometric sum with 128m>=2 gives

 ||T[p]||<=2ED_r sum_(k<r)(128m)^k
                        <=4ED_r(128m)^r.              (19)

This proof covers collisions of roots directly. The explicit root ordering
need not preserve real coefficients term by term, since the final remainder
of a real polynomial by real chi is real.

Apply (19) to p=P_q and p=zP_q using (13) and (16). With

 K_mr=12 c^(-m)(384m r^(3/2))^r exp[-3n/(4m)],

we get ||x_q||<=K_mr and ||g_q||<=2K_mr. For 0<=b<=2 the actual final
position y=x_q-b g_q satisfies ||y||<=5K_mr. Since F has minimum zero at zero
and is 1-smooth, 2F(y)<=||y||^2. This proves (7), including every possible
terminal interpolation and the original last step d_0.

### 4.4 Exact benchmark comparison and the explicit degree range

We retain the actual B_n from b98bb807d514e80d, rather than replacing the
target by a guessed profile constant. The bound on the actual normalized
ratio is B_n times the right side of (7). The accepted elementary estimate

             B_n<=1+4n^p, p=log_2(3),                 (20)

is sufficient to show this exact ratio tends to zero under (8): its logarithm
contributes O(m), whereas n/m grows exponentially in m. No unverified C54
comparison is a premise, and no inference about a whole-schedule upper bound
is made.

For r<=n/(32m^2), use r<n<2^m. The inequalities log384<6, logm<=m/16 for
m>=128, 6<=m/16, and log2<3/4 give

 log(384m r^(3/2))<2m.

For logm<=m/16, the difference m/16-logm is positive at128 and has positive
derivative for m>=128. Consequently the positive exponent in (7) is at most
4mr<=n/(8m). Dropping c^(-2m)<=1 gives (9). Multiplying (9) by the exact B_n
and then using (20) proves the asserted uniform negligible profile ratio.

## 5. Actual nonquadratic, full-rank realizations in the growing class

The class in Section4 is not empty and is not restricted to quadratic data.
Here is a finite, symbolic, quantitative construction for every m>=128 and
2<=r<=q. This is a nonvacuity calibration, not a new strong lower witness.

Choose a rational alpha in

              (1-1/(64m), 1-1/(128m))                 (21)

such that all P_i(alpha), 0<=i<=q, are nonzero and pairwise distinct. Such a
choice exists: each P_i is a nonzero polynomial, and P_i-P_j is nonzero for
i!=j because their degrees differ. Only finitely many real roots are
forbidden, whereas the interval contains infinitely many rational numbers.
This is a finite algebraic specification, not a request for numerical search.

Let e_0,...,e_(r-1) be the orthonormal basis of R^r and let N_shift e_k=e_(k+1)
for k<r-1, N_shift e_(r-1)=0. For epsilon>0 set

 A_epsilon=alpha I+epsilon N_shift,
 X_i=P_i(A_epsilon)e_0, G_i=A_epsilon X_i,
 V_i=(alpha/2)||X_i||^2.                               (22)

Take optimum data zero, and put nu=1/4. Define transformed jets

 G'_i=(G_i-nu X_i)/(1-nu),
 V'_i=(V_i-nu||X_i||^2/2)/(1-nu).                      (23)

At epsilon=0 these are the jets of the scalar quadratic of curvature
alpha'=(alpha-nu)/(1-nu), lying strictly in (0,1), at points
P_i(alpha)e_0. All points, including the optimum, are distinct. Thus EVERY
ordered residual is strictly negative:

 Q'_ij(0)=-alpha'(1-alpha')||X_i(0)-X_j(0)||^2/2<0.

For epsilon variable each Q'_ij(epsilon) is a real polynomial, since all
quantities in (22)--(23) are polynomial. Define the exact finite constants

 delta=min_(i!=j)(-Q'_ij(0))>0,
 D=1+sum_(i!=j) sum_(k>=1) |coefficient_(epsilon^k) Q'_ij|,
 epsilon=min(1/2,delta/(2D)).                           (24)

All indices here include star and every query. The sums are finite and
provide a computable algebraic law, not a solver oracle. Since epsilon<=1,
|Q'_ij(epsilon)-Q'_ij(0)|<=epsilon D<=delta/2. Thus all transformed ordered
inequalities are strictly satisfied. Fact1252 constructs a convex 1-smooth
interpolant K of them. The single function

                F(x)=(1-nu)K(x)+nu||x||^2/2           (25)

is convex, 1-smooth and nu-strongly convex, has its UNIQUE minimizer at zero,
and interpolates EVERY original (X_i,G_i,V_i). Its actual GD follows (22)
with the prescribed steps. X_0=e_0 has TRUE initial distance exactly one.

These data have the representation (1) with chi=(z-alpha)^r and
T[p]=p(A_epsilon)e_0. The r vectors e_0,A_epsilon e_0,...,
A_epsilon^(r-1)e_0 are linearly independent: in the standard basis their
coefficient matrix is triangular with nonzero diagonal epsilon^k. Thus T is
injective and its REAL common metric is positive definite. The first r
positions X_0,...,X_(r-1) also form a basis, since their polynomials have
successive degrees and nonzero leading coefficients.

No quadratic function with a minimizer at zero can interpolate these
gradients. Its Hessian would be symmetric and would have to equal
A_epsilon on that basis, whereas A_epsilon is nonsymmetric for r>=2 and
epsilon>0. Thus (25) is genuinely nonquadratic on the required jet data.

There is an exact positive terminal payoff, even uniformly over all real b.
Write p0=P_q(alpha)!=0 and p1=P'_q(alpha). The first two coordinates of X_q
and G_q are respectively

            (p0,epsilon p1),
            (alpha p0,epsilon(alpha p1+p0)).

Their determinant is epsilon p0^2. Minimizing the squared distance from the
first vector to the line spanned by the second gives, for every real b,

 2F(X_q-b G_q)
 >=nu||X_q-b G_q||^2
 >=nu epsilon^2 p0^4 /
                 [alpha^2 p0^2+epsilon^2(alpha p1+p0)^2] >0.  (26)

The line-distance identity follows by completing the scalar square in b;
the denominator is nonzero since alpha p0!=0. All quantities are fixed by
(21)--(24) and the actual word. For degrees covered by Section4, (7) also
applies, so this positive payoff is nevertheless asymptotically negligible
against the exact B_n profile. It is not presented as an improved adversary.

## 6. Source audit, scope and the remaining interface

Carl de Boor, "Divided Differences," Surveys in Approximation Theory1
(2005),46--69, arXiv:math/0502036, Proposition7 identifies Newton truncation
with the Hermite remainder; Proposition25 gives the bidiagonal multiplication
formula; equations(49)--(51) give its contour representation. The exact paper
and these proofs were read. Polynomial, repeated-node and real-coordinate
definitions match this application. Sections2 and4.3 re-prove the required
finite algebra rather than importing an unchecked numerical formula.

Downloaded PDF: downloads/deboor_divided_differences.pdf.
Relevant browser extraction: downloads/deboor_relevant_browser_extract.txt.
The local pdftotext command and existing Python PDF extractors were unavailable;
no installation was attempted. Matlas returned no directly applicable theorem
for the two mechanism queries; browser search supplied the exact paper.

These drafts close two SPECIFIED growing-span classes. Exact reciprocal roots
at every scale annihilate, while a near-unit cluster with growing Jordan depth
is controlled uniformly over all common metrics by legal early positions.
Neither proof extends by spectral radius alone to arbitrary metrics or to
slow nodes of order1/S_m. In particular, adding a zero root of multiplicity
>=2 to the resonant class leaves polynomial/constant-gradient suffix data and
is genuinely outside the annihilation proof. A changed generator at each
block is also outside this common-quotient class.

The all-pair law (2) for unrestricted chi of degree n+1 would simply reproduce
the original finite interpolation problem. Nothing here treats that
theorem-strength interface as a solved mechanism. The exact all-schedule
profile target remains unresolved, and there is no new verified fact.

## 7. All ruler levels retained: robust lower-level clusters and exact upper roots

Here is a stronger growing-span obstruction tied to the actual recursive
levels rather than only to a cluster near one. It is another part of the same
UNVERIFIED joint draft; all new estimates are proved in this section.

Keep Section1's actual word and put

    L=m-ceil(3 log_2 m), mu_j=1/d_j,
    D_j={z:|z-mu_j|<=mu_j/200}, 0<=j<=L.               (27)

For m>=128, 0<=L<m. Let chi be ANY real monic polynomial of degree
1<=r<=2m with the following root organization, counting multiplicity:

 * A zero root of multiplicity e in {0,1} is allowed.
 * Any number of roots, with arbitrary multiplicities, may lie in the union
   of D_0,...,D_L. They may be nonreal and need not be individually legal.
 * Every remaining root is an EXACT mu_j with L<j<m, of multiplicity at most
   the actual retained count N_j in (3).

As always nonreal roots occur in conjugate pairs. This class permits one root
at EVERY ruler level, independent directions for all of them, perturbations
of relative size 1/200 at all but the top ceil(3log_2 m)-1 levels, a constant
coordinate, and further confluent directions within the degree bound.

For EVERY common real PSD metric, all normalized legal joint data (1), every
interpolant F of those data and every final b in [0,2],

               2F(x_q-b g_q)<=100000 exp(-m^3/4).      (28)

In particular its ratio to the EXACT U_n^ZJ tends uniformly to zero. This is
a full obstruction for the stated growing, level-dependent Newton class.
The exact upper-level roots are essential to this proof and are not silently
replaced by shifted ones. The zero root cannot be doubled in this argument.

### 7.1 Separated level circles and their actual product damping

The ratios mu_(j+1)/mu_j=d_j/d_(j+1) decrease with j: the map
(1+u)/(1+cu) has derivative (1-c)/(1+cu)^2<0. By (10),

 d_0/d_1 < (3/2)/(2-1/128)=192/255<4/5.

Thus every successive mu ratio is less than4/5. Let Gamma_j be the
positively oriented circle |z-mu_j|=mu_j/100, for 0<=j<=L. These circles
are disjoint and enclose their own D_j but no other root clusters, exact
upper-level roots, or zero. Their total radii are at most

                 sum_(j<=L)mu_j/100 < mu_0/20<1.       (29)

Fix j and z on Gamma_j, writing z=(1+w)/d_j, |w|=eta=1/100. For ell<j,
t=d_ell/d_j lies in (0,1), and

             |1-d_ell z|<=1-t+eta t<=1.

Let mathcal_P_k be the full H_k product with the SAME a,c. We therefore have
|mathcal_P_j(z)|<=1 and

                 |mathcal_P_(j+1)(z)|<=eta.           (30)

For k>=j+1, d_k/d_j<=c^(k-j), whence

                |1-d_k z|<=3c^(k-j).

Define Z_k=3c^(k-j+1)|mathcal_P_k(z)|. The actual repeated-word identity
mathcal_P_(k+1)=mathcal_P_k^2(1-d_kz) gives Z_(k+1)<=Z_k^2. At the seed,

             Z_(j+1)<=3c^2/100<3/16<1/4.

Iterating this scalar inequality yields

                |mathcal_P_m(z)|<=4^(-2^(m-j-1)).     (31)

At j=0, |1-d_0z|=1/100. For j>=1, put t=d_0/d_j<4/5; then
|1-d_0z|>=1-t-eta t>1/100. Division by the actual omitted last factor gives

   |P_q(z)|<=100*4^(-2^(m-L-1))=:E_level             (32)

on the union Gamma of these circles. Also |z|<1 there: mu_0<=8/11, and
|z|<=101mu_0/100<1. Thus |zP_q(z)|<=E_level on Gamma as well.

### 7.2 Every denominator is controlled, including all exact upper levels

For every z on Gamma and EVERY root theta of chi,

                        |z-theta|>=mu_L/200.           (33)

For the same cluster this is the outer-minus-inner radius. For a lower
center mu_ell<=4mu_j/5, the distance to its perturbed disk is at least
(99/100)mu_j-(201/200)mu_ell >=(186/1000)mu_j. For a larger center the
distance is at least (199/200)mu_ell-(101/100)mu_j, which is larger still
using mu_ell>=5mu_j/4. Exact upper-level centers only improve these estimates.
Distance to zero is at least99mu_j/100. All bounds imply (33).

Temporarily surround the exact upper-level roots, and zero if present, by
additional sufficiently small disjoint circles. With the low circles Gamma,
these enclose all roots. The Newton coefficient formula from (17), proved
by its telescoping identity, holds using the union of these circles. It
only needs Cauchy's identity near each root to establish the required
vanishing multiplicities; the enclosed set need not be connected.

For p=zP_q, the numerator vanishes at every exact upper-level root to at
least its full chi multiplicity. It also vanishes at a zero root of
multiplicity one. In each Newton coefficient integral p/N_(k+1), those poles
are consequently removable. The integrals on the added circles are ZERO.
Every coefficient is therefore obtained by integrating ONLY over Gamma.

For e=0, the same is true for p=P_q. For e=1 define the real polynomial

 E_0(z)=product_(theta!=0, with multiplicity)(1-z/theta).

It has value1 at zero and all the required zero multiplicities at the other
roots. For p=P_q-E_0 the numerator again cancels zero and every exact upper
root. Moreover E_0/N_(k+1) is analytic inside every LOW circle as well.
Hence the coefficients of [P_q-E_0] are the integrals of P_q/N_(k+1) over
Gamma: E_0 contributes nothing there. This argument avoids bounding E_0's
possibly large size on the low circles.

Let E_0=0 when e=0. For both e=0 and e=1, (29), (32), (33) now bound every
Newton coefficient of [zP_q] and of [P_q-E_0] by

                 E_level (200 d_L)^(k+1).             (34)

All roots have modulus<1. Repeating the elementary P-basis calculation
(14) gives ||T[N_k]||<=3^k D_r. Summing (34), using the geometric series
and D_r<=(3r^(3/2))^r, gives the safe common bound

 ||g_q||<=K_level,     ||x_q-T[E_0]||<=K_level,

 K_level=200(1800 d_L r^(3/2))^r
                                  4^(-2^(m-L-1)).     (35)

Explicitly the sum before this rounding is at most
(2/3) E_level D_r (600d_L)^r, since600d_L>=2. Thus the displayed constant200
is an upper bound. This proof does not diagonalize the physical metric and
does not impose legality separately on any coefficient or root.

### 7.3 The surviving simple zero component and the objective bound

For e=1, every nonzero root obeys

 |theta|>=(199/200)mu_(m-1).

Multiplication by 1-z/theta in the P-basis multiplies coefficient l1 norm
by at most1+2/|theta|<=4d_(m-1)<4c^m. The factors have total degree r-1;
all intermediate degrees are less than r, so (14) applies throughout.
Consequently

 ||T[E_0]||<=D_r(4c^m)^(r-1)
                         <=(12c^m r^(3/2))^r=:J_level. (36)

For e=0 the same upper bound holds since E_0=0. This is a bound on the
ACTUAL initial-normalized projector image, not an assumption on its metric.

The optimum inequality at the last QUERY q and smoothness for the final
step b in[0,2] give

 2F(x_q-bg_q)<=2f_q-b(2-b)||g_q||^2
              <=2<x_q,g_q>-||g_q||^2
              <=2J_level K_level+2K_level^2.           (37)

Every inequality here concerns the same actual interpolant F. In particular
a large surviving constant position component is multiplied by a proved
small gradient; it is not discarded from the distance normalization.

For r<=2m, d_L<c^m and c<5/2<exp(1), we have, for m>=128,

 log(1800d_L r^(3/2))<2m,
 log(12c^m r^(3/2))<2m.

One explicit check uses log1800<8<=m/16 and log(2m)<=m/8. The latter holds
at128 since log256=8log2<6<16, and m/8-log(2m) increases thereafter.
By the definition of L, 2^(m-L)>=m^3. Thus

 J_level<=exp(4m^2),
 K_level<=200 exp[4m^2-(log4)m^3/2].                   (38)

Since log4>1 and8m^2<=m^3/4 for m>=128, substitution into (37) yields

 2J_level K_level+2K_level^2
 <=80400 exp(-m^3/4)<100000 exp(-m^3/4).

This proves (28). The ratio to the exact benchmark is B_n times this bound;
the accepted B_n<=1+4n^(log_2 3) makes that product tend to zero. No fixed
asymptotic profile constant, and no unverified benchmark sharpening, is used.

### 7.4 A full-rank NONQUADRATIC calibration with one root at every level

The level-dependent class has genuine nonquadratic legal members retaining
all m directions. This example is only a nonvacuity calibration of (28).
Choose a rational theta_0 in

       (mu_0(1+1/800),mu_0(1+1/400))

at which P_0,...,P_q are nonzero and pairwise distinct. The finite-polynomial
root argument in Section5 proves existence, without running a search.
For j>=1 put theta_j=mu_j and set

 D_diag=diag(theta_0,...,theta_(m-1)),
 X_0=(1,...,1)/sqrt(m), A_epsilon=D_diag+epsilon N_shift,
 X_i=P_i(A_epsilon)X_0, G_i=A_epsilon X_i,
 V_i=<X_i,D_diag X_i>/2.                               (38a)

Here N_shift is the lower shift on R^m, and take nu=mu_(m-1)/2>0. All
diagonal entries of D_diag lie strictly between nu and1. Form transformed
jets as in (23). At epsilon=0 they are jets of the positive quadratic Hessian
(D_diag-nu I)/(1-nu), which is strictly below the identity. The first coordinate
of X_i is P_i(theta_0)/sqrt(m), so all queried points and zero are distinct.
Every ordered transformed Q' is consequently strictly negative, by the exact
quadratic formula -<X_i-X_j,B(I-B)(X_i-X_j)>/2 for B=(D_diag-nu I)/(1-nu).

Define delta and the coefficient bound D_coeff as in (24), using these new
polynomial residuals. Also let R(epsilon) have columns X_0,...,X_(m-1), put
d_det=det R(0), and put D_det=1+sum_(k>=1)|[epsilon^k] det R(epsilon)|.
The m distinct diagonal entries and the successive degrees of the P_i give
d_det!=0 by the polynomial evaluation/Vandermonde argument: a degree<m
polynomial vanishing at all m distinct entries is zero. Set

 epsilon=min(1/2,delta/(2D_coeff),|d_det|/(2D_det))>0.   (38b)

Every Q' remains strictly negative and det R(epsilon) remains nonzero, by
the same coefficient-sum bounds as (24). Fact1252 constructs K, and
F=(1-nu)K+nu||x||^2/2 gives one1-smooth nu-strongly convex function with
the stated jets, UNIQUE minimizer0 and initial distance exactly1.

The polynomial chi=product_(j=0)^(m-1)(z-theta_j) is an allowed member of
Section7 with r=m: only the bottom-level root is shifted, within its allowed
disk. Moreover chi(A_epsilon)=0. One direct check uses the lower bidiagonal
Newton multiplication matrix J with diagonal theta_j and lower entries1:
A_epsilon=S J S^(-1) for S=diag(1,epsilon,...,epsilon^(m-1)), and chi(J)=0
by its quotient definition. Thus T[p]=p(A_epsilon)X_0 is the required REAL
quotient map. The nonzero determinant in (38b) makes it injective, so its
common metric is positive definite and all m temporal directions are present.

No quadratic with minimum at zero interpolates these data, since its symmetric
Hessian would have to equal the nonsymmetric A_epsilon on the position basis
X_0,...,X_(m-1). The function therefore has genuinely nonquadratic queried data.
At the original final step b=d_0, the first coordinate is unaffected by the
strictly lower triangular part, giving the quantitative positive payoff

 2F(X_q-d_0G_q)>= (nu/m)(1-d_0 theta_0)^2 P_q(theta_0)^2>0.  (38c)

The factors are nonzero by construction. Inequality (28) also holds, so this
is a positive but negligible payoff. It is not claimed as a new mechanism
that improves the quadratic or single-cut benchmark.

## 8. A double-zero root really survives: an actual legal scope counterexample

It would be false to extend the annihilation conclusion from a simple zero
root to a double zero root, or to assume that the terminal constant gradient
has nonnegative correlation with the eliminated component. The counterexample
below uses the ACTUAL critical word and has a unique minimizer and distance1.
It is the already verified single-cut mechanism in b98bb807d514e80d, written
explicitly in the new growing Newton coordinates to audit their scope.

Let S_total=sum H_m, d=d_0 and T_tail=S_total-d. Define

 A_tail=(d-1)/(2T_tail+1)>0,
 f'(x)=max(-A_tail,min(1,x)), f(0)=0, x_0=1.           (39)

This derivative is monotone, continuous and1-Lipschitz, so its integral is
one convex1-smooth function with unique minimizer0. The first query has
gradient1; after the actual first step d the point is

 x_1=1-d=-A_tail(2T_tail+1).

Every later query stays at most -A_tail(T_tail+1)<-A_tail, since the remaining
total step mass is T_tail. Thus every later gradient is the constant -A_tail.
At the actual end of H_m the full normalized payoff is exactly

             2f(x_n)=(d-1)^2/(2T_tail+1)>0.            (40)

In fact this also REFUTES extension of the super-small bound (28) to a double
zero root, for every m>=128. Count the levels to obtain

 1+S_total=A_mass c^m+(1-A_mass)2^m,
 A_mass=a/(c-2) in(0,1).

Here A_mass<1 follows from ac<1=lambda(lambda-2)<c(c-2). Hence S_total<c^m,
and (10) implies

                  2f(x_n)>(3/64)c^(-m).               (40a)

This exceeds100000 exp(-m^3/4): logc<1 and
log(100000*64/3)<15, while m^3/4-m>15 for m>=128.
Thus the zero-root multiplicity in Section7 is quantitatively load-bearing,
not only needed for the exact-zero statement in Section3.

All ordered constraints, including every queried value and the optimum, hold
because these are actual jets of the single explicitly defined function (39).

This transcript belongs to the growing class

              chi=z^2 product_(j=0)^(m-1)(z-mu_j),     (41)

of degree m+2. To specify the real map T on the quotient, define it directly
on a polynomial p by

 T[p]=u p(0)+v p'(0)+d w p(mu_0),
 v=-A_tail, w=1+A_tail, u=1-dw.                       (42)

Evaluation at0, the derivative at0 and evaluation atmu_0 all vanish on
multiples of chi, so this is a well-defined quotient map. The remaining
level coordinates are allowed but have zero image. For every i,

 x_i=u-s_i v+d w P_i(mu_0),
 g_i=v+w P_i(mu_0),  s_i=sum_(t<i)h_t.

P_0(mu_0)=1 and P_i(mu_0)=0 for every i>=1, because the first step is d_0.
Thus these formulas reproduce exactly (39), and T[1]=u+dw=1. The common
metric T^T T is real PSD; no nonlinear or time-dependent map has been used.
In particular v*(dw)=-A_tail d(1+A_tail)<0. Hence the tempting cross-term
inequality <v,eliminated_component>>=0 is already false on legal data.

The function is genuinely nonquadratic on the transcript: a quadratic with
minimum at0 and g_0=1 atx_0=1 would have curvature1, while its gradient atx_1
would be1-d, not -A_tail. For large positive T_tail these differ. Its exact
payoff is the known weak first-cut bound, so (40) is NOT an improved
adversary or a profile resolution. It proves only that a double-zero
residual and its negative cross terms must be handled in any extension of
Sections3 or7. Arbitrary shifted upper-level nodes, double-zero blocks and
changing generators remain open after these scoped obstructions.
