# Exact triangle gap transport and a signed face-switch energy ledger

JOINT UNVERIFIED. Worker max2, invocation76 after ended baseline75.
Project gd-exact-profile-20260926. Guidance055f8e0d879c2c6e.
One invocation, at most two mechanisms. Formal verification is not authorized.
All new arguments below are one joint candidate, not accepted intermediate facts.
Original unrestricted minimax target OPEN.

## 0. Verdict

The ordered two-gap dynamics is derived below for every physical step, every
projection face, and every coordinate reordering. The true squared norm is
u^2+uv+v^2; neither transverse energy nor its cross term is discarded.

Mechanism1 tests a concrete region retaining both triangle facet gauges.
For a long step h>=2 its exact image support radius is

  max{h-1, R-h/2, (R+h-3/2)/2}, R>=1.

The third branch is necessary. Omitting it fails on a legal original H_2
prefix followed by its level2 bridge, with actual output support radius5k/4
where the proposed image allows only k=h-1. This is a failure of the proposed
region erosion, not a counterexample to the triangle terminal bound.

After that decisive failure, Mechanism2 retains a direction-dependent
fixed-face suffix forecast and its full transverse energy. It gives an exact
nonrecursive identity: actual terminal objective equals the initial forecast
plus a signed sum of actual face-switch corrections. The initial reserve is
an explicit nonnegative square plus a paid transverse square. Every original
step, both children, and the free actual terminal occur in this identity.

The tempting local repair is false: a legal strict vertex-to-different-vertex
transition has positive forecast increment3d while the before/after transverse
squares are identical. More generally, no phase-dependent constants and
quadratic transverse coefficients attached only to the three vertex labels
can make every such correction nonpositive. A three-way symmetry argument
proves this pointwise obstruction without asserting an actual cyclic orbit.

A uniform bound on the total SIGNED switch charge by the initial reserve
remains unproved. No bound V_triangle<=1/D_m or17/(16D_m), and no uniform
terminal gain, is claimed. The local image formula and the energy identity
are not presented as a quantitative full-word resolution. No arbitrary-N
simplex comparison or full-function W upper bound follows from this report.

## 1. Frozen word, function, and original scope

Fix an integer m>=1536 once and for all. Define

  epsilon=1/(4m), beta=1-epsilon, lambda=1+sqrt(2),
  c=lambda(1+epsilon), a=beta/c,
  H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j), 0<=j<m,
  n=2^m-1, h_i=(H_m)_i, S=sum_(i=0)^(n-1)h_i, D_m=1+2S.

The same m and parameters are used in every child. In particular the final
physical step is1+a. No child is treated as a separately parameterized word.

Take the three fixed real vertices

  v_1=(1,0), v_2=(-1/2,sqrt(3)/2), v_3=(-1/2,-sqrt(3)/2),
  D=conv{v_1,v_2,v_3},
  F(x)=max_(g in D)[<x,g>-||g||^2/2], x in R^2,
  T_h(x)=x-h P_D(x), x_(i+1)=T_(h_i)(x_i),
  Psi(x)=2F(x), E_m(x_0)=Psi(x_n)/||x_0||^2,
  V_triangle(m)=sup_(x_0!=0) E_m(x_0).

All inner products and norms are Euclidean. P_D denotes Euclidean projection
onto this one fixed triangle. The original W_n(h) is the supremum of
2(f(x_n)-f_star)/(L||x_0-x_star||^2) over all finite-dimensional convex
L-smooth functions with a minimizer, under steps h_i/L. Hence W=2R, and
V_triangle is a restricted-family quantity at the specified word. A triangle
upper bound, even if proved, would not be an upper bound on W.

Accepted fact931b9653ce0f7201, read in full, gives the all-split benchmark
ratio B_(2^m-1)/D_m<47/50. Its799/800 consequence requires the UNPROVED full
function-class bound W_(2^m-1)(H_m)<=17/(16D_m). No such implication is
invoked here. Fact1252b8823bb5a290 supplies the ordered interpolation
convention and the need to retain the actual terminal; the legality needed
here is also derived directly in Section2. Previous unverified reports are
awareness only, not premises of this joint candidate.

## 2. Static legality and exact original interpolation rows

The vertices satisfy ||v_i||=1, sum_i v_i=0, <v_i,v_j>=-1/2 for i!=j, and
sum_i v_i v_i^T=(3/2)I. Thus

  D={x:<x,v_i>>=-1/2 for all i},
  ball(0,1/2) subset D subset ball(0,1).

Completing the square in F identifies its unique maximizer as g=P_D(x).
The defining necessary and sufficient projection inequality is

  <x-g,z-g><=0 for every z in D.                         (2.1)

Necessity follows by differentiating squared distance along a segment toward
z; sufficiency follows by expanding ||x-z||^2-||x-g||^2. Adding (2.1) at two
inputs x,y gives ||P_D(x)-P_D(y)||^2 <= <P_D(x)-P_D(y),x-y>, and then the
Cauchy--Schwarz inequality proves1-Lipschitzness. The maximizing inequalities
bound F(x+t)-F(x)-<P_D(x),t> between0 and ||t||^2. This proves that F is
differentiable with gradient P_D. F is finite and convex because D is compact
and F is a maximum of affine functions of x.

F(0)=0 and F>=0. For x!=0 choose0<r<min(1,1/(2||x||)); then rx belongs to
D, and F(x)>=(r-r^2/2)||x||^2>0. The complete minimum set is{0}. Consequently
||x_0|| is the actual distance to the closest minimizer.

For every original query and terminal i=0,...,n, put g_i=P_D(x_i),f_i=F(x_i),
and include x_*=g_*=f_*=0. For EVERY ordered pair,

  Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2
      =<g_i-g_j,x_j-g_j><=0.                            (2.2)

The inequality is (2.1) at j with z=g_i. Thus incoming/outgoing terminal,
remote, reversed, and optimum rows all hold for these actual samples.
Multiplying F by L and using steps h_i/L preserves the points and W score.

## 3. Ordered gaps, metric, values, and face guards

Define labeled coordinates q_i=(2/3)<x,v_i>. Their sum is0, x=sum_i q_i v_i,
and ||x||^2=(3/2)sum_i q_i^2. At the current point relabel only for notation
so that q_1>=q_2>=q_3. A relabeling means a triangle symmetry, not a change
of the physical function or a replacement of the actual state.

Set

  u=q_1-q_2>=0, v=q_2-q_3>=0,
  (q_1,q_2,q_3)=((2u+v)/3,(v-u)/3,-(u+2v)/3),
  A=(2u+v)/3, B=(u+2v)/3,
  Q(u,v)=||x||^2=u^2+uv+v^2.                            (3.1)

Thus the assigned sorted triple(A,B-A,-B) is recovered, and A/2<=B<=2A.
The two gaps contain all the actual planar state up to a triangle symmetry.

The projection regions in this order are

  C: u+2v<=1;
  V: u>=1;
  E: 0<=u<=1 and u+2v>=1.                              (3.2)

Their boundaries overlap and the formulas agree there. With r_i=(2/3)<g,v_i>
for g=P_D(x), the respective projection coordinates are

  C: r=q;
  V: r=(2/3,-1/3,-1/3);
  E: r=(1/6+u/2,1/6-u/2,-1/3).                         (3.3)

For C, membership in D is exactly q_i>=-1/3. For V, the projection normal
inequalities at v_1 are q_1-q_2>=1 and q_1-q_3>=1; ordering reduces these
to u>=1. For E, the point in (3.3) lies on the edge opposite v_3 precisely
when u<=1. Its residual q-r is a nonnegative multiple of(1,1,-2) precisely
when u+2v>=1; this is the outward edge normal, proving (2.1). These cases
cover every u,v>=0. This derives (3.2)--(3.3) without an external theorem.

The actual objective is

  Psi_C=Q(u,v),
  Psi_V=2u+v-1,
  Psi_E=(3u^2+2u+4v-1)/4.                              (3.4)

For the vertex chart set s=<x,v_1>=u+v/2 and let t be the signed coordinate
perpendicular to v_1. Then t^2=3v^2/4, Q=s^2+t^2, Psi_V=2s-1.
For the edge chart let n_E=-v_3, choose a unit edge tangent t_E, and write
z=<x,n_E>=u/2+v, y=<x,t_E>. Then y^2=3u^2/4, Q=z^2+y^2 and
Psi_E=z-1/4+y^2. In particular both charts retain their full transverse energy.

## 4. Every exact physical update and every reordering

All original steps have h>1. Put k=h-1>0. In the following table an ordering
such as213 gives the identities of the old coordinates in new descending
order. The displayed output is the NEW ordered gap pair(U,V).

CORE, u+2v<=1:

  order321: (U,V)=(k v,k u).                             (4.1)

This follows from q'=(1-h)q=-kq; it includes ties.

VERTEX, u>=1:

  h<=u:       order123, (U,V)=(u-h,v);
  u<=h<=u+v:  order213, (U,V)=(h-u,u+v-h);
  h>=u+v:     order231, (U,V)=(v,h-u-v).                 (4.2)

Indeed the labeled adjacent differences after the actual step are u-h and v.
The sign of u-h and then of u+v-h gives exactly these three orders. The last
two old coordinates remain ordered because their gradient coordinates agree.

EDGE, 0<=u<=1 and u+2v>=1: define w=v-h(1-u)/2. Then

  w>=k u:     order213, (U,V)=(k u,w-k u);
  0<=w<=k u:  order231, (U,V)=(w,k u-w);
  w<=0:       order321, (U,V)=(-w,k u).                 (4.3)

Here the labeled adjacent differences are -k u and w. Thus the old second
coordinate is never below the old first; the signs of w and w-k u give the
complete list. No order-persistence assumption enters (4.1)--(4.3).

For each output use precisely these next-query guards:

  next C iff U+2V<=1;
  next V iff U>=1;
  next E iff U<=1 and U+2V>=1.                          (4.4)

Inside a row of (4.1)--(4.3), every boundary in (4.4) is affine in u,v.
Together with u,v>=0, the current face boundaries u+2v=1,u=1 and order
boundaries h=u,h=u+v,w=0,w=k u, this retains all guards. The A,B update is
A'=(2U+V)/3,B'=(U+2V)/3, so the table is also an explicit map in A,B.

The formulas agree on shared boundaries. At u+2v=1, w=-k v and the edge
row becomes(k v,k u), the core formula. At u=1, w=v and (4.3) becomes the
applicable vertex row. The adjacent rows agree when w=0,w=k u,h=u or h=u+v.
No additional branch is inserted by allowing closed inequalities.

The exact new squared norm is

  core:   k^2 Q(u,v);
  vertex: (u-h)^2+(u-h)v+v^2;
  edge:   k^2 u^2-k u w+w^2.                            (4.5)

These are permutation-invariant norm expressions for the raw differences.
In particular the edge cross term has its displayed MINUS sign.

## 5. Mechanism1: exact two-facet image and its decisive failure

Use the direction-sensitive, two-facet hexagonal gauge

  G(x)=max_i |<x,v_i>|=max(2u+v,u+2v)/2,
  C_R={u,v>=0:2u+v<=2R and u+2v<=2R}                   (5.1)

in the ordered chamber, with its six symmetry copies in the physical plane.
This is a genuine planar region, not a Euclidean ball or a selected ray.
The proposed erosion invariant was

  T_h(C_R) subset C_max(k,R-h/2), h>=2,R>=1.            (5.2)

Both facets and the exact norm were retained. The issue is the IMAGE of the
region under the actual nonlinear update, not whether the region is defined
in two coordinates.

The exact image support maximum is instead

  sup_(x in C_R) G(T_h x)
      =M_h(R):=max{k,R-h/2,(R+h-3/2)/2},              (5.3)
  h>=2,R>=1.

Proof of upper bound. It suffices to bound all three absolute labeled dot
products after the step in the ordered chamber. In the core ||x||<=1, hence
G(T_hx)=kG(x)<=k. In the vertex region the three dot products are

  s_1'=(2u+v)/2-h,
  s_2'=(v-u+h)/2,
  s_3'=-(u+2v)/2+h/2.

Since u>=1, the first has positive part at most(R-h)_+ and negative part at
most k. The third has negative part at most(R-h/2)_+ and positive part at
most k/2. The negative part of the second is at most(R-h)_+/2. For its
positive part, the two facets give

  v-u<=min(R-3u/2,2R-3u)<=min(R-3/2,2R-3).

If R>=3/2 this yields s_2'<=(R+h-3/2)/2. If1<=R<=3/2 it yields
s_2'<=R+h/2-3/2<=h/2<=k. All are bounded by M_h(R).

In the edge region the three labeled dot products are

  s_1'=v/2+(1-3h/4)u-h/4,
  s_2'=v/2+(3h/4-1/2)u-h/4,
  s_3'=-(u+2v)/2+h/2.

The lower edge guard v>=(1-u)/2 yields s_1'>=-k(1+3u)/4>=-k and
s_2'>=k(3u-1)/4>=-k/4. Also s_1'<=s_2'. The upper facet v<=R-u/2 gives

  s_2'<=R/2-h/4+3k u/4<=(R+h-3/2)/2.

Finally s_3' lies between h/2-R and k/2. This proves the upper bound.

Attainment of the maximum requires no optimization procedure. The k term
is attained at x=v_1, which lies in C_R. If R-h/2 is a maximizing term,
choose u=0,v=R: its third dot product has absolute value R-h/2, which is
nonnegative since it is at least k. If the third term strictly exceeds k,
then R>h-1/2>=3/2; choose u=1,v=R-1/2. This point lies in C_R and its second
post-step dot product equals(R+h-3/2)/2. If that term equals k, the first
point already attains the maximum. These cases prove (5.3), including ties.
This is an exact nonlinear region-image calculation, not a numerical sample.

A legal original-prefix failure of (5.2). Put k=beta*c and h=1+k, the actual
level2 bridge after H_2. Since epsilon<1/4 and lambda>12/5,

  k=(1-epsilon^2)lambda>(15/16)(12/5)=9/4>2.

At its query take u=1,v=3k/2. Its projection is v_1 and (4.2) gives the
actual new order213 and gaps(k,k/2). Consequently

  G(x)=(1+3k)/2,
  max(k,G(x)-h/2)=k,
  G(T_hx)=5k/4,                                      (5.4)
  Q(x)=1+3k/2+9k^2/4, Q(T_hx)=7k^2/4.

For actual reachability let S_2=sum H_2 and choose x_0=x+S_2 v_1.
Adding r v_1 increases the upper gap by r and leaves the lower gap unchanged.
At every earlier query the upper gap is at least1, so projection is v_1.
The literal H_2 prefix therefore reaches x. Its own initial squared radius
is(1+S_2)^2+(1+S_2)(3k/2)+9k^2/4, fully paid. The remaining word is simply
continued on the fixed legal F; nothing is asserted about its terminal payoff.

The extra branch in (5.3) reflects the coordinate order213, where a formerly
second coordinate becomes the largest positive supporting coordinate before
the opposite facet has drained enough. Thus (5.2) is decisively false.
Iterating a corrected scalar bound would again discard the orientation and
its cross energy; (5.3) by itself does not yield the desired full-word estimate.
The permitted second mechanism begins only after this concrete failure.

## 6. Mechanism2: full suffix forecasts with transverse energy

Use the seven physical face charts f: the core C, three edges E, and three
vertices V. Associate a symmetric orthogonal projector P_f and vector b_f
orthogonal to its range:

  core: P_f=I,b_f=0;
  edge with normal n_E: P_f=t_E t_E^T,b_f=n_E/2;
  vertex v_j: P_f=0,b_f=v_j.

On the corresponding closed actual projection region,

  P_D(x)=P_f x+b_f,
  Psi(x)=||P_f x||^2+2<b_f,x>-||b_f||^2.             (6.1)

Section3 proves these formulas and all their guards. Choose at each actual
query/terminal a valid chart f_i; for definiteness choose the smallest face
containing P_D(x_i). The identity below also holds with other valid boundary
choices. They alter individual forecast terms but not the total identity.

For i=0,...,n define the ACTUAL remaining mass and excess product

  s_i=sum_(t=i)^(n-1) h_t,
  p_i=product_(t=i)^(n-1)(h_t-1),
  d_i=1+2s_i,

with s_n=0,p_n=1,d_n=1. These are finite sums/products of the original fixed
word. They are not evaluated by a program or by a replayed trajectory.
For any chart, including a hypothetical continuation beyond its region, set

  L_i^f(x)=p_i^2 ||P_f x||^2+2<b_f,x>-d_i||b_f||^2.  (6.2)

In chart coordinates these are

  vertex: L_i^V=2s-2s_i-1;
  edge:   L_i^E=z-s_i/2-1/4+p_i^2 y^2;
  core:   L_i^C=p_i^2||x||^2.                         (6.3)

The use of s for a vertex dot product in (6.3) is local; s_i always denotes
the remaining schedule mass. In particular (6.3) pays y^2 with its exact
full-suffix contraction multiplier. It does not reset the right child.

For any x in the actual chart f at physical step i,

  L_i^f(x)=L_(i+1)^f(T_(h_i)x).                       (6.4)

To prove it put k_i=h_i-1. The actual affine update is
T_(h_i)x=(I-h_i P_f)x-h_i b_f. Orthogonality gives
P_f T_(h_i)x=-k_i P_f x and
<b_f,T_(h_i)x>=<b_f,x>-h_i||b_f||^2.
Now p_i=k_i p_(i+1) and d_i=d_(i+1)+2h_i imply (6.4) term by term.
This uses the affine update only at the actual present point, where its guard
holds. It NEVER assumes the hypothetical future remains in this chart.

Define the signed correction at the actual next point by

  Delta_i=L_(i+1)^(f_(i+1))(x_(i+1))
                -L_(i+1)^(f_i)(x_(i+1)), 0<=i<n.     (6.5)

Substitution into (6.4) and telescoping gives the exact all-word identity

  Psi(x_n)=L_0^(f_0)(x_0)+sum_(i=0)^(n-1) Delta_i.    (6.6)

Indeed L_n^(f_n)(x_n)=Psi(x_n) by p_n=d_n=1 and (6.1).
Thus terminal chart changes after the last actual leaf are included.
Both child trajectories are those produced by the SAME x_0 on the SAME F.
No independent choice of a child output enters (6.6).

An explicit correction formula, useful for every ordered face pair, is

  Delta_i=p_(i+1)^2 <x',(P_new-P_old)x'>
          +2<b_new-b_old,x'>
          -d_(i+1)(||b_new||^2-||b_old||^2),          (6.7)

where x'=x_(i+1), old=f_i and new=f_(i+1). Combining the gap table with the
chart definitions makes every term in (6.7) explicit, with no unstated
support or order selection. The values ||b_f||^2 are0,1/4,1 for core,edge,
vertex respectively. In particular edge/vertex/core transitions carry the
remaining-mass term in (6.7); dropping it would not be this identity.

## 7. Exact initial reserve and the unresolved uniform inequality

Write D=D_m and p=p_0 for this section only. Completing the square in (6.2)
gives for the actual initial chart f=f_0

  R_f(x_0):=||x_0||^2-D L_0^f(x_0)
     =||(I-P_f)x_0-D b_f||^2
                +(1-D p^2)||P_f x_0||^2.             (7.1)

Thus the reserves are

  vertex: (s-D)^2+t^2;
  edge:   (z-D/2)^2+(1-Dp^2)y^2;
  core:   (1-Dp^2)||x_0||^2.                          (7.2)

The coefficient1-Dp^2 is positive for this original word. Here is a direct
same-parameter derivation. For an integer ell>=1 let nu_2(ell) be its
2-adic valuation and sbit(ell) the count of ones in its binary expansion.
The original recursive concatenation implies

  h_(ell-1)-1=beta*c^(nu_2(ell)-1).

The left child, bridge ell=2^j, and right child with
nu_2(2^j+r)=nu_2(r),0<r<2^j, prove this by induction. Counting the multiples
of each power of2 yields
sum_(r=1)^ell nu_2(r)=ell-sbit(ell); expanding ell into binary proves the
last equality by summing(2^j-1) for each nonzero bit. Consequently

  p=beta^n c^(-m).

For the mass let A_j=1+sum H_j and eta=a/(c-2). The literal word gives
A_0=1,A_(j+1)=2A_j+a c^j, and direct substitution gives
A_j=(1-eta)2^j+eta c^j. Since c>lambda>2, lambda(lambda-2)=1, and beta<1,
0<eta=beta/[c(c-2)]<1. Hence A_m<c^m and

  D=2A_m-1<2c^m,
  Dp^2<2beta^(2n)c^(-m)<1,

using c^m>2. This derives positivity in (7.1) without importing an unverified
fixed-face comparison. It does not assert that the actual run stays in a face.

Combining (6.6) and (7.1) now gives

  D Psi(x_n)-||x_0||^2
       =D sum_i Delta_i-R_(f_0)(x_0).                 (7.3)

In particular the EXACT remaining comparison for the baseline is

  sum_i Delta_i <= R_(f_0)(x_0)/D
                    for every original x_0.          (7.4)

For the requested relaxed bound17/(16D), its exact replacement is

  sum_i Delta_i <=[R_(f_0)(x_0)+||x_0||^2/16]/D
                    for every original x_0.          (7.5)

Equations (7.4)--(7.5) are UNPROVED, not named as small technical lemmas.
They are equivalent to the respective full triangle comparisons through the
proved candidate identity. The identity organizes the still-missing estimate;
it does not lower its mathematical burden or establish a quantitative bound.

## 8. Local switch-payment attempts and strict legal obstructions

First direct attempt: require each Delta_i<=0. This would prove (7.4) because
(7.1) is nonnegative. For a vertex-to-vertex transition from v_j to v_l,
formula (6.7) instead reads

  Delta_i=2<x',v_l-v_j>.                              (8.1)

It is positive at a strict new-vertex point whose supporting vertex changed.
Unlike an edge correction, this increment has no suffix-product multiplier
and no remaining-mass cancellation: both charts have squared offset norm1.
Thus merely noting that p_i is small cannot pay (8.1).

Second direct attempt within Mechanism2: add the FULL squared transverse
coordinate with a phase-dependent nonnegative coefficient, paying a positive
switch from its reduction. The following strict legal transition refutes that
pointwise rule even for an arbitrary real coefficient.

At the same original level2 bridge let h=1+k,k=beta*c>2. Set

  u=k/2>1, d=h-u=1+k/2>2, v=d/2>1.                  (8.2)

The current projection is strictly vertex v_1. Its labeled next adjacent
raw differences are(-d,v); hence the actual next order is231 and the next
ordered gaps are(v,d-v)=(d/2,d/2). Their upper gap is greater than1, so the
next projection is strictly vertex v_2. No boundary label choice is involved.
The old-vertex longitudinal coordinate after the step and the new one are

  <x',v_1>=v/2-d=-3d/4,
  <x',v_2>=(v+d)/2=3d/4.

For every remaining suffix mass, the two vertex forecasts differ by

  L_(i+1)^(V_2)(x')-L_(i+1)^(V_1)(x')=3d>0.          (8.3)

Meanwhile the squared transverse coordinates at the same physical point,
relative to the old and new vertex axes, are BOTH

  ||x'||^2-<x',v_1>^2
    =||x'||^2-<x',v_2>^2=3d^2/16.                   (8.4)

The transverse square before the actual vertex step is also3v^2/4=3d^2/16,
because a vertex update preserves that component. The actual post-step norm
is3d^2/4. Thus no omitted norm or cross term could turn (8.4) into a release.
No coefficient times the difference of these transverse squares pays (8.3).

This is an actual original-prefix test, not an arbitrary unreachable bridge
input. Set x_0=x+S_2 v_1 with x specified by (8.2). Every earlier query has
upper gap at least u>1 and gradient v_1. The complete H_2 prefix reaches x
exactly. The real squared initial radius is
(u+S_2)^2+(u+S_2)v+v^2. All later original updates and the terminal remain
those of the fixed F; no favorable suffix is substituted.

Even face-specific local constants cannot repair every such transition.
Fix this physical time and its actual remaining suffix. Suppose vertex charts
are corrected by arbitrary real constants C_j and transverse coefficients
Theta_j, so that their switch comparison uses

  L^(V_j)(x')+Theta_j[||x'||^2-<x',v_j>^2]+C_j.        (8.5)

The constants and coefficients may depend on m and this time but not on the
initial point. Rotate the legal construction (8.2) successively by120 degrees.
These are THREE SEPARATE original starts at the SAME physical time, giving
transitions1->2,2->3,3->1, each with forecast gain3d and the same transverse
square T=3d^2/16. Nonpositive corrected increments would require

  3d+(Theta_2-Theta_1)T+C_2-C_1<=0,
  3d+(Theta_3-Theta_2)T+C_3-C_2<=0,
  3d+(Theta_1-Theta_3)T+C_1-C_3<=0.

Adding yields9d<=0, contradicting d>0. No actual three-switch cyclic orbit
is asserted or needed: a universal pointwise invariant must hold at all three
separate legal inputs. The argument excludes precisely the local correction
class (8.5), not every direction-dependent or history-dependent potential.

A viable proof of (7.4) or (7.5) must therefore use the accumulated initial
reserve and signed cancellations across the actual child trajectories, or
retain further longitudinal/history information. The transition (8.2), at
a strict V_1->V_2 face change with no transverse release, is a specific
necessary test. The region-image transition (5.4) is another. The current
report supplies neither that global cancellation bound nor a violating
full-word start, so the actual triangle comparison remains OPEN.

## 9. Retrieval, audit limits, and bounded handoff

Skills applied: query-memory, search-math-results, direct-proving, and
construct-counterexamples. Search stage was mature_subproblem; mode repair;
program the full-state triangle hierarchy; missing mechanism a whole-word
bound retaining actual face changes and Euclidean energy.

Matlas's first query returned2006.08426 Theorem9,2009.10540 Proposition4.2,
and1006.4719 Theorem2.1. None gives a discrete long-step triangle image
estimate. The exact already-local paper by Weiran Wang and Miguel A.
Carreira-Perpinan, Projection onto the probability simplex: An efficient
algorithm with a simple proof, and an application, arXiv:1309.1541 (2013),
was read again through the public PDF extraction and its KKT proof. Its
active-coordinate threshold concerns a single Euclidean simplex projection;
it supplies no order-preservation assertion for an overrelaxed GD step.
All needed projection and ordering statements are derived here directly.

The second query returned2304.00803 Theorem5,1907.06246 Lemma5.5, and
2103.10078 DefinitionII.2. The policy-value difference identity in1907.06246
is an analogy for telescoping, not a theorem about this nonlinear switching
process. No external proof is used. The suffix ledger reduces to the explicit
finite algebra (6.4)--(6.6), so no additional theory or external black box is
needed to establish it. No broader switching-system branch is rejected.

The author checks include all face/order boundaries, both signed gaps in
(4.5), the threshold R>=3/2 in attainment of (5.3), actual reachability of
both counterexamples, strict faces in (8.2), identical same-point transverse
squares, and the distinction between three legal inputs and a single orbit.
This is an AUTHOR check only, not verifier acceptance or independent audit.
The exact missing inequalities (7.4)--(7.5) are openly retained.

Some initial broad recall outputs were truncated; the actual current guidance,

# Consolidated notation recommendation; frozen source unchanged

Source: bfff4aa387a9d818, 53449 bytes,
SHA256 bb0fb6c31e6f2711493fec2558266e896b5c0c42a8f70c76e83c4bcf7d767ca2.

The independent analytical audit found no substantive repair needed
for the assigned L=1 two-family theorem. This file records the sole
editorial clarification and does not propose a changed theorem or
authorize another review/submission round.

In Section1, E is displayed with denominator dist(x_0,argmin f)^2 and
is explicitly introduced at advertised smoothness1. The next scaling
sentence says that multiplying f by L and using h_t/L preserves E.
Section12 repeats the scaling claim. Taken literally with the displayed
L-free definition, the numerator and hence E are multiplied by L.

Make the intended normalized extension explicit:

    E_L(f,h)=2(f(x_n)-min f)/(L*dist(x_0,argmin f)^2),
    x_(t+1)=x_t-(h_t/L)*grad f(x_t).

Then use the sentence:

    For any L>0, replacing the constructed function f by L*f and
    using the physical update h_t/L preserves every point and gives
    E_L(L*f,h)=E_1(f,h).

With the original L=1 iteration notation instead, the literal identity
is E_1(L*f,h/L)=L*E_1(f,h), where the formula for E_1 is viewed just as
a raw gap/distance ratio. For example the candidate's own main k=0
Huber member has E_1(f,h)=1/(1+2*S_m); doubling the function and halving
the physical updates preserves the points and doubles that raw ratio.
Division by the advertised L restores the invariant normalized payoff.

This clarification is outside the fixed-L=1 comparisons. It requires
no change to the parameters, eligibility thresholds, static functions,
closest radii, finite constants, or family optima. No source file was
edited and no repaired candidate was subjected to another audit.

# Arbitrary checkpoint pairs: a proved draft region and the remaining flow inequality

JOINT UNVERIFIED. Worker max4, invocation79, project
gd-exact-profile-20260926. All new mathematics below is one proof attempt;
none has been submitted to the verifier or entered the fact graph.

The all-pair assertion is NOT established. This report gives a complete
candidate certificate for every pair whose second checkpoint is outside
the first checkpoint's complete dyadic subtree. It then derives an exact
scalar residual for a larger paid triangular/bidirectional support on
the remaining ancestor--descendant pairs. A determinant factor in that
residual is strictly positive throughout the hierarchy. Compatibility
with nonnegative original row multipliers remains unproved. No legal
instance exceeding the requested bounds was found; an explicit legal,
non-refuting instance at an uncovered pair is supplied below. A failed
certificate choice is nowhere called a primal counterexample.

The two mechanisms are (1) the paid backward cycle with a chronological
dyadic classification, including its forward-chain boundary case; and
(2) general triangular flow with paid backward cycles, its complete
contact equations, and its exact residual inequality. The finite endpoint
lift, masses, and example are components of these mechanisms.

This invocation follows TASK.md and master_guidance ad191c13674b2437,
plan8024e3d32a53d8b7. Mathematical/numerical programs, arithmetic or
trajectory/recurrence replays, solvers, benchmark DP, formal calls,
fact_submit, subagents, installations, and paid consults: all ZERO.
There is no automatic renewal or project-completion action.

## 1. Scope and accepted premises

Fix ANY integer m>=1536. Set

    lambda=1+sqrt(2), epsilon_h=1/(4m), beta_h=1-epsilon_h,
    c=lambda*(1+epsilon_h), a=beta_h/c,
    H_0=(), H_(l+1)=(H_l,1+a*c^l,H_l),
    S_l=sum H_l, X_l=1+S_l, n=2^m-1, Z=D_m=1+2S_m.

These hierarchy symbols are report-local aliases of the hierarchy in
accepted931b9653ce0f7201; H_l here is a word, not the conjugate quadratic
called H_j in accepted1252b8823bb5a290. Every child retains the SAME
enclosing m, a, c, beta_h, and epsilon_h. The actual final step is
d0=1+a. All indices are zero based.

For every pair 0<=j<k<=n-2 consider ONE static globally convex
differentiable function f with 1-Lipschitz gradient and an attained
minimum. Its actual queried gradients must have exactly the form

    g_i=p for 0<=i<=j,
    g_i=q for j+1<=i<=k,
    g_i=r for k+1<=i<n.                                      (1.1)

There is no restriction on the real vectors p,q,r, their lengths,
angles, dimension, sampled heights, affine offsets, or x_0 orientation.
Family E also requires g_n=r. Family Q permits every legal terminal
jet and every static completion of the entire queried prefix. For
each function and ANY of its own minimizers x_star!=x_0 put

    V=2(f(x_n)-min f)/||x_0-x_star||^2.

In particular the bound, when proved, applies to the function's OWN
closest minimizer. For L>0, translate x_star to0 and replace f by
(f-min f)/L. Then this is exactly 2(f(x_n)-min f)/(L||x_0-x_star||^2),
the original W=2R normalization, with unchanged physical steps.

The complete candidate conclusion established in Sections2--8 is,
for every pair with k outside the complete subtree rooted at j,

    sup_E V=1/Z,
    1/Z <= sup_Q V <=1/(Z-d0^2),
    B_n sup_E V<47/50,
    B_n sup_Q V<940/999<19/20.                              (1.2)

It is a restricted-family conclusion only. The original every-n,
every-nonnegative-word minimax problem, including zero and arbitrarily
large steps, is unchanged and OPEN. No restriction (1.1) has been
shown lossless for W. In particular the conditional full-W hypothesis
W_n(H_m)<=17/(16D_m) in accepted931b9653ce0f7201 is still unproved.

Correctness premises imported from the fact graph, at their scopes:

* 1252b8823bb5a290: all ordered smooth-convex interpolation rows,
  necessity and sufficiency, and its explicit conjugate interpolant.
* b98bb807d514e80d: the definition and lower-witness scope of the
  COMPLETE Huber/single-cut maximum K_n, and original benchmark notation.
* 931b9653ce0f7201: B_(2^m-1)/D_m<47/50 at precisely the hierarchy
  above, and only the unconditional part of that accepted statement.

Their relevant actual statements/proofs were read. The complete shared
reports37987ddad8be4c0b and6b8cd30a96a9165b were recalled as UNVERIFIED
awareness. None of their new mathematical claims is an accepted premise.
All new formulas used below are jointly rederived here.

## 2. Physical classification before any certificate choice

For index i let l_i=nu_2(i+1), the largest nonnegative integer l with
2^l dividing i+1. Literal concatenation shows

    h_i=1+a*c^(l_i),
    I_i=[i-(2^(l_i)-1), i+(2^(l_i)-1)].                    (2.1)

The interval I_i is the complete physical copy of H_(l_i+1) rooted
at i; the two children are copies of H_(l_i). These assertions follow
by induction through (H_l,1+a*c^l,H_l): the middle position has
i+1=2^l, and translating a child by2^l preserves valuations below l.

To classify a pair, descend from the whole root while both indices
are in the same child. At the least common ancestor one of exactly
three things happens: j is that root and k is in its right child;
k is that root and j is in its left child; or j and k lie in its
different children. An ancestor with both points in the same child
is not the least common ancestor. This covers adjacent positions,
shallow common subtrees, and arbitrarily separated checkpoint levels.

At that least common subtree write E_pre and E_post for the sums of
ALL physical steps before and after the subtree. If P_loc,M_loc,T_loc
are the sums before j, between j and k, and after k inside it, the
actual full masses are

    P=E_pre+P_loc, M=M_loc, T=T_loc+E_post,
    b=h_j, c_step=h_k, d=b-1, e=c_step-1,
    u=P+1, v=M+1, z=T+1,
    A=u+d=P+b, B=v+e=M+c_step, U=u+v+d.                  (2.2)

No outer mass is discarded or presumed monotone. All later formulas
use these exact u,v,z. In particular U is 1 plus the entire mass
before the second checkpoint.

Because j<k, k belongs to I_j if and only if it is in j's right child.
Call this the remaining ancestor case. Every other pair has k>I_j's
right endpoint and is covered by (1.2). This includes all pairs in
different children of their least common ancestor and all pairs with
k as their least common ancestor. Section7 treats the exceptional
consecutive roots on the leftmost branch without deleting their tail.

## 3. Exact endpoint system and one global completion

Normalize the chosen optimum jet to (0,0,0). Let F_p,F_q,F_r be the
values at the first query of the three sectors, and set

    Z_p=x_0, Z_q=x_0-Ap, Z_r=x_0-Ap-Bq.

At every ACTUAL cumulative mass s inside its sector the jets are

    (Z_p-sp,p,F_p-s||p||^2), 0<=s<=P,
    (Z_q-sq,q,F_q-s||q||^2), 0<=s<=M,
    (Z_r-sr,r,F_r-s||r||^2).                            (3.1)

The last range is 0<=s<=T for E and 0<=s<=T-d0 for the queried part
of Q. The separate terminal position is x_n=Z_r-Tr. Equal gradients
force the displayed values by the two convexity inequalities between
any two samples. Thus these formulas pay the full physical decrease
inside each constant-gradient sector.

Retain both actual endpoints of each range, the optimum, and the
separate terminal jet for Q, identifying coincident index labels.
For ALL ordered distinct retained samples impose

    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2 <=0.       (3.2)

There is no omitted remote or optimum condition. For source sector a
at mass s and target sector b at mass t, substitution gives

    Q_(a(s),b(t))=F_a-F_b+<g_a,Z_b-Z_a>
       +||g_a-g_b||^2/2+t(||g_b||^2-<g_a,g_b>).          (3.3)

The source mass cancels and target dependence is affine. Rows with
an optimum or a separate terminal are also affine in the sector mass.
Consequently every original query row is an endpoint convex
combination; same-sector rows are zero. Endpoints are actual samples,
so necessity holds as well. Singleton sectors, including k=n-2 with
the sole r query n-1, are included.

For each retained jet put y_i=x_i-g_i and delta_i=f_i-||g_i||^2/2.
The explicit function supplied by accepted1252b8823bb5a290 is

    H(vect)=||vect||^2/2+max_i{<y_i,vect>-delta_i},
    F(x)=sup_vect{<x,vect>-H(vect)}.                    (3.4)

It is everywhere finite, convex, differentiable and globally1-smooth,
and interpolates all retained jets. Interior anchors are affine
combinations of the two endpoint anchors because
y(s)=Z_a-(s+1)g_a and delta(s)=F_a-(s+1/2)||g_a||^2.
Adding every original interior anchor changes neither H nor F.
Induction through every physical update proves that the supplied
points are the actual GD transcript of this ONE static function.

Optimum rows give delta_i>=0, with the zero optimum anchor present.
Hence H(0)=0, min F=0, and its complete attained minimum set is

    argmin F=conv{y_i:delta_i=0}.                       (3.5)

Indeed grad F(x)=0 precisely when x is a subgradient of H at0.
The active affine slopes at0 are exactly those displayed in (3.5).
Their convex hull gives supporting slopes; separation of a point
outside that hull gives the reverse inclusion. This is the entire
minimum set, not a selected subset. A different completion may have
a different minimum set. The upper proof starts at each actual
minimizer of THAT completion, so it never inherits another function's
radius. Finite-dimensional closed convex minimum sets have closest
points, and (3.5) is compact for the explicit completion.

## 4. Mechanism1: original rows, backward cycle, and full identity

Let L_p=j, L_q=k, R_0=k+1. Directly expanding (3.2) gives

    Q_(L_p,star)=F_p-<p,x_0>+||p||^2/2,
    Q_(L_q,star)=F_q-<q,x_0>+A<p,q>+||q||^2/2,
    Q_(R_0,star)=F_r-<r,x_0>+A<p,r>+B<q,r>+||r||^2/2,
    Q_(L_q,L_p)=F_q-F_p+(P+1/2)||p||^2+||q||^2/2+d<p,q>,
    Q_(R_0,L_q)=F_r-F_q+(M+1/2)||q||^2+||r||^2/2+e<q,r>,
    Q_(L_p,L_q)=F_p-F_q+(1/2-A)||p||^2
                          +(M+1/2)||q||^2-v<p,q>.

The exact paid cycle is

    C_pq=Q_(L_p,L_q)+Q_(L_q,L_p)
         =-d||p||^2+(d-v)<p,q>+v||q||^2.               (4.1)

Its negative p-square is retained. Choose alpha,beta,gamma,eta with
alpha+beta+gamma=1, theta=alpha+beta, and use the rows

| row | multiplier mu |
|---|---:|
| Q_(L_p,star) | alpha |
| Q_(L_q,star) | beta |
| Q_(R_0,star) | gamma |
| Q_(L_q,L_p) | alpha+eta |
| Q_(L_p,L_q) | eta |
| Q_(R_0,L_q) | theta |

All other core multipliers are0. At L_p and L_q incoming and outgoing
flows agree; R_0 emits1 and the optimum absorbs1. The cycle changes
no value flow. With t_pay a terminal charge and w=t_pay+1/2, the
weighted core rows plus t_pay||r||^2 equal

    F_r-<gbar,x_0>+Q, gbar=alpha*p+beta*q+gamma*r,
    Q=(alpha*u-eta*d)||p||^2+(theta+eta)*v||q||^2+w||r||^2
       +[alpha*d+beta*A+eta*(d-v)]<p,q>
       +gamma*A<p,r>+[theta*e+gamma*B]<q,r>.            (4.2)

Put D=U+e+w. The common-gradient coefficient is D. The p row sum
is [alpha*u+A-eta*(d+v)]/2, and the cumulative p,q row sum is
[(1+theta)U+e]/2. Thus the exact contact equations are

    theta=(U+e)/(2D-U),
    alpha=[A-eta*(d+v)]/(2D-u).                       (4.3)

Writing s=p-q and tvec=q-r, subtraction of D||gbar||^2 gives the
ENTIRE increment quadratic J11||s||^2+2J12<s,tvec>+J22||tvec||^2,
where

    J11=alpha*u-eta*d-D*alpha^2,
    J22=theta*(U-D*theta),
    J12=(1-theta)*(2D*alpha-A)/2.                     (4.4)

For the mixed term, its coefficient before subtracting the square is
alpha*u+theta*A-eta*(d+v); substitution of (4.3) yields exactly2J12.
This checks the full matrix, including mixed directions.

For E use t_pay=T and add Q_(n,R_0) with multiplier1. For Q let
N_-=n-1, g_n=s_n, use t_pay=T-d0^2/2, and add Q_(N_-,R_0) and
Q_(n,N_-) with multiplier1 each. Omit the first if N_-=R_0.
The respective terminal expressions are

    f_n-F_r+T||r||^2,
    f_n-F_r+(T-d0^2/2)||r||^2+||s_n+(d0-1)r||^2/2.     (4.5)

The latter follows from x_n=x_(N_-)-d0*r and
Q_(n,N_-)=f_n-f_(N_-)+d0<s_n,r>+||s_n-r||^2/2.
No leaf is removed and the free terminal vector is unrestricted.
Set lambda_ij=2mu_ij including the terminal path. The full identity is

    ||x_0||^2/(2D)-2f_n+sum lambda_ij Q_ij
     =2D||gbar-x_0/(2D)||^2
       +2[J11||s||^2+2J12<s,tvec>+J22||tvec||^2]
       +terminal_square,                             (4.6)

where terminal_square is0 for E and ||s_n+(d0-1)r||^2 for Q.
Every original finite node has zero flow except n, which emits2;
the optimum absorbs2. All sampled values and heights cancel by these
explicit original rows. Formula (4.6) is on ALL real (x_0,p,q,r,s_n),
with s_n omitted for E, and therefore on the entire equal-sector
subspace of the original formal gradients. It is NOT claimed PSD
when gradients inside a sector become independent.

Whenever the stated multipliers are nonnegative, D>0 and J is PSD,
(4.6) and Q_ij<=0 imply 2f_n<=||x_0||^2/(2D).

## 5. Uniform hierarchy and last-step estimates

Literal summation gives

    X_(l+1)=2X_l+a*c^l, X_0=1,
    X_l=v_h*c^l+(1-v_h)*2^l, v_h=a/(c-2).             (5.1)

The closed form has the stated initial value and satisfies the
recurrence because v_h(c-2)=a. This is a symbolic identity, not an
executed recurrence. Squaring positive rationals gives
7/5<sqrt(2)<99/70. Therefore

    12/5<c<(169/70)(6145/6144)<29/12,
    2/5<a<c-2<5/12, 0<v_h<1,
    2^l<=X_l<=c^l,
    2/5<a<=a*c^l/X_l<c-2<5/12.                       (5.2)

For the upper c comparison, cross multiplication reduces to
169*12<2*6144. For a>2/5 use beta_h>99/100 and c<29/12.
For a<c-2 use c(c-2)>lambda(lambda-2)=1>beta_h. The last mass ratio
follows by dividing (5.1) by c^l: its denominator lies in (v_h,1].

For both terminal regimes define

    epsilon_E=1/2, epsilon_Q=(1+d0^2)/2,
    epsilon=epsilon_E or epsilon_Q,
    w=T+1-epsilon=z-epsilon,
    D=X_m-epsilon,
    2D=Z for E, 2D=Z-d0^2 for Q.                     (5.3)

Here epsilon is the terminal charge, distinct from epsilon_h.
Since 1<d0<17/12<3/2, 0<epsilon<13/8. Also k<=n-2 implies T>=d0,
so

    w>=1-a^2/2>9/10.                                (5.4)

If the second checkpoint has level l and X=X_l, both of its complete
children occur in the full prefix and suffix. Consequently

    U>=X, z>=X, e<(5/12)X,
    e/w<(5/12)(1+epsilon/w)<505/432<6/5.             (5.5)

The last bound uses epsilon/w<65/36. We will need the STRICT inequality

                      U*w>e*(e+w).                   (5.6)

For l>=2, X>=4 and w>=X-epsilon>(19/32)X. Thus
(U-e)w>(7/12)(19/32)X^2>25X^2/144>e^2.
For l=1, X=2+a>12/5, e=a*c=beta_h<1 and (5.4) give
(U-e)w>(7/5)(9/10)=63/50>1>e^2.
For l=0, U>=1, e=a<5/12 and (5.4) give
(U-e)w>(7/12)(9/10)>25/144>e^2.
These cases prove (5.6), including the final admissible checkpoint
k=n-2 and singleton last queried sector. In particular theta in (4.3)
satisfies 0<theta<1 and

    J22=(U+e)[U*w-e*(e+w)]/(2D-U)^2>0.               (5.7)

## 6. Every non-ancestor pair except consecutive leftmost roots

Let l=l_j, X=X_l, d=a*c^l. If k is outside I_j, all of j's right
child is between j and k. Hence u>=X and v>=X.

If j is not the first occurrence of level l, its index is at least
3*2^l-1. Before that occurrence there is a complete H_(l+1), the
next bridge of excess c*d, and an H_l. Their total gives

    u>=3X+(1+c)d>3X.                                (6.1)

All further prefixes only add actual positive steps. In that case

    d*(u+v+d)/(u*v)
      <(5/12)[1+1/3+(5/12)/3]=265/432<1.             (6.2)

If j is the first occurrence, j=2^l-1 and u=X. The immediately
following ancestor is k0=2^(l+1)-1. At k=k0 one has v=X.
If k>k0, the middle includes j's right child and the actual parent
step1+c*d. Thus v>=X+1+c*d>(7/5)X, and

    d*(u+v+d)/(u*v)
      <(5/12)[1+(1+5/12)*(5/7)]=845/1008<1.           (6.3)

There is no index between I_j's right endpoint and k0. Therefore
these cases exhaust k outside I_j except

    j=2^l-1, k=2^(l+1)-1, 0<=l<=m-2.                (6.4)

For every pair covered by (6.2) or (6.3), set

    alpha=A/(2D), eta=alpha*u/(d+v),
    theta=(U+e)/(2D-U), beta=theta-alpha, gamma=1-theta.

The contact equations hold because eta(d+v)=alpha*u and A=2D*alpha.
Every multiplier is strictly positive except possible unused rows:
eta>0, alpha>0, gamma>0, and theta>alpha since U+e>A and 2D-U<2D.
Direct substitution gives

    J12=0,
    J11=alpha*[u*v-d*(u+v+d)]/[2(d+v)]>0.             (6.5)

For (6.5), expand 2uv-A(d+v)=uv-d(u+v+d). Equations (5.7),(6.5)
prove full positivity of the increment matrix with both charges.
Together with (4.6) this proves (1.2)'s upper bounds on this entire
physical region, with all free terminal vectors retained.

## 7. Consecutive leftmost roots, including shallow subtrees

For (6.4) put X=X_l, t=d/X, E=e/X=c*t, Ubar=U/X=2+t,
y=D/X. The actual outer prefix is0; the entire outer suffix remains
in D. The following uniform estimates hold:

    2/5<t<5/12, 12/5<c<29/12, y>=23/4,
    A/X=1+t<3/2,
    Cbar=Ubar+E>10/3, Ubar-E>4/3, Ubar^2<6,
    23/4<2Ubar+E<6.                                 (7.1)

For y, if l<=m-3, repeated X_(i+1)>2X_i gives X_m>=8X, hence
y>8-13/(8X)>=51/8>23/4. If l=m-2, then
X_m/X=4+(2+c)t, and X>=2^(m-2)>=2048, so
y>4+(22/5)(2/5)-1/1000>23/4.
The other bounds follow by substitution: Cbar>84/25>10/3;
Ubar-E=2-(c-1)t>203/144>4/3;
Ubar<29/12 so Ubar^2<6; and
144/25<2Ubar+E<841/144<6. All are strict except the convenient weak
lower y bound.

Use eta=0, alpha=A/(2D-u), theta=(U+e)/(2D-U), beta=theta-alpha,
gamma=1-theta. All row weights are nonnegative: both denominators
are positive, 0<alpha<theta since A<U+e and 2D-u>2D-U, and
theta<1 by w>0,e>0. In units of X the exact matrix entries are

    J11/X=(1+t)[(1-t)y-1]/(2y-1)^2,
    J22/X=Cbar[(Ubar-E)y-Ubar^2]/(2y-Ubar)^2,
    J12/X=(1+t)(2y-2Ubar-E)/[2(2y-1)(2y-Ubar)].         (7.2)

The two diagonals are positive by (7.1). After clearing the positive
denominators, det J>0 follows from positivity of

    Cbar[(1-t)y-1][(Ubar-E)y-Ubar^2]
                     -(1+t)(2y-2Ubar-E)^2/4.         (7.3)

Both factors in the first product are positive; also
0<2y-2Ubar-E<2y-23/4. Thus (7.3) is strictly greater than

    (10/3)[(7/12)y-1][(4/3)y-6]-(3/8)(2y-23/4)^2.

Writing x=y-23/4>=0, this last polynomial is EXACTLY

    (59/54)x^2+(1097/216)x+2351/3456>0.               (7.4)

Every coefficient is positive. This supplies a symbolic full-PSD
proof for every such actual pair, including arbitrarily shallow
least-common subtrees inside the huge enclosing word. No monotonicity
of the endpoint problem under suffix addition was assumed; (7.4)
proves the needed inequality at the actual D.
The complete increment square is
J11||s+(J12/J11)tvec||^2+(J22-J12^2/J11)||tvec||^2.

Sections6 and7 exhaust ALL k outside I_j. In particular every
whole-root-second pair is included, but that old draft was not used
as a premise.

## 8. Sharp member, complete K, and quantitative benchmark comparison

Put kappa=1/Z and define the one scalar Huber function

    f_H(x)=x^2/2 if |x|<=kappa,
    f_H(x)=kappa*|x|-kappa^2/2 if |x|>=kappa,
    x_0=1.

Its derivative is continuous, nondecreasing and1-Lipschitz; its
unique minimizer is0. At every actual cumulative physical mass s<=S_m,
x=1-kappa*s>kappa, the gradient is kappa, and the value is
kappa*(1-kappa*s)-kappa^2/2. All physical updates are therefore
executed, with p=q=r=g_n=kappa, closest radius1, and

    2f_H(x_n)=2/Z-Z/Z^2=1/Z.                          (8.1)

All original ordered rows hold because these are jets of this one
static function; same-gradient rows are identically zero. This proves
the matching fixed-terminal lower bound for every pair.

For completeness, the COMPLETE accepted channel is

    K_n(h)=max{1/Z, max_i d_i^2/[u_i^2*(1+2T_i)]},
    u_i=1+sum_(t<i)h_t, T_i=sum_(t>i)h_t, d_i=h_i-1.

If X=X_(l_i), its physical children give u_i>=X,T_i>=X-1 and
0<d_i<(5/12)X. Consequently

    (u_i-d_i)(1+2T_i)>(7/12)X(2X-1)
                         >2*(25/144)X^2>2d_i^2.

Here 2X-1>=X and7/12>25/72. Since S_m=u_i+d_i+T_i,

    u_i^2(1+2T_i)-d_i^2 Z
      =(u_i+d_i)[(u_i-d_i)(1+2T_i)-2d_i^2]>0.

Every single-cut term is below1/Z, so K_n(H_m)=1/Z with EVERY cut
included. This does not identify unrestricted W with K.

The benchmark retains every original integer split:
C_0=B_0=1, Omega(x,y)=(x+y+sqrt((x+y)^2+4xy))/2,
G(x,y)=2x+y/2+sqrt(y^2+8xy)/2,
C_n=max_(0<=i<n)Omega(C_i,C_(n-i-1)),
B_n=max_(0<=i<n)G(C_i,B_(n-i-1)), U_n^ZJ=1/B_n.
Accepted931b9653ce0f7201 gives B_n/Z<47/50. Also
Z=2X_m-1>=2^(m+1)-1>1000*(9/4)>1000*d0^2.
Thus (1.2) follows, including
B_n/(Z-d0^2)<(47/50)(1000/999)=940/999<19/20.

## 9. Mechanism2: fully paid triangular and bidirectional geometry

The remaining case has j an ancestor of k. We now derive a broader
support for ANY pair without asserting its feasibility. Start with
nonnegative alpha,beta,gamma,ell satisfying
alpha+beta+gamma=1, 0<=ell<=alpha. Add the pq cycle with weight eta>=0.
The core rows are

| row | multiplier mu |
|---|---:|
| Q_(L_p,star) | alpha |
| Q_(L_q,star) | beta |
| Q_(R_0,star) | gamma |
| Q_(L_q,L_p) | ell+eta |
| Q_(L_p,L_q) | eta |
| Q_(R_0,L_q) | beta+ell |
| Q_(R_0,L_p) | alpha-ell |

The remote row is the ACTUAL expression

    Q_(R_0,L_p)=F_r-F_p+(P+1/2)||p||^2+||r||^2/2
                                      +d<p,r>+B<q,r>.

For a second paid cycle use the last available r endpoint R_1:
R_1=n for E and R_1=n-1 for Q. Its sector mass is t_r=T or T-d0,
and put z_q=t_r+1. Add both Q_(L_q,R_1) and Q_(R_1,L_q) with
weight xi>=0. Their EXACT sum is

    C_qr=-e||q||^2+(e-z_q)<q,r>+z_q||r||^2.           (9.1)

It follows either by substituting the two rows or by the same-sector
value equalities with the actual target mass t_r. For Q this uses
the queried endpoint, never an assumed terminal equality.

The full gradient quadratic after the same terminal charge is

    Q=(alpha*u-eta*d)||p||^2
      +[(beta+ell+eta)*v-xi*e]||q||^2+(w+xi*z_q)||r||^2
      +[ell*d+beta*A+eta*(d-v)]<p,q>
      +[(alpha-ell)*d+gamma*A]<p,r>
      +[(beta+ell)*e+(1-beta-ell)*B+xi*(e-z_q)]<q,r>.
                                                               (9.2)

Both cycles have zero value flow. The triangular rows balance at
L_p and L_q and emit1 at R_0, with the same terminal path as Section4.
Thus EVERY row multiplier and flow remains explicit, even if R_1
coincides with R_0. Retain the complete primal endpoint system of
Section3; a zero multiplier never deletes a legality condition.

Use the local abbreviations

    Lc=d+v, E_c=2D-u, K_c=2D-U, C=U+e,
    theta=alpha+beta, R=alpha-ell.

Here R is a remote-row multiplier, not the original normalization
R_n and not a radius. Taking p and cumulative p,q row sums of (9.2)
gives the PAID contact equations

    alpha*E_c=A-eta*Lc,
    theta*K_c=C-R*Lc-xi*(e+z_q).                     (9.3)

On s=p-q,tvec=q-r the entire remainder Q-D||gbar||^2 is

    J11=alpha*u-eta*d-D*alpha^2,
    J22=theta*U-R*Lc-xi*e-D*theta^2,
    J12=[(1-theta)*(2D*alpha-A)-R*d]/2.               (9.4)

For the mixed coefficient in Q, substitute ell=alpha-R and
beta=theta-alpha into 2(alpha*u-eta*d) plus the pq coefficient;
then use eta*Lc=A-alpha*E_c. The result minus2Dalpha theta is
(1-theta)(2Dalpha-A)-R d. Cycle (9.1) has no s term, so (9.4)
accounts for every increment and both backward costs. The full
initial-point and terminal-square identity is exactly (4.6) with
this J and this row table. Signs and PSD have NOT been assumed.

## 10. Exact residual for xi=0; no unspecified optimization oracle

Set xi=0 for the explicit scalar interface below. Define

    h_c=e+2w, sigma=1-theta,
    R(sigma)=(K_c*sigma-h_c)/Lc,
    alpha_max=A/E_c,
    alpha_opt(sigma)=(u*v+2D*d)/(2D*Lc)
                              -d*h_c*sigma/(2w*Lc),
    Delta=u^2*v^2*w-d^2*e^2*D.                       (10.1)

These are closed formulas in the actual masses. R(sigma)>=0 is
sigma>=sigma0=h_c/K_c. The remaining sign conditions are EXACTLY

    R(sigma)<=alpha<=min(alpha_max,1-sigma),
    0<=sigma<=1,
    eta=[A-E_c*alpha]/Lc,
    beta=1-sigma-alpha, gamma=sigma, ell=alpha-R(sigma).

For a strict J22>0 certificate take sigma<sqrt(w/D). Its admissible
scalar interval is therefore

    sigma0<=sigma<sqrt(w/D),
    sigma<=1,
    sigma<=(h_c+Lc*alpha_max)/K_c,
    sigma<=(h_c+Lc)/(K_c+Lc).                         (10.2)

The latter two constraints ensure the alpha interval is nonempty.
At sigma0 it is nonempty and J22>0 by (5.6); this fact alone does
not guarantee PSD. Define alpha_clamp(sigma) to be alpha_opt if it
lies in that interval, and otherwise the nearer endpoint of the
interval (equivalently max(lower,min(upper,alpha_opt))).

Here is the exact determinant calculation. Set t_c=2Dalpha-A and
y_c=t_c-d*K_c/Lc. Equations (9.3)--(9.4) give

    J22=w-D*sigma^2,
    J11=[u^2*v^2+4d^2D(e+w)]/(4D*Lc^2)-y_c^2/(4D),
    J12=(sigma*y_c+d*h_c/Lc)/2.                      (10.3)

For the J11 constant, use
A*Lc-d*U=u*v and
A*Lc*(u*v-d*U)+d^2*U^2=u^2*v^2.
Since K_c^2-U^2=4D(e+w), (10.3) follows by completing its square.
Expanding det J and completing the square in y_c gives the EXACT identity

    det J=(w-D*sigma^2)*Delta/(4D*w*Lc^2)
                         -D*w*(alpha-alpha_opt(sigma))^2.       (10.4)

Indeed the y_c-square coefficient is -w/(4D), its linear coefficient
is -sigma*d*h_c/(2Lc), and
4w(e+w)-h_c^2=-e^2; these yield precisely (10.4).

Consequently the following is the exact remaining inequality for
this explicitly advertised support, with all signs already encoded:

  Find sigma satisfying (10.2) such that

    D*w*[alpha_clamp(sigma)-alpha_opt(sigma)]^2
       <=(w-D*sigma^2)*Delta/(4D*w*Lc^2).              (10.5)

If (10.5) holds, the nonnegative multipliers just printed and (4.6)
give the desired bound. J22>0 and det J>=0 imply J11>=0, and the full
increment quadratic is
J22||tvec+(J12/J22)s||^2+(det J/J22)||s||^2.
This checks full PSD, not only the two diagonal entries.

For xi=0 and strict J22>0, (10.5) is necessary and sufficient for
a certificate within this row support: for fixed sigma, (10.4) is
maximized on the permitted alpha interval at alpha_clamp. It is NOT
a necessary condition for the full endpoint problem, which retains
additional original ordered rows; nor is xi>0 exhausted.

No mathematical program, SDP, numerical pair enumeration, or search
over an unspecified feasible set is invoked here. Equation (10.5)
is an explicit scalar inequality. Its uniform feasibility is the
remaining theorem-level gap in this attempted all-pair certificate.

## 11. Delta is positive on EVERY remaining ancestor pair

This section removes one possible obstruction in (10.5), but does
not prove its multiplier-clamping term small enough.

If j is an ancestor of k, let l=l_j and X=X_l, d=a*c^l. Its right
child is a complete H_l containing k. Write z_loc=1 plus the mass
after k inside that right child, and O for the FULL mass after I_j.
Then the exact identities and child bounds are

    u>=X, v+e+z_loc=X, O>=0,
    w=O+z_loc-epsilon,
    D=u+d+X+O-epsilon,
    d<(5/12)X, e<(5/12)min(v,z_loc).                 (11.1)

The left child of k is inside the middle block, and its right child
is inside z_loc, proving the last inequality. Necessarily l>=1.
In this section set tau=5/12. We prove

           d^2*e^2*D/(u^2*v^2*w)<1,                 (11.2)

which is Delta>0.

First suppose l>=4, so X>=16, and z_loc>=13/4. At fixed u,d,v,e,z_loc,
D/w=1+(u+d+v+e)/w decreases as O increases. Since
z_loc-epsilon>z_loc/2, it is enough to bound the expression at O=0.
There

    D/u^2 <=(2+tau)/X,
    e^2/[v^2*(z_loc-epsilon)]
       <2tau^2*min(v,z_loc)^2/(v^2*z_loc)
       <=2tau^2/max(v,z_loc)
       <=2tau^2*(2+tau)/X.

The first estimate uses u>=X and d<tau X. The middle estimate
splits into v<=z_loc and v>=z_loc. The last follows from
X=v+e+z_loc<=(2+tau)max(v,z_loc). Multiplying these estimates with
d^2<tau^2 X^2 bounds (11.2)'s left side by
2tau^4(2+tau)^2<2*(1/2)^4*(5/2)^2=25/32<1.
This monotonicity is proved for this explicit scalar expression only;
it asserts no monotonicity of the endpoint optimization problem.

Still with l>=4, suppose z_loc<13/4. Then e<65/48<3/2,
v=X-e-z_loc>X-5, e^2<3, and by (5.5), e^2/w<2. Since
D=w+u+d+v+e and d+v+e<(1+tau)X<3X/2,

    d^2e^2D/(u^2v^2w)
      <tau^2*X^2/(u^2v^2) * [3+2u+3X]
      <=tau^2*(3+5X)/v^2
      <(3+5X)/[4(X-5)^2]<1.

For the final inequality write X=16+x, x>=0; then
4(X-5)^2-(3+5X)=4x^2+83x+401>0.

Finally let l<=3. From (5.2), X<=c^3<(5/2)^3<16, so d<7,e<7.
The whole-word D=X_m-epsilon>2^20. If u>=D/4, then v>=1,w>9/10 give

    d^2e^2D/(u^2v^2w)<384160/(9D)<1.

If u<D/4, (11.1) gives
O=D-u-d-X+epsilon>3D/4-23>D/2,
and w>O-13/8>D/3. Also d/u<tau and e/v<tau. Hence
d^2e^2D/(u^2v^2w)<3tau^4<3/16<1.
This exhausts the shallow cases while retaining their actual huge
outer prefix or suffix. Thus Delta>0 for EVERY ancestor pair and both
terminal charges. Positivity of Delta does not remove the nonnegative
multiplier constraints in (10.2)--(10.5).

## 12. A physical uncovered configuration and a complete legal test

An explicit physical pair outside the portion (1.2) is

    j=2^(m-2)-1, k=j+1.

Here j is the root of the whole word's left child and k its immediately
following leaf. Neither checkpoint is the whole root. Put
X=X_(m-2), d=a*c^(m-2). The FULL masses, including the entire right
half of the original word, are

    u=X, v=1, e=a,
    X_m=4X+(2+c)d,
    T=3X+(1+c)d-2-a,
    D=4X+(2+c)d-epsilon.                              (12.1)

The diagonal-cycle condition fails physically:
uv-dU=X-d(X+1+d)<0, because d>(2/5)X and X>=2048.
This is a failure of that choice only. It does not refute (10.5),
the full endpoint upper bound, or the all-pair assertion.

To exhibit a LEGAL full transcript at this configuration, rather
than substitute an arbitrary indefinite Gram direction, take scalar
p=1,q=1/2,r=1/4. Use A=u+d, B=v+e from the ACTUAL masses and define

    Z_r=r*(T+1), Z_q=Z_r+B*q,
    x_0=Z_q+A*p, x_n=r,
    t_+=max(t,0),
    f(x)=x^2/2-(x-r)_+^2/2
      +(x-Z_r)_+^2/2-(x-Z_r-(q-r))_+^2/2
      +(x-Z_q)_+^2/2-(x-Z_q-(p-q))_+^2/2.            (12.2)

This is ONE static global function. Its derivative equals x for
x<=r; it is r on [r,Z_r], increases with slope1 from r to q on
[Z_r,Z_r+q-r], is q up to Z_q, increases with slope1 from q to p
on [Z_q,Z_q+p-q], and is p thereafter. The ramps do not overlap:
T>=0 and B*q>=q-r since B>=c_step>1. Thus f' is continuous,
nondecreasing and1-Lipschitz. The derivative is negative for x<0,
zero only at0, and positive for x>0. Its COMPLETE minimum set is
{0}, with minimum0 and OWN closest radius x_0>0.

Every actual query is explicitly

    x_i=x_0-p*sum_(t<i)h_t                         (i<=j),
    x_i=Z_q-q*sum_(j<t<i)h_t                     (j<i<=k),
    x_i=Z_r-r*sum_(k<t<i)h_t                     (k<i<=n).

The last p query is Z_q+b*p>=Z_q+p-q; the first q query is Z_q;
the last q query is Z_r+c_step*q>=Z_r+q-r; the first r query is Z_r;
and all r queries through the ACTUAL terminal r are on its r plateau.
Thus the derivative of (12.2) at every displayed query is precisely
(1.1), and every physical GD step is used. Set each f_i=f(x_i),
g_i=f'(x_i). ALL original ordered rows, including remote, terminal,
and optimum rows, are the explicit expressions

    f(x_i)-f(x_j)+f'(x_i)(x_j-x_i)
                      +[f'(x_i)-f'(x_j)]^2/2<=0.

They hold by the necessity proof in accepted1252b8823bb5a290 applied
to this globally convex1-smooth function, not by a Gram-only test.
Equivalently one can insert these actual values into (3.3) and the
optimum/terminal rows; the same global necessity argument proves
each sign. Heights and offsets in this construction are fixed by
(12.2), never chosen independently after a query.

The actual final value is r^2/2. Hence

    V=r^2/x_0^2,
    x_0=A*p+B*q+(T+1)*r >=(S_m+1)*r,
    V<=1/(S_m+1)^2<1/(1+2S_m)=1/Z.                   (12.3)

This is a non-refuting example escaping the PROVED INDEX REGION,
not an example escaping the proposed UPPER BOUND. It verifies that
the remaining region contains physical full transcripts with two
genuine gradient changes. No legal counterexample beating the channel
by a nonvanishing amount was obtained, and no such claim is made.

## 13. Remaining interface and honesty of the handoff

The candidate portion (1.2), the exact endpoint lift, the general
paid identity, the positive determinant factor, and the legal example
are jointly derived above, but remain UNVERIFIED. There is no new
fact_id. The all-pair extension is blocked at the explicit uniform
inequality (10.5), or at a replacement using xi>0 and (9.3)--(9.4)
or further original endpoint rows. One must prove their signs and
full PSD; dropping the clamping cost because Delta>0 is invalid.

Two direct attempts were distinguished: the diagonal pq cycle gives
the complete outside-subtree region but fails at (12.1); the general
triangle/cycle calculation removes that fixed-choice limitation but
leaves nonnegative-flow compatibility. The counterexample test gives
(12.2)--(12.3), not a refutation of the upper bound. Therefore neither
an all-pair theorem nor a physical upper-bound counterexample is
asserted at the cap.

Even a future proof of (10.5) for every pair would only establish the
three-sector family bound. The additional full-W interface consists
of actual gradients changing inside one or more of the three blocks,
or more than two gradient changes. Then (3.1), the affine endpoint
lift, and the restricted formal-space PSD identity cease to be a
lossless representation. No result here licenses deleting those
directions from the original function-class supremum.

Applied skills: query-memory, direct-proving, construct-counterexamples.
Matlas was called once for finite smooth-convex interpolation; it
returned two unrelated statements, neither used. One web background
search located Taylor--Hendrickx--Glineur, "Smooth Strongly Convex
Interpolation and Exact Worst-case Performance of First-order Methods",
arXiv:1502.05666, https://arxiv.org/abs/1502.05666. The existing own
downloaded text was read around its conjugate-interpolant construction.
No external theorem from that paper is used as a correctness premise;
the necessary interpolation result is accepted1252b8823bb5a290.

Initial broad retrieval output was truncated; the specified reports
were then saved from shared memory and read in bounded passages.
Accepted relevant scope/proof passages were read separately. No
truncated excerpt is a proof premise. Administrative reads, text
writes, integrity checks and shared-memory publication are the only
local operations beyond retrieval. The original PROBLEM.md and TASK.md
were not modified. All prior grants remain closed, and this cap1
invocation ends without renewal, formal submission, outward
publication, finalization, revocation, shutdown or Goal completion.
