# Positive correlated tails on the original ruler: exact family closure

Status: COMPLETE JOINT RESEARCH DRAFT, UNVERIFIED. No fact submission is
authorized. This is max's first round of the new two-round allocation:
completed baseline 43, proposed completed count after return 44, ceiling
45, attempted ceiling 46 including the historical interruption. Returning
this manuscript is not evidence that the outer loop has already exited.

The original all-word, all-sufficiently-large-horizon Goal remains OPEN.
The normalization throughout is W=2R: at smoothness one the payoff is
twice the final objective gap divided by the squared distance from the
initial point to the ENTIRE minimizer set.

Accepted inputs are 1252b8823bb5a290 for the original interpolation
convention, b98bb807d514e80d for K and the original benchmark,
1fca3e5dd555cb4a for the COMPLETE alternating-ray reference,
dfde2fa326c908bf for the FULL schedule-dependent complex reference,
and the UNCONDITIONAL denominator-ray assertion of 04219554e41d9750.
No conditional seed existence is assumed. No earlier unverified family
closure is a mathematical premise. All new ingredients below are proved
together, rather than promoted to facts and reused without a gate.

## 1. New state, explicit data, and result

For any physical word h=(h_0,...,h_(n-1)) with every h_i>1, set
d_i=h_i-1. Choose eta>=1, tail coefficients t_i in [0,1], and innovation
slacks sigma_i>=0. These are explicit scalar inputs, not a Gram or
function-value feasibility oracle. Use the orthonormal basis
e_0,...,e_n of R^(n+1). Starting at

    g_n=e_n,  x_n=eta e_n,  q_n=1,  delta_n=eta-1,

construct backward, for i=n-1,...,0, by

    q_i=t_i q_(i+1)+(1-t_i)(delta_(i+1)+q_(i+1))/d_i+sigma_i,
    v_i^2=q_i-t_i^2 q_(i+1),
    g_i=t_i g_(i+1)+v_i e_i,
    x_i=x_(i+1)+h_i g_i.                                      (1)

Here v_i is the NONNEGATIVE square root. The reserve delta_i will be
computed by (2) below; it is <x_i,g_i>-q_i. Thus every scalar in (1)
is obtained by a finite specified recursion before the next index is
constructed. No sample is discarded or re-indexed.

Define the one static function for this full run by

    C=conv{0,g_0,...,g_n},
    F(x)=max_(v in C) {<x,v>-||v||^2/2}.

The new dependence is an overlapping future gradient plus a fresh
orthogonal innovation at each physical step. For example,
t_i=beta_m and sigma_i=0 at every i gives a genuinely changing,
positively correlated law on the original H_m below. Its dimension
and number of listed gradient vertices grow with m. Every coordinate
that survives several steps can change amplitude at all those steps.
This is not a common added gradient or an orthogonal sum of old
triangle constructions.

This draft proves that ALL choices in (1) are legal, with exact payoff

    V=(2eta-1)/Rtail_0_sq,  Rtail_0_sq=||x_0||^2
      =dist(x_0,argmin F)^2.

Nevertheless, for the ORIGINAL enclosing-m parameters

    lambda=1+sqrt(2), beta_m=1-1/m,
    c_m=lambda(1+1/m), a_m=beta_m/c_m,
    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a_m c_m^j,H_j), 0<=j<m,
    n=2^m-1,

the entire declared family has, for every m>=32, exactly

    sup_(eta,t,sigma) V = 1/(1+2 sum H_m).                     (T)

In particular this new positive-correlation law cannot improve on
Huber, the complete same-word K, or the complete same-word ray channel.
The exact original-B normalization and full-complex comparison are in
Section 8. The limiting family/B ratio is strictly below one.

The law leaves the nonpositive-adjacent-correlation hypotheses of
C98/C100. It also permits short-step reserves larger than the C99 cap:
already the all-t=1 choice has delta_i/q_i equal to a whole remaining
mass plus eta-1. Thus the new closure is not obtained by renaming a
previously excluded negative-correlation or capped-reserve law.

## 2. Scalar identities and actual positive semidefinite geometry

Suppose the suffix has been constructed. Its points and gradients lie
in span{e_(i+1),...,e_n}. Put q=q_(i+1), delta=delta_(i+1), t=t_i,
d=d_i, h=1+d, and sigma=sigma_i. Then

    v_i^2=(1-t)[t q+(delta+q)/d]+sigma >=0.

The vectors in (1), not merely their pairwise proposed correlations,
therefore give a real positive semidefinite Gram matrix. Taking inner
products in x_i=x_(i+1)+h g_i gives

    delta_i = delta+(1+d t)q+d sigma,                         (2)
    Rtail_i_sq = Rtail_(i+1)_sq+h^2 q_i+2h t(delta+q),                     (3)

Here Rtail_i_sq=||x_i||^2; R_sq denotes a generic squared-radius state.
The fresh Rtail notation avoids the accepted ray channel's different r_i.
 These identities also prove delta_i>=0 and q_i>0
at every index. All dimensions are physical dimensions, not radii.

For i<j, orthogonality of the innovations gives

    <g_i,g_j>=(product_(k=i)^(j-1) t_k) q_j >=0.              (4)

Also q_i>=t_i q_(i+1). Repeated use of this inequality implies

    <g_i,g_j><=q_i  for every i<j.                            (5)

For the concrete all-t=beta_m pilot, 0<t_i<1 and v_i>0 at every
physical index. Its gradients are all distinct, and adjacent inner
products are strictly positive. This statement follows symbolically
from (1), without evaluating any instance.

## 3. Every original ordered row, including the remote rows

Let y_i=x_i-g_i. We prove that

    <y_i,g_j-g_i><=0 for every i,j in {0,...,n},
    <y_i,-g_i><=0.                                          (6)

The second inequality is -delta_i<=0. The adjacent forward comparison
has the exact value

    <y_i,g_(i+1)-g_i>
      =(1-t_i)(delta_(i+1)+q_(i+1))
        +d_i(t_i q_(i+1)-q_i)
      =-d_i sigma_i <=0.                                   (7)

For a remote j>i+1, use y_i=y_(i+1)+g_(i+1)+d_i g_i. The fresh
innovation is orthogonal to g_j and g_(i+1), so

    <y_i,g_j-g_(i+1)>
      =<y_(i+1),g_j-g_(i+1)>
       +(1+d_i t_i)<g_(i+1),g_j-g_(i+1)> <=0.                (8)

The first term is nonpositive by backward induction; the second is
nonpositive by (5). Adding (7) proves the remote comparison. At i=n
there is no future vertex.

For a past j<i, write

    g_j=(product_(k=j)^(i-1)t_k)g_i+w,
    w in span{e_j,...,e_(i-1)}.

Since y_i is orthogonal to w, the corresponding expression in (6) is
(product t_k-1)delta_i<=0. This also establishes all the past
comparisons at the terminal index. Thus (6) includes every ordered
pair, however remote or in different recursive children.

The maximizer defining F is P_C(x), the Euclidean projection onto C.
Indeed completing the square turns maximization into minimization of
||v-x||^2. The projection characterization (6) gives P_C(x_i)=g_i.
For completeness the two projection inequalities at arbitrary x,z
give

    <P_C(x)-P_C(z),x-z> >= ||P_C(x)-P_C(z)||^2.

Consequently P_C is 1-Lipschitz. Comparing the two maximizing affine
functions in the definition of F gives

    <P_C(x),u> <= F(x+u)-F(x) <= <P_C(x+u),u>.

The difference from the leftmost linear expression is between zero
and ||u||^2. Therefore F is differentiable, grad F=P_C, and its
gradient is 1-Lipschitz. F is finite because C is compact, and convex
because it is a supremum of affine functions of x.

The actual sampled values are

    f_i=F(x_i)=delta_i+q_i/2.                                (9)

Include x_*=g_*=f_*=0 and y_*=0. For the original convention in
1252b8823bb5a290, direct expansion gives the COMPLETE row identity

    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2
        =<y_j,g_i-g_j> <=0.                                (10)

In particular Q_(i,*)=0 and Q_(*,j)=-delta_j<=0. No optimum row,
incoming terminal row, or cross-block row was relaxed.

## 4. Minimum set, true radius, and excess innovation price

Let C^polar={z:<z,v><=0 for every v in C}. Since 0 belongs to C,
F>=0. If z is in C^polar, every maximand is at most zero, so F(z)=0.
If z is not in C^polar, choose a listed vertex g with <z,g>>0;
at a sufficiently small positive multiple of g the maximand is
positive. Thus

    argmin F=C^polar,  min F=0.

Telescoping the actual updates yields

    x_0=eta g_n+sum_(i<n)h_i g_i,

a nonnegative combination of vertices. For every z in C^polar,
<x_0,z><=0, and consequently

    ||x_0-z||^2=||x_0||^2+||z||^2-2<x_0,z>>=||x_0||^2.

Equality is attained at z=0. This proves the exact nearest-minimizer
normalization in Section 1, even though the minimum set need not be
a singleton. Equations (2),(3) price the whole dense initial anchor.
At the terminal point, (9) gives 2f_n=2eta-1. Multiplying F by any
L>0 and using the physical steps h_i/L preserves all points and this
normalized payoff.

For fixed eta,t, increasing any sigma_i can only increase Rtail_0_sq.
To see this without ignoring its influence on earlier gradients,
observe that the recurrences (1)--(3), as maps of the suffix state
(R_sq,delta,q), have nonnegative coefficients whenever 0<=t<=1 and d>0.
An increment in sigma adds respectively h^2 sigma, d sigma, sigma
to the current state (Rtail_i_sq,delta_i,q_i). These nonnegative increments
remain nonnegative at all earlier indices. Thus the best payoff in
the WHOLE declared family has sigma_i=0 for every i. This removes
arbitrary forward-adjacent normal slack by a paid monotonicity proof,
not by assuming that every optimal construction must saturate it.

Isometric embeddings do not change the distances. A finite orthogonal
sum of members of this family has a product minimizer set and payoff
equal to the radius-squared-weighted mean of its component payoffs.
It therefore cannot exceed the family bound proved below.

## 5. Exact reduction of all continuous correlations to binary choices

Set sigma=0 for the rest of the optimization. At fixed eta the
terminal state is (eta^2,eta-1,1). Each map (1)--(3) is linear in the
suffix state and affine in its own t_i. Each parameter occurs in only
one map. Their composition Rtail_0_sq(t_0,...,t_(n-1)) is thus affine
separately in every t_i. Repeatedly using

    p(...,t_i,...)=(1-t_i)p(...,0,...)+t_i p(...,1,...)

gives the exact convex-combination identity

    Rtail_0_sq(t)=sum_(epsilon in {0,1}^n)
       [product_(epsilon_i=1)t_i product_(epsilon_i=0)(1-t_i)]
       Rtail_0_sq(epsilon).                                       (11)

The weights are nonnegative and sum to one. The numerator 2eta-1
is the same for every term. Therefore

    V(t,eta)<=max_(epsilon in {0,1}^n) V(epsilon,eta).         (12)

This is a mathematical vertex reduction, not an enumeration that was
run. It remains valid for horizon-dependent choices and after taking
the supremum over eta>=1. The fresh-vector square roots do not need
to be affine: it is the exact radius state (1)--(3) that is affine.

For a binary choice, t_i=1 repeats the same gradient. At t_i=0 the
current gradient is orthogonal to EVERY later gradient, with norm
q_i=(delta_(i+1)+q_(i+1))/d_i. Thus the binary choices partition the
word into constant-gradient phases separated by selected physical
checkpoint steps. Repeated physical queries remain in the word;
only the algebra for a phase is compressed.

Write the exact partition as

    (block_0,b_0,block_1,b_1,...,b_(k-1),block_k),

where s_j is the mass of block_j and d_j^cp=b_j-1>0. The superscript
cp distinguishes checkpoint offsets from the physical indexed d_i.
Define a paid scalar A by starting at A=0 and, for each checkpoint,
updating

    T=s_j+b_j,    A <- T(T+A)/d_j^cp.                        (13)

At the end let r=s_k and X=r+eta. The exact squared radius is

    Rtail_0_sq=X(X+A),   V=(2X-D)/[X(X+A)],  D=2r+1.                (14)

To verify (13), denote the mutually orthogonal phase gradients by
p_j and their squared norms by Q_j^phase. Put T_j=s_j+b_j for j<k
and T_k=X=s_k+eta. At the checkpoint ending phase j, its current
coordinate is b_j p_j and is extinguished by that step. Hence the
initial point is exactly sum_(j=0)^k T_j p_j. The norm recursion
in (1), evaluated at each checkpoint, is

    Q_j^phase=(T_(j+1)/d_j^cp)Q_(j+1)^phase,
    Q_k^phase=1.

For a nonterminal next phase this uses
delta_next+q_next=(s_(j+1)+b_(j+1))Q_(j+1)^phase;
for the final phase it uses delta_next+q_next=X. Define

    A_j=[sum_(ell<j)T_ell^2 Q_ell^phase]/[T_j Q_j^phase].

Then A_0=0 and
A_(j+1)=T_j(T_j+A_j)/d_j^cp by substitution of the norm ratio.
The complete squared radius is
sum_(j=0)^k T_j^2 Q_j^phase=X^2+A_k X, proving (13),(14).
For k=0, A=0 and X=sum h+eta, so the same formula applies.

The maximum over eta>=1 is attained at

    X_*=(D+sqrt(D^2+2AD))/2,
    V_* = 2/[A+D+sqrt(D^2+2AD)].                             (15)

Indeed differentiating (14) gives a numerator
-2X^2+2DX+AD, positive before X_* and negative after it.
Also X_*>=D>=r+1, so the maximizer lies in the permitted domain.

## 6. A local checkpoint-removal lemma with both boundary cases paid

Consider a checkpoint b=1+d with ordinary mass s immediately before
it, and put T=s+b. Let A>=0 be the accumulated paid state BEFORE this
phase. Including this checkpoint changes it to

    A_b=T(T+A)/d.

If a later checkpoint c exists, let r be the ordinary mass between
b and c, and X=r+c. At c, including b produces the numerator
X(X+A_b) in update (13); deleting b produces
(T+X)(T+X+A). Their exact difference is

    Delta = X[A_b-2T-A]-T(T+A)
          = T[X(T-2d)/d-T]
             +A[X(T-d)/d-T].                              (16)

If b is the last checkpoint, precisely the same difference is the
difference of the two final radii, now with X=r+eta.

If T>2d and

    X >= dT/(T-2d),                                       (17)

both brackets in (16) are nonnegative. The condition for the second
bracket is weaker because T-d>T-2d. Deleting b then cannot increase
the radius at that same eta. If c exists, its denominator c-1 is
unchanged, and every later update (13) is increasing in its input
A; thus this local comparison propagates to the actual terminal.

There is a separate last-checkpoint argument. Suppose

    s+1>=2d,   r+1>=2d.                                   (18)

Then T>=3d and D=2r+1>=2d. At the optimizer of the split word,
formula (15), with A_b in place of A, gives

    X_*>=sqrt(A_b D/2)>=sqrt(T^2 D/(2d))>=T.

Since T>=3d, one has T>=dT/(T-2d). Thus (17) holds at the
SPLIT word's optimizer. The merged word at this same eta has at
least the split word's optimized payoff. Its own optimized payoff
can only be larger. This proves the required comparison even though
deleting the last checkpoint need not improve radius at every eta.

## 7. Applying the removal lemma to EVERY checkpoint subset on H_m

Fix m>=32, and suppress the subscript m on a,c,beta. All children
retain these same parameters. Write S_j=sum H_j and M_j=1+S_j.
Then

    M_0=1, M_(j+1)=2M_j+a c^j,
    M_j=A_mass c^j+(1-A_mass)2^j,
    A_mass=a/(c-2).                                       (19)

Here 0<A_mass<1. Indeed c>lambda>2, c(c-2)>lambda(lambda-2)=1,
whereas ac=beta<1. Moreover sqrt(2)<99/70, because
99^2>2*70^2. Therefore

    c <= (169/70)(33/32)=5577/2240 < 5/2.

For a physical step of ruler level ell, its offset is d=a c^ell.
Equation (19) gives

    d/M_ell <= c-2 < 1/2.                                (20)

Now take ANY nonempty selected checkpoint set, not necessarily a
dyadic partition. Choose a checkpoint of smallest ruler level ell
among those selected. Each of its two adjoining actual H_ell
children contains no selected checkpoint, since every level in such
a child is smaller than ell. Hence the ordinary blocks immediately
before and after it both have mass at least S_ell. Put M=M_ell.

If a later selected checkpoint c_cp exists, its level is at least ell,
so its offset is at least d. With the notation of Section 6,

    T>=M+d,   X>=M+d,   d<M/2.

The function T -> dT/(T-2d) decreases for T>2d. Thus

    dT/(T-2d) <= d(M+d)/(M-d) <= M+d <= X.

The deletion is legitimate by (16),(17). No other selected step
or physical ordinary step is altered.

If the chosen checkpoint is last, the same adjoining-child property
gives s+1>=M>2d and r+1>=M>2d. Conditions (18) apply. Hence its
deletion cannot decrease the optimized payoff either.

After deletion, choose a smallest-level checkpoint in the remaining
set and repeat the SAME argument. It applies to arbitrary subsets,
so it does not assume that the new set is dyadic. Finitely many
deletions end with no checkpoints. Formula (14) then becomes

    V=(2eta-1)/(S_m+eta)^2.

Its maximum over eta>=1 is at eta=S_m+1 and equals
1/(2S_m+1). Combining this with (12) and the monotonicity in sigma
proves the upper bound in (T) for every original parameter choice.
The all-t=1, all-sigma=0 member attains it. This proves equality.

## 8. Same-word references and the exact original benchmark price

Set Huber_m=1/(2S_m+1). Accepted b98bb807d514e80d and
1fca3e5dd555cb4a, with their COMPLETE checkpoint definitions, give

    sup V=Huber_m <= K_n(H_m) <= F_ray_n(H_m).

This comparison does not select one ray checkpoint or ignore any
feasible multi-cut choice. It does not require deciding whether
either reference is strictly larger than Huber on this word.

For clarity let C_full(h)=sup_(z in D_h) Gap_h(z), using EVERY original
interpolation cycle in D_h from accepted dfde2fa326c908bf, and the
accepted specified initial point 1 and specified minimizer 0.
Then

    sup V <= max{Huber_m,C_full(H_m)}.

There is also an explicit comparison to the UNaugmented full complex
channel: for every nonnegative word of mass S, set theta=1/(2S+1)
and P=product_(i<n)(1-h_i theta)>0. Then

    Gap_h(theta)=(2theta-theta^2)P^2 >= theta/2.              (21)

Here theta lies in the FULL D_h because its imaginary part is zero
and theta-theta^2>=0. To prove equality, all queried scalar points
Poly_i(theta) lie in [P,1]. Define a continuous nondecreasing
1-Lipschitz derivative on the real line by

    F'(x)=x                         for x<=theta P,
    F'(x)=theta P                   for theta P<=x<=P,
    F'(x)=theta x                   for x>=P,
    F(0)=0.

The matching values at the two joins make this derivative well
defined; its slopes are 1,0,theta. Its unique zero is 0. Integration
gives F(P)=(theta-theta^2/2)P^2, while at each queried positive point
its derivative is theta Poly_i(theta). This is the required one
static scalar realization. The accepted upper bound on Gap_h at
real theta is the same number, proving equality. Finally,
product(1-h_i theta)>=1-S theta=(1+theta)/2: prove this by induction
using nonnegative factors and partial sums at most S theta<1.
Thus Gap_h(theta)>=theta(2-theta)(1+theta)^2/4>=theta/2,
as (2-theta)(1+theta)^2=2+3theta-theta^3>=2.

Consequently the exact family supremum satisfies

    (sup V)/C_full(H_m) <= 2.

No assertion C_full>=Huber is made. The full complex supremum is
NOT replaced by the universal lens or by the single parameter in
(21); (21) is only a valid lower bound within that full channel.
The family already loses to the combined accepted references
because it cannot exceed their Huber component.

For the ORIGINAL benchmark define C_0^bench=B_0=1 and

    Omega(u,v)=(u+v+sqrt((u+v)^2+4uv))/2,
    G(u,v)=2u+v/2+sqrt(v^2+8uv)/2,
    C_n^bench=max_(0<=k<n) Omega(C_k^bench,C_(n-k-1)^bench),
    B_n=max_(0<=k<n) G(C_k^bench,B_(n-k-1)).

These are exactly the accepted recurrences, not a balanced-tree
surrogate. Equations (T),(19) give the exact normalized family ratio

    B_(2^m-1) sup V
      = B_(2^m-1)/[2 A_mass c_m^m+2(1-A_mass)2^m-1].        (22)

The unconditional assertion in accepted 04219554e41d9750 supplies

    Z_1=lim_(m->infinity)lambda^(-m)B_(2^m-1),
    0<Z_1<=(sqrt(B_1)+3/100)^2=(203/100)^2,

where B_1=G(1,1)=4. Meanwhile A_mass tends to one and

    c_m^m/lambda^m=(1+1/m)^m -> exp(1),
    (1-A_mass)(2/lambda)^m ->0.

Therefore the exact limiting price is

    lim_(m->infinity) B_(2^m-1) sup V = Z_1/[2 exp(1)] < 1.  (23)

For a rational strict separation, exp(1)>5/2 and
(203/100)^2<5, so this limit is strictly below 41209/50000.
This is a fixed relative shortfall of the NEW declared family,
not a certified full-class upper improvement for unrestricted W.

## 9. Adversarial checks and the exact boundary of the conclusion

First, (T) is not a theorem for all step words. On h=(10), choose
eta=2, t_0=0, sigma_0=0. Then

    g_1=e_1, x_1=2e_1, g_0=(sqrt(2)/3)e_0,
    x_0=(10sqrt(2)/3)e_0+2e_1,
    Rtail_0_sq=236/9, V=27/236 > 1/21.

All data belong to the same explicit family, and its static realization,
all ordered rows and true minimum distance were proved in Sections
2--4. The last inequality is 27*21>236. Huber dominance here would
be false; the small-ruler-offset condition (20) does real work.

Second, deletion at a FIXED terminal eta is false even on the
original H_m. This guards the optimizer step in Section 6. Use only
the central highest-level checkpoint, and put

    M=M_(m-1), d=a_m c_m^(m-1), q=d/M,
    s=r=M-1, T=M+d, eta=1, A=0.

Then (16) with X=M gives

    Rtail_split_sq-Rtail_merged_sq = T M(1/q-2-q).                        (24)

For m>=32, q>sqrt(2)-1, so (24) is negative. Here is a direct
enclosing-m check rather than an illicit critical-parameter substitution.
At level 2,

    d_2=lambda(1-1/m^2),
    M_2=lambda^2-1/m-4/[lambda(m+1)].

The inequality lambda d_2>M_2 is equivalent to
m+4m^2/[lambda(m+1)]>lambda^2, which follows from m>=32 and
lambda^2<25/4. Moreover
(d_(ell+1)/M_(ell+1))=c(d_ell/M_ell)/(2+d_ell/M_ell),
and d_ell/M_ell<c-2, so these ratios increase with ell. Thus the
top-level ratio is at least the level-2 ratio. Since
1/q-2-q<0 exactly when q>sqrt(2)-1, the counterexample follows.
It does not contradict (T), which optimizes the terminal ratio.

Third, nonnegative backward coefficients larger than one do not
silently reopen this same geometry. In any legal realization with
the same fresh-innovation orthogonality and radial terminal point,
the reverse adjacent normal gives

    <y_(i+1),g_i-g_(i+1)>=(t_i-1)delta_(i+1)<=0.             (25)

For eta>1 every reserve is positive, forcing t_i<=1. For eta=1,
all reserves before the terminal one are still positive: from
delta_i=t_i(delta_(i+1)+q_(i+1))+d_i q_i, they are positive when
t_i>=0 and q_i>0. A legal nonzero-terminal run cannot have q_i=0,
since it would reach a minimizer and then remain there. Thus only
t_(n-1)>1 is possible, and only when eta=1. In that case
q_(n-1)>=t_(n-1)^2 by the orthogonal decomposition. Replacing this
last extension by t=1,q=1 reduces each of q_(n-1),delta_(n-1),
Rtail_(n-1)_sq. Keep all earlier t in [0,1] and their nonnegative sigma
as derived from their original forward adjacent inequalities.
The nonnegative state maps then reduce the initial radius as well.
The replacement is a member of (1). Therefore the same upper bound
also covers this exceptional nonnegative coefficient case. This is
an optional enlargement of the obstruction, not an assumption used
to construct the pilot.

The closure DOES NOT cover negative backward coefficients; a new
innovation correlated with a second future mode; arbitrary conjugate
heights in place of the projection-value form (9); or an independently
moving transverse terminal position. It is not a closure of all
positively correlated gradients, all Markov laws, or all smooth
interpolants. It does not reinterpret the accepted finite H6 witness
as an asymptotic object.

## 10. Literature, proof state, and remaining allocation

Matlas searches were performed for smooth projection-conjugate
realizations and for the separate-affinity vertex principle. The
first returned the generic Moreau-envelope Lemma 4.1 of Khanh,
Mordukhovich and Tran, arXiv:2303.08785. It supplies no special
correlation law or quantitative radius bound here, so it is not
imported. The exact local Taylor--Hendrickx--Glineur paper
arXiv:1502.05666 was reread at Sections 2.4--2.5, Theorem 4's proof
and Corollary 1. Its interpolation construction motivates the
normal-coordinate audit; all needed smoothness and pair claims
are nevertheless derived directly in Section 3, using the accepted
project convention.

Matlas identified Ben Sassi and Girard, "Computation of Polytopic
Invariants for Polynomial Dynamical Systems using Linear Programming,"
arXiv:1012.1256, Lemma 2.2. Its statement is: a multi-affine function
on a rectangle has the same minimum on the rectangle as on its
vertex set. Definition 2.1 means degree at most one in each separate
variable, with mixed products allowed. The preceding paragraph
explains the convex-combination argument. The exact PDF was
downloaded into downloads/1012.1256.pdf and the relevant definitions
and argument were read through the web PDF text extractor after
local pdftotext failed with "command not found". No package was
installed. Equation (11) gives the full needed proof independently;
no LP or polynomial computation from that paper was run or invoked.

Adversarial checks were symbolic: both row orientations, all remote
rows, both optimum rows, terminal eta=1, zero/fractional/one tail
coefficients, positive slack, the true polar minimum set, the last
checkpoint optimizer, arbitrary checkpoint subsets, and the
fixed-eta counterexample (24). No numerical evaluation, solver,
machine algebra, replay, verifier call, consult, or subagent was used.

One allocated round remains AFTER this round actually returns. The
specific positive-tail family (1), even with arbitrary positive
slack and the exceptional coefficient in (25), is now closed at
draft level and must not be retuned. A genuinely different next law
must change its dependence or value/terminal geometry. A concrete
next candidate is a TWO-future-mode backward dependence
g_i=t_i g_(i+1)+u_i g_(i+2)+v_i e_i, with mixed signs allowed and
the original full normal inequalities retained. Unlike (1), its
current reserve and radius state need the independent correlation
<x_(i+1),g_(i+2)> and cross-gradient term; the three-scalar
separate-affinity argument does not apply automatically. This is a
next research direction, not a proved legal or competitive family.

Even a competitive construction on all original H_m would leave the
all-n/all-nonnegative-word bridge to the original Goal. Neither that
bridge nor a full-class upper improvement is supplied here. No
formal gate or further round is self-authorized.

Fix k>=4, 0<beta<=1/4, sigma_i>0 and ell_i>=0 with ell_1=0 and A_i=ell_i+ell_(i+1)/beta. Let K=conv({0} union {(beta+sqrt((1+beta)^2-(q+beta)^2),-q):0<=q<=1}), and Phi_i(X,Y)=max_(g in sigma_i K){<g,(X-A_i,Y-ell_(i+1))>-||g||^2/2}. Use F=one-half sum Phi_i plus any terminal one-sided Huber depending only on x_(k+1). Put kappa=1+beta^2 and choose x_1=A_1,x_2=ell_2-kappa sigma_1,x_3=ell_3,x_4=...=x_k=0, and x_(k+1)>=ell_(k+1). Every input x_i<=A_i. For X<=A_1, the maximizing first body point has p=beta q because decreasing p to beta q increases the objective and stays feasible; thus its scalar maximizer is q=clip((beta X-Y)/kappa,0,sigma_1). At this point beta A_1=ell_2, so q=sigma_1 and P_1=beta sigma_1. The second envelope has X<A_2,Y=ell_3, and its projection is0: its centered point has nonpositive first coordinate and zero second, while all body points have nonnegative first coordinate. Thus P_2=Q_2=0. Components j>=3 are zero: their inputs are at most ell_j and outputs nonnegative; the polar inequalities X-A_j<=0 and beta(X-ell_j)-Y<=0 hold. The terminal scalar does not affect coordinate2. Consequently the exact GD update is x_2^+=ell_2-kappa sigma_1+h sigma_1/2, which exceeds A_2 precisely when h>2kappa+2ell_3/(beta sigma_1). This is a concrete algebraic counterexample with all component values and gradients taken from one fixed function. In the frozen round60 policy, the joint draft84a4435a88a9f487 separately derives ell_3/(beta sigma_1)=O(k exp(2k)), k=floor(sqrt(m)), and verifies that the suffix step b_(m-2) exceeds the threshold eventually. That extension remains part of the same unverified joint draft. No claim is made that this chosen state occurs from the prescribed initialization.

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-001267 -->
# C95: weak-background static Huber ridge dynamics

Status: COMPLETE JOINT UNVERIFIED DRAFT. Formal verifier allowance is zero.
This work belongs to completed round 26 (baseline 25, ceiling 27).
Accepted mathematical sources: b98bb807d514e80d (single cuts),
1fca3e5dd555cb4a (alternating rays), e9a55f57461f253c (finite original H6).
C93/C94 are motivation only. Every new assertion used here is derived below.

## Static model and exact legality

For positive radii r_- and r_+, let phi(0)=0 and
phi'(s)=max(-r_-,min(s,r_+)). Thus phi=s^2/2 on the core,
phi=r_+ s-r_+^2/2 to the right, and phi=-r_- s-r_-^2/2 to the left.
Let A be a symmetric PSD matrix, u_j unit vectors, and alpha_j>0.
Define one static function

    F(x)=x^T A x/2 + sum_j alpha_j phi_j(u_j^T x),
    C=A+sum_j alpha_j u_j u_j^T,   0<=C<=I.

Every scalar clip is monotone and 1-Lipschitz. For any x,y its difference
is theta_j u_j^T(x-y), with 0<=theta_j<=1. Therefore

    grad F(x)-grad F(y)=(A+sum_j alpha_j theta_j u_j u_j^T)(x-y).

The matrix in parentheses lies between zero and I, so its norm is at
most one. This proves global 1-smoothness; convexity also follows termwise
from monotonicity of each scalar derivative and A>=0. No local Hessian
rescaling of the physical steps is permitted or needed.

Every summand is nonnegative, and phi_j(s)=0 iff s=0. Consequently

    argmin F=ker A intersect intersection_j u_j^perp=ker C,
    min F=0.

The initial distance is the norm of the orthogonal projection onto
(ker C)^perp. When C is positive definite this is exactly ||x_0||;
the one-dimensional calibration also has its unique minimizer at zero.
All matrices below have explicit rational certificates, with no eigenvalue
clipping. A small eigenvalue is not silently removed from the distance.

Useful exact planar directions are

    u_+=(4/5,3/5), u_-=(4/5,-3/5), v=(0,1).

Weights alpha_+=alpha_-=25/32 give total matrix diag(1,9/16).
Adding weight 7/16 on v gives I. With A=mu I and multiplying every
ridge weight by 1-mu, 0<=mu<1, these become respectively
diag(1,mu+(1-mu)9/16) and I. The background floor can be zero or
arbitrarily weak. A fixed gate change changes one rank-one matrix and
can therefore change the principal axes; no independent switching matrix
is supplied to gradient descent.

## Original finite words and references

For m in {6,7,8}, set c=(1+sqrt(2))(1+1/m), beta=1-1/m,
a=beta/c, and H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j), 0<=j<m.
All subwords retain the enclosing-m values c,beta,a. Let h=H_m,
n=2^m-1, S=sum h. Physical GD is x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i grad F(x_i).
The normalized actual objective is V=2F(x_n)/dist(x_0,argmin F)^2.
These finite horizons do not test any m>=128 theorem.

Accepted b98bb807d514e80d gives

    K=max(1/(1+2S), max_j (h_j-1)^2/((1+P_j)^2*(1+2T_j))),

where P_j,T_j are the preceding and following step masses.
At m=6 the stronger accepted e9a55f57461f253c gives
W_63(H_6)>56/2711>1/50. It uses its own actual high-dimensional function;
its 4160 checks are not replayed, and this reference is not transplanted
to m=7 or m=8. Saved original benchmark CSV entries may be read but no
benchmark dynamic program is rerun.

Here is a direct sufficient test for the complete accepted ray family to
equal K. For i<j put P=sum_(t<i)h_t and D=sum_(i<t<j)h_t. If

    (h_i-1)(h_j-1)<(1+P)(1+D)                 (R)

for every i<j, no feasible accepted ray selection has two checkpoints.
Indeed its first two checkpoint gradients are
m_0=1/(1+P), m_1=(h_i-1)m_0/(1+D), with old offset P*m_0.
The second-return budget is (h_j-1)m_1-P*m_0<m_0 by (R).
With exactly two checkpoints the old-gradient requirement is at least
m_0, and with more checkpoints the third magnitude would be below m_0.
Both contradict the explicit feasibility conditions of 1fca3e5dd555cb4a.
Zero and one checkpoint produce exactly K. Any numerical check of (R)
will be labeled numerical until an exact symbolic bound is supplied.

## Calibration and accounting

The initial calibration is the accepted one-dimensional central single cut.
If j=2^(m-1)-1, P=T=sum H_(m-1), b=h_j, choose
r_+=1/(1+P), r_-=(b-1)/((1+P)(1+2T)), A=0, alpha=1, x_0=1.
Its exact payoff is (b-1)^2/((1+P)^2(1+2T)). Its gradient is r_+ until
the central cut and -r_- afterward, with a possible boundary label at
the cut itself. This is one charged calibration, not a free preliminary run.

All forward work is standard-library Python, through the main-owned
bounded_ridge_forward_meter.py and run_ridge_with_remaining_budget.py.
The model source is research/weak_background_forward.py. The allocation is
36 total cases, at most 32 discovery, four total calibration/reconstruction
reserves, 30 measured/accounted seconds across both rounds and all scripts.
No case is run while authoring this draft. Source and request snapshots,
hashes, raw iterates/gradients/values, true distance, static parameters,
gate labels at queried and terminal points, and failures will be shared.

No numerical observation is a verified fact, a minimax disproof, or an
asymptotic conclusion. The original project target remains open.

## Exact finite-word ray comparison, with no exponential enumeration

Write r_j=a*c^j and M_j=1+sum H_j. Then

    M_0=1, M_(j+1)=2*M_j+r_j,
    t_j=r_j/M_j, t_0=a, t_(j+1)=c*t_j/(2+t_j).

For m>=6, c>1+sqrt(2) and beta<1 imply a<c-2. Hence t_j increases
and stays below c-2. Also

    beta-(c-2) = 3-(1+sqrt(2))-(2+sqrt(2))/m
       >= (10-7*sqrt(2))/6 >0,

so t_j>=a>(c-2)/c.

For any two chronological indices i<j with levels u,v, define the ratio
R_ij in (R) with the right side as its denominator. If u>=v, the prefix
before i contains H_u and the gap before j contains H_v; thus
R_ij<=t_u*t_v. The largest level m-1 occurs only once, so at least one
of u,v is at most m-2. It follows that

    R_ij<=t_(m-1)*t_(m-2)<c*t_(m-2)^2.

If u<v, both the prefix before i and the gap from i to j contain H_u.
Their total mass is at least sum H_v-(1+r_u), since the prefix before
j contains H_v. Consequently, putting x=1+P and y=1+D,
x,y>=M_u and x+y>=M_v-r_u. From
(x-M_u)(y-M_u)>=0 we obtain

    x*y>=M_u*(M_v-M_u-r_u).

Let d=v-u>=1 and t=t_u. Directly iterating the recurrence for M gives

    (M_v-M_u-r_u)/M_u
      =2^d-1+t*((c^d-2^d)/(c-2)-1)
      >=c^(d-1)+(c-1)*(2^d-2)/c>=c^(d-1).

The first inequality uses t>=(c-2)/c and the nonnegative coefficient
((c^d-2^d)/(c-2)-1). Therefore R_ij<=c*t_u^2<=c*t_(m-2)^2.
Equality in the complete maximum occurs at the first level-(m-2) bridge
and the immediately following level-(m-1) bridge: both intervening
prefix/gap masses are sum H_(m-2). Thus

    max_(i<j) R_ij = c*t_(m-2)^2.                         (C95.1)

This proves the full pair test symbolically; it does not rely on sampled
pair scans. Here are short rational upper certificates for the three
authorized horizons. Monotonicity of c*t/(2+t) in both positive variables
allows use of the displayed upper parameters throughout.

* m=6: c<17/6 and a<3/10. Four recurrence steps give
  t_4<83521/144462<7/12. Thus c*t_4^2<833/864<1.
  The intermediate bounds are 17/46,289/654,4913/9582,83521/144462.
* m=7: c<83/30 and a<5/16. Bounds for t_1,...,t_5 are respectively
  3/8,7/16,1/2,83/150,3/5. For the last step,
  6889/11490<3/5; hence c*t_5^2<747/750<1.
* m=8: c<68/25 and a<13/40. The upper recurrence has reciprocal at
  its sixth step equal to
  25/18+(395/234)*(25/34)^6. This is greater than 33/20 because
  (25/34)^3>79/200 and (79/200)^2>611/3950. Therefore t_6<20/33,
  and c*t_6^2<27200/27225<1.

All parameter enclosures follow by squaring positive rational bounds for
sqrt(2). In particular sqrt(2)<10/7, sqrt(2)>29/21 prove the m=6
bounds; sqrt(2)<341/240 and sqrt(2)>7/5 prove m=7;
sqrt(2)<319/225 and sqrt(2)>163/117 prove m=8.
Combining (C95.1) with the accepted ray feasibility conditions proves

    F_ray_(2^m-1)(H_m)=K_(2^m-1)(H_m), m=6,7,8.           (C95.2)

No m>=128 result is used here. This is a new UNVERIFIED finite symbolic
comparison derived jointly, not an accepted graph fact.

For completeness the largest single-cut term is the central cut. For a
fixed level u put p=1+P, q=1+T; both are at least M_u and
p+q=M_m-r_u. The denominator p^2*(2q-1) has no interior minimum on
that interval: its derivative is 2p*(2*(M_m-r_u)-3p-1), with at most
one interior critical point, a maximum. The two endpoint denominators
are ordered by monotonicity of z^2/(2z-1) for z>=1, so the earliest
occurrence of level u is best. Its value is

    t_u^2/[2*M_m-2*M_u-2*r_u-1].

The numerator increases and the positive denominator decreases with u.
Thus the central level m-1 maximizes it. In particular K is exactly
the larger of the global Huber term and the central single-cut term;
no choice of untested checkpoints is hidden in the numerical reference.

## Exact capture criterion for a saturated core curvature budget

Assume C=A+sum_j alpha_j u_j*u_j^T=I, and let D be the closed convex
intersection of all core strips -r_(j,-)<=u_j^T x<=r_(j,+).
At every point in D, grad F(x)=x and 2F(x)=||x||^2.

Fix any k>=1 with k<=m, retaining the same enclosing-m parameters. A
run of H_k starting at z stays in D at every query AND at the terminal
point if and only if both z and -a*z lie in D. In that event its exact
iterate after t steps is

    x_t=(-1)^t*beta^t*c^(-s_2(t))*z, 0<=t<=2^k-1,         (C95.3)

where s_2(t) counts binary ones and s_2(0)=0. In particular

    x_end=-beta^(2^k-1)*c^(-k)*z,
    2F(x_end)=beta^(2*(2^k-1))*c^(-2k)*||z||^2.           (C95.4)

Proof: the level at step t-1 is v_2(t), the exponent of 2 dividing t.
The identity sum_(j=1)^t v_2(j)=t-s_2(t) follows either by summing
floor(t/2^q), or by writing t as a sum of distinct powers of 2. Multiplying
the actual scalar factors -a*c^(v_2(j)) yields (C95.3). For positive t,
its absolute multiplier is at most beta/c=a because s_2(t)>=1 and
beta^t<=beta. When t is even the multiplier is nonnegative and at most1;
when t is odd it lies between -a and0. Thus every candidate x_t lies
on one of the segments [0,z] and [0,-a*z], both contained in D. Induction
now proves the actual recursion never leaves D, including the terminal
point. Necessity follows from the first actual step, whose multiplier is
-a when z is in D. At t=2^k-1, s_2(t)=k, giving (C95.4).

The reflection condition is essential for asymmetric ridges. Take one
scalar ridge with r_-=1,r_+=4,A=0,alpha=1,z=4. On any m>=6,
a>=5/(7*(1+sqrt(2)))>1/4. The first actual point -4a lies below -1.
The objective is legal and z is in the core, but all-core invariance fails.
This is an analytical counterexample, not a numerical case or a new fact.

## A scalable, conditional weak-background obstruction

The preceding criterion has an explicit normalization cost. Let d0=||x0||,
which is the true initial distance because C=I, and put

    Gcap=sum_j alpha_j*max(r_(j,-),r_(j,+)),
    hmax=max h_i, Gcap*S_m<=Ccap*d0,
    0<=A<=2*I/hmax,

where Ccap>=0 is a constant independent of m. The radii may be asymmetric.
Since ||I-h_i*A||<=1 and the clipped-ridge gradient has norm at most Gcap,
the true recursion gives ||x_i||<=d0+Gcap*sum_(j<i)h_j<=(1+Ccap)*d0.

Suppose the actual state z at the start of the FINAL H_k suffix satisfies
z,-a*z in D. No condition is imposed on any earlier queried regions.
By (C95.4), the normalized payoff obeys

    V<= (1+Ccap)^2*beta^(2*(2^k-1))*c^(-2k),
    V/K <=2*(1+Ccap)^2*c^(m-2k)*beta^(2*(2^k-1)).         (C95.5)

Here K>=1/(2S_m+1), and M_m<=c^m: the latter follows by induction from
M_(j+1)=2M_j+a*c^j and 2+a<c, proved above.

For m>=16 take k=ceil(log_2(m^2)). Then k<=m, 2^k>=m^2, and the final
suffix has fewer than 2m^2 steps. The elementary inequality log(1-1/m)
<=-1/m and c<3 imply

    V/K <=2*(1+Ccap)^2*exp(2/m)*(3/exp(2))^m ->0.        (C95.6)

The ratio 3/exp(2) is below1, since exp(2)>1+2+2=5. Thus a family meeting
these hypotheses that remains scalar-competitive cannot pass this capture
test at the start of that final suffix for infinitely many unbounded horizons.
This prices weak (even zero) backgrounds and arbitrarily many preceding
gate changes. It does not prove that capture occurs. Cases that stay
outside D, fail the reflected-point condition, have C!=I, or violate the
displayed cap/background hypotheses remain outside the conclusion.

## Completed finite discovery and exact same-object reconstruction

The first allocation round used 16 charged cases: one calibration, fourteen
discovery trajectories, and one exact reconstruction of discovery case10.
All returned finite data, with no timeout, stale lock, failed prefilter,
installation, optimization solver, or formal verifier call. The public ledger
records 0.6265509749991907 measured/accounted seconds and no external timeout
debit. Twenty cases remain: at most eighteen discovery plus two reserves.
The ledger remains open across the next authorized round; no renewal occurs.

Calibration case1 matched accepted central-cut behavior to a relative
floating discrepancy below 7e-14. Its initial source looked for a stored
benchmark in c76_dp_run01.csv, whose saved table ends at31; the absent63
entry is honestly recorded as null. Subsequent source versions read the
existing c76_dp_run02.csv through4095. No dynamic program or calibration
trajectory was rerun to make this change.

No discovery case beats K. Cases2 and3 never change a queried gate. Twelve
of the fourteen discovery cases do change queried gates. Among these, the
largest floating ratio is case12, approximately0.8972369014*K, or
approximately0.9486665934 times the saved original U_63. It uses the rational
near-parallel directions (12/13,+/-5/13), weights169/288 on each, and an
additional vertical ridge of weight119/144, so C=I and A=0. This observed
payoff is far below the accepted H6 reference56/2711. Neither a global
impossibility claim nor an improving lower witness is inferred.

The central-split family in cases10,14,15 was run on the three separate
original words, each as its own charged case. Their floating V/K ratios
are respectively approximately4.6184e-6,1.5747e-7,2.9887e-9. The raw data
suggested late capture; these three observations do not prove an asymptotic
law or that all nearby models capture. Equations(C95.3)--(C95.6), with their
explicit hypotheses, are the separate symbolic relation extracted here.

The selected same-object reconstruction is case16, reproducing case10:

    m=6, A=0, x0=(1,0),
    u1=(4/5,3/5),u2=(4/5,-3/5),u3=(0,1),
    alpha1=alpha2=25/32, alpha3=7/16.

Put P=sum H5, b=the central bridge, p=1/(1+P),
q=(b-1)/((1+P)*(1+2P)), and d=1/(1+2S6). The two first ridges have
radii (r_minus,r_plus)=(4q/5,p) and(4q/5,3p/5), while the vertical
ridge has(d,d). These are exact elements of Q(sqrt(2)), not rounded inputs.
The two sources use identical defining matrices, directions, weights,
radii formulas, physical word and initial point. The exact run retained
every sampled value, gradient and point, including the terminal sample.

It reproduced the entire numerical gate itinerary exactly:

    ridge1 changes at28,31,32,33,34,35;
    ridge2 changes at32,60;
    ridge3 changes at1,32,60.

All terminal gates are core. Exact arithmetic checked that z=x60 and -a*z
both lie in every closed core strip. The remaining suffix is H2 with the
original m=6 parameters, so (C95.4) gives

    x63=(-125/98+(125/147)*sqrt(2))*x60,
    V=(-125/98+(125/147)*sqrt(2))^2*||x60||^2 < K/100000.

The finite exact check of that strict inequality passed; its approximate
V is1.0540484156979085e-8. This finite reconstruction is supporting evidence
for the joint draft, not a verifier verdict. The analytic legality proof
above and the exact core multiplier explain what was checked; correctness
has not been promoted to the fact graph.

The public raw directory is research/weak_ridge_forward_raw. It contains
the immutable source and request snapshots plus all sixteen raw cases.
The exact source SHA256 is
08af55d51da0a3ada29b2ba0f31e6101941562bcdbbb5b3eaa524dead8a3bee8;
the exact request SHA256 is
479203688567b53b55b3c938335d87ac6e186a781abb157da20600c7d557a87f;
case16 raw SHA256 is
2cc1090655cc4a20547328150b52dd797e026c8367a389f5509a9118485662b7.
The companion summary indexes every raw record and all earlier hashes.

The useful open continuation is precise: seek an actual weak-background
static object whose late state fails the two-point capture condition, or
whose total core matrix C differs from I, while improving on the now-priced
same-word ray reference. Multiple sign or gate changes alone did not supply
that gain in the fourteen completed cases. Eighteen authorized discovery
cases and two reserves remain, under the original cumulative time cap.

# Saved full-dual structure and two paid optimum-profile reallocations

**JOINT UNVERIFIED analytical draft and numerical discovery.** Worker max,
ONE allocated max78 invocation (baseline77, ceiling78/attempted79). No
controller completion count is inferred. The original exact-profile problem
remains OPEN. No formal call, fact submission, new accepted fact, or full-class
upper bound is supplied. Two new explicit coefficient laws are examined.

The saved duals change the optimum profile, retain substantial incoming
terminal mass, and act positively on the tested finite-depth zero-displacement
spaces. They remain numerically defective. The two analytic laws below pay for
original optimum-row decrements and change that profile while preserving the
whole Huber contact equations exactly. The first fails on the actual first
original gradient. The second repairs that diagonal but cannot offset the
inherited all-depth negative action on a two-gradient zero-displacement wave.
These are scoped failures of the specified laws, not infeasibility of a full
PEP, a bound on W, or a resolution of the original minimax question.

## 1. Original problem and accepted inputs

For EVERY integer n>=0, EVERY physical word h in [0,infinity)^n, and L>0,
W_n(h) is the supremum of 2(f(x_n)-f(x_star))/(L||x_0-x_star||^2) over
all finite dimensions, convex differentiable L-smooth functions with an
attained minimizer x_star, and x_0!=x_star, with the actual updates
x_(i+1)=x_i-(h_i/L)grad f(x_i). In particular W=2R. The original benchmark is

    C_0=B_0=1,
    Omega(u,v)=(u+v+sqrt((u+v)^2+4uv))/2,
    G(u,v)=2u+v/2+sqrt(v^2+8uv)/2,
    C_n=max_(0<=k<n)Omega(C_k,C_(n-k-1)),
    B_n=max_(0<=k<n)G(C_k,B_(n-k-1)), U_n^ZJ=1/B_n.

Every split k is an integer. The original target asks whether
lim_n B_n inf_(h in [0,infinity)^n) W_n(h)=1. Its equivalent lower-bound
quantifier is: for every positive eta there is a word-independent N_eta
such that EVERY actual n>=N_eta and EVERY nonnegative word h of length n
satisfies B_n W_n(h)>=1-eta. Neither the present diagnostic hierarchy nor
these two candidate laws restrict those target quantifiers.

The accepted inputs are fact1252b8823bb5a290 (full original interpolation
and certificate convention), fact04219554e41d9750 Sections10--11 (full
primitive/objective conventions and its unconditional benchmark), and ONLY
the scalar benchmark consequence in fact5fd396eba75651e7 Section10:

    B_(2^m-1)/(1+2S_m)<237/250, m>=1536, tau=1/4.       (1.1)

The full interpolation fact and these exact accepted scopes were reread.
No seed hypothesis or restricted-family optimum is imported. A true full
certificate at q=21/[20(1+2S_m)] would imply a fixed benchmark gap
B_n W_n(h)<4977/5000. No such certificate is obtained here.

TASK.md, guidance f4f9450aef9e7697, and latest observed guidance
9a86c3435878b039 govern the same bounded allocation. Full shared reports
ad24079f80cd6a3a and 9ceef38b9c3f0498 were read as awareness. The latter is
NOT a correctness premise: all base coefficients and all mathematical
identities needed for the new argument are derived together below.

## 2. Bounded saved-array extraction: what actually changed

The frozen public inputs are ONLY calibration h=(3/2), H4(n15), H5(n31),
and H6(n63), each at its OWN enclosing m and tau=1/4. INPUT_MANIFEST.json
and EXECUTION_FREEZE.json preserve paths, bytes and SHA256 hashes. The raw
files are not changed. The source is the public small_tau_full_pep_20261001
index, not another worker's private memory. No H3 job, new word, trajectory,
hierarchy recurrence, benchmark DP, solver or optimizer was executed.

Every child used the documented public run_shared_math_python.sh launcher,
PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1, and single-thread numerical environment variables.
Each reserved30 accounted seconds and one job BEFORE importing NumPy, loading
raw arrays, doing algebra, diagnostics, or exporting. An EXTERNAL30-second
child timeout covered the entire child. The wrapper performed only
administrative file I/O, reservations and timing.

All original ordered rows were independently expanded from the SAVED
position coefficient vectors. With q in original units and original pair
weights lambda, the reconstructed matrix was q E00+sum lambda A_ij.
This matched the saved original matrix entrywise in the reported binary64
calculation. Every original incoming/outgoing terminal and optimum row was
retained. No eigenvalue clipping, matrix repair, rank truncation, support
threshold deletion or numerical-to-exact promotion occurred.

Calibration used the exact identity

    ||x0||^2/4-2f1+Q_(0,star)+Q_(1,star)+Q_(1,0)
       =||x0/2-g0-g1||^2.

The exact row map, orientation, known flow2e1, original units and raw flow
checks passed on job1. Job1 nevertheless exited3 because an extra
entrywise-nearness requirement1e-5 failed: the saved approximate matrix
is4.70287706799688e-5 away from that exact square. The ONE correction slot
removed that unnecessary raw-certificate-matching requirement, retained its
measured discrepancy, and added original C/G eigenvalue defect checks at the
original report's1e-7 tolerance. The exact map/flow/scaling checks were
unchanged. The correction passed; this is a model calibration, not exact
feasibility. Initial code, freeze, failed outputs and CORRECTION.md are
preserved. No retry beyond that sole correction took place.

The following are COPIES of the charged-child outputs, not certified bounds:

| saved case | qD | min original dual eigenvalue | original stationarity defect | min full initial Schur eigenvalue |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| H4 | 0.9999999942240854 | -1.0353695211179965e-10 | 2.3850660557656056e-8 | -2.8548287922082678e-8 |
| H5 | 0.9999999933916163 | -4.4449185361851374e-11 | 5.7433767963743776e-8 | -3.776988986744671e-8 |
| H6 | 0.9999999921914533 | -2.0713873915231942e-11 | 1.4393644320875865e-7 | -5.337695569667924e-8 |

The whole contact vector residual max norms are respectively
4.718481784182771e-6,1.7485052563936954e-5,6.328318730319804e-6.
The initial/common determinants are negative numerically in all three cases.
All three original primal Grams also have negative computed minimum
eigenvalues. Conditioning cannot erase any of these reported defects.

The inherited root-left optimum profile is exactly2h_i/D. In the stored
arrays its signed differences have3 decrements/4 increments at H4,
7/8 at H5, and15/16 at H6. The first subchild's entries decrease, while
its bridge and following subchild increase. The raw root optimum coefficient
divided by its base value is0.8981960654650736,0.9002587628206459,
0.8869116169110768. Thus keeping the optimum profile frozen is contradicted
by the observed arrays as a descriptive model of them.

Incoming TERMINAL finite mass is0.18928842003159055,
0.12629457664146687,0.08489463669209918. Incoming OPTIMUM mass is only
1.561501725235441e-9,9.535343527485113e-10,5.821298867349323e-10,
respectively. These are different row types; neither was deleted. In H6
the left-to-root and root-to-left total finite masses are9.117134880978973
and9.474527621940528; left-to-right and right-to-left masses are
0.03857595195117698 and0.09901930849280804. No exact sparsity follows.

In the full stored matrix the complete root-left zero-displacement
restriction, using an orthonormal basis but preserving its original-space
embedding, has computed minimum eigenvalues0.001770064688887271,
0.00017120692101997955,1.0911046137055806e-5. All individual tested
first-gradient/bridge waves also have positive computed action; their full
initial and common couplings are exported. These tests do not certify the
whole matrix and occur only at m<=6, far outside the eventual domain1536.

Exactly THREE feature families were preregistered and fitted in the ONE
cross-case job: physical source/destination features of finite rows;
dyadic source/destination and containing-cell scale features of finite rows;
and physical-prefix/step/level features of the optimum profile. No feature
selection or additional fit was run. F1 finite-row relative errors were
1.1807295211349638,0.9638567564479851,0.9878710345697729; F2 errors were
2.163982775338135,0.8989173505036572,0.9575378600201162. F1 is rank9
with10 columns because its signed distance column is a linear combination
of the physical-prefix columns; its least-squares coefficients are not
unique. F3 errors were0.299062929376627,0.2538239721973367,
0.44042110387348976. None supplies an exact law. In particular the two laws
below are NOT fitted coefficients masquerading as a certificate.

## 3. Complete original-space algebra

Normalize L=1, x_star=g_star=f_star=0. All n+2 vectors
(x_0,g_0,...,g_n) remain independent formal variables, including the
unqueried terminal gradient. Put

    x_i=x_0-sum_(t<i)h_t g_t,
    P_i=sum_(t<i)h_t, S=sum h_t, D=1+2S, h_n=0,
    y_i=x_i-g_i,
    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2.

Let K_ij, i!=j finite, be all finite-row multipliers, nu_i the
Q_(i,star) weights, and xi_i the Q_(star,i) weights. Diagonals are zero.
Value cancellation is exactly

    sum_j K_ij-sum_j K_ji+nu_i-xi_i=2*1_(i=n).         (3.1)

After this cancellation the full polynomial is

    M(q)=q||x_0||^2-sum_i nu_i<x_0,g_i>+C(g),
    C(g)=sum_(i,j) C_ij<g_i,g_j>.                     (3.2)

Define out_i=nu_i+sum_j K_ij. Every entry of C is

    C_ii=out_i-1_(i=n)-h_i sum_(v>i)K_iv,
    2C_ij=-(K_ij+K_ji)-h_j sum_(v>j)K_iv
                       +h_i[nu_j+sum_(v<=i)K_jv], i<j. (3.3)

Indeed half the incident row mass is out_i-1_(i=n), by(3.1).
Only a forward row sourced at i contributes -h_i K_iv to its diagonal.
For i<j the difference squares give the first term, rows out of i landing
after j give the second, and rows out of j landing at/before i or at the
optimum give the third. This derives the entire matrix, not a restriction.
For q>0 the exact initial Schur expenditure is nu nu^T/(4q).

In the analytic laws xi=0 and sum nu=2. Set e=(1,...,1).
Full flow then gives e^T C e=D. On the whole Huber contact tuple
(x_0,g)=(D,e), all finite and outgoing-optimum Q rows vanish, and

    M(q;D,e)=(qD-1)D.                                (3.4)

There is no incoming-optimum Huber price in either proposed law. Their
stronger derivative contact identity Ce=D nu/2 will be proved, not assumed.
The initial/common block is therefore [[q,-1],[-1,D]]. At21/(20D)
its determinant is1/20. This block alone does not establish full PSD.

## 4. The inherited base, rederived within this joint draft

Fix an integer m>=1536 and the SAME enclosing parameters at EVERY depth:

    lambda=1+sqrt(2), epsilon=1/(4m), beta=1-epsilon,
    c=lambda(1+epsilon), a=beta/c,
    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j), 0<=j<m,
    n_j=2^j-1, S_j=sum H_j, A_j=1+S_j, D_j=2A_j-1,
    d_j=a*c^j, t_j=d_j/A_j, tstar=1/lambda.

The final h=H_m has n=n_m and D=D_m. These are mathematical definitions;
none of these recurrences was executed in this invocation. The finite
sum formula for masses is

    v_h=a/(c-2), A_j=v_h*c^j+(1-v_h)*2^j,
    t_j=(c-2)/[1+(1/v_h-1)(2/c)^j].                   (4.1)

It follows from c(c-2)>1>beta that0<v_h<1. Thus A_j>=2^j,
t_j increases strictly, and t_j<c-2<1/2. With b=h_0=1+a,

    b=sqrt(2)-2epsilon/[lambda(1+epsilon)],
    A_1=lambda-2epsilon/[lambda(1+epsilon)], d_1=1-epsilon,
    A_2=lambda^2-epsilon[1+4/(lambda(1+epsilon))],
    d_2=lambda(1-epsilon^2),
    lambda*d_2-A_2
      =epsilon[1+4/(lambda(1+epsilon))-lambda^2 epsilon]>0.

Also lambda*d_1-A_1<0. Consequently

    7/5<b<sqrt(2), A_1>12/5, A_2>5,
    t_0,t_1<tstar<t_2,
    0<t_j-tstar<lambda*epsilon for j>=2.              (4.2)

For the rational signs use epsilon<=1/6144, lambda^2<6 and
lambda>241/100; the displayed subtractions are smaller than epsilon.
The exact relations A_(j+1)=(2+t_j)A_j and
t_(j+1)=c*t_j/(2+t_j) also follow directly from the word.

Here is EVERY primitive finite coefficient. Start Lambda^0=0. In a
join of two depth-j children let p=n_j, z=n_(j+1), and let i run over
the nonterminal right queries. Embed the left Lambda^j with weight1
and the right with weight k_j. Add

    i->p: u_j h_i, i->z: v_j h_i, z->p: w_j, p->z: z_j,
    u_j=2lambda,
    v_j=[2lambda d_j+2(A_j+d_j)]/A_j,
    k_j=2+lambda+(lambda+1)t_j,
    w_j=4A_j+2d_j-2lambda(A_j-1), z_j=2A_j.           (4.3)

Equivalently each original coefficient is the sum of its indicated local
contributions over every dyadic node, multiplied by k_l for each ancestor
whose right child contains that node; left ancestor weights are1. This
finite sum/product is an explicit law, not an optimization or executed
recurrence. For w_j write2lambda+2(d_j-A_j/lambda);(4.2) proves its
positivity for j>=2, while w_0=4+2a and w_1>2lambda-2. All other signs
are positive from their formulas. The identities

    u_j+v_j=2(k_j-1),
    u_j(A_j-1)+w_j-z_j=2(A_j+d_j)

give divergence -2h_i at a nonterminal and2S_j at the terminal.
Define the value-cancelled primitive polynomial

    V_j=2sum_(i<n_j)h_i(f_i-f_(n_j))
      +sum_(i<n_j)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2
      -A_j(A_j-1)||g_(n_j)||^2,
    Psrc_j=V_j+sum Lambda^j_iv Q_iv.

For r_R=sum_(right nonterminal i)h_i g_i, put
Y=r_R-(A_j-1)g_z and Z=g_p-g_z. Expanding the four row types in(4.3)
before their weights gives the following gradient expressions:

    d_j<r_R,g_p>+||r_R||^2/2+sum h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2/2
                                    +(A_j-1)||g_p||^2/2;
    -||r_R||^2/2+sum h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2/2
                    +(A_j-1)||g_z||^2/2-<r_R,g_z>;
    d_j<g_z,g_p>+<g_z,r_R>+(||g_p||^2+||g_z||^2)/2;
    -(d_j+1/2)||g_p||^2+||g_z||^2/2-<g_p,r_R>-<g_p,g_z>.

Collecting these terms together with V and the two children yields

    Psrc_(j+1)=Psrc_j^left+k_j Psrc_j^right
      +(tstar-t_j)[(lambda+1)||Y||^2-2lambda A_j<Y,Z>
                        +A_j^2(2+tstar+t_j)||Z||^2]. (4.4)

In particular the pre-collection coefficients of r_R^2,r_R*g_p,r_R*g_z
are (u_j-v_j)/2,u_j*d_j-z_j,w_j-v_j, verifying the mixed-term signs.
The bracket has positive first diagonal and determinant divided by A_j^2
at least lambda>0. Positive epsilon therefore DOES NOT make these
primitive children PSD. That is never assumed below.

The original objective base is Kbase^0=0, mu^0_0=2, xi=0. At a join
set A=S_j, Bjoin=1+d_j, Dp=D_(j+1), Dc=D_j, and
s_j=(A+Bjoin)/(3A+2Bjoin), theta_j=1-s_j. With p=n_j, L_l=l and
R_i=n_j+1+i, define

    Kbase_(L_l,L_v)=Lambda^j_lv/Dp,
    Kbase_(R_i,R_v)=Kbase^j_iv,
    Kbase_(R_i,p)=s_j mu^j_i,
    mu_(L_l)=2h_l/Dp for l<n_j,
    mu_p=2s_j-2A/Dp,
    mu_(R_i)=theta_j mu^j_i.                         (4.5)

All other entries are zero; coincident contributions are summed. For a
fully unrolled description the right-spine depth-l node starts at
2^r-2^l. Its left primitive block is Lambda^(l-1)/D_l; each right query
feeds its bridge with s_(l-1) times that right child's explicitly defined
mu. A query's optimum weight is2h_i/D_l if it is in that node's left
nonterminal block, or2s_(l-1)-2S_(l-1)/D_l if it is its bridge, multiplied
by product_(t=l)^(r-1)theta_t. The final terminal weight is
2product_(t=0)^(r-1)theta_t. Each query has exactly one such category.
Together with(4.3), these are finite original-index formulas for all rows.

The bridge sign follows because
(A+Bjoin)Dp-A(3A+2Bjoin)=A^2+4A*Bjoin+2Bjoin^2+A+Bjoin>0.
Also0<s_j<1 and theta_j>0. The primitive divergence cancels its left
optimum rows; the bridge divergence is2s_j, canceled by the incoming
right weights of total2s_j. Every right query's old flow is unchanged
since s_j+theta_j=1. Thus full flow(3.1) holds and sum mu=2.
All mu_i are strictly positive. The base has no incoming terminal rows.

Write T_R=sum_i mu^j_i g_(R_i),
x_p=x_0-sum_(l<n_j)h_l g_(L_l),
Rchild=Cbase^j(g_R)-Dc||T_R||^2/4,
and kappa_j=Dc-Dp theta_j^2. All original rows(4.5) give the identity

    Mbase_(j+1)(1/Dp)=Psrc_j(g_L)/Dp+Rchild
     +||x_p+(A-Dp s_j)g_p-(Dp theta_j/2)T_R||^2/Dp
     +(kappa_j/4)||T_R-2g_p||^2.                    (4.6)

For an independent coefficient audit the remaining polynomial after
subtracting Psrc/Dp and Rchild is

    ||x_p||^2/Dp+2(A/Dp-s_j)<x_p,g_p>
    +(A^2/Dp+2s_j)||g_p||^2-theta_j<x_p,T_R>
    +(Bjoin-s_j)<g_p,T_R>+Dc||T_R||^2/4.

Expanding the two squares in(4.6) agrees term by term because
s_j(3A+2Bjoin)=A+Bjoin. No child PSD or sign of kappa is needed for
this identity. At x_0=Dp and all gradients1, x_p=Dp-A and T_R=2;
every square argument is zero. The bracket in(4.4) has Y=Z=0.
Induction from ||x_0-g_0||^2 shows zero value AND zero derivative:

    Cbase^j e=D_j mu^j/2.                           (4.7)

Two further entries used below follow without any numerical computation:

    mu_0=2b/D,    Cbase_00=2/D.                     (4.8)

Here Cbase_00 denotes its first GRADIENT diagonal. In fact only the
first depth1 primitive bracket contributes when g_0=1 and every other
gradient is0; it contributes2-b^2. At the root objective join x_p=-b,
g_p=T_R=0. The x_p square contributes b^2/D, so(4.8) follows.

## 5. Exact paid two-row transport and the two coefficient laws

Write w78_i=2S-P_i, so S<=w78_i<=2S and every w78_i>0. Define the
fixed set E78 of dyadic matched pairs as follows. For each depth
1<=l<=m, each offset o=k*2^l with0<=k<2^(m-l), and
0<=r<2^(l-1), take

    a78=o+r, b78=o+r+2^(l-1).

These are exactly bit-flip edges of the m-dimensional binary cube on
all2^m original gradient indices0,...,n, oriented toward the larger
index. Both child terminal indices participate. Every source belongs
to at most m pairs, and index0 belongs to exactly m. Distinct pairs
are counted once. This is a sparse, explicit dyadic law; it is not a
separable product kernel or an inferred numerical support.

For one pair a78<b78 abbreviate a0=a78, b0=b78 in THIS paragraph only,
ell=P_b0-P_a0>0, wa=w78_a0, wb=w78_b0, so wa-wb=ell.
For a prescribed transfer delta>=0 set

    Tforward=delta*wa/ell, Tbackward=delta*wb/ell.     (5.1)

Add Tforward to K_(a0,b0) and Tbackward to K_(b0,a0), DECREASE the
original Q_(a0,star) multiplier by delta, and INCREASE the original
Q_(b0,star) multiplier by delta. The net value flow is zero at every
node because Tforward-Tbackward=delta. This is a paid change of the
optimum profile, not an additive balanced finite circulation.

The exact complete polynomial increment is

    Delta_ab=Tforward*(Q_ab+Q_(b,star)-Q_(a,star))
             +Tbackward*(Q_ba+Q_(a,star)-Q_(b,star))
       =<g_a-g_b,Tforward*y_b-Tbackward*y_a>.        (5.2)

To check the basic identity, expand the three Q rows. Their function
values cancel. The position terms equal<g_a-g_b,x_b>, and the
difference-square and optimum-square terms sum to
||g_b||^2-<g_a,g_b>. Hence Q_ab+Q_(b,star)-Q_(a,star)
=<g_a-g_b,y_b>. This proves(5.2) without an external theorem.

For BOTH laws put vartheta78=epsilon/m. Use the original base mu from
(4.5), without funding a debit from newly received mass. The ONLY TWO
choices of transfer are

    Law I:  delta_ab=vartheta78*mu_a;
    Law II: delta_ab=vartheta78*mu_a*(P_b-P_a)/w78_a. (5.3)

No additional law or tuned parameter search is part of this invocation.
Law II is the prescribed physical-distance attenuation of Law I.
For every original i,j the final coefficients are explicitly

    K78_ij=Kbase_ij
      +sum_((a,b) in E78)[1_(i=a,j=b)Tforward_ab
                        +1_(i=b,j=a)Tbackward_ab],
    nu78_i=mu_i-sum_((i,b) in E78)delta_ib
                  +sum_((a,i) in E78)delta_ai,
    xi78_i=0, K78_ii=0.                             (5.4)

Every decrement is paid: in either law delta_ab<=vartheta78 mu_a,
so the total outgoing debit at node a is at most epsilon*mu_a.
Thus nu78_i>=(1-epsilon)mu_i>0, and every finite coefficient is
nonnegative. Incoming optimum multipliers are explicitly zero as a
choice of these laws, not a deletion theorem. The total gross optimum
mass moved over the ENTIRE word is at most2epsilon, since sum mu=2.
Outgoing optimum mass remains exactly2. Law II adds at most4epsilon
finite multiplier mass because Tforward=vartheta78 mu_a and
0<Tbackward<=Tforward. Law I's exact finite added mass is
sum_(E78)delta_ab(wa+wb)/ell; it is not silently assumed small.
All added incoming and outgoing terminal rows remain part of(5.4).

## 6. Whole-word contact, initial/common and terminal charges

At the original Huber contact tuple x_0=D, g_i=1,

    y_i=D-P_i-1=w78_i.

Both vector factors on the right side of(5.2) vanish:
g_a-g_b=0 and Tforward*wb-Tbackward*wa=0. Consequently each increment
has zero value AND zero full derivative there, including its initial
coordinate. Combining this with(4.7) proves exactly

    C78 e=D nu78/2.                                 (6.1)

The changes in nu are therefore accompanied by changes in the entire
contact row-sum vector; the old optimum profile is not frozen. There
is ZERO incoming-optimum Huber cost, ZERO contact mismatch, and ZERO
per-node or per-level scalar Huber allowance. The one whole-word
initial/common block and its budget are precisely(3.4). At q=1/D
its kernel is(D,1); at q=21/(20D) it has determinant1/20. Neither
statement certifies the transverse space.

For completeness, let v be any gradient column with e^T v=0, and
write u=nu78^T v. Its coupling to the initial/common coordinates is
(-u/2,Du/2). At q=zeta/D, zeta>1, inversion of the whole block
[[zeta/D,-1],[-1,D]] gives the exact Schur expenditure D*u^2/4:
the numerator is D*u^2*(zeta-1)/4 and its determinant is zeta-1.
Thus the required residual is the COMPLETE matrix

    R78=C78-D*nu78*nu78^T/4,  R78 e=0.               (6.2)

Indeed the entire polynomial has the identity

    M78(q)=||x_0-(D/2)sum_i nu78_i g_i||^2/D
       +sum_(i,j)(R78)_ij<g_i,g_j>
       +(q-1/D)||x_0||^2.                           (6.3)

Conversely any negative R78 direction can have its common component
shifted to make sum nu78_i g_i=0, without changing R78; setting x_0=0
then gives a negative value at EVERY q. This explains why merely
raising q cannot repair a fixed exactly contacting array. It does
not require arbitrary alternative arrays to preserve exact contact.

There is also no omitted terminal cost. The pairs entering n have
sources a=n-2^l,0<=l<m. Let Tin be the sum of their Tforward values.
The base terminal diagonal is1 and has no incoming rows. Full flow(3.1)
therefore makes the actual new terminal gradient diagonal

    gamma78=1+Tin>0.

Writing C78 for(3.3) with the explicit coefficients(5.4), set

    vterm78=(nu78_n/2)x_0-sum_(i<n)(C78)_(i,n)g_i.

The exact terminal completion is

    M78(q)=gamma78*||g_n-vterm78/gamma78||^2
     +q||x_0||^2-sum_(i<n)nu78_i<x_0,g_i>
     +sum_(i,j<n)(C78)_ij<g_i,g_j>
     -||vterm78||^2/gamma78.                         (6.4)

All remaining coefficients in(6.4) are given by(3.3),(4.3),(4.5),(5.4).
An incoming-terminal-free square theorem is NOT applied to this altered
array. Equations(6.2)--(6.4) retain all initial, common, terminal and
cross-block expenditures. The failures below are evaluations of this
whole polynomial, before any coordinate is removed.

## 7. Law I: the first original gradient is already negative

Take x_0=0, g_0=1 and every other original gradient zero, including g_n.
For a pair0->v, write ell=P_v>0. Then y_0=-1, y_v=-b, where b=h_0
is the physical first step from Section4.
Equations(5.1)--(5.2) give

    Delta_(0,target)=-delta*[1+(b-1)*2S/ell]<0.      (7.1)

Every pair not containing0 contributes zero, regardless of its position
vectors, since g_a-g_b=0. There is no pair ending at0. The adjacent
pair(0,1) belongs to E78 and has ell=b, delta=vartheta78*2b/D.
Using the EXACT base diagonal(4.8), the complete value obeys

    M_I(q;g_0=1,x_0=0)
      <=(2/D)*[1-vartheta78*((b-1)D+1)]<0.          (7.2)

The final sign holds for every m>=1536: b-1>2/5,
vartheta78=1/(4m^2), and D>=2^(m+1)-1>10m^2. For the last elementary
inequality, it holds at m=16, and doubling2^(m+1)-1 and adding1
preserves it because2m^2>=(m+1)^2 for m>=3. No recurrence was evaluated
by a program. The inequality is therefore far inside its stated domain.

This is an original gradient diagonal with x_0=0, so changing q does
not alter the negative value. All final coefficients remain nonnegative,
all value flow and Huber contact equations hold, and the optimum profile
has changed. The PSD conclusion nevertheless fails. The mechanism is
specific: paying even a small FRACTION of an optimum row to a nearby
query requires contact-balancing finite weights of order2S/ell.
Those weights consume the first-gradient diagonal.

## 8. Law II fixes that diagonal, but the full deep-wave test fails

For Law II, Tforward=vartheta78*mu_a and
Tbackward=Tforward*w78_b/w78_a. On the first-gradient test, each of
its m outgoing pairs has value

    vartheta78*mu_0*(-b+w78_b/w78_0).

Therefore its entire diagonal is at least

    (2/D)-epsilon*mu_0*b
      =(2/D)*(1-epsilon*b^2)>0,                     (8.1)

since b^2<2. Thus the second law repairs the precise diagonal failure
of Law I. A different full-space test is required.

We derive the inherited negative wave, including ALL depths, here.
Let pterm_j=Psrc_j with only its terminal gradient1, and define

    Fterm_j=A_j^2(1+t_j)-2A_j+(lambda+1).

In(4.4) take Y=-(A_j-1),Z=-1. Expansion of the bracket is Fterm_j.
Thus the finite expression is

    pterm_j=sum_(l<j)(tstar-t_l)Fterm_l
                                product_(v=l+1)^(j-1)k_v. (8.2)

At depth1 pterm_1=2-b^2<3epsilon. The formulas(4.2) give
k_1<7,0<tstar-t_1<epsilon,Fterm_1<13, whence
pterm_2<34epsilon<2epsilon*A_2^2. The depth2-to3 new term is
negative. Its normalized old coefficient is strictly between0 and1,
so pterm_3/A_3^2<2epsilon even if the old coefficient is negative.

For l>=3, monotonicity and the exact t relation give

    t_3>c*tstar/(2+tstar)=(1+epsilon)*tstar,
    t_l-tstar>(2/5)epsilon.

Since A_l>=8 and t_l>2/5, Fterm_l>A_l^2. Together with
A_(l+1)<(5/2)A_l this yields

    (t_l-tstar)Fterm_l/A_(l+1)^2>(8/125)epsilon.

The propagation multiplier is exactly

    k_l/(2+t_l)^2
      =1-(t_l-tstar)(1+t_l)/(2+t_l)^2,
    1-epsilon<k_l/(2+t_l)^2<1, l>=2.                (8.3)

Use (1+t)/(2+t)^2<=1/4 and t_l-tstar<lambda epsilon<5epsilon/2
for the lower inequality. Every product of at most m such factors
is greater than (1-epsilon)^m>=1-m epsilon=3/4; the last inequality
follows by induction on the integer power. Unrolling(8.2) with these
bounds retains every negative contribution and gives

    pterm_j/A_j^2<[2-(6/125)(j-3)]epsilon,
                                      3<=j<=m.     (8.4)

Now take j=m-3, r=2^j-1 and Hstep=h_r=1+d_j. On the FULL original
scalar formal space assign

    x_0=0, g_0=-Hstep/b, g_r=1,
    g_i=0 for every other i, including the actual terminal n. (8.5)

The actual formal displacement is b*(-Hstep/b)+Hstep=0. All later
formal positions vanish. In the first H_j there is no primitive cross
coefficient between its first and terminal gradients: g_0 enters only
the first depth1 bracket of(4.4), while its final terminal enters disjoint
brackets. The first diagonal is2-b^2 and the final is pterm_j. The next
join has Y=0,Z=1 and contributes

    -Edef_j, Edef_j=(t_j-tstar)A_j^2(lambda+t_j)>0.

All larger primitive brackets have Y=Z=0. At the root objective join,
x_p=g_p=T_R=0. Consequently the ENTIRE base polynomial at any real q is

    D*Mbase(q;8.5)
      =(2-b^2)(Hstep/b)^2+pterm_j-Edef_j.            (8.6)

Here Hstep/(b A_j)<1/2, since A_j>5,d_j<A_j/2,b>7/5.
Combining(8.4),(8.6) and2-b^2<3epsilon, and discarding only the
strictly negative -Edef_j, proves

    Mbase(q;8.5)
      <[11/4-(6/125)(j-3)]epsilon*A_j^2/D.          (8.7)

No global-memory statement was used as a premise in this derivation.

It remains to bound EVERY Law II row increment, including terminal rows.
The complete proximal coordinates of(8.5) are

    y_0=Hstep/b,
    y_i=Hstep for1<=i<r,
    y_r=Hstep-1,
    y_i=0 for i>r.

For each dyadic pair u<v the increment is

    vartheta78*mu_u*(g_u-g_v)
                      *(y_v-(w78_v/w78_u)*y_u).    (8.8)

All possible nonzero cases are the following; they exhaust every pair:

* u=0,v<r: the increment is negative, since
  y_v-(w78_v/w78_0)y_0>=Hstep(1-1/h_0)>0.
* u=0,v=r: the increment is negative, since
  Hstep-1-Hstep/h_0>0; Hstep>7 and h_0>7/5 suffice.
* u=0,v>r: it is nonnegative and at most
  vartheta78*mu_0*Hstep^2/h_0^2. There are at most m such pairs.
* 0<u<r,v=r: it equals
  vartheta78*mu_u*[1-Hstep*(P_r-P_u)/w78_u]
  and is at most vartheta78*mu_u.
* u=r,v>r: it is negative.
* Neither endpoint is0 or r: it is exactly zero.

The sum over all positive contributions is therefore bounded by

    M_II(q;8.5)-Mbase(q;8.5)
       <=2epsilon*Hstep^2/(bD)+2epsilon/m.          (8.9)

This uses mu_0=2b/D, sum mu=2, and at most m source0 pairs. It DOES
NOT discard positive remote or terminal contributions. For A_j>=10,
Hstep/A_j<3/5 and b>7/5, so2Hstep^2/(bA_j^2)<18/35<1.
There are exactly three remaining SAME-m joins; hence
A_m<(5/2)^3 A_j and D<(125/4)A_j. In particular
2D/(m A_j^2)<125/(2m A_j)<1. Equation(8.9) is strictly less than
2epsilon*A_j^2/D. Combining with(8.7), j=m-3, yields the full-space bound

    M_II(q;8.5)
      <[19/4-(6/125)(m-6)]epsilon*A_(m-3)^2/D<0,
                       every m>=1536 and EVERY real q. (8.10)

The final numerical constants are exact rational inequalities; no
floating-point calculation or executed recurrence establishes them.
Law II has all signs, all original flow, all exact contact equations,
a changed optimum profile, and the positive first-gradient diagonal(8.1).
It still fails PSD in the full original space by(8.10).

## 9. Scope of the two failures and the remaining interface

The two concrete laws(5.3) are excluded in this joint UNVERIFIED draft.
Their common mechanism was contact-preserving transport of original
optimum mass across dyadic paired queries, with explicit paid debits.
Law I pays too much near the initial query because its finite-row mass
contains the physical factor2S/(P_b-P_a). Law II suppresses that factor;
its total available positive correction on the old deep wave then stays
below the accumulated negative base contribution. These are different
failures, both measured on the WHOLE original polynomial.

The draft does not exclude arbitrary transfer amounts, other pairing
supports, signed decrements of finite rows, alternative base arrays,
nonzero incoming optimum rows, non-contacting arrays at an inflated q,
or any unrestricted PEP certificate. No assertion is made that the raw
finite arrays belong to either of these laws. In particular the raw
arrays were never rounded or projected into the proposed family.
The counterdirections(7.2),(8.5) are formal PSD tests, NOT interpolable
primal trajectories: x_0=0 with a nonzero gradient is incompatible with
starting an actual differentiable function at its normalized minimizer.
They imply no lower bound on W.

What changed relative to the previous round is concrete: original
optimum-profile decrements/increments and nontrivial terminal incoming
mass are now observed in all three saved arrays; the full original
matrices act positively on finite-depth versions of the old displacement
space; two explicit nonseparable dyadic profile transports now have exact
sign, flow, contact and complete terminal/initial formulas. What remains
missing is a global nonnegative multiplier law with a FULL-space PSD proof
at a same-m bound such as21/(20D_m), or a static full primal witness
blocking that bound. Finitely many m<=6 samples cannot supply either.
This is a theorem-level interface, not a routine comparison or a fitted
profile. The original exact-profile target remains OPEN.

https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.12395 .

Skills used: query-memory, search-math-results, direct-proving,
